# Deviations from upstream

The port is exact: the same architecture, contracts, members and behaviour as upstream, file by file. This page is the register of every place where the Rust code nonetheless differs from the upstream code it ports. Each entry says what differs, why, and whether a user of the framework can observe it. A deviation that is not here is a bug to fix or to record.

Other pages hold the entries of their own area, and this page does not repeat them:

- `browser-platform.md` section 14: the browser backend and the browser hosts of the samples (host-level differences, upstream quirks not copied).
- `xaml.md`: the markup pipeline and the ahead-of-time compiler.
- `winui-composition.md` section 7: the port of the WinUI 3 composition API on top of the compositor (differences from WinUI). The changes it makes to upstream behaviour are listed in the section "WinUI composition" below.
- `winui-controls.md`: the native ports of WinUI 3 controls in `ferroui-controls` (differences from WinUI, per control). The WinUI members added to upstream-ported controls are listed in the section "WinUI additions to upstream controls" below.
- `winui-calculator-gaps.md`: the plan of the visual state manager, ListView, GridView, SwipeControl, Pivot and system high contrast; the differences from WinUI are recorded here and in `winui-controls.md` as each lands.
- `winui-charts.md` section 4: the port of the WinUI Charts public contract (`src/FerroUI.Controls.Charts`; differences from the published API and tests). The decisions the port makes where the implementation is not published are in its section 3.1.
- `PORTING-GUIDE.md`: the systematic mappings that apply everywhere (class model, nullability, events, generics, naming). An application of those rules is not a deviation and is not listed here.

## How to record a deviation

- Add a row to the table of the area, in the same pull request as the code. The pull request lists the deviation as well, as `CLOUD-WORKERS.md` asks.
- Put a comment at the site in the code, naming the upstream member and the difference, for example: `// Deviation (DEVIATIONS.md, Layout): upstream uses Stopwatch; ...`.
- Classify the entry:
  - **Representation**: Rust needs a different form (ownership, no `unsafe`, no array pools, unsigned lengths) and nothing observable changes.
  - **Behaviour**: something a user of the framework can observe differs: a value, an order, an exception, a log message or a timing.
  - **Missing**: an upstream step the port leaves out. It is either waiting for a member that is not ported yet (a seam) or deliberately left out.
  - **Test**: only a test or its support code differs.
- When a later change removes the difference, move the row to "Corrected divergences" with the pull request that removed it.

## Register

### Layout (`src/FerroUI.Base/layout/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RaiseEffectiveViewportChanged` computes the viewport of each listener by a walk from the root down to it (`CalculateEffectiveViewport`). | The viewports of the ancestors are kept for the pass by address and reused by the listeners below them, while a version of the geometry of the visuals of the thread (bounds, visual parents, `ClipToBounds`, `RenderTransform` and its value, `RenderTransformOrigin`) is unchanged; a handler that changes any of them empties the kept viewports before the next listener. | Representation | The same viewports with one walk per ancestor: a TreeDataGrid has a listener per realized row under the same ancestors, and the walks were the largest cost of scrolling it. | #242 |
| `LayoutManager.GetTimestamp` uses `Stopwatch.GetTimestamp()` (sub-millisecond ticks). | `get_timestamp` in `layout_manager.rs` reads `Dispatcher::current_dispatcher().now()`, which is in milliseconds. | Behaviour | The port times layout with the dispatcher's clock. Durations below 1 ms read as 0 in `LayoutPassTimed` and the layout time graph. | #25 |

### Colors (`src/FerroUI.Base/media/color.rs`, `hsl_color.rs`, `hsv_color.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `HslColor.ToRgb`, `HslColor.ToHsv`, `HsvColor.ToRgb` and `HsvColor.ToHsl` bring the hue into range with `while (hue >= 360.0) hue -= 360.0;` and `while (hue < 0.0) hue += 360.0;`, which never end for an infinite hue or one so large that subtracting 360 does not change it. | `wrap_hue` stops when a step does not change the hue and goes on with the hue as it is. | Behaviour | A hang on a non-finite or huge hue becomes a result. Finite hues of ordinary magnitude convert as upstream. | before #51 |
| `Color.Parse(string)` throws `ArgumentNullException` for null, and `TryParse(string?)` returns false for null. | `parse` and `try_parse` take `&str`, which cannot be null. | Representation | The non-nullable form of the string argument; `Parse_Throws_ArgumentNullException_For_Null_Input` and the null row of `TryParse_Returns_False_For_Invalid_Input` have no counterpart (`color_tests.rs`). | before #51 |

### Fonts (`src/FerroUI.Base/media/fonts/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FontCollectionBase.TryAddGlyphTypeface(familyName, key, glyphTypeface)` publishes the family (`AddFontFamily(new FontFamily(Key + "#" + familyName))`) when it creates the cache entry of the family, also when `glyphTypeface` is `null`: `SystemFontCollection.TryGetGlyphTypeface` caches the `null` of a family the platform cannot create, so a family that was only asked for becomes a family of `FontManager.SystemFonts`. | `FontCollectionBase::try_add_glyph_typeface_by_name` caches the miss and publishes the family with the first glyph typeface cached for it. A family with misses only is not listed. | Behaviour | A family listed by the system fonts carries the key of the collection (`fonts:SystemFonts#Name`), and `FontManager.TryGetGlyphTypeface` does not fall back to the default family for a family with a key, so `Typeface.GlyphTypeface` throws for it. Where the platform has no such font (a browser has no system fonts; fontconfig and DirectWrite answer nothing for an unknown name, CoreText substitutes a font), any text naming `Cascadia Mono,Consolas,Menlo` made those names system fonts, and a list of the system fonts with each name drawn in its own family (the ComboBox page of the catalog) ended the application. Upstream has the same defect. | Typeface of a missing family |
| `FontMetrics` has no cap height (`GlyphTypeface` reads the `OS/2` table for the ascent, the descent and the line gap only). | `FontMetrics::cap_height` (and `TextMetrics::cap_height`): the `sCapHeight` of the `OS/2` table from version 2, else the top of the outline of "H", else seven tenths of the ascent. | Addition | The box of a line trimmed to the cap height (`TextLineBounds`, Text block) needs it; DirectWrite gives WinUI the same value (`DWRITE_FONT_METRICS::capHeight`). | branch `text-line-bounds` |

### Render data (`src/FerroUI.Base/rendering/composition/drawing/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RenderDataWriter` blits unmanaged payload structs into the stream. | `render_data_writer.rs` and `render_data_reader.rs` encode payloads with `BatchValue`, the little-endian encoding of the batch transport. | Representation | A raw struct copy needs `unsafe`; the stream's format, opcodes and order are upstream's. | #26 |
| `RenderDataWriter` rents its buffer from `ArrayPool<byte>` and uses `int` lengths. | A `Vec<u8>` (initial capacity 256, amortised doubling) and `usize` lengths. | Representation | Rust's standard library has no shared array pool. | #26 |
| `RenderDataStream.Visit` has no default case: an invalid opcode loops forever. | `Visit` panics on `RenderDataOpcode::Invalid`. | Behaviour | A panic stops at the fault instead of hanging. Only reachable with a corrupt stream. | #26 |
| Each payload has a static `Opcode` field. | The associated const `IRenderDataPayload::OPCODE`. | Representation | The Rust form of a per-type constant. The tracking scanner does not match it, so `RenderDataPayloads.cs` shows 35/50 members. | #26 |
| The batch stream is made of 64-byte pooled segments. | No pooled segments. | Representation | The port's batch stream never had them, so `Round_Trip_Spanning_Multiple_Stream_Segments` cannot cross a segment boundary (see the test's header). | #26 |
| `ServerCompositor.CreateCompositionVisualSnapshot` returns the offscreen render target itself when it has no render context affinity. | It returns `IDrawingContextLayerImpl::create_shared_snapshot` of the target and disposes the target; the member is an addition to the layer contract. | Addition | A layer may own objects of the render thread and is not `Send`; the snapshot crosses to the UI thread. With Skia the snapshot of a raster surface shares its pixels until the surface is written again, and the target is disposed right after. | Render thread R1.3 |
| `IRef<T>` over render resources is shared between threads by convention. | `RefCounted<T>` holds an `Arc` and counts atomically; its release action is `Send`. | Representation | The counted references of bitmaps are held by batches. | Render thread R1.3 |
| An immutable brush or pen drawn by render data is sent to the render thread as the object itself. | Its shared form is sent: `SharedBrush` / `SharedPen` (`media/shared_brush.rs`), a copy of the values that implements the same contracts, created once by the immutable object and kept, with the identity of the values. | Representation | The handles the brush contract returns (transforms, gradient stops, the source of an image, the brush of a pen) are `Rc`, because mutable brushes are objects of the UI thread; an immutable brush holding them cannot be shared. The stable identity keeps caches keyed by a brush working (the stroke cache of a geometry). A brush type that only implements the contract is copied on every send. | Render thread R1.4b2 |
| A resource value of a composition object (an immutable brush, transform or gradient stop; a dash style) is written to the batch as the object. | Its wire form is written (`BatchResourceValue`: `SharedBrush`, `Matrix`, offset and colour; a dash style by value) and the server makes its own handle from it. | Representation | The handles are `Rc`; a batch only carries what is `Send`. A transform or a stop is a small value, so a copy costs what a count did. | Render thread R1.4c |
| `PlatformSpecificSceneInfo` is an `object`. | `Arc<dyn Any + Send + Sync>` from `ITopLevelImpl` to the server target. | Representation | The value is produced by the window implementation and read by the render target on the render thread. | Render thread R1.4c |
| `CompositionAnimation.CreateInstance` returns the animation instance, which the batch carries to the server. | `create_instance` returns an `AnimationInstanceFactory`: a `Send` closure over what the instance is made of, called on the server. The parameters are captured as a `PropertySetSnapshotSource` (values and ids), from which the snapshot is built there. | Representation | An instance resolves server objects and keeps its state in cells: its type is not `Send`, although nothing of the server is in it when it is sent. | Render thread R1.4d |
| A key frame of a composition animation holds the `IEasing` object, which the render thread evaluates. | It holds `IEasing::to_shared()`: the easing itself when it has no parameters, a copy of its parameters for the spline and spring easings (`KeySplineSolver` is the curve of a key spline as a plain value). A change of the parameters of an easing after the animation started does not reach a running animation. | Behaviour | An easing is an object of the UI thread; evaluating it on another thread while it can be changed is a data race that the original leaves to convention. | Render thread R1.4d |
| `Compositor.CreateCustomVisual(handler)` takes the handler object, which the render thread owns from then on. | `create_custom_visual` takes a `Send` closure that creates the handler on the render thread. A message for the handler is an `Arc<dyn Any + Send + Sync>`. | Representation | A handler keeps a handle to its server visual and state in cells: its type cannot cross threads, although nothing of the server is in it before it is attached. | Render thread R2 |
| `InvokeServerJobAsync` returns a `Task`; a failed job faults it with its exception. | `ServerJobTask<T>` holds the result under a lock, `T` is `Send`, the error is `ServerJobError` (`Arc<dyn Error + Send + Sync>`); continuations belong to the thread that created the task and run there, from its dispatcher when the job completed on another thread. | Representation | The job completes the task from the render thread. | Render thread R2 |
| The render surfaces of a target, the update of a drawing surface and the import and disposal of an interop object are captured by jobs of the render thread as they are. | Nothing a job captures is bound to the UI thread any more. The render surfaces are `Send + Sync` (R5.3). The jobs of a drawing surface and of the interop capture plain values and the server part of the imported object in a `LockBound` (next row). An image of a shared GPU context (`import_shared_image`) is the one object the two threads share as upstream: `ICompositionImportableSharedGpuContextImage: Send + Sync`, held in `Arc` by the caller, by the import job and by `IExternalObjectsRenderInterfaceContextFeature::import_shared_image` (R5.7). An implementation keeps what the render thread reads as plain values or behind a lock, and what belongs to the GL context of the caller in a `ThreadBound`. | Representation | Upstream shares these objects by convention and the garbage collector; here what crosses a thread says so in its type. No backend creates shared textures yet (upstream `GlSkiaSharedTextureForComposition`), so the contract has no implementation outside the tests. | Render thread R2, R5.6, R5.7 |
| `CompositionInterop`, `CompositionImportedGpuImage` and `CompositionImportedGpuSemaphore` are single objects used by both threads: the UI thread creates and keeps them, jobs of the render thread call `Import`, read `Image`/`Semaphore`/`IsUsable`/`Context` and `Dispose`. | Each has two parts. The object the caller holds stays on its thread: the compositor, the task of the import, and a handle (`Arc<LockBound<...>>`) of the server part. The server part (`ServerImportedGpuImage`, `ServerImportedGpuSemaphore`, the state of the interop) holds the context, the feature, the imported object and the outcome of the import; it is confined to the compositor lock by `LockBound<T>` (`server/compositor_lock.rs`): reached only with the server compositor in hand, dropped inside the lock. `ServerCompositionDrawingSurface` takes the server parts. `import_result` of the server part stands for the status of `ImportCompleted` that the jobs read upstream. | Representation | The objects of the render interface are `Rc` and cells, and the garbage collector that shares them upstream has no counterpart: a reference count touched by two threads has to be touched under one lock. The jobs already run under the compositor lock on either thread, so the lock is the owner. | Render thread R5.6 |
| The UI thread reads the feature and the context as they are: `SupportedImageHandleTypes`, `SupportedSemaphoreTypes`, `SupportedDmaBufFormats`, `GetSynchronizationCapabilities`, `IsLost`, `WrapImageHandleOnAnyThread`, `WrapSemaphoreHandleOnAnyThread`; `TryGetCompositionGpuInterop` constructs the interop in a job of the render thread. | The UI thread enters the compositor lock for each of them (`Compositor::with_server`), and `try_get_composition_gpu_interop` makes the server part of the interop (`CompositionInterop::server_part`: the context, the feature, the identifiers of the device) inside the lock where it reads the feature: in the job of the render thread that gives the features when they are not cached, otherwise on the thread of the compositor; that thread makes the interop around the part. `CompositionInterop::new` expects the feature handed over whole. | Behaviour | Same reason. A call may wait for the frame the render thread is rendering. | Render thread R5.6 |
| `Compositor.TryGetRenderInterfaceFeature` and `IRenderer.TryGetRenderInterfaceFeature` answer with the feature object itself (`object?`), which the UI thread keeps and calls while the render thread keeps the dictionary it came from (`GetRenderInterfacePublicFeatures`). | They answer with a future (`LocalFuture`, awaited on the thread of the compositor) of a `RenderInterfaceFeature` (`rendering/composition/render_interface_feature.rs`): a handle to the same feature, cloned from the map inside the compositor lock and bound to it (`LockBound<Rc<dyn Any>>`). The feature is lent, never the `Rc`: `with::<dyn IFoo, _>(..)` enters the lock for the call, `get::<dyn IFoo>(server)` serves a caller with the server compositor in hand, `is::<T>()` tells what it is registered as. The handle keeps the feature alive after its context is replaced, and is dropped inside the lock. `OpenGlCompositionInterop` and `CompositionGlContext` hold the handle of the texture sharing feature and ask it inside the lock. | Representation | A feature is an `Rc` in the map of the server compositor; a clone held by the UI thread would change the same count as the frame that drops the map with its context, without synchronisation. A call of a feature may wait for the frame the render thread is rendering. On a compositor confined to its render thread the handle is leaked by the thread of the compositor, as every `LockBound` is there. | Render thread R5.8 |
| The import job passes the caller's `IPlatformHandle` object to `ImportImage`/`ImportSemaphore` when the backend did not wrap it. | The job passes a `PlatformHandle` with the same `handle` and `handle_descriptor`, copied when the import was requested; a wrapped handle (`IExternalObjectsWrappedGpuHandle`) is made inside the lock and passed as it is. | Behaviour | The caller's handle is an `Rc` of the UI thread that the caller may keep. A backend that recovers a concrete handle type with `as_any` sees a `PlatformHandle` unless it wraps the handle. | Render thread R5.6 |
| `ServerCompositor` and the server objects are confined to `lock (_lock)` by convention (`VerifyAccess`). | `LockedServerCompositor` holds the server compositor and hands it out only inside `CompositorLock`; it carries `unsafe impl Send + Sync`. | Representation | The graph of server objects is made of `Rc` and cells, which the compiler binds to a thread; upstream binds it to a lock, and both threads render under it on macOS. The invariant (nothing reaches the graph outside the lock) is stated at the impl. | Render thread R5.1 |
| `PlatformRenderInterfaceContextManager` resolves `IPlatformRenderInterface` from `AvaloniaLocator.Current` when it creates the backend context. | It resolves it when the manager is created (and at the creation of the context only if it was registered later). | Behaviour | The service locator belongs to a thread here; the context may be created by the render thread. | Render thread R5.1 |
| The text renderer of the debug overlays is created by the first target that draws an overlay, wherever the frame runs; each target has its own. | One per server compositor, created on the UI thread under the compositor lock when debug overlays are switched on. | Behaviour | It is made from the default typeface, an object of the thread of the font manager; a frame may run on the render thread, which has no services. | Render thread R5.2 |
| `DrawingContextImpl` reads `SkiaOptions` from `AvaloniaLocator.Current`. | From the service locator of the thread, else from the options the backend was initialized with (`SkiaPlatform::options`). | Representation | The thread that draws may be the render thread. | Render thread R5.2 |
| `Compositor.UseUiThreadForSynchronousCommits` false with `Loop.RunsInBackground` true is tested where it matters (`MediaContext.SyncWaitCompositorBatch`, `GetRenderInterfacePublicFeatures`), each time it is asked. | The compositor decides once, when it is created in the render-thread mode: `Compositor::is_confined_to_render_thread`. A confined compositor binds the server graph to the first thread that renders it (`LockedServerCompositor::confine_to_current_thread`); a frame from another thread panics, and so does `render_on_this_thread`. | Behaviour | What the flag means for a render target that exists in one thread only (a canvas transferred to a worker) has to hold for the life of the compositor, including its release; upstream has no release to order. A loop that changes where it runs after the compositor was created is not followed. | Browser B2.4, `browser-render-worker.md` |
| The server compositor is never released: the garbage collector reclaims the graph, on its own thread. | A confined compositor does not release the graph in `Compositor::drop`. It lets go, on its thread, of the three handles the graph shares with that thread (`PlatformRenderInterfaceContextManager::release_platform_handles`: the platform graphics, its ready state feature, the render interface), and asks the loop task for the release; the next tick of the render thread drops the graph instead of rendering, and the task then has itself removed from the loop through the dispatcher. If no tick comes, the task keeps the graph, and the thread that drops the last handle leaks it (`Drop for LockedServerCompositor`). In the render-thread mode each side also has its own handle to the shared clock. | Behaviour | The graph holds the render targets and the graphics context, which only the render thread may drop; the handles it shares with the UI thread have counts that only the UI thread may change. A leak is what `ThreadBound` does in the same position. Render targets of a confined compositor whose loop has stopped are not released. | Browser B2.4 |
| `IPlatformGraphics` and `IPlatformGraphicsReadyStateFeature` are plain interfaces; the platform registers one object (`AvaloniaLocator.Bind<IPlatformGraphics>()`) that the UI thread and the render thread both hold, and `PlatformRenderInterfaceContextManager` gets the ready state with `TryGetFeature`. | `IPlatformGraphics: Send + Sync`, held as `Arc<dyn IPlatformGraphics>` by the platform, the service locator (bound as `Arc<dyn IPlatformGraphics>`), `Compositor::new`/`with_scheduler`/`with_render_thread`, `ServerCompositor::new` and the context manager. `IPlatformGraphicsReadyStateFeature: Send + Sync`, announced as an `Arc<dyn ...>` and read with `IOptionalFeatureProvider::try_get_shared`. `MetalPlatformGraphics` is `Send + Sync` by an `unsafe impl` argued from the native display (a stateless process-lifetime singleton with an atomic reference count). | Representation | The object is created by the thread of the platform and asked for the graphics context by the thread that renders, and each side clones and drops its handle: an `Rc` cannot be that handle, and the ready state is read by one thread while the other changes it. | Render thread R5.8 |
| `EglPlatformGraphics` may be asked for a context by any thread. | Its display (`Rc<EglDisplay>`) is bound to the thread that created the platform graphics (`ThreadBound`): `create_context` and `display()` panic on another thread. | Behaviour | The display and its contexts are `Rc` and cells and were not read for use by two threads (open item 6 of R5.5), so the object cannot honestly be `Send + Sync` otherwise. No platform creates these graphics yet; a compositor that renders on another thread needs a shareable display first. | Render thread R5.8 |
| Nothing releases what a server compositor drew with: its render targets and its backend context are finalized by the garbage collector. | Before the render thread drops the graph of a confined compositor it calls `ServerCompositor::release_gpu_resources`: with the graphics context of the compositor current, the layer and the render target of every active target and the backend context are released, and the context that was current before is restored. The function asks for nothing that would have to be created (it does not go through `ensure_valid_backend_context`), does nothing without a backend context and nothing for a lost graphics context. `ServerCompositionTarget::release_render_target` is the part of `reset_render_target` that does not make the context current. | Behaviour | A thread that renders several compositors has several graphics contexts, and between two frames any of them or none is current: an OpenGL object dropped then is deleted in the wrong context, or the call fails (in the browser: `glFinish` of Skia's context with no context current, an exception of the script that ended the frame loop of every view). Seen in the first run of a second view (`browser-render-worker.md`, "B2.7"). The graph of a compositor that is not confined is dropped as before. | Browser B2.7 |
| A batch that was committed is applied by the server compositor, which lives as long as the process. | `Compositor::drop` completes the batches that are still in its queue, without applying them (`BatchQueue::complete_all`: processed and rendered are signalled, the content is dropped), in every mode. | Behaviour | The graph of a dropped compositor applies nothing any more, and the media context commits no compositor while a batch it committed is not processed: a view that was closed and dropped stopped the drawing of every other view for good (`browser-render-worker.md`, "B2.7"). | Browser B2.7 |
| A server compositor renders for as long as it is in the render loop; the browser backend takes it out of the loop when its view is disposed (`Loop.Remove(Server)`). | `ServerCompositor::stop_rendering`: from then on a frame of the compositor applies the batches that arrive, drops their jobs, renders nothing and asks for no tick. The compositor stays in the loop until it is dropped. | Addition | The thread of the compositor waits for the batch that disposes the composition target after the surface is gone, so the batches still have to be applied; and a compositor whose graphics is not ready any more would ask for the next tick for ever. | Browser B2.7 |
| Work for the render thread captures the server compositor (`Compositor.Server`). | `Compositor::locked_server` hands out the `Arc<LockedServerCompositor>`: work that is queued for the thread that renders enters the graph with `try_with`, under the compositor lock, and is told when the graph is released. | Addition | The release of a closed surface cannot be a job of a batch: the batch of a view that is closed and dropped is not applied, and jobs do not run while the graphics are not ready. | Browser B2.7 |
| `IRenderLoop` has `Add`, `Remove`, `RunsInBackground`, `Wakeup`; `IRenderTimer` has `Tick` and `RunsInBackground`. `MediaContext.SyncWaitCompositorBatch` waits for the batch. | `IRenderLoop::request_frame_out_of_turn` and `IRenderTimer::request_tick_out_of_turn`, both with a default that does nothing; `DefaultRenderLoop` forwards the first to the second. The media context calls the loop's before `BatchCompletion::wait`. | Addition | A timer that ticks on the animation frames of a worker may not tick while the thread that waits holds the browser's main thread, or only a display frame later; it can implement the request by ticking once on its own thread. No timer in the tree implements it yet. | Browser B2.4 |
| `new CompositionInterop(compositor, externalObjects)` takes `compositor.Server.RenderInterface.Value`, which creates the context when there is none or the old one is lost. | `CompositionInterop::server_part` takes `RenderInterface.Value` where the calling thread may create the context: in the job of the render thread (the answer of `try_get_composition_gpu_interop` when the features are not cached), and on the thread of a compositor that is not confined. On the thread of a confined compositor (the features are cached) it takes the context the render thread has (`existing_backend_context`) and answers `None` without one; `CompositionInterop::new` panics in that case | Behaviour | The thread of a confined compositor may not create the context. | Browser B2.4 |
| The parts of an imported GPU object are reclaimed by the garbage collector. | A `LockBound` of a confined compositor that is dropped by a thread other than the render thread leaks its value. | Behaviour | The value holds objects of the render interface (the context, the feature, the imported image); the disposal of the image is a job of the render thread, and what remains must not be dropped elsewhere. The interop object of a confined compositor therefore leaks two reference counts when its owner drops it. No platform that is confined has the external objects feature today. | Browser B2.4 |
| A UI-thread composition object holds its server object (`CompositionObject.Server`), which lives while something refers to it. | The UI-thread object names its server object by a `ServerObjectId`, and the server compositor keeps the table from id to object. `Compositor::create_server_object` hands out an id and queues the creation for the next batch. A disposal keeps the object under its id (`dispose_and_keep_on_next_batch`); the drop of the UI-thread object releases it with a later batch, and the server takes it out of its table at the end of that batch. An id returns to the free list in the commit that writes its release, and once however often the release is asked for (`server_object_id_in_use`): a creation under the same id can only travel in a later batch, so an id is never handed out while its server object is alive. | Representation | The server graph belongs to the thread that renders: the UI thread cannot hold an `Rc` into it. | Render thread R1; the single return to the free list with the fix of the id reuse (no pull request yet) |
| `Compositor.OobDispose` calls `Dispose` of the target, which queues its disposal for the next batch as well, and sends a batch that disposes the server target, which exists since the constructor of the UI-thread target. | `oob_dispose` sends the batch of the disposal; when no commit has carried the creation of the server target yet, the creation is written into the same batch, before the disposal, and a disposal queued for the next batch is taken out of it. The server target is disposed, not released: it keeps its id until the UI-thread target is dropped, as any other composition object. | Representation | A creation travels in a batch here. The out-of-band batch overtook the batch of the creation: the server target was created after its disposal and never disposed, and its id, which was returned to the free list at once, was handed out to another object while the target was alive (`a server object id was reused while still alive`; the batch was lost and the media context committed that compositor no more). A top level closed before the first commit after its creation did that. | The fix of the id reuse (no pull request yet) |

### Server composition visuals (`src/FerroUI.Base/rendering/composition/server/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FpsCounter.RenderFps(context, managedBytes, nativeBytes, visitedVisuals, renderedVisuals, hasLayer, oldRect)` always draws the memory text ` M:.. / N:..` of the batch pools of the compositor (`BatchMemoryPool`, `BatchObjectPool`), between the FPS and the visual counts. | `FpsCounter::render_fps` takes the two sizes as `memory: Option<(u64, u64)>` and draws the memory text only for `Some`; `CompositionTargetOverlays` passes `None`, so the FPS line of the overlay has no memory text. | Missing | The compositor of the port has no batch pools to measure (see the waiver of `ServerCompositor.Batch*Pool` in `member-waivers.toml`); the counter keeps the text for a caller that has sizes to report. | upstream f4591beca4 (no pull request yet) |
| The `FpsCounter` constructor shapes its labels (`Frame #`, ` FPS: `, ` V:`, ` R:`) with the text renderer; the counter is created by the first overlay drawn, wherever that is. | `FpsCounter::new` takes the labels from `DiagnosticTextRenderer::shape_label`, which shapes a label once and shares it; `ServerCompositor::diagnostic_text_renderer` shapes the four labels (`FpsCounter::LABELS`) when it creates the renderer on the UI thread. | Representation | Shaping creates glyph runs, whose platform implementation comes from the services of the UI thread; the counter is created by the first frame of the render thread, which on the browser with threads has no services (the render thread panicked there with "Unable to locate IPlatformRenderInterface"). | upstream f4591beca4 |
| The server visual of an adorner subscribes to the transforms of the ancestors of the visual it adorns with a delegate (`AttHelper_SubscribeToActNotification(GetAttHelper().AdornedVisualActSubscriptionAction)` in `OnAdornedVisualChanged`), which the set of the adorned visual holds; it is removed only when the adorner is given another visual (`OnAdornedVisualChanging`). An adorner that is no longer used stays in that set, and alive, for as long as the visual it adorned. | The entry is a weak reference (`ActSubscriber`; `server_composition_visual/act.rs`), and a server visual that is disposed takes its entry out of the visual it adorns (`IServerObject::dispose`, where it also leaves its cache mode; `server_composition_visual/mod.rs`). | Representation | A server visual is disposed by the UI thread when its visual is dropped, where upstream leaves it to the collector, and the collector does not free an adorner the adorned visual still refers to. The weak entry kept the memory of the disposed adorner instead: a control kept one for every focus adorner it ever had, 0.9 KB each time the keyboard moved the focus to it (catalog gap C331: +2.3 MB for every tour of the catalog with interaction, from the controls of the main view; `GAPS.md`, "What interaction retains"). Nothing observable changes: a disposed adorner is never updated again. | 2026-10-09 |

| A render target that cannot begin a frame throws: `RenderTargetNotReadyException` or `RenderTargetCorruptedException` out of `IRenderTarget.CreateDrawingContext`, through `ISkiaGpuRenderTarget.BeginRenderingSession` and `IGlPlatformSurfaceRenderTarget.BeginDraw`. `ServerCompositionTarget.Render` catches the two around `CreateDrawingContext` and leaves the frame out (`Rendering/Composition/Server/ServerCompositionTarget.cs`, lines 205 to 220 at the tracked commit: not ready sets `IsWaitingForReadyRenderTarget` and returns, corrupted returns; the target is released and made again by the state check of the next pass, lines 151 to 158). | The members that return a drawing context or a session are kept, and each has a second member that returns the two conditions as an error, `RenderTargetError` (`platform/i_render_target.rs`): `IRenderTarget::try_create_drawing_context`, `ISkiaGpuRenderTarget::try_begin_rendering_session`, `IGlPlatformSurfaceRenderTarget::try_begin_draw`, and `try_begin_draw` and `try_begin_draw_core` of the two EGL render target traits. Every one has a default that calls the member without the error, so a target of another backend is as it was (a condition it cannot report is still a panic the render loop catches and logs). The compositor calls `try_create_drawing_context` and does what the two catches do. Overridden by the render target of the Skia backend, its OpenGL target, the EGL window surface (a lost context) and the texture render target of ANGLE on Windows (the corrupted target of a DirectComposition surface). The same two catches around the creation of the render target (lines 160 to 181) have no counterpart yet: `create_render_target` of the port returns a target. | Representation | An exception the caller catches is a result in Rust. A panic that is caught still runs the panic hook (twenty messages with backtraces of the timer thread in the integration tests of the Windows backend through DirectComposition, run 38067972798) and ends the process where panics abort. | 2026-10-10 |
| `ServerCompositionDrawListVisual` replays its render data into the drawing context every time it is drawn (`RenderCore`). | A drawing context may be an `IDrawingContextImplWithFragments` (`platform/i_drawing_context_impl_with_fragments.rs`, reached with `as_drawing_context_impl_with_fragments`, `None` by default): the draw-list visual then keeps the fragment the backend made of its commands and has the backend draw it again in later frames, until the commands or one of their resources change; the backend draws it only where it gives the pixels the commands give, and otherwise the commands are replayed. Only the GPU mode of the Vello backend keeps fragments. | Representation | The classic renderer of the Vello project appends a recorded scene to another (`Scene::append`) for a fraction of the cost of recording it again; nothing observable changes (`gpu/fragment_tests.rs` of the Vello backend). `vello-performance.md`, section 5.6. | #268 |

### Visual tree (`src/FerroUI.Base/visual_tree/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TransformToVisual` finds the common ancestor of the two visuals (both walked up to the root to compare their depths) and then the offsets of both from it. | `transform_to_visual` first walks up from the visual looking for `to`; when `to` is the visual or one of its ancestors the offset found on the way is the transform. Otherwise it continues as upstream. | Representation | The same matrix in one walk for the common case (a descendant translated into a container: scroll anchoring, bringing into view). Each step of a walk upgrades a weak handle and reads a property in the port, where upstream reads a field; scrolling a TreeDataGrid spent most of its time there. | #240 |
| The transforms of the visual tree read `RenderTransform` of every visual they pass. | A visual keeps whether `RenderTransform` has a value (set by the change handler of the property), and the transforms of the visual tree read the property only when it has one. | Representation | A property read of the port goes through the value store and its metadata table, which upstream's field-backed read does not pay; most visuals have no render transform. | #240 |
| `VisualLocator.Track(Visual, int ancestorLevel, Type?)`: a negative level makes `ElementAtOrDefault` return null. | `VisualLocator::track` takes `ancestor_level: usize`. | Representation | An unsigned level, as `VisualAncestorElementNode` already took; a negative level cannot be passed. | #27 |
| `Visual` subscribes to the values of its render-affecting properties with a weak event (`InvalidatedWeakEvent`, `TargetWeakEventSubscriber`): the list of the value holds the visual weakly and drops the entries of collected visuals when it is compacted. | The handler holds the visual weakly, and a visual that is dropped disposes its subscriptions (`impl Drop for Visual`; `visual.rs`). | Representation | Without it the list of a value many visuals share (the geometry of an icon that is a resource) kept one entry for every visual that ever drew it, with the memory of the visual its weak reference held (catalog gap C330: 6 KB for every navigation of the catalog, from the two path icons of the back button). The bound is upstream's, reached at the drop instead of a compaction. | 2026-10-09 |

### Logical tree (`src/FerroUI.Base/logical_tree/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ControlLocator.ControlTracker` holds the element it tracks from (`_relativeTo`) and the ancestor it found (`_value`) as references; `VisualLocator.VisualTracker` holds `_relativeTo` the same way. | Both trackers hold them weakly (`logical_tree/control_locator.rs`, `visual_tree/visual_locator.rs`). | Representation | A tracker is held by the subscription of the ancestor node of a binding of the element it tracks from: held strongly the element keeps itself alive, and the ancestor with it (catalog gap C319: every element with a `$parent` binding). A tracker whose element is gone publishes nothing more. | 2026-10-09 |
| `LogicalExtensions.GetLogicalChildren` returns the `LogicalChildren` collection itself; `GetLogicalSiblings` enumerates the parent's collection lazily. | `get_logical_children` and `get_logical_siblings` return a snapshot (`Rc<Vec<..>>`) of the collection. | Behaviour | As `get_visual_children` does. A change to the collection after the call is not seen through the result. | #32 |
| `LogicalExtensions.IsLogicalAncestorOf(this ILogical? logical, ILogical? target)` accepts a null receiver and returns false. | `StyledElement::is_logical_ancestor_of(&self, target: Option<&StyledElement>)`. | Representation | The receiver of a Rust method cannot be null; the target stays optional. | #32 |
| `ControlLocator.Track(ILogical, int ancestorLevel, Type?)`: a negative level makes `ElementAtOrDefault` return null. | `ControlLocator::track` takes `ancestor_level: usize`. | Representation | As `VisualLocator::track`; a negative level cannot be passed. | #36 |

### Name scopes (`src/FerroUI.Base/controls/name_scope.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NameScope` holds every element it names as a reference, the element it is attached to (`NameScope.SetNameScope`) among them. `INameScope` has `Register`, `Find`, `FindAsync`, `Complete` and `IsCompleted`. | The scope holds the element it is attached to weakly and every other element as a reference. `NameScope::set_name_scope` tells the scope which element holds it: `INameScope::attached_to` and `detached_from`, two members with empty defaults that the managed interface does not have; `ChildNameScope` passes them to its own scope. | Behaviour | The root of a document holds its name scope and the scope the elements it names: a root with a name of its own kept itself alive, which the collector frees upstream (catalog gap C321). A find returns the element while it is alive, whenever it was registered, and the name of an element that is gone stays taken. What differs: a scope that something else keeps alive after its root was dropped no longer finds the root by its name. | 2026-10-09 |

### Routed events (`src/FerroUI.Base/interactivity/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `InteractiveExtensions.AddDisposableHandler` captures the object in the disposable it returns. | The disposable holds the object weakly (`interactive_extensions.rs`); disposing it after the object is gone does nothing. | Representation | An element that keeps the disposable of a handler it added to itself held itself through it (catalog gap C324: a slider and the handler of its own pointer events). The handlers of an object that is gone are gone with it, so nothing is left to remove. | 2026-10-09 |

### Data validation (`src/FerroUI.Controls/data_validation_errors.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DataValidationErrors.OwnerProperty` is a `DirectProperty<DataValidationErrors, Control?>`, and the control holds its owner (`_owner`). | The value type of the property is `Option<ElementRef<Control>>`: the owner is held weakly. `owner()` and `set_owner` have the handle of the control as before. | Representation | The owner is the control the errors are shown in, which owns the errors control through its template; the themes also make it the data context of a part of that template (`DataContext="{TemplateBinding Owner}"`), which now holds the element reference (see the row of element references under Bindings). Held as handles, every control with validation in its template stayed alive after it left the tree (catalog gap C322: the text box, combo box, numeric up-down, date pickers, auto-complete box and slider of the Fluent theme). | 2026-10-09 |

### Input (`src/FerroUI.Base/input/`, `src/FerroUI.Controls/tool_tip_service.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `IInputRoot.RootElement` of a presentation source is its root visual, which is null once the top-level has closed; the code that still has the root of an event that closed it reads null (`ToolTipService.InputManager_OnProcess`: `e.Root.RootElement == _tipControl?.VisualRoot`). | `IInputRoot::root_element` returns the element and panics for a closed presentation source; `IInputRoot::try_root_element` returns `None` there, and the tooltip service compares that. | Representation | The element of a root is not optional in the port. The event of a press that closes the window it was delivered to (an item of a drop-down) is still handed to the tooltip service with that window as its root: the service panicked (found by the tours with interaction, `GAPS.md`). | 2026-10-09 |
| `TextInputMethodClient.TextViewVisual` is declared `Visual`, not nullable, and `TextBoxTextInputMethodClient` returns `_presenter!`, which is null for a text box without a template; `InputMethodManager` hands `_client?.TextViewVisual` to its transform tracker, and `AvaloniaNativeTextInputMethod` and the `TextInputResponder` of iOS check it for null. | `TextInputMethodClient::text_view_visual` returns `Option<Ref<Visual>>`; the client of the text box returns its presenter, `None` without one. The input method manager tracks no visual for `None`, the native input method sets no cursor rectangle and the responder of iOS returns its empty position, as upstream does with null. The test doubles that have no visual (Browser, D-Bus, Android, Windows) return `None`. | Representation | The value upstream declares and the one it returns differ: a Rust reference has no null, and the nullable form is the one of what upstream returns. Before 2026-10-15 the contract was `Ref<Visual>` and the client of the text box answered with the last presenter it had, and panicked ("The text input method client has no text view visual.") when it never had one: a text box without a template that got the focus with an input method active (`win32-platform.md`, section 13). `text_box_without_template_is_focused_with_an_input_method` (`text_box_tests.rs`). | 2026-10-15 |
| `FocusHelpers.GetInputElementChildren` returns the input elements among the visual children, with the comment "TODO: add control overrides to return custom focus list from control"; `CanHaveFocusableChildren` walks the same children. `ICustomKeyboardNavigation` has `GetNext` only. | The TODO is filled with WinUI's `UIElement.GetChildrenInTabFocusOrder`: the virtual `InputElement::children_in_tab_focus_order` (default `None`, the visual children as upstream) names the children of an element in tab order, and `get_input_element_children` and `can_have_focusable_children` (`focus_helpers.rs`) take them, so tab navigation (`navigation/tab_navigation.rs`, `focus_manager.rs`) skips a child the element leaves out and follows its order. `ICustomKeyboardNavigation` is unchanged. | Behaviour | Missing in upstream (TODO filled). A virtualizing panel (WinUI's `ItemsRepeater`) keeps recycled elements as visual children and shows its items in another order than its children have. The hook is a virtual of the element, not a member of `ICustomKeyboardNavigation`: a custom navigation handler is the nearest ancestor that has one (`KeyboardNavigationHandler::find_custom`), so a panel that became one would hide the handler of a `TreeView` around it. | 2026-10-23 |

### Property system (`src/FerroUI.Base/property_store/`, `src/FerroUI.Base/ferro_object.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EffectiveValue<T>` compares values with `EqualityComparer<T>.Default.Equals`, and `Animatable` and the boxed values with `object.Equals`: to both a `double` or `float` that is not a number equals another that is not a number. | Values are compared with `value_equals` (`ferro_property.rs`): `==`, and NaN with NaN for `f64`, `f32` and their options; the effective value uses it at every comparison upstream makes with the comparer, and the equality of an untyped value (`dyn AnyValue`) is it. | Representation | `PartialEq` of the floating-point types is the IEEE comparison, to which NaN differs from itself. With it a width that is not set was a new base value every time it was set again, and a transition of such a width started another transition from the report of its own first value until the stack overflowed (the pane of the split view of the Fluent theme; found by the tours with interaction, `GAPS.md`). A structure that holds floating-point fields compares them with `==` in both implementations. | 2026-10-09 |
| A `BindingEntry` subscribes itself to its source (it is the observer), and is collected with its value store when the object is, subscribed or not. | The observers of an entry are objects of their own that hold the entry weakly, and an entry that is dropped while it is subscribed disposes its subscription (`Drop for BindingEntry`; `property_store/binding_entry.rs`). | Representation | The entry holds its source and the source its observers: an entry whose value store was dropped with its object, without the binding being disposed, stayed alive with the source and the last value (catalog gap C320: the content of a scroll viewer, which its presenter binds from the viewer). No difference for an entry whose frame is alive. | 2026-10-09 |
| `LocalValueBindingObserver<T>` and `DirectBindingObserver<T>` subscribe themselves to their source, and are collected with the value store that holds them when the object is, the binding disposed or not. | The observers the source holds are objects of their own that hold the binding observer weakly, and a binding observer that is dropped with its value store disposes its subscription (`Drop`; `property_store/local_value_binding_observer.rs`). | Representation | The source holds its observer and the observer its subscription: when the object went without the binding being disposed, the two stayed alive, with the memory of the object their weak references held (catalog gap C327: the default data template binds the text of its text block to an observable of the data context of the block, and every such block of a page left 2.2 KB). The value store holds the observer for as long as the binding lasts, so nothing differs while the object is alive. | 2026-10-09 |
| `ValueStore` propagates inherited values over `GetInheritanceChildren()` by index up to the count at the start; an index past the end throws. | `FerroObject::for_each_inheritance_child` stops at an index past the end, and skips a child that was dropped without being detached. | Behaviour | The port's list holds weak references, and `inheritance_children` prunes dropped children, which can shorten the list during the loop; upstream's list of strong references cannot shrink that way. | #29 |
| `ValueStore.SetInheritanceParent` pairs the old and the new inherited values in an `AvaloniaPropertyDictionary`, which is sorted by property ID, and calls `InheritedValueChanged` for the pairs that differ in that order. | `ValueStore::set_inheritance_parent` keeps the pairs in the order it finds them (the nearest old ancestor first, each store by property ID, then the properties only the new ancestors hold) and raises them in that order (`value_store.rs`). | Behaviour | Found while verifying performance design 03, not introduced by it. The order across properties differs when more than one ancestor store contributes values; within one property the order over the objects is upstream's. Property IDs follow the order of registration, which is not the same in the port as in upstream, so neither order reproduces upstream's sequence exactly. Sorting the pairs by ID would change the order of notifications of the port and needs a decision. | Performance design 03 |

### Bindings (`src/FerroUI.Base/data/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A reference of an interface type is the object that implements it: untyped code (`content as Control`, a binding path on it) sees the object whatever the static type. | The handle of an interface is an adapter (`Rc<dyn IFoo>`, PORTING-GUIDE: a class that implements an interface). Untyped code sees the object behind it when the contract registers how to reach it (`ValueTypes::register_interface_object::<Rc<dyn IFoo>>(\|h\| h.as_object())`); `ValueTypes::as_object` then answers the object, so a content control presents a control held by the handle as the control, and a binding converts the handle to the handles of the class. The handle of a contract that registers nothing is an opaque value (a content shows its text). | Representation | `Ref<T>` cannot be an `Rc<dyn IFoo>`, and the object behind an adapter is known only to the contract (its `as_object` hook). The class states the conversion of its handles to the interface (`ferro_class_info!`, `interfaces:`); the reverse is stated once by the contract, where its property is registered (`GpuDemo.Demo` of the GpuInterop sample). | framework-fixes-gpuinterop (no pull request yet) |
| An `ExpressionNode` holds its value (`_value`) as a reference, whatever the node. | The nodes that locate an element from the target of the binding hold their value weakly: `NamedElementNode`, `LogicalAncestorElementNode`, `VisualAncestorElementNode` and `TemplatedParentNode` (`NodeState::locating_an_element`, `data/core/expression_nodes/expression_node.rs`). The value of every other node is held as upstream holds it. | Representation | The element such a node finds is kept alive by the tree it was found in, and that tree most often owns the target of the binding, which owns the binding and its nodes (catalog gap C319: the presenter of a scroll viewer of the Fluent theme, whose gesture recognizer binds to it by name). The value reads null once the element is gone, where upstream would still hold it. | 2026-10-09 |
| `ParentDataContextNode` holds the parent of its source (`_parent`) as a reference. | The parent is held weakly (`WeakRef<FerroObject>`; `data/core/expression_nodes/data_context_node.rs`). | Representation | The node belongs to a binding of the element, which the parent owns as its child: held strongly the two keep each other alive (catalog gap C318: an element whose `DataContext` is bound, and its parent). No difference while the parent is alive. | 2026-10-09 |
| `ElementNameElement` of a compiled binding path holds the name scope (`NameScope`) as a reference. | `PathElement::ElementName` holds it weakly (`Weak<dyn INameScope>`; `data/compiled_binding_path.rs`); `CompiledBindingPathBuilder::element_name` takes the scope as before. | Behaviour | The binding of a setter of a style declared under a named element, or under an element with a named ancestor, is held by that element, and the scope holds the element: held strongly the element, the scope and everything it names stayed alive (catalog gap C329: the Focus page of the catalog). The node the path creates has always held the scope weakly, as a reflection binding holds its `NameScope` upstream. What differs: a path whose scope is gone when an instance of the binding is created (a setter of a style kept after the tree that declared it was dropped, or declared in a data template, whose scope nothing else holds) creates a node without a scope, which reports "NameScope not found." where upstream would find the name. | 2026-10-09 |
| The delegate a reflection binding reads for a method (`ReflectionMethodAccessorPlugin.Accessor`: `method.CreateDelegate(PropertyType, target)`) has its target as a reference, and so has the command the converters make from it (`MethodToCommandConverter`: the compiled lambdas close over `Expression.Constant(target)`; only its subscription to the property changes of the target is weak, `WeakPropertyChangedProxy`). The command of a compiled binding path has its target weakly (`MethodCommandNode.Command`, `WeakReference<object?> _target`). | The delegate of a method of a binding source holds the source weakly (`MarkupDelegate::for_method_of_source`, which `MethodAccessorPlugin` creates; `metadata/markup_type.rs`), and `MethodToCommandConverter` has the target only through its delegate. Once the source is gone the delegate has no target and does nothing when it is invoked, and the command cannot execute and does nothing when it is executed, as the command of a compiled path. A delegate created with `MarkupDelegate::for_method` keeps its target as before. | Behaviour | The accessor is a node of the binding and the command the value of the property that is bound, both owned by the target of the binding. A template that binds the command of a part to a method of the templated control (`Command="{Binding LineUp, RelativeSource={RelativeSource TemplatedParent}}"`, the scroll buttons of the menu scroll viewer of the themes) is a cycle: the control, its template, the part, its command, the control. The collector frees it upstream; here every menu that was opened stayed with its items (catalog gap C332). What differs: an object that nothing but such a delegate or command holds is freed, where upstream it lives as long as the delegate. | 2026-10-09 |
| A value that is a control is the control wherever a binding reads it; there is no weak form of a value. | A registered element reference (`ElementRef<T>`, the value type of properties that point at an element they do not own) that is an untyped value is the element it refers to for a binding: `ValueTypes::as_object` resolves it while the element is alive, and the source a binding holds weakly (`WeakValue`) is rebuilt as the reference (`ValueTypes::from_object`; `data/core/value_type.rs`). | Representation | A `DataContext` that is the element itself or an element above it is a reference up the tree: as a handle it keeps the tree alive, which the collector resolves upstream. Stored as an element reference it does not, and the bindings against that data context (a property path, a cast to the class of the element) read the element through it (catalog gaps C322 and C326). Once the element is gone the value reads as no object. | 2026-10-09 |
| `TypeUtilities.TryConvert(to, value, culture)` (the default value converter, the reflection target type converter) and the default target type converter convert between numbers, dates and text with `Convert.ToString(value, culture)`, `Convert.ChangeType(value, to, culture)` and the type converters of the runtime. | `ValueTypes::try_convert_with_culture` formats a number or a date as the culture does (`composite_format`), and converts text to a number by replacing the signs, the decimal separator and the group separators of the culture with the invariant ones and applying the invariant conversion; text is not converted to a date with the culture, and the conversions types declare (`parse:`, registered conversions) do not receive the culture. The invariant culture is `try_convert` unchanged. | Behaviour | The conversion table of `ValueTypes` holds functions without a culture. Numbers written with the common symbols convert as upstream; a text the number parser of the runtime reads differently (currency or parenthesised forms are not affected: `Convert` does not accept them either; an exponent with a localised sign is) can differ. The current culture is the invariant culture until an application sets one, so nothing changes without a culture. | #71 |
| `TypeUtilities.Conversions` and `ImplicitConversions` convert a `char`, a UTF-16 code unit, to and from the integer types with `Convert.ChangeType`: every code from 0 to 0xFFFF is a character, a lone surrogate among them. | `char` is a Unicode scalar value: an integer of the surrogate range (0xD800 to 0xDFFF) does not convert to a character, and a character above 0xFFFF, which upstream does not have, converts to the integer types that hold its code (not to the 16-bit ones) and from no integer (`character_conversions!`, `implicit_numeric`, `data/core/value_type.rs`). | Representation | The character type of the language cannot hold a surrogate. Every other cell of the rows and columns of the boolean and of the character converts or is refused as `Convert.ChangeType` converts or fails. | 2026-10-09 |
| The default `TargetTypeConverter` converts a value to text with `value.ToString()`, which formats with the current culture whatever the converter culture is. | The conversion to text uses the culture `try_convert` is given (the converter culture), like every other conversion of the target type converter. | Behaviour | One conversion function serves both target type converters; the two cultures differ only when a binding names a converter culture other than the current one and has a compiled path to a text target without a converter. | #71 |
| `CultureInfoIetfLanguageTagConverter` is a `TypeConverter` that the `ConverterCulture` property of the four bindings names with `[TypeConverter]`. | `CultureInfoIetfLanguageTagConverter` is a type with the two functions of the converter (`can_convert_from`, `convert_from`); the markup metadata of `CultureInfo` names `convert_from` as its `parse:`, so every property of the type takes an IETF language tag, the converter culture among them. | Representation | The type converter contract is in `ferroui-markup-xaml`, above the base crate, and a type states its conversion from text with `parse:` (`PORTING-GUIDE.md`, "Text conversion"). `ConverterCulture="ar-SA"` loads as upstream; a property of the type that upstream converts with `CultureInfoConverter` (a culture name) accepts the same names, since the names of the cultures are IETF tags, except the three names the IETF conversion refuses. | #71 |
| A binding path can be built from a LINQ expression tree: `CompiledBinding.Create<TIn, TOut>(Expression<Func<TIn, TOut>>)` through `BindingExpressionVisitor<TIn>`, with the markers of `StreamBindingExtensions` for `^`. | Not ported. The path is written with `CompiledBindingPathBuilder` (which is what the visitor produces) and given to `CompiledBinding::new`. | Missing | Rust has no expression trees: a closure cannot be inspected. The tests of `CompiledBindingTests_Create` are ported with paths written by hand; the 36 tests of `BindingExpressionVisitorTests` have no subject. | before this change |
| String paths (`ReflectionBinding`) and the dynamic plugins read members by reflection over the run-time type of the source. | They read the markup metadata (`metadata::MarkupType`) and the binding metadata (`data::model::ModelTypes`) the type of the source declares. | Behaviour | There is no reflection. A member of a type that declares no metadata is not found, and the binding reports the error upstream reports for a missing member (`xaml.md` 3.7). | before this change |
| `TypeUtilities.TryConvert` converts a value that is not of an enumeration to an enumeration with `Enum.ToObject(to, value)` once the value converts to the underlying type: any number becomes a value of the enumeration, the value of a member or not. | `ValueTypes::try_convert` converts a number to a plain enumeration only when it is the value of a member, and to a set of flags when it is a combination of the bits of its members (`MarkupType::enum_from_value`); any other number does not convert, and the binding reports that it could not convert the value. The conversion of an enumeration to a number (`Convert.ChangeType((int)value, to)`) is the one of upstream. | Behaviour | An enumeration of the port holds its members only: there is no value of it for a number no member has. A binding of a number to a property of an enumeration (the selected index of a combo box to a placement, as the ControlCatalog does) differs only for an index outside the members. | 2026-10-09 |
| `DataAnnotationsValidationPlugin` validates a property against its `System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations` attributes. | Not ported; the registered data validators are `IndeiValidationPlugin` and `ExceptionValidationPlugin`. | Missing | Members have no attributes, and the port declares no validation attributes in metadata. A model reports validation errors through `INotifyDataErrorInfo` or by failing in its setter. | before this change |
| `BindingValue<T>.FromUntyped` converts null to `T` when `T` is a reference type or `Nullable<>`. | `BindingValue::<T>::from_untyped` converts null only when `T` is a nullable form with a registered null value (`Option<X>`); for any other `T` null gives a binding error. | Representation | A Rust `T` has no null of its own; nullable properties are typed `Option<X>`. | this change |
| `BindingValue<T>.ToString()` of a value without a value prints the default of `T` (`0` for a number). | `Display` prints `(null)`. | Behaviour | The port holds no default value for the "no value" states (`Option<T>`). Only the text of an unset or do-nothing binding value of a value type differs. | this change |
| `CompiledBinding.Create<TIn, TOut>(Expression<Func<TIn, TOut>>, ...)` builds a compiled binding path from a LINQ expression tree through `BindingExpressionVisitor<TIn>`. | Not ported; a path is built with `CompiledBindingPathBuilder`. `BindingExpressionVisitorTests.cs` is not ported. | Missing | Rust has no expression trees. Design note and options in `xaml.md` 3.6.1; the decision is with the owner (`CONTINUATION.md`). | #51 |
| `DataContextNode.OnPropertyChanged` is `if (sender == Source && e.Property == StyledElement.DataContextProperty) SetValue(e.NewValue)`: the node takes the value of the notification. `ParentDataContextNode.OnParentPropertyChanged` does the same. | The handlers of `DataContextNode` and `ParentDataContextNode` read the data context of the object again when the notification arrives (`expression_nodes/data_context_node.rs`). | Behaviour | Found while verifying performance design 05, not introduced by it. The two are the same value unless the data context is changed again before the handler runs (by a handler of the same notification that was registered earlier): upstream then sets the value of each notification in turn, the port sets the latest value each time. Taking the value of the notification would change what the node publishes in that case and needs a decision. | Performance design 05 |

#### What stands for the reflection-based parts of bindings

The port binds through compiled paths and through the metadata types declare, never through run-time reflection (`xaml.md` 3.6 and 3.7). Per upstream type of `Data/Core/ExpressionNodes/Reflection`, `Data/Core/Parsers` and `Data/Core/Plugins` that depends on reflection or on expression trees, this is what stands for it. `docs/porting/data/path-overrides.toml` and `member-waivers.toml` carry the same decisions for the tracking pages.

| Upstream type | Counterpart in the port | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `DynamicPluginPropertyAccessorNode` | `PropertyAccessorNode::new_dynamic` (`data/core/expression_nodes/property_accessor_node.rs`) | Same node as for a compiled path, without a fixed plugin: it asks `BindingPlugins::property_accessors()` for the first plugin that matches the source, as upstream. |
| `DynamicPluginStreamNode` | `StreamNode::new_dynamic` (`expression_nodes/stream_node.rs`) | The same, over `BindingPlugins::stream_handlers()`. |
| `ReflectionIndexerNode` | `ReflectionIndexerNode` (`expression_nodes/reflection_indexer_node.rs`) | Kept by name. The indexer is found in the binding metadata of the source (a list, a dictionary, a declared indexer) or in its markup metadata (`markup_members::find_indexer`) instead of `Type.GetProperties`; the arguments are converted to the declared parameter types. |
| `ReflectionTypeCastNode` | `TypeCastNode` with a `CastTarget` (`expression_nodes/type_cast_node.rs`) | `Type.IsInstanceOfType` becomes `CastTarget::is_instance`: a class of the object model by its `TypeInfo`, any other type by its `ValueType`. The type name of the path is resolved by the `TypeResolver` of the binding. |
| `ExpressionTreeIndexerNode` | None | Built only from a LINQ `IndexExpression`. An indexer of a compiled path is a property element (`CompiledBindingPathBuilder::indexer_property`, `list_item`, `dictionary_item`, read through `PropertyInfoAccessorFactory::create_indexer_property_accessor`); one of a string path is `ReflectionIndexerNode`. Unreachable without expression trees. |
| `BindingExpressionVisitor<TIn>`, `BindingExpressionVisitorMembers` | None | Translate an expression tree into path elements. Unreachable without expression trees; `CompiledBindingPathBuilder` is called directly. |
| `StreamBindingExtensions` | None | Marker methods that exist to be found in an expression tree; `CompiledBindingPathBuilder::stream_task` / `stream_observable` say the same in a path. |
| `ReflectionClrPropertyInfo` | `MarkupPropertyInfo` (`data/core/plugins/markup_members.rs`) | A property description over the plain property a type declares in its markup metadata, in place of one over `System.Reflection.PropertyInfo`. Compiled paths use `ClrPropertyInfo` / `TypedClrPropertyInfo` over accessor closures, as upstream uses generated accessors. |
| `ReflectionMethodAccessorPlugin` | `MethodAccessorPlugin` (`plugins/method_accessor_plugin.rs`) | Matches a method by name and chooses the overload a command can call with upstream's priority (`markup_members::find_best_command_method`), over the methods declared in markup or binding metadata. |
| `MethodAccessorPlugin(MethodInfo, Type)` | `UntypedAccessorPlugin` with `UntypedMember::Method` (`plugins/untyped_accessor_plugin.rs`) | The plugin of one fixed method of a compiled path: a closure creates the delegate in place of `MethodInfo.CreateDelegate`. |
| `ObservableStreamPlugin`, `ObservableStreamPlugin<T>` | `ObservableStreamPlugin` over `ObservableValue` (`plugins/i_stream_plugin.rs`) | Upstream finds `IObservable<T>` among the interfaces of the value and boxes each item by a reflected generic call. Here a property that can be streamed holds an `ObservableValue`, which boxes each item when the observable is wrapped. |
| `InpcPropertyAccessorPlugin` (reflection over the properties of an `INotifyPropertyChanged` object) | `InpcPropertyAccessorPlugin` (`plugins/inpc_property_accessor_plugin.rs`) | Kept by name; it reads the property from the binding or markup metadata of the source. |
| `DataAnnotationsValidationPlugin` | None | See the register above. |
| `MethodToCommandConverter` (compiled `Expression` lambdas over a `Delegate`) | `MethodToCommandConverter` over a `metadata::MarkupDelegate` (`converters/method_to_command_converter.rs`) | The delegate carries its method metadata, from which the `Can<Name>` companion and its `DependsOn` properties are read. |

### ControlCatalog sample (`samples/ControlCatalog/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The upstream catalog has Fluent and Simple themes, with no macOS theme settings. | The optional `macos-theme` feature adds `CatalogTheme::MacOS` and the separately compiled `MacOSTheme`; Settings exposes live control size, accent, reduced motion/transparency, contrast and active-window appearance. Desktop accepts `FERROUI_CATALOG_THEME=macos`; browser accepts `?Theme=macos` when built with the feature. The Settings markup addition is recorded in `scripts/convert_catalog_xaml.py`. | Addition | Exercise the macOS 26 theme across the full existing control gallery while retaining the default WinUI-only build footprint. Native visual accuracy is tracked separately in `docs/porting/macos26-theme.md`. | #340 |
| The upstream catalog has Fluent and Simple themes only. | The optional `material-theme` feature adds `CatalogTheme::Material` and the separately compiled `MaterialTheme` with its bundled Roboto (`App::with_theme_fonts`); desktop accepts `FERROUI_CATALOG_THEME=material`, the browser `?Theme=material`. | Addition | Exercise the Android Material 3 Expressive theme across the existing gallery beside the WinUI and macOS themes, without changing the default build. The Material-specific components are shown by `samples/MaterialThemeGallery`. | #341 |
| `CompositionPage.ButtonThreadSleep` calls `Thread.Sleep(5000)`. | `button_thread_sleep` in `Pages/composition_page.rs` calls `std::thread::sleep` for 5 s. | Behaviour | Ported as upstream has it: the button demonstrates a blocked UI thread. Recorded because the port otherwise rules out blocking waits on the UI thread. | #34 |
| `ButtonsPage` sets `DataContext = this`. | The page sets its data context to an element reference to itself (`ElementRef<ButtonsPage>`; `Pages/buttons_page.rs`). | Representation | A page that is its own data context holds itself, and the elements under it that inherit the value hold it too; the collector frees it upstream, and here the page with everything it shows stayed alive (catalog gap C326: 7.7 MB a visit). The bindings of the document read the page through the reference (`((pages:ButtonsPage)DataContext).CountCommand`). Code that reads `DataContext` as a handle of the page finds an element reference instead. | 2026-10-09 |
| `MiniMvvm.PropertyChangedExtensions.WhenAnyValue` captures the model in the observable it returns. | The observable holds the model weakly (`samples/MiniMvvm/property_changed_extensions.rs`); a subscription to the observable of a model that is gone produces nothing. | Behaviour | A view model that keeps an observable of its own properties (`ListBoxPageViewModel.SelectionMode = this.WhenAnyValue(..)`) holds itself through it, which the collector frees upstream (catalog gap C328: the view model of the list box page with its 10 000 items, 343 KB a visit). An observable no longer keeps its model alive: code that holds only the observable must hold the model too. | 2026-10-09 |
| `CompositionPage` starts key frame animations that run forever on the brushes and the gradient stops its buttons create (`AnimateGradientStops`, `ConicBrushCreateAnimate_Click` and the like), and never stops them. | The same (`Pages/composition_page.rs`). | Behaviour | Ported as upstream has it. The server compositor holds a running animation in its clock (`ServerCompositorAnimations._clockItems`), a brush and a gradient stop are active from their creation (`InitializeDefaultsExtra`: `Server.Activate()`) and nothing deactivates them, so the animations of a brush nothing draws any more run on in both implementations (27 KB for a visit that presses the buttons). Recorded because it is what a tour over the catalog with interaction adds beyond the three pages below (`GAPS.md`, "What interaction retains"). | 2026-10-09 |
| The catalog has two themes, Fluent and Simple (`Models/CatalogTheme.cs`, `App.axaml`, `Pages/SettingsPage.axaml`), and starts in the Fluent theme. | A third, `CatalogTheme::WinUI`, the WinUI 3 theme of the port (`ferroui-themes-winui`), in which the catalog starts (`App::initialize`): `App.xaml` declares `<WinUITheme x:Key="WinUITheme" />`, the settings page lists it, `App::set_catalog_themes` makes it the only theme with the Fluent styles of the colour picker (the colour picker library has no WinUI styles), and the desktop entry point starts in another theme with `FERROUI_CATALOG_THEME=fluent` or `simple` (`App::select_initial_catalog_theme`, which selects a theme before the main window exists). The markup lines are the `ADDITIONS` of `scripts/convert_catalog_xaml.py`, so `scripts/sync-control-catalog.sh --check` holds the two documents to their upstream files with them. | Behaviour | Shows the WinUI theme of the port (docs/porting/winui-theme.md), which styles every control, as the catalog's first theme, next to the themes of the original (`GAPS.md`, "The WinUI theme of the port"). | 2026-10-16 |
| The catalog declares its Fluent and Simple themes and the Simple styles of the colour picker in `App.axaml`, and lists the two themes in the theme selector of `Pages/SettingsPage.axaml`. | The catalog is built with the WinUI theme of the port alone by default. The Fluent and Simple themes, the Simple styles of the colour picker and the generic styles of the TreeDataGrid are in `ClassicThemes.xaml` (a resource dictionary class of the port), which the build compiles and the application merges into its resources only with the Cargo feature `classic-themes` of `control-catalog` (forwarded by `control-catalog-desktop`); the theme selector lists `App.AvailableThemes`, the themes the catalog is built with (`ItemsSource="{x:Static local:App.AvailableThemes}"` in place of the two items), and `FERROUI_CATALOG_THEME=fluent` or `simple` needs the feature. The changes to the two documents are the `ADDITIONS` and `REMOVALS` of `scripts/convert_catalog_xaml.py`. The TreeDataGrid demo, whose views the catalog shows, has its own application (`App.xaml` with the Fluent theme, the main window, the entry point) behind its default feature `app`, which the catalog does not enable. | Behaviour | Issue 306: a catalog with three themes compiled and linked all three (the generated code of the Fluent and Simple themes was 7.9 MB of the release executable on macOS); the owner asked for the WinUI theme alone for now, with the others easy to restore (`docs/binary-size.md`). | 2026-10-23 |
| `NotificationsPage` and `ClipboardPage` create a `WindowNotificationManager` for their top level every time they are attached, and `ScreenPage` subscribes to the position of its window and to its screens; none of them is removed. | The same (`Pages/notifications_page.rs`, `clipboard_page.rs`, `screen_page.rs`). | Behaviour | Ported as upstream has them: the window holds the managers in its adorner layer and the handlers in its events, so a visit leaves them in both implementations for as long as the window lives (22 KB and four objects of the server compositor for a manager, 11 KB for the presenter of the screens page). Recorded because they are what a tour over the catalog still adds (`GAPS.md`, "What a visit to a page retains"). | 2026-10-09 |
| The messages of `CompositionPage.CustomVisualHandler` are four `static readonly object` instances compared by reference. | Four statics holding `Arc<dyn Any + Send + Sync>`, compared with `Arc::ptr_eq`. | Representation | A message is sent from the UI thread and compared on the render thread; the identity of the object is the message. | #34; render thread R2 |
| `CareCompanionAppPage.MakePipsPager` creates the two-way binding of `PipsPager.SelectedPageIndex` with `CompiledBinding.Create<TIn, TOut>(c => c.SelectedIndex, carousel, mode: BindingMode.TwoWay)`, which derives the path from an expression tree. | `make_pips_pager` in `Pages/CarouselPage/care_companion_app_page.rs` writes the path out with `CompiledBindingPathBuilder` (one element, the property `SelectingMultiPage.SelectedIndex`) and sets the source and the mode on the `CompiledBinding`. | Representation | Rust has no expression trees, so `CompiledBinding.Create` has no counterpart; the binding has the same source, path and mode. | #60 (no pull request yet) |

| `AutoCompleteBoxPage.PopulateAsync` awaits `Task.Delay(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1.5), cancellationToken)`. | `CancellableDelay` in `Pages/auto_complete_box_page.rs`: the delay of `mini-mvvm` (a dispatcher timer) together with a callback registered with the token, which wakes the population so that it ends with `OperationCanceledError` as soon as the token is cancelled. | Representation | There is no `Task.Delay`; the delay of the samples takes no token.
