| `NavigationPerformanceMonitorHelper` and the five performance monitor pages (`ContentPagePerformancePage`, `NavigationPagePerformancePage`, `TabbedPagePerformancePage`, `DrawerPagePerformancePage`, `CarouselPagePerformancePage`) show the size of the managed heap and its change (`GC.GetTotalMemory`), the total of the allocations (`GC.GetTotalAllocatedBytes`), write the heap size into every log entry, and force a full collection (`GC.Collect`, `GC.WaitForPendingFinalizers`) before a refresh. | `Pages/navigation_performance_monitor_helper.rs` tracks the pages through weak references and times operations with a stopwatch over `std::time::Instant`, as the original does; `update_heap_delta` writes "Managed Heap: not available" and an empty delta, the tabbed and the carousel page write "Heap: not available" and "Total allocated: not available", a log entry has no heap size, and `force_gc` only calls the refresh. `InitHeap`, the previous heap size and the three brushes of the delta are not ported. | `DialogsPage.GetStorageProvider` returns a `ManagedStorageProvider` when "force managed" is checked, on every platform. | `try_get_storage_provider` in `Pages/dialogs_page.rs` does so everywhere but in the browser, where it returns the storage provider of the top-level. | Missing | `ferroui-dialogs` does not build the managed storage provider for WebAssembly: it works on the local file system, which the browser does not have. | #59 |
| The markup compiler of the build populates a class from its document: `InitializeComponent()` is generated per class, and the compiled resource is removed from the assembly. | The build script of the sample compiles its documents (`build.rs`, `ferroui-build` against the type models; `docs/porting/xaml.md` 9.5.22). `xaml_class!` (`markup.rs`) declares `initialize_component()`, which populates the instance through a table the build writes (`COMPILED`: the rooted path of the document and the `populate` of its compiled markup), and the entry of the class in the table of the classes with a document (`XamlClass`), which the page list and the tests create a class with. | No counterpart: the upstream sample is always built with its markup compiled. | The feature `runtime-markup` of `control-catalog`: the build compiles nothing, every document is an embedded asset, the classes are populated by the run-time loader (`markup::run_time`), `App` loads `App.xaml` with `CustomThemes.xaml` as one group and registers the run-time loader, and the loader table is the one written by hand over `XamlClass`. | `OpenGlContent.St` is a `Stopwatch`; `uTime` is `St.Elapsed.TotalSeconds`. | A `LazyLock<Instant>` forced by the first `OpenGlContent::new`; `uTime` is its `elapsed()` in seconds. | Representation | `std::time::Instant` is the monotonic clock of the standard library; the static is started when the class is first used, as the static field is. | catalog-opengl-pages (no pull request yet) |
| `ControlCatalog.Browser`: `ParseArgs` reads `PreferFileDialogPolyfill` and `RenderingMode`; the page creates the runtime and starts it. | `parse_args` also reads `RenderThread` (`?RenderThread=false` keeps a module built with threads on the thread of the page). and `Renderer` (`?Renderer=Vello` draws with the Vello backend in a module built with the feature `vello` of the package; a name that is no backend fails as an unknown rendering mode does, a backend the module lacks leaves Skia), and prints the renderer after the rendering modes; `catalogRendering` ends with `renderer=`. The host page isolates itself for a module built with threads (`ensureCrossOriginIsolated`) before it creates it. Exports for the tests and measurements, none a port: `catalogRendering` (which thread drew the frames), `catalogMemory` (the end of the dynamic memory of the module, its peak and the size of the memory; sampled by the page with `?MemoryReport=true`), `catalogPanicInFrame` (a job that panics in the next frame), `catalogRequestFrame` and `catalogFrameDrawn` (a commit of the compositor of the view is asked for, and whether its frame has been drawn); the page offers `controlCatalogPanics()`. | Addition | The catalog renders from the render thread in a threaded build and needs a way back for comparison; a threaded module cannot start in a page that is not isolated; the fixed memory of a threaded build has to be sized from a measured peak; a view is hit from what its last frame drew, and a test that clicks where the state of the application says an element is has to know that the view has drawn that state, which with a render thread is as much later as the browser makes the animation frames of the worker (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 5 and "B2.7"). | Browser B2.7 |
| No counterpart: the desktop entry point of upstream's catalog has no screenshot run. | The screenshot run of `ControlCatalog.Desktop` (`FERROUI_SMOKE_SCREENSHOTS`, `program.rs`) writes two pictures of every page: the window rendered into a render target bitmap at the DPI of its render scaling (`NN-<header>.png`, the immediate renderer), and a snapshot of the composition visual of the window at its render scaling (`NN-<header>-composed.png`, `save_composed`: the compositor draws it on the render thread through the same code as the frames it presents; the batch is committed at once, so the snapshot is of the page that is shown). | Addition | A render target bitmap of more than 96 DPI drew the children of a border with a box shadow at twice their scale, as upstream does (`decisions/render-target-bitmap-scaled-box-shadow.md`; corrected, Skia backend); the compositor's picture is what the window shows. | macos-render-fixes (no pull request yet) |
| No counterpart: the page list of `MainWindowViewModel` has 74 entries. | The page list (`ViewModels/main_window_view_model_page_list.rs`) also offers the page "TreeDataGrid" (section "Collections & Data", the pages of the TreeDataGrid demo) and the page "Composition (WinUI)" (section "Media & Graphics", `Pages/winui_composition_page.rs`). The test `the_view_model_lists_every_upstream_page_as_available_or_unavailable` names each added page in `additional_pages`, checks that it is available exactly once, and counts 74 plus the added pages. | Behaviour | The catalog shows the controls the port adds beside upstream's. A pull request that adds a page appends its header to `additional_pages`, so that pages added by parallel pull requests merge as list entries, not as competing totals. | 2026-10-23 |

### RenderDemo sample (`samples/RenderDemo/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CustomStringAnimator.Interpolate` returns `newValue.Substring(0, length + 1)` with `length = (int)(progress / step)`: at a progress of one that is one character more than the text has, and `Substring` throws. | `interpolate` takes at most the whole text. | Behaviour | The framework asks an animator for its value at a progress of one when it ends an animation whose element leaves the visual tree (`AnimationInstance.DoComplete(false)` on `DetachedFromVisualTree`, then `FindEdgeValue`): the upstream sample throws when the page is left or the window closed. A panic there would end the application of the port. `samples/RenderDemo/GAPS.md`, R002. | Samples |
| The subscriptions `MainWindow` makes to its view model (`vm.WhenAnyValue(..).Subscribe(..)`, one for each debug overlay) capture the window; the window holds the view model as its data context. | The subscriptions hold the window weakly. So do the handlers of the timers, pointer events and composition updates of the pages and controls of the sample, where the managed original captures `this`. | Representation | A cycle the collector of the managed original frees is a leak under reference counting. | Samples |

### Run-time type system of markup (`src/Markup/FerroUI.Markup.Xaml.Loader/runtime/type_system/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `<generic:List x:TypeArguments="T">` and `<collections:ArrayList>` in markup create the collections of the runtime library (`List<T>` with typed storage, `ArrayList`), which do not notify of changes. | Both evaluate to a `RuntimeList` (`src/Markup/FerroUI.Markup.Xaml/xaml_il/runtime/runtime_list.rs`; the run-time loader and generated code create the same type): the element type and the items as untyped values in a shared `FerroList<Option<BoxedValue>>`, which notifies of changes. An instantiation of `List<T>` that metadata registers keeps the Rust type of its metadata. | Representation | Rust cannot instantiate a generic type for an element type known only at run time. Markup only adds the items and hands the list to a property; nothing observes the notifications. | branch `markup-gaps-types` (no pull request yet) |
| `ItemsControl.ItemsSource` (and the items sources of `AutoCompleteBox`, `MenuFlyout` and `MultiPage`) is an `IEnumerable`: any enumerable is assignable. | The collection handle `ItemsSource` is published to markup as `System.Collections.IEnumerable` (`src/FerroUI.Controls/markup_types/contracts.rs`), so the compiler accepts every enumerable; at run time the property takes a handle, a list created in markup, a view of an items source (`ItemsSourceView`, the view itself) and what a binding delivers through a registered conversion, among them a list created in markup that an untyped property (`Tag`, an `object`-typed property of a control) holds and a binding passes on (`register_value_types` of `src/Markup/FerroUI.Markup.Xaml/register_types.rs`). Any other enumerable (a `FerroList<T>` named by `x:Static` or a resource) is an invalid cast when it is assigned. | Behaviour | The handle is cast from a collection type by a cast registered for that type (`ValueTypes::register_cast`); there is no cast for every `FerroList<T>`. | branch `markup-gaps-types` (no pull request yet); the view and the list of an untyped property 2026-10-23 |
| Every class has a constructor; the type system of reflection lists the non-public ones with their parameters, and `ConstructableObjectTransformer` gives the root of a document whose class has no public constructor the first one, without arguments. `Build` then compares its parameters with the arguments and throws `InvalidOperationException` ("Cannot compile Build method. Parameters mismatch.") for the private `ObjectWithPrivateCtor(string)` of the compiler tests; `Populate` of an existing instance works. | Markup metadata declares the public constructors only. A class of markup metadata that declares none has one that is not public and takes no parameters (as a class of the object model already had: `project_markup_type` of `runtime_type_system.rs`, and the same in the build-time type system, `model_type_system.rs` of `src/FerroUI.Build.Tasks`), so the transformer gives it to the root and `Populate` works as upstream. `Build` refuses a root whose constructor is not public with the same `InvalidOperationException` (`Interpreter::build`, `runtime/interpreter/interpreter.rs`). | Behaviour | The parameters of a constructor that is not public are not known; the constructor cannot be called either way. Before, the transformer failed with "Type ... doesn't have any constructors" for both the build and the populate of such a class. Forced by `compiler_should_populate_xaml_without_public_ctor` and `compiler_should_fail_to_build_xaml_without_public_ctor` (`runtime/interpreter/tests/compiler_tests.rs`). | branch `tests-gaps` (no pull request yet) |

### Items sources (`src/FerroUI.Controls/items_source.rs`, `items_source_view.rs`, `i_key_index_mapping.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ItemsSourceView.HasKeyIndexMapping` is internal and always `false`, and `KeyFromIndex` is internal and throws `NotImplementedException` ("Not implemented in Avalonia, preserved here for ItemsRepeater's usage"); there is no `IKeyIndexMapping`. | The members of WinUI's `ItemsSourceView` (microsoft-ui-xaml `7e4b0c2`, `controls/dev/Repeater/ItemsSourceView.cpp`, `InspectingDataSource.cpp`): the contract `IKeyIndexMapping` (`key_from_index`, `index_from_key`), which a list states through `IItemsList::key_index_mapping` (default `None`); `ItemsSourceView::has_key_index_mapping` is whether the source has it, and `key_from_index` and `index_from_key` (public) ask it. Without it they panic, where WinUI throws `hresult_not_implemented`; `key_from_index` also panics for an index that is not the index of an item ("Argument index is invalid."), where WinUI passes the index on. The markup members `KeyFromIndex` and `IndexFromKey` are errors of the call instead. | Behaviour | A control that realizes items (WinUI's `ItemsRepeater`) keeps the element of an item across a reset of its source by the key of the item; the members are ported for it, as upstream preserved them. The index is checked before it reaches an implementation of the contract, which indexes with it. | 2026-10-23 |
| The constructor of `ItemsSourceView` is `private protected`; a view is created by `GetOrCreate`. | `ItemsSourceView::new` and `ItemsSourceViewOf::new` are public, and markup constructs a view (`ItemsSourceView(ItemsSource)`, `src/FerroUI.Controls/markup_types/plain.rs`) with WinUI's members `Count`, `GetAt`, `IndexOf`, `HasKeyIndexMapping`, `KeyFromIndex` and `IndexFromKey`. | Behaviour | WinUI's constructor is public (`ItemsSourceView(Object source)`), and a document can give a control a view as its items source. | 2026-10-23 |
| `ItemsSourceView` implements `IReadOnlyList<object?>`, `IList` and `INotifyCollectionChanged`: a view is an enumerable, assignable to an items source, and `GetOrCreate` returns a view it is given as it is. | `ItemsSourceView` implements `IItemsList` (its items, its changes and the unique keys of its source), `ItemsSource` converts from `Rc<ItemsSourceView>` (and a cast and a binding conversion are registered for it), and `get_or_create` returns the view a source holds. | Representation | The interfaces of the runtime library are the contract of the untyped lists of items here. Before, a view could not be an items source at all. | 2026-10-23 |

### Text block (`src/FerroUI.Controls/text_block.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TextBlock.ArrangeOverride` disposes the text layout and creates it again for the arranged size, always (`DisposeTextLayout(); _constraint = availableSize; var textLayout = TextLayout;`). | `arrange_override` keeps the layout when the arranged constraint is the one it was created for and the resolved text alignment is the one it was created with (`layout_text_alignment`); otherwise it does what upstream does. | Behaviour | Found while verifying performance design 06, not introduced by it. The layout kept is equal to the one upstream would create (the glyphs and the metrics are the same), but `create_text_layout`, which a subclass can override, is called once where upstream calls it twice, and the layout read after the arrange is the object of the measure. It was made to avoid a second layout and was not listed here; whether it stays needs a decision. | Before performance design 06 |
| `TextBlock.MeasureOverride` and `ArrangeOverride` dispose the text layout whenever the constraint differs from the one it was created for, and the next read creates it again. | `measure_override` and `arrange_override` keep the layout for a new constraint when it is the layout that constraint would create (`keeps_text_layout_for`): the block has no complex content, does not wrap (`TextWrapping::NoWrap`), does not trim (`TextTrimming::none()`), the layout is left aligned (`layout_text_alignment`, which is always the case for a layout created by a measure), and the new height neither drops a line the layout has nor brings back one it dropped (the layout fits the new height, and the new height is at most the one it was created for, or that one was unbounded). The constraint is updated as upstream does. | Performance | Such a line is the whole paragraph and starts at zero whatever the width (`TextLineImpl::get_paragraph_offset_x`, the no-wrap branch of the formatter), so the layout created again is equal to the kept one. Cells, labels and headers are typically measured with an unbounded width and arranged at a column or slot width: each of those arranges created the layout again. In the developer tools, 369 layouts were created per selection of a control. `create_text_layout` is called fewer times than upstream calls it; a subclass that overrides it to depend on the constraint should also wrap or trim. Two reference tests (`calling_measure_should_update_text_layout`, `calling_arrange_with_different_size_should_update_constraint_and_text_layout`) now set `TextWrapping::Wrap`, so they still test the reference behaviour. | #305 |
| No counterpart: a text block draws its text from the top of its content (`RenderCore`: the top padding), so the height layout rounding adds to its desired height is below the text. | `TextBlock.LayoutRoundingPlacement`, an inherited attached property registered on `TextBlock` for `Control` (`layout_rounding_placement_property`, type `TextLayoutRoundingPlacement { Below, Above }`, default `Below`; markup also knows it as a property of a text block). With `Above` the text moves down by what layout rounding adds to the block (`layout_rounding_offset`: the height of the text and the vertical padding rounded up at the layout scale, minus that height), and so do the selection and the hit testing of `SelectableTextBlock`, the underline of the access key of `AccessText` and the controls embedded in the inlines; the size of the block does not change. The WinUI theme sets `Above` on its top-levels (`Window`, `EmbeddableControlRoot`, `PopupRoot`, `OverlayPopupHost`); the Fluent and Simple themes do not set it. | Addition | WinUI's TextBlock puts that height above its text (`CTextBlock::MeasureOverride`, `m_layoutRoundingHeightAdjustment`, `GetContentRenderingOffset`), so FerroUI's text sat up to one device pixel higher than WinUI's (-0.99 px at 200 %, +0.01 px with `Above`; `text-rendering.md`, section 6). A property keeps the reference's placement the default and lets the theme that imitates WinUI place text as WinUI does. | branch `text-baseline` |
| No counterpart: the box of a line runs from the ascent of its font to its descent with the line gap shared above and below (`TextLineImpl.CreateLineMetrics`), and a text block centred beside an icon centres that box, not its letters; `TextBlock` clips to its bounds (`ClipToBoundsProperty.OverrideDefaultValue<TextBlock>(true)`). | `TextBlock.TextLineBounds`, an inherited attached property registered on `TextBlock` for `Control` (`text_line_bounds_property`, type `ferroui_base::media::TextLineBounds { Full, TrimToCapHeight, TrimToBaseline, Tight }`, default `Full`; markup also knows it as a property of a text block), after WinUI's `TextBlock.TextLineBounds` (`CCompositeFontFamily::GetTextLineBoundsMetrics`). The paragraph properties carry it (`TextParagraphProperties::text_line_bounds`, `GenericTextParagraphProperties::set_text_line_bounds`) and `create_line_metrics` bounds every line by it: with the top trimmed the baseline is the cap height below the top of the box, with the bottom trimmed the box ends at the baseline; a `LineHeight` centres the trimmed box. `Full` is the reference's box, unchanged. `ClipToBounds` of a text block is coerced to false while its lines are trimmed. The WinUI theme sets `Full` where WinUI's styles do and `Tight` on the labels of command bar buttons beside their icons (winui-theme G-29). | Addition | Centring the box of a line leaves the capitals off the middle by the difference of the ascent above them and the descent below them: 1 px above it for Helvetica at 12 px, the default font of Skia on macOS, 0.7 px below it for Segoe UI; the reference and WinUI's `Full` do the same. Trimming to the capitals and the baseline, as WinUI can, centres the capitals for every font. The ink of a trimmed line is outside its box by design, so a clipped text block would cut its descenders. | branch `text-line-bounds` |

### Templates (`src/FerroUI.Controls/templates/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FuncDataTemplate<T>` matches with `TypeUtilities.CanCast<T>`: null data matches a reference type `T` (`string`, a control type) and is passed to the build function as null. | `FuncDataTemplate::for_type` and `for_type_with_match` match null only when `T` is a nullable form (`Option<String>`, `Option<Ref<Control>>`, `Option<i32>`) (`func_data_template.rs`). | Representation | A Rust type has no null of its own: the C# `string` that accepts null is `Option<String>` here, and a template for it matches null. A template for `String` or `Ref<Control>` is the non-nullable form and is not chosen for null content. | #40 |

### Skia backend (`src/Skia/FerroUI.Skia/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The backend recovers its typeface with the type test `PlatformTypeface is SkiaTypeface`. | `IPlatformTypeface::as_any`, and `SkiaTypeface::try_get` downcasts through it. | Representation | The Rust form of a runtime type test; it replaces the thread-local registry of live typefaces by address that stood in for it. | #52 |
| `RenderTargetBitmapImpl` keeps its framebuffer render target as a plain field. | The field is a `ThreadBound<FramebufferRenderTarget>`: reached only on the thread that created the bitmap. | Representation | A render target bitmap is shared with the render thread (`IRenderTargetBitmapImpl: Send + Sync`), and the render target holds a surface and a framebuffer that are not. | Render thread R1.3 |
| `WriteableBitmapImpl` keeps `_bitmap`, `_image` and `_imageValid` as fields guarded by `lock (_lock)` in some members. | One lock over the pixels and the image; an invalid image is an absent one. | Representation | The UI thread writes and the render thread draws. | Render thread R1.3 |
| `DrawingContextImpl.RestoreCanvas` forgets the transform (`_currentTransform = null`) and the getter of `Transform` reads it back from the canvas (`Canvas.TotalMatrix44`), with the post transform of a scaled context (`ScaleDrawingToDpi`) in it. | `restore_canvas` in `drawing_context_impl.rs` takes the post transform off the matrix of the canvas: the transform of the context after a restore is the logical one. | Behaviour | Upstream's fault: in a render target bitmap of more than 96 DPI the transforms pushed after a restore (the children of a border with a box shadow, drawn by the immediate renderer) were scaled twice (`decisions/render-target-bitmap-scaled-box-shadow.md`; test `shadowed_border_child_is_in_place_in_a_render_target_bitmap_of_192_dpi` of the headless platform). Contexts without a post transform (the frames of windows) are unchanged. | macos-render-fixes-rtb-transform (no pull request yet) |
| `SurfaceRenderTarget` has no shared snapshot (a layer is drawn by the thread that made it). | `create_shared_snapshot` (an addition of the render-thread work) of a surface with a GPU context is always a copy in memory: the copy the context makes, else the pixels read from the surface (`read_raster_snapshot`), else transparent pixels of its size. It used to hand out the image of the GPU surface when the copy failed. | Behaviour | The snapshot crosses from the render thread to the thread that asked; an image of a GPU surface belongs to its context and its thread. Found by the audit of the offscreen paths for a confined compositor (`browser-render-worker.md`, "B2.7"). | Browser B2.7 |
| `GlSkiaSharedTextureForComposition.Context` is the context object, which the render thread reads for `Context.IsSharedWith` when it imports the texture; `Dispose()` deletes the texture with it from whichever thread calls. | The context is a `ThreadBound<Rc<dyn IGlContext>>` of the thread that made the texture, and the identifier is atomic. `GlSkiaGpu::create_shared_texture_for_composition` asks `is_shared_with` when the texture is made (as upstream does there too) and marks the texture with a token of the GPU (`share_group`); `import_shared_image` asks the context on its own thread and compares the token on another. `dispose()` on a thread other than the one of the context deletes nothing and keeps the identifier; that thread deletes with a context of its own (`dispose_with`, upstream's `Dispose(IGlContext)`), as the imported image does. | Behaviour | A context is an `Rc` of its thread; the texture is `Send + Sync` by its contract (render thread R5.7). A texture made for one Skia GPU is refused by another GPU on a thread that cannot ask the contexts, where upstream would ask them; the only caller that disposes off the thread of the context is the imported image, which passes its own. | Row 6 of the tracking list |
| `GlSkiaExternalObjectsFeature`, `GlSkiaImportedImage` and `GlSkiaImportedSemaphore` hold their `GlSkiaGpu`. | They hold what they read of it: the OpenGL context, the Ganesh context and the token of the share group. | Representation | The GPU owns the feature; a handle back to the GPU would be a cycle. | Row 6 of the tracking list |
| The snapshot of an imported OpenGL image is an `ImmutableBitmap` whose dispose action makes the context current, ignoring a failure, and disposes the image. `ImmutableBitmap` calls the action instead of disposing its image. | `ImmutableBitmap::from_image` takes an action that is handed the image (`FnOnce(Image) + Send`). The action of the snapshot holds the context in a `ThreadBound`: on the thread that took the snapshot the image is released with the context current, on another it is released without. | Behaviour | The bitmap is `Send + Sync` by its contract and the context is not. The server side disposes a snapshot with the render interface current (`ServerCompositionDrawingSurface`), so the second case releases the texture in the right context too; a context shared by the two threads of the lock model comes with Ganesh on the desktop (open item 6 of render thread R5.5). | Row 6 of the tracking list |
| The backends recover their own objects from the contracts by casts: `(GlSkiaSharedTextureForComposition)image`, `(GlSkiaImportedSemaphore)waitForSemaphore`. | `ICompositionImportableSharedGpuContextImage::as_any` and `IPlatformRenderInterfaceImportedSemaphore::as_any`; a failed cast is a panic. | Representation | The Rust form of a runtime type test. | Row 6 of the tracking list |
| SkiaSharp carries Ganesh on Metal and Ganesh on OpenGL in one library; `SkiaMetalGpu` is Ganesh. | On Apple targets the default build has Graphite on Metal only, and an OpenGL context is refused at the first frame. With `RUSTFLAGS="--cfg ferro_skia_apple_ganesh"` the build has Ganesh on OpenGL and Ganesh on Metal instead (the files of `gpu/metal` compile `SkiaMetalGpu` and the external objects feature on Ganesh, as upstream), which the rendering mode OpenGL of macOS (CGL) and of iOS (EAGL) needs. | Behaviour | No published Skia binary has Ganesh on OpenGL next to Graphite (`apple-opengl.md`, section 2), and Cargo features cannot remove one; Skia is not built from source. | 2026-10-15 |
| `SkiaMetalExternalObjectsFeature` and its imported image hold their `SkiaMetalGpu` and read its `GrContext`. | They hold the cell with the Graphite context that the GPU shares with its render targets (`SharedContext`); a disposed GPU fails a snapshot with the same message as `gr_context()`. | Representation | The GPU owns the feature; a handle back to the GPU would be a cycle. | Row 9 of the tracking list |
| `ImportedImage.SnapshotWithTimelineSemaphores` wraps the Metal texture as a Ganesh render target with the origin of the image (`GRBackendRenderTarget`, `SKSurface.Create(..., origin, colorType)`) and takes `surface.Snapshot()`, a copy that carries the origin; `GrContext.Flush(true, false)` before the wait and `GrContext.Flush()` before the signal. | On Graphite: the texture is wrapped as a surface of the recorder (`surfaces::wrap_backend_texture`) and its copy is taken (`surfaces::as_image_copy`); for an image whose first row is the bottom one the copy is drawn once more with its rows reversed. Both flushes are `GraphiteGrContext::flush` (snap the recorder, insert the recording, submit). | Representation | The Skia backend on Metal is Graphite, which has no surface origin and no flush of a context apart from a recording. The picture, and the order of the wait, the copy and the signal on the command queue, are upstream's; an image with a bottom-left origin costs a second copy. In the configuration `ferro_skia_apple_ganesh` (Ganesh on Metal) the port does what upstream does. | Row 9 of the tracking list |
| `(ImportedSemaphore)waitForSemaphore` in the Skia backend and `(SharedEvent)@event` in the native backend are casts. | `IPlatformRenderInterfaceImportedSemaphore::as_any` and `IMetalSharedEvent::as_any`; a failed cast is a panic. | Representation | The Rust form of a runtime type test. | Row 9 of the tracking list |
| `DrawingContextHelper.RenderAsync` takes an `SKCanvas` (one the application received from another API, such as the page of a document) and returns `Task.CompletedTask`. | `render_async` and `render_async_clipped` take the `Surface` the canvas belongs to and return a completed future (`std::future::Ready<()>`). | Behaviour | A `DrawingContextImpl` owns what it draws to (`CanvasSource`: a surface or a picture recorder), and a canvas of the binding is a borrow of its owner; `wrap_skia_surface` (`WrapSkiaCanvas`) has the same shape. A canvas that has no surface (the page of a document) cannot be rendered to until `CanvasSource` can borrow one. The visual is rendered before the function returns, as upstream. | Row 15 of the tracking list |
| `GlyphRunImpl` and `DrawingContextImpl.DrawGlyphRun` render text whose `TextOptions` and `RenderOptions` leave the mode unspecified with subpixel antialiasing (`SKFontEdging.SubpixelAntialias`) on every surface. | `effective_text_options` in `drawing_context_impl.rs` takes the default of the surface (`TextRenderingSettings::default_text_rendering_mode`): greyscale unless the platform renders subpixel text on the display, the order of the subpixels is known, the surface is not composited, the scene is opaque and the render scaling is below 1.5; `GlyphRunImpl::get_text_blob` falls back to greyscale. Explicit modes are kept. | Behaviour | Native text is greyscale on WinUI 3, macOS, iOS, Android, browsers, composited and transparent surfaces and at high density; subpixel text there gave colour fringes the platform does not have (`text-rendering.md`, sections 1 and 5). Render target bitmaps get greyscale text by default. | Text rendering (`text-rendering.md`) |
| `SurfaceRenderTarget`, `FramebufferRenderTarget`, `GlRenderTarget` and `FboSkiaSurface` create their surfaces with `new SKSurfaceProperties(SKPixelGeometry.RgbHorizontal)` (Skia's default contrast and gamma); the Metal and Vulkan targets with no properties. | Every surface is created with the properties of `TextRenderingSettings`: the pixel geometry of the display when the platform reports it (else horizontal RGB) and the platform's text contrast and gamma (else Skia's). The render targets read the parameters of the platform from the surfaces of the top-level every frame (`TextRenderingSource`); `ISkiaGpuRenderTarget::begin_rendering_session` and `ISkiaGpu::try_create_surface` take the surface properties; layers take those of their context. | Behaviour | Text on Windows as heavy as DirectWrite's (contrast 1.0 as Chromium) and LCD text for the real subpixel order (`text-rendering.md`, sections 3 and 7). | Text rendering (`text-rendering.md`) |
| `IPlatformRenderSurface` has no text rendering parameters. | `IPlatformRenderSurface::text_rendering_parameters` (default `None`) and `PlatformTextRenderingParameters` in `ferroui_base::platform::surfaces`. | Behaviour | The seam through which a platform reports ClearType, the subpixel order, composition, contrast and gamma for a surface (`text-rendering.md`, section 7). | Text rendering (`text-rendering.md`) |
| `SurfaceRenderTarget.IsCorrupted` (a layer) is true when the GPU context is lost. | It is also true for a layer created for the render target of a window (`SkiaGpuRenderTarget`, `FramebufferRenderTarget`) once the text rendering of the window's frames is not the one the layer's surface was created with: the render target shares the settings of its last frame with the layers created for it (`frame_text_rendering` of `CreateInfo` and `SurfaceRenderTargetCreateInfo`). | Behaviour | The text rendering of a surface is decided per frame in the port (the rows above), the surface properties of a Skia surface are fixed, and the composition target keeps the layer of a window between frames, creating it again only when it is corrupted or the size changes: a change of the platform's parameters (ClearType tuned) or of the development override reached the window only after a resize. Found by the calibration run of `text-rendering.md`, section 9.1, where no contrast or gamma changed the window; test `a_layer_of_a_window_is_stale_once_the_text_rendering_of_the_frames_changes`. | branch `text-baseline` |

### Vello backend (`src/Vello/FerroUI.Vello/`, an addition)

The whole backend is an addition of the port: upstream has one render backend per platform family and none on Vello. It implements the same platform contracts as the Skia backend and follows its files; `vello-backend.md` has the table of the files, the stages and the members that fail with their stage. The rows below are where it behaves differently from the Skia backend, which is its reference.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| No Vello backend. | The crate `ferroui-vello`: `VelloPlatform`, `VelloOptions`, `use_vello`, the render interface, geometries, the drawing context, bitmaps and render targets, with the rendering modes `VelloRenderingMode { Cpu, Hybrid, Gpu }` behind `scene::IVelloSceneSink` (the CPU mode is built). | Addition | Owner's request: a second renderer behind the same contracts (row 25 of the critical path). | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| The Skia backend draws immediately onto a canvas, into the framebuffer when Skia has its format. | A frame is recorded as a scene and rendered when the drawing context is disposed, in memory, then written in the format of the framebuffer; a target that keeps its content starts its scene with that content as an image. | Behaviour | The renderers of the Vello project draw a whole scene into premultiplied RGBA; nothing is visible before the context is disposed, and a frame costs a copy of the target each way. | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| The matrix of the drawing context is 4x4 in Skia: a perspective transform is drawn. | The perspective column is dropped (`vello_extensions::to_affine`). | Missing | kurbo and the renderers draw affine transforms only. | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| `IGeometryImpl::contour_length` and the queries along a contour go through `SkPathMeasure`, which measures curves along chords. | `helpers/path_measure.rs` measures the arc length of the curves; like `SkPathMeasure` it measures the first contour that has a length. An ellipse of 160 by 110 is 427.761 long here and 427.073 in the Skia backend (427.759 exactly). | Behaviour | kurbo has the arc length of a curve; the values differ by up to 0.7 units in the comparison harness. | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| `get_fill_intersection_result` compares the Skia regions of the two paths: the pixels they cover. | The areas are compared with boolean operations: they intersect when the intersection has an area. | Behaviour | There is no region of a path here; shapes that overlap by less than a pixel intersect here and not in the Skia backend. | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| Combined geometries use Skia's path operations. | The boolean operations of the `linesweeper` crate; a panic inside it gives the empty geometry. The figures of the result begin elsewhere than Skia's, so lengths along the contour and the dashes of a pen differ; the area is the same. | Behaviour | kurbo has no boolean operations. | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| A tile brush that is not repeated uses Skia's "decal" tile mode; a radial gradient with an offset origin is a shader composed over a color. | The shape is clipped to the tile; the color is drawn first, under the gradient, in a layer when the brush is translucent. | Representation | A paint of peniko is one brush with pad, repeat or reflect. | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| The Skia backend reads and writes RGB565 bitmaps and decodes and encodes what Skia's codecs do; its default pixel format is the native 32 bit order of Skia. | RGBA8888 and BGRA8888; PNG only (JPEG fails as unsupported, other formats fail to load); the default pixel format is RGBA8888. | Missing | Stage 6 of `vello-backend.md`; the renderers draw RGBA. | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| Effects, box shadows, scene brushes, acrylic materials, a platform graphics context. | Not built: each fails with the stage it belongs to (a panic naming it, an error, or the contract's `None`). | Missing | Stages 6 to 8 of `vello-backend.md`. | Vello backend, stages 1 to 4 |
| The platform typeface wraps a typeface of Skia; a glyph run builds a text blob for each set of text options and keeps them. | `VelloTypeface` is the bytes of the font file read with `skrifa`; a glyph run is glyph indices and positions with the font (`VelloFontFace`), drawn by the scene. Bounds, intersections and the geometry of a run come from the outlines: the bounds are those of Skia (the pixels the outline touches and one more on each side) but for an edge on a pixel boundary; an intersection is where the outline crosses the band, up to a third of a pixel narrower than Skia's, which takes control points for points of the outline. | Representation | The renderers of the Vello project take font data, glyph ids and positions; `vello-backend.md`, section 8, "Text" has the numbers. | Vello backend, stage 5 |
| Skia draws glyphs with the rasterizer of the platform (CoreText on macOS), with LCD text where the surface allows it. | Glyphs are the fill of their outlines, grey-scale: the sub-pixel text rendering mode gives the pixels of the anti-aliased one. Text is lighter than in the Skia backend on macOS by about an eighth of its coverage (3.2 % of the pixels of the text scenes differ beyond the tolerance). Hinting is the vertical hinting of `glifo` for the light and the strong mode alike, and none under a rotation or for an emboldened run. | Behaviour | No renderer of the Vello project has LCD text; CoreText makes stems heavier than the outline. | Vello backend, stage 5 |
| The font manager is Skia's (`SkFontMgr`): a fallback font for a character from the platform. | `fontique`: the families, the matches and the simulations agree with the Skia backend's on the fonts measured; a fallback is the family the platform names for the script and the language of the character where it has a script, else the first of the default family, the generic families and the installed families that has the character. The four PingFang families are not offered (Heiti is the fallback for Han); 128 families are listed that Skia's manager matches but does not list. | Behaviour | `fontique` falls back by script and reads fonts with `read-fonts`, which has no `hvgl` outlines. | Vello backend, stage 5 |
| A colour font is drawn by the platform: bitmaps, COLR, SVG. | COLR glyphs and PNG bitmap glyphs are drawn in colour; a glyph that is an SVG document is drawn as the plain outline of the font; a bitmap font of Apple (no `head` table) is a typeface with metrics of which the runs draw nothing. | Missing | What `glifo` 0.4 draws. | Vello backend, stage 5 |
| A variable font is the instance Skia matched. | A typeface carries the values of its axes (`VelloTypeface::new`, `from_bytes_with_variations`; the font manager sets the weight or the slant of a variable font of the system), which the contract has no member for. | Addition | The renderers take normalized coordinates with a run. | Vello backend, stage 5 |

### Declarative Rust API (`src/FerroUI.View/`, `src/FerroUI.View.Macros/`, an addition)

The crates are an addition of the port: upstream writes user interface in XAML or in imperative code. They change nothing in the crates they use; `docs/declarative-ui.md` is their design.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| User interface in XAML or imperative code; code-first properties and bindings through indexers and operators on properties (`control[Grid.RowProperty] = 1`, `control[!Property] = binding`). | `ferroui-view`: elements built with setters generated for every control (`scripts/generate_view_builders.py`), signals the properties follow, keyed children that follow state, `launch`; `ferroui-view-macros`: `rsx!` and `#[component]`. The indexers and operators are methods of the element (`set`, `bind`, `two_way`). Samples: the 7GUIs tasks in `samples/SevenGuis`. | Addition | Owner's request (issue #237, phase 0): user interfaces without XAML, built so that a reconciler of element trees can come later. | #237 |
| (none: upstream has no component model) | Components that run again (`rerender`, `#[component(rerender)]`, `#[component(rerender, memo)]`), hooks (`use_hook`, `use_signal`, `use_memo`, `use_effect`, `use_effect_with`), contexts (`provide_context`, `use_context`), and a reconciler that patches mounted controls into a new element tree (`mount.rs`). A routed handler attached by an element is detached when its owner ends. Samples: the 7GUIs tasks as components that run again (`samples/SevenGuis/Hooks`). `docs/declarative-ui-react.md` compares the API with React. | Addition | Owner's request (issue #237, phase 1): the React-style model on the element tree of phase 0. | #237 |
| (none: upstream has no component model) | `ComponentHost` (a `Decorator` that shows a component; `Component` names one registered with `register_component`), `template(..)` (a recycling data template whose items are components, with a pool of hosts), `use_data_context`, and with the feature `xaml` `load_xaml`, `load_xaml_uri`, `xaml`, `use_xaml`. The crate registers its types for markup under `FerroUI.View`. Sample: `samples/ViewInterop`. | Addition | Owner's request (issue #237, phase 2): XAML and components together, and virtualized lists of components. | #237 |
| (none: upstream has no component model) | The builders of the other crates of controls behind features; `style(..)`, `style_of(..)` and `.styles([..])` (nested selectors parsed by the run-time selector parser, which parses `^`: see Parsers of markup); `use_parent_property`, `use_theme_variant`, `use_resource`, `use_resource_as`; `transition`, `transitions`, `animation(..)`, `use_animation`; a `Popup` and a property that holds a control of a class take elements. `ferroui-view` exports its type model for the markup compiler. Sample: `samples/ViewGallery`. | Addition | Owner's request (issue #237, phase 3): the rest of the API. | #237 |
| (none: upstream has no component model) | Hot reload of the literals of `rsx!` (`hot.rs`, feature `hot-reload`: the macro routes its literals through `hot::lit`, the sources are read again with `proc-macro2`), diagnostics of the declarative API in debug builds (`diagnostics.rs`), and an inspector of the mounted trees (`inspect.rs`, Shift+F12). `Element::List` marks the elements of an iteration. | Addition | Owner's request (issue #237, phase 4): tools. | #237 |
| (none: upstream has no component model) | Keyed children reordered with the fewest moves (`patch_list`, the longest increasing subsequence), handlers whose closures are swapped in place (`Handler`), values compared by the identity of the signal they follow (`PropValue::Follows`), memo by default for comparable parameters (autoref specialization in the expansion of `#[component(rerender)]`), `view_stats()`, and benchmarks in `tests/FerroUI.Benchmarks/declarative` (an addition to the ported benchmarks). | Addition | Owner's request (issue #237, phase 5): performance and scale. | #237 |

### Hot reload (`src/FerroUI.HotReload/`, `src/FerroUI.HotPatch/`, an addition)

The two crates are an addition of the port, compiled only with debug assertions; `docs/development/hot-reload.md` is their design and the record of their verification. The hooks they need in the ported crates are behind the `hot-reload` features of `ferroui-base`, `ferroui-markup`, `ferroui-markup-xaml` and `ferroui-markup-xaml-loader` and behind debug assertions: a build without them compiles and behaves as before.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (none: upstream's XAML is reloaded by external tools, not by the framework) | `ferroui-hot-reload`: transactional reload sessions on the UI thread, with content, component, panel, resource and style adapters that prepare detached candidates and commit or roll back together, and the transfer of control state. `ferroui-hot-patch`: registered Rust functions patched through Subsecond, source admission and a restart supervisor. | Addition | Owner's request: hot reload of XAML documents and of Rust code. | #233, #234 |
| The populate function generated for a class populates the instance. | With `hot-reload` of `ferroui-markup-xaml`, a generated populate function first records the instance weakly (`FerroXamlLoader::track_instance`) and runs a registered populate override instead when one is set; runtime includes and URI loads read a source overlay before the compiled assets. | Addition | A session populates new instances from the accepted source and finds the live ones. | #233 |
| Event setters, bindings and resource includes leave no record of the document that made them. | In development builds the compiled and interpreted loaders record the routed subscriptions they install, the binding descriptions of root properties, development keys, template declaration provenance and the source URI of resource and style includes, each weakly. | Addition | Reload adapters claim exactly what a document created and leave the application's own subscriptions, values and resources alone. | #233 |
| A value store or binding expression keeps no history of writes and cannot be paused. | With `hot-reload` of `ferroui-base`, the value store counts explicit writes to properties a reload owns, and a local binding can be prepared inactive, paused and restored (`data::LocalBindingReload`); resource dictionaries take candidate previews and ownership transfers. | Addition | An application write takes a property over from the document, and a failed revision restores the previous bindings and resources. | #233 |

### Headless platform (`src/Headless/FerroUI.Headless/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `HeadlessPlatformTypeface` keeps the font in an `UnmanagedFontMemory`, which is internal to the base assembly and visible to the headless assembly. | `FontMemory` in `headless_platform_stubs.rs` repeats the lookup of the sfnt table directory. | Representation | `UnmanagedFontMemory` is private to `ferroui-base` (`pub(crate)` in `media/fonts/mod.rs`), and that directory belongs to another batch. Making the type public removes the copy. | #66 (no pull request yet) |
| `HeadlessPlatformTypeface` reads the family name, weight, style and stretch from a dummy `GlyphTypeface` created over itself in its constructor. | The dummy glyph typeface is created over a private probe typeface with a copy of the font bytes (`IdentityProbeTypeface`). | Representation | A glyph typeface holds an `Rc` of its platform typeface, which does not exist while the typeface is being constructed. | #66 (no pull request yet) |
| `HeadlessWindowExtensions.GetLastRenderedFrame` tests the render interface of the locator with `is HeadlessPlatformRenderInterface`. | `HeadlessPlatformRenderInterface::is_current` compares the service by identity with the instances `initialize` created on the thread. | Representation | `IPlatformRenderInterface` has no way back from the trait object to its type. | #66 (no pull request yet) |
| `AvaloniaHeadlessPlatform.Compositor`, the render timer and `HeadlessWindowImpl._nextGlobalZOrder` are static fields; the last rendered frame of a window is guarded by a lock. | Thread-local values for the first three; the frame is guarded by a lock as upstream (see the next rows). | Representation | The compositor is an `Rc`. A headless application lives on the thread that initialised the platform. | #66 (no pull request yet) |
| The compositor of `AvaloniaHeadlessPlatform` lives as long as the process. | The thread of a `HeadlessUnitTestSession` drops the compositor of its platform when it ends, before the scope of its dispatcher (`FerroHeadlessPlatform::release_compositor`). | Representation | The compositor is a thread-local value. Left to the destructors of the thread it was dropped after the dispatcher of the thread was gone; `Compositor::drop` takes the compositor out of the render loop, which asserts the UI thread, and the second session thread of a process to end panicked there, inside a destructor, which aborts the process. | The fix of the id reuse (no pull request yet) |
| `HeadlessWindowImpl` is its own render surface (`Surfaces = [this]`, `IFramebufferPlatformSurface` on the window). | `HeadlessWindowSurface` (`headless_window_surface.rs`) is the surface: it holds the client size, the render scaling, the framebuffer format and the last rendered frame behind locks, and the window implementation reads and writes them through it. | Representation | The window implementation is an object of the UI thread (cells, `Rc`), and a surface is used by the thread that renders (`IPlatformRenderSurface: Send + Sync`). | Render thread R5.3 |
| `Lock` creates a `WriteableBitmap` per frame and keeps it as `_lastRenderedFrame`; `GetLastRenderedFrame` copies it into a new bitmap. | The framebuffer of a frame is pixel memory of its own (rows aligned to 4 bytes, in the framebuffer format of the options), kept as the last frame when the framebuffer is disposed; `get_last_rendered_frame` creates the bitmap from it on the UI thread. A framebuffer format the render interface cannot hold is reported by `get_last_rendered_frame`, not by the frame. | Representation | A `WriteableBitmap` cannot cross threads (its transcoding buffer is an `Rc`) and is created through the render interface of the calling thread, which the render thread does not have. The pixels handed back are the same. | Render thread R5.3 |
| `HeadlessGlyphRunStub.GlyphTypeface`, a property nothing reads. | Not kept. | Missing | The glyph typeface belongs to the UI thread and a glyph run is shared with the render thread (`IGlyphRunImpl: Send + Sync`); a field that cannot cross threads for a property without readers is not worth a lock. | Render thread R1.2 |
| `HeadlessUnitTestSession.StartNew(Type entryPointType)` and `AvaloniaTestApplicationAttribute(Type)`: a type that has a `BuildAvaloniaApp` method or inherits the application class, found through reflection; `GetOrStartForAssembly(Assembly)` reads the two assembly attributes. | An entry point is the function that builds the application (`fn() -> AppBuilder`; `AppBuilder::configure::<TApp>` for an application class). An assembly with its two attributes is a `FerroTestAssembly` value (`name`, `test_application`, `test_isolation`) that a test crate declares as a `static`; the sessions are kept by its name. | Representation | No reflection and no assembly attributes. | 2026-10-09 |
| `HeadlessUnitTestSession.Dispatch` returns a `Task`; an exception of the action or of the cleanup faults it. `DispatchCore` captures the `ExecutionContext` and runs the work item in it. | `HeadlessUnitTestTask<T>` (`wait`, `wait_timeout`, `is_completed`), which another thread waits for; a panic of the action or of the cleanup is raised again in the thread that observes the task. Three methods for the three overloads (`dispatch`, `dispatch_result`, `dispatch_task`); the future of `dispatch_task` is created and polled on the session thread. No execution context. A work item still queued when the session is disposed is dropped and its task cancelled (upstream leaves the task incomplete). | Representation | A task of the dispatcher (`DispatcherTask`) belongs to a dispatcher, and the caller is on another thread; exceptions are panics. | 2026-10-09 |
| `HeadlessUnitTestSession` holds its `AppBuilder` and the shared synchronization context in fields, and resets the one UI thread dispatcher of the process around an isolated test (`Dispatcher.ResetBeforeUnitTests`, `ResetForUnitTests`). | The builder and the shared context are state of the consumer loop on the session thread (`SessionThread`); the session thread is a unit test thread for its whole life (`Dispatcher::unit_test_scope`), so the dispatcher of the session is the dispatcher of its thread. The cleanup of an isolated application also clears the queue of the controls that wait for their loaded event (`Control::reset_loaded_queue_for_unit_tests`), as the scope of the unit test application of the controls does. | Representation | The builder is `Rc`-based. The tests of a test binary run in parallel on threads of their own, so a process-wide reset would take the dispatcher from under them. | 2026-10-09 |
| `HeadlessUnitTestSession.EnsureSharedApplication`: the cleanup after a test of a shared application runs the jobs of the dispatcher (`Dispatcher.UIThread.RunJobs()`) and nothing else. | The cleanup then processes the composition batches the test left in flight: while the media context has one (`MediaContext::has_composition_batches_in_flight`, an addition), a forced tick of the render timer and the jobs, at most ten rounds (`finish_composition_in_flight` in `headless_unit_test_session.rs`). | Behaviour | Upstream's fault: a test that closes a window ends with a batch in flight (the window hides before its renderer is disposed, which asks for a commit, and the jobs after the test commit it), and the render timer of the headless platform ticks on the dispatcher of the session, which does not run between two dispatches. The next test of the shared application then has its commits held back until a tick, and a window it shows is not composed by its jobs: `should_render_to_a_compositor_snapshot_capture` failed with "the visual is not attached to a composition target" in about one run of the suite in four (the leak test before it, in the order the threads of the test harness dispatched them); upstream's session, media context and close of a window have the same sequence (read at the tracked commit, not run). Test: `shared_session_tests.rs` (fails every time without the change). An isolated application is set up afresh for every test and is unchanged. | macos-render-fixes (pull request 195) |

### VNC server of the headless platform (`src/Headless/FerroUI.Headless.Vnc/`)

The design, the choice of the RFB server and the corrections of the ported library are in `headless-vnc.md`.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The project references the NuGet package of RemoteViewing, a .NET VNC client and server library. | The parts of RemoteViewing the original uses are ported into the crate (`remote_viewing/`, BSD-2-Clause, see the `NOTICE.md` of the crate), with `flate2`, `jpeg-encoder`, `des` and `getrandom` in place of SharpCompress, libjpeg-turbo and the cryptography of .NET. | Representation | No maintained Rust crate with a compatible licence implements the server side of RFB in the shape the original uses (a framebuffer source asked for frames, resize requests); `headless-vnc.md`, section 3. | `headless-vnc` |
| `StartWithHeadlessVncPlatform` accepts the clients in an asynchronous loop on the UI thread (`await AcceptTcpClientAsync`). | A thread of the server accepts them and posts each connection to the UI thread, which creates the session as upstream. | Representation | The port has no asynchronous sockets. | `headless-vnc` |
| `HeadlessVncFramebufferSource.Capture` reads the last rendered frame of the window on the thread of the session. | `capture` runs its work on the UI thread (a synchronous invoke) and the session thread waits for it; when the UI thread has shut down the framebuffer of before is returned. | Representation | The window and its platform implementation belong to the UI thread. | `headless-vnc` |
| The pointer and key handlers post each event to the UI thread on its own. | The events wait in a queue of the source and are delivered in the order they came; a delivery that starts inside another leaves the queue to it. | Behaviour | The input extensions of a headless window run the jobs of the dispatcher before they deliver an event, so an event posted while another is delivered overtook it (a release right after a press lost the click). | `headless-vnc` |
| `StartWithHeadlessVncPlatform(builder, host, int port, [password,] args, shutdownMode = OnLastWindowClose)`; a bad address throws. | `start_with_headless_vnc_platform(host, u16, args, shutdown_mode)` and `start_with_headless_vnc_platform_with_password`; the shutdown mode has no default; a bad address panics. | Representation | Rust has no default arguments or overloads; a port is 16 bits. | `headless-vnc` |

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ColorPickerHelpers.CreateComponentBitmapAsync` and `ColorSpectrum.CreateBitmapsAndColorMap` compute their pixels in `Task.Run` on a thread-pool thread, filling the pooled lists they are given in place. | `create_component_bitmap_async` and `create_bitmaps_and_color_map` compute them in a job of the dispatcher of the control's thread at `DispatcherPriority::BACKGROUND`; the buffer is moved into the job and handed back as its result (`helpers/color_picker_helpers.rs`, `color_spectrum/color_spectrum.rs`). | Behaviour | The port has no thread pool and the browser has no threads. The methods still return before the bitmaps exist and apply them afterwards, as upstream, but the pixels are computed on the UI thread. | #55 |

The skipped entries cannot be the nearest colour (the comparison is strict), and which name .NET returns for a shared value is unspecified. | #55 |

| `FlatColorPalette.FlatColor` and `MaterialColorPalette.MaterialColor` are nested enumerations, with members that alias others (`Pomegranate = Pomegranate6`). | Enumerations of the module of the palette; the aliases are associated constants (`FlatColor::Pomegranate`). | Representation | Rust has no nested types and no enumeration members with equal values. | #55 |
| `IValueProvider.SetValue` may throw any exception; `ColorSpectrumAutomationPeer.SetValue` throws `FormatException` for text that is not a colour. | `IValueProvider::set_value` returns `Result<(), ProviderError>` (`Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync>`), and the peer returns a `FormatError` (`ferroui-controls`, `automation/provider/i_value_provider.rs`). | Representation | Errors are results in the port; the error type of the contract was `ElementNotEnabledException` only. | #55 |
| The theme documents of the library are compiled into the assembly, and the compiler removes a compiled resource from it. | The documents are compiled by the build of the crate (`build.rs`; `docs/porting/xaml.md` 9.5.20) and are not assets of the assembly; with the feature `document-assets` of the crate (and in its own tests) they are embedded as assets too. An application that compiles its markup is linked to their build functions; one that loads its markup at run time states the feature and includes them through the asset, and the include then loads the compiled markup through the loader table. | Addition | The run-time loader leaves an include of a document of another assembly to run time only for a document the asset loader has (`XamlRuntimeIncludeFallback`, `xaml.md` decision 22). The ControlCatalog compiles its markup and does not state the feature; its feature `runtime-markup` and its tests, which load `App.xaml` with the run-time loader too, do (`xaml.md` 9.5.22). | branch `catalog-compiled-markup` (no pull request yet) |
| `ColorSpectrumAutomationPeerTests` is part of the tests of `Avalonia.Controls`. | It is in the colour picker crate (`automation/peers/color_spectrum_automation_peer_tests.rs`). | Test | `ferroui-controls` does not depend on the colour picker crate. | #55 |
