### OpenGL (`src/FerroUI.OpenGL/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EglContext` keeps its `EglExternalObjectsFeature` among its features after `Dispose` (the feature is not `IDisposable`), so `TryGetFeature(typeof(IGlContextExternalObjectsFeature))` of a disposed context still returns it. | The feature is registered with a disposable that does nothing, so `EglContext::dispose` removes it with the disposable features and a disposed context answers `None` (`egl_context.rs`). `EglExternalObjectsFeature` itself refers to its context weakly. | Behaviour | The feature holds the feature of the OpenGL extensions (`ExternalObjectsOpenGlExtensionFeature`), which holds the context: without a garbage collector the context and the feature would keep each other alive. Nothing can use the feature of a disposed context: its members need the context. | #66 |
| `EglGlPlatformSurface` is one object, at once the render surface a top level hands out and the `IGlPlatformSurface` the backend finds by a cast. Its `IEglWindowGlPlatformSurfaceInfo` is any object, in practice the window implementation, whose `Handle`, `Size` and `Scaling` the rendering thread reads without a rule. | The surface is shared between the threads (`Arc`, `Send + Sync`) and holds nothing but the info. The view as an OpenGL surface (`try_get_surface_kind`) is a handle of the calling thread: a second `EglGlPlatformSurface` over the same info, not the same object. `IEglWindowGlPlatformSurfaceInfo` is `Send + Sync` and held in `Arc`: an implementor is what a window publishes of itself (atomics or a lock), not the window object of the UI thread. | Representation | A frame is rendered by the render thread or by the UI thread (R5), so a surface is used by a thread other than the one that made it; the contract of the view returns a handle that is not shared between threads, and the surface has no state beyond its info, so two surfaces over one info cannot be told apart. The render target, which holds the EGL surface and the context, stays on the thread that renders. | Render thread R5.3 |

### Browser backend (`src/Browser/FerroUI.Browser/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BrowserWebGlRenderTarget` and `BrowserSoftwareRenderTarget` are the render surfaces of a canvas; with a render worker they are objects of the worker, created over an `OffscreenCanvas` that was transferred to it. | Three objects (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 4). `BrowserRenderSurface` (`rendering/browser_render_surface.rs`) is the surface handed to the compositor: it holds only the `Arc<BrowserSurfaceShared>`, answers `is_ready` from its atomics, and answers what kind of surface it is, and creates its render targets, from the render target of the calling thread. The two upstream types are that render target: `Rc` objects of the thread that draws (the object of the script side, the WebGL context with its `GlInterface`), kept in a `thread_local!` table by target id and handed out by `get_render_target(id)`, which wraps the target of the script of the calling thread on first use. The render targets made from them (`GlSurface`, `FramebufferRenderTarget`, the retained framebuffer) read the size from the shared object at the start of each frame. No `ThreadBound` is left in the browser crate. | Representation | A render surface is `Send + Sync` and is created by the thread of the user interface, while the script object and the WebGL context exist only on the thread that draws (the worker, for a transferred canvas). A thread without the target is told that the surface is of no kind instead of panicking. The build without threads has the same three objects on its one thread. | Browser B2.5 (replaces the `ThreadBound` of Render thread R5.3) |
| `BrowserWebGlRenderTarget` is cast to `IGlPlatformSurface`. | `BrowserRenderSurface::try_get_surface_kind` answers with a second `BrowserRenderSurface` over the same shared state, in an `Rc`, when the render target of the calling thread is a WebGL one. | Representation | The surface kind is handed out as `Rc<dyn IGlPlatformSurface>` while a surface lives in an `Arc`; a surface is nothing but its shared state, so a second one is the same surface. | Render thread R5.3, Browser B2.5 |
| `BrowserTopLevelImpl.Surfaces` is read from the top level by the renderer each time it creates its render target. | `render_surfaces()` returns a function over a shared `Arc<Mutex<Vec<..>>>`; the top level writes its current surfaces to it when the function is asked for, when the canvas changes its size and when it is disposed (then the list is empty). From B2.5 the list has the `BrowserRenderSurface` of the canvas from the start: `RenderTargetBrowserSurface::get_render_surfaces` no longer answers with an empty list while the render target does not exist, the surface is not ready instead (upstream: `GetRenderSurfaces` is empty until `Target` is set). | Representation | The function may be called by a thread that cannot reach the top level; the default (a snapshot at the creation of the renderer) would keep handing out the surface of a disposed top level, and would stay empty if the page had not created the render target yet. | Render thread R5.3 |
| `WebRenderTargetRegistry.initializeWorker` (script) stores the render target a worker creates for a transferred canvas and tells nobody; the managed side asks the registry of its thread for the target on each frame (`BrowserPlatformGraphics.Target`, `IsReady`). | The handler also calls a new export of the worker's module, `CanvasHelper_OnRenderTargetRegistered(id, kind)` (`interop/canvas_helper.rs`, kind 2 for WebGL and 1 for software), which reaches the subscribers of that thread (`add_render_target_registered`). The message key is `ferrouiCmd`. | Behaviour | The thread that created the canvas has a registry of its own, which never has the target, so it cannot learn by asking that the target exists; and the first frame of the worker should not wait for a poll (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 4). An integer, not the name of the kind, so that the report needs no string to cross into the module from a worker. | Browser B2.1 |
| The service worker (`avalonia-sw.ts`) answers the streamed downloads of the save picker polyfill and nothing else; `AvaloniaModule.ResolveServiceWorkerPath` is `./avalonia-sw.js`. | `ferroui-sw.ts` has a second part: registered as `ferroui-sw.js?coi=1` it fetches every other request and hands the response on with the headers of cross-origin isolation (and adds them to the download response). `resolve_service_worker_path` answers with that address in a module built with threads (`target_feature = "atomics"`) and with `./ferroui-sw.js` otherwise. | Behaviour, threaded build only | A module with threads needs an isolated page, a static host cannot send the headers, and a scope has one service worker: the worker that isolates the page and the worker of the platform have to be one script under one address, or the second registration would replace the first and the page would lose its shared memory at the next load (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 3 and "B2.2"). Without the parameter the worker behaves as upstream's. | Browser B2.2 |
| `StorageProvider.saveFileDialog` (`storage/storageProvider.ts`) calls the polyfill's `showSaveFilePicker`, whose fallback handle (`native-file-system-adapter`, `src/adapters/downloader.js`) posts the stream to the service worker and navigates the hidden download frame in the same turn. | Without a native save picker, or with the polyfill preferred, `saveFileDialog` wraps the port's `DownloadFileHandle` (`storage/downloadFileHandle.ts`, a TypeScript form of that file) in the polyfill's `FileSystemFileHandle`. The handle navigates the frame when the worker's first request for a chunk arrives. `ferroui-sw.ts` records the address before it makes the stream. | Behaviour | The message with the stream and the request of the frame are not ordered: when the request reaches the worker first, the address is unknown, the server answers 404 and the download never starts, without an error (one run in five of `storage_view.test.mjs` on a busy machine, with and without threads). The worker's first request proves that it has the stream. See `browser-render-worker.md`, "The streamed save that never arrived". | Browser B2.2 |
| The script reads the memory of the module through the runtime (`Module.HEAPU8`, or the views the .NET runtime hands out). | `FerroExports.heapU8()` makes its view from the memory object the module exports (`wasmMemory`) and makes it anew when the buffer of the memory is another object. | Representation | A view of a shared memory is not detached when another thread grows the memory, and the `HEAPU8` property of a module is only refreshed by that module's own accesses (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 5). | Browser B2.2 |
| `BrowserRenderTimer.Tick` starts the frame loop on the thread that sets it unless `BrowserWindowingPlatform.IsThreadingEnabled`; with threads only `RenderWorker` starts it (`StartOnThisThread`). The timer is created with `RunsInBackground` false in both modes. | `set_tick` starts the loop only for a timer created with `is_background` false, and (B2.5) only while the page has no render thread (`RenderWorker::exists()`: upstream's condition as a fact of the running page instead of the build, so that a module built with threads still runs on one when the thread is not started). A timer created with true is started by the thread that renders (`start_on_this_thread`), records that thread, and implements `IRenderTimer::request_tick_out_of_turn`: one tick queued for that thread, run from its event loop, with a timestamp from `TimerHelper.now` (script, `performance.now()` of that thread). | Behaviour | The condition is the timer's own instead of a global of the platform, so that one module can hold both kinds (the comparison switch of B2.6). A worker's next animation frame may be a frame away, or may not come while the thread of the page does not return to the browser; the thread that is about to wait asks for the frame (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 3). | Browser B2.3 |
| The dispatcher of the threaded mode is `ManagedDispatcherImpl`, woken from any thread through an event of the runtime; the browser's single-threaded dispatcher is only used without threads. | `BrowserSingleThreadedDispatcherImpl` stays the dispatcher in both builds. Its signal handle, called on a thread that has no dispatcher, sets a flag and queues a call for the thread of the dispatcher (`interop/thread_proxy.rs`, a proxying queue of Emscripten); the call signals the dispatcher there, which posts its task as for a signal of its own thread. | Addition | The UI thread stays on the main thread of the page (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 3), and the render thread has to deliver the completions of batches and jobs to it. The call does not raise `signaled` itself, so dispatcher work never runs nested in whatever the main thread was doing. | Browser B2.3 |
| The surface of a view is one object both threads read (`BrowserSurface`, `RenderTargetBrowserSurface`: `_canvasSize`, `Scaling`), by convention. | `BrowserSurfaceShared` (`rendering/browser_surface_shared.rs`): the id of the render target, the size in device pixels with the scaling (read as one value through a version counter, no lock), the kind of the target and a disposed flag, all atomics. From B2.5 it is what `RenderTargetBrowserSurface`, `BrowserRenderSurface` and `BrowserPlatformGraphics` are built on, and a canvas is found by the id of its render target (`register`, `find`, `report_target`, `unregister`: one table behind a lock, entered when a canvas is created or closed and when a worker reports a target; a report that arrives before the canvas is registered is kept for it). `add_size_changed` (`interop/canvas_helper.rs`) lets a canvas without a top-level be followed. | Addition | A canvas transferred to a worker exists there only, and its element only in the page; what both threads need has to be `Send + Sync`, and the thread of the page may not wait for a lock. | Browser B2.3 |
| `BrowserRenderTarget` is an abstract class (`PlatformGraphicsContext`, the static `GetRenderTarget(id, sizeGetter)`) with the two kinds as subclasses, each taking the function that reads the size of the canvas. | An enum with the variants `WebGl(Rc<BrowserWebGlRenderTarget>)` and `Software(Rc<BrowserSoftwareRenderTarget>)`, with `platform_graphics_context()` and `kind()`; `get_render_target(id)` takes no size function. | Representation | The kinds are closed, and each needs its own way of creating a render target; the size is read from `BrowserSurfaceShared` by what draws a frame, so the target itself holds nothing that the other thread writes. | Browser B2.5 |
| `BrowserPlatformGraphics` (in `RenderTargetBrowserSurface`) keeps `_target` and `CanvasSize` in fields, and `TryGetFeature` answers with the object itself. | It holds the `Arc<BrowserSurfaceShared>` and nothing else: `is_ready` is "the target exists, the view is not disposed and the size is not empty" and `uses_contexts` the kind, both from atomics that the thread owning the target publishes (the thread of the page when it kept the canvas, in `RenderTargetBrowserSurface::new`; the render thread when its worker reports the target). `get_shared_context` resolves the render target of the calling thread. The ready state feature is a second object over the same shared state (an `Arc`, by the contract of R5.8). | Representation | The object is created by the thread of the user interface and asked by the thread that renders; a field written by one and read by the other would be a data race, and the registry of the thread of the page never has the target of a transferred canvas, so that thread cannot answer by asking (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 3 and 4). | Browser B2.5 |
| `RenderWorker.InitializeAsync` starts a worker of the runtime and is awaited in `PreSetupBrowser` before any view exists; `RenderWorker.WorkerThreadId` is then the id every canvas is created with. | `RenderWorker::start(timer, on_thread)` (`rendering/render_worker.rs`) starts a thread of the module and returns at once. The thread subscribes to the reports of its registry, calls `initialize_worker`, keeps itself alive (`emscripten_runtime_keepalive_push`), starts the timer it was given and publishes its id (`thread_id()`); then a call queued for the thread of the page (`interop/thread_proxy.rs`) tells the registry of that thread's script (`WebRenderTargetRegistry.workerStarted`, new). A canvas created in between is created with `PENDING_RENDER_THREAD` (-1, `RenderWorker::canvas_thread_id()`): the script transfers its control at once and holds the `registerCanvas` message back until `workerStarted`. `RenderWorker::post` queues a call for the thread. Compiled in every build and started by nothing in the platform yet (B2.6); without threads `start` is an error. | Behaviour | A start function of the port cannot wait for a thread on the thread of the page, and a message posted before the handler is installed goes to the handler of the runtime (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 4, "Who creates, who transfers"). Section 4 called the notification an export, `RenderWorker_OnStarted`; nothing in script calls it, so it is a queued call inside the module and an import. | Browser B2.5 |
| `BrowserSharedRenderLoop.RenderTimer` is `new BrowserRenderTimer(false)` in both modes, and the compositor of a surface is `new Compositor(RenderLoop, platformGraphics)` (`RenderTargetBrowserSurface.CreateCompositor`): with a render worker the loop still reports that it does not run in the background. | `BrowserSharedRenderLoop::start_render_thread` (called by `BrowserWindowingPlatform::register` in a module built with threads) creates the timer with `is_background` true and starts the render thread with it; `renders_on_render_thread` says so afterwards. A view whose canvas goes to that thread gets `Compositor::with_render_thread(.., false, ..)`, which over a background loop is a compositor confined to its render thread: the thread of the page commits and waits at the synchronous points, and never renders. Without a render thread the timer and the compositor are created as before (`new(false)`, `Compositor::with_scheduler`). | Behaviour, threaded build only | Read literally, upstream's synchronous commits render on the UI thread in the threaded mode, where the WebGL context refuses the call and the registry has no target (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 1, "Frame pacing"). A canvas whose control was transferred can be drawn to by one thread only, so the mode of the compositor has to say that. | Browser B2.6 |
| `RenderWorker.InitializeAsync` is awaited in `BrowserAppBuilder.PreSetupBrowser`, before the platform is set up. Whether threads are used is a fact of the build (`BrowserWindowingPlatform.IsThreadingEnabled`). | The render thread is started in `BrowserWindowingPlatform::register`, after the dispatcher is installed and before anything asks for the render loop, and is not waited for. `BrowserPlatformOptions::render_thread` (new, `true` by default) keeps a module built with threads on the thread of the page when it is `false`; the examples set it from `?RenderThread=false`. | Addition | The render thread wakes the dispatcher of the page, so that has to exist first; a start function cannot wait for a thread (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 4). The switch is the comparison the stages B2.6 and B3 need: one module, both modes. | Browser B2.6 |
| `BrowserRenderTimer` has no way to tick out of turn; nothing waits for a frame of the worker. | A request for a tick out of turn that reaches a background timer before a thread has started it is kept, and the thread that starts the timer ticks once when it does (`browser_render_timer.rs`). | Behaviour | The thread of the page asks before it waits for the first frame, and the render thread it started a moment ago may not have started the timer yet. The wait would then depend on the first animation frame of the worker, which a page that is loaded hidden does not have. | Browser B2.6 |
| Nothing reports where frames are drawn. | `rendering::RenderStatistics` (`rendering/render_statistics.rs`): counters of the frames that reached a canvas (counted when the session of a WebGL render target ends and after `putPixelData`), the thread, kind, OpenGL ES version and size of the last one, the ticks of the frame loop of a render thread, and the calls those ticks had the runtime carry to the main thread. `themed_view` exports them as `themedViewRendering()`. | Addition | With a render worker nothing on the thread of the page sees a frame; the behaviour test of the stage has to assert which thread drew. | Browser B2.6 |
| The runtime's proxying of calls from a thread to the main thread is not observed. | In the worker of a thread, `ferroui-worker-attach.js` (linked into the script of a threaded module) wraps Emscripten's `proxyToMainThread` and counts its calls on the worker's `Module`, with the index of the last function; `FerroExports.proxiedCalls()` and `lastProxiedFunction()` (script) hand the numbers to `interop/thread_proxy.rs`, and a background `BrowserRenderTimer` reads the count around each tick. | Addition, threaded build only | A frame that makes the render thread wait for the main thread defeats the render thread (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 7, risk 9); the count is how a test shows there is none, and the index how a count above zero is explained. It relies on a name of the generated script, like the rest of that file. | Browser B2.6 |
| `RenderTargetBrowserSurface.Dispose` posts a job that takes the server compositor out of the render loop, then destroys the surface of the script side. Nothing releases the render target, Skia's context or the WebGL context of the canvas, and nothing sends `unregisterCanvas`, whose handler the script has. | No job. `dispose` hands the release to the thread that renders (`rendering/render_target_browser_surface.rs`, `release_canvas`): done on the spot, under the compositor lock, when the calling thread renders the view; queued for the render thread (`RenderWorker::post`, with `Compositor::locked_server`) when the compositor is confined to it. There, with the graphics context of the canvas current, the render targets and the backend context are released (`ServerCompositor::release_gpu_resources`) and the compositor stops rendering (`stop_rendering`, in place of the removal from the loop); the shared state is marked disposed; the render target leaves the table of the thread; the script of the thread that created the canvas is told (`WebRenderTargetRegistry.unregister`), which releases the target it kept or posts `unregisterCanvas` to the worker, where the context leaves Emscripten's table (`GL.deleteContext`). A surface that is dropped without `dispose` calls it (`Drop`). A canvas that never reached the render thread is released by the thread of the page; one whose release cannot be queued is only marked disposed and stays alive in the worker. | Behaviour | Upstream leaves a closed view to its garbage collector and never frees the context. Here the objects are `Rc`s of the render thread, a worker draws several canvases with several contexts (an object released while another context is current is released there), and a context that stays in Emscripten's table lives for ever. As a job (the first version) the release never ran: the batch of a view that is closed and dropped is committed and not applied (`browser-render-worker.md`, section 4 and "B2.7"). | Browser B2.7 |
| The surface is not marked when its view is disposed; `IsReady` of the platform graphics is `Target != null && CanvasSize.Size != default`. | `BrowserSurfaceShared::dispose` is called by the release of the canvas, on the thread that renders, after which the graphics of the compositor and the render surface are not ready and `BrowserRenderSurface` resolves no render target. | Behaviour | After the release the render target is gone from the table of its thread; a frame that found the graphics ready would create a backend context for a canvas that has none. Set by the release, not by the thread of the page: the composition target is disposed after the top-level and, when its batch reaches the render thread first, needs the graphics ready to release its render target. | Browser B2.7 |
| `AvaloniaView` cannot be closed. | `FerroView::dispose` disposes the top-level of the view. | Addition | Without it nothing outside the crate reaches the disposal of a view, and a page that removes a view keeps its canvas and its context. | Browser B2.7 |
| `RenderLoop.TimerTick` catches the exception of a frame and logs it through the logger; the loop goes on. | The same in the render loop. In addition `RenderWorker::start` wraps the panic hook of the process: a panic of the render thread is counted (`RenderStatistics::render_thread_panics`) and a call queued for the thread of the page logs it as an error on the console of the page (`FerroExports.reportRenderThreadPanic`, which also keeps the message in `FerroExports.renderThreadPanics`). | Addition, threaded build only | The render thread has no log sink, and what a worker writes to its console is not on the console of the page: a frame that panicked was invisible to the page and to its tests. | Browser B2.7 |
| `RenderStatistics` (an addition of the port) | Two more counters: `canvases_released` and `render_thread_panics`. | Addition | What the tests of the disposal and of the panic assert. | Browser B2.7 |
| `WebRenderTarget` has no way to let go of its canvas. | `WebRenderTarget.release()` (nothing) and `WebGlRenderTarget.release()` (`GL.deleteContext` of its handle), called by the registry when a canvas is unregistered. | Addition | See the first row of this stage. | Browser B2.7 |
| `AvaloniaView` closes the splash screen of the host page in the first animation frame of its top-level (`topLevel.RequestAnimationFrame`), on the thread that also draws. | `FerroView::with_host` gives the handler that closes the splash to the shared state of its canvas (`BrowserSurfaceShared::on_first_frame`, `rendering/browser_surface_shared.rs`). The thread that draws counts the frames of the canvas where `RenderStatistics` counts them, and at the first one runs the handler (when it is the thread that asked) or queues a call for the thread that asked (`thread_proxy::run_on_thread`). | Behaviour | With a render thread the animation frame of the top-level runs on the thread of the page and draws nothing: the page showed the closed splash over an empty canvas, which input cannot hit, until the worker drew (2.1 s in the catalog under the software rasteriser; `browser-render-worker.md`, "B3" and "B3 follow-ups"). On one thread the class was set a task before the frame was drawn (0.45 s earlier under the software rasteriser); now it is set in the task that drew it. A view that never draws keeps its splash. | Browser B3 |
| `BrowserPlatformOptions` has `RenderingMode` (`WebGL2`, `WebGL1`, `Software2D`) and one render backend, Skia (`UseBrowser` calls `UseSkia`). | `BrowserPlatformOptions::renderer` (`BrowserRenderer { Skia, Vello }`, Skia by default) chooses the render backend of the module; `start_browser_app` and `setup_browser_app` put `use_vello` in the place of the backend the builder had when it asks for Vello. The rendering modes keep their names and their meaning, the kind of canvas a view gets: with the Vello backend `WebGL2` is drawn by its hybrid mode and `Software2D` by its CPU mode, and `WebGL1`, for which it has no renderer, is taken out of the list before the script side is asked for a canvas (`BrowserRenderer::rendering_modes`; a list with nothing else gives a 2D canvas). The backend is in a module built with the feature `vello` of the crate; without it the option fails at start with a message that names the feature. | Addition | The port has a second render backend (`vello-backend.md`, section 12); upstream's mode names say what the canvas is, which both backends can serve, so no mode was added and Skia's behaviour is unchanged. | Vello backend, stage 9 |
| `WebGlContext` (in `BrowserWebGlRenderTarget.cs`) hands out `IGlContext` and `IGlSkiaSpecificOptionsFeature`. | With the feature `vello`, the context of a WebGL 2 canvas also hands out `ferroui_vello::web_gl::IWebGlCanvasFeature`: the canvas of the script side (`canvas` of the script's `WebGlRenderTarget`). | Addition | The renderer of `vello_gpu` calls the WebGL2 context of the page through `web-sys`, not the entry points of the module's WebGL emulation that `GlInterface` holds, and gets the context from its canvas. | Vello backend, stage 9 |
| `BrowserInputHandler` stamps every raw input event with the time it handles it (`Timestamp`, a `Stopwatch` started with the handler). | A pointer event is stamped with the time the page raised it: the page passes `performance.now() - event.timeStamp`, the age of the event, and `BrowserInputHandler::event_timestamp` takes it from the time of the handler; an age that is negative, not a number or older than 5 s stands for now, and the time stamps of pointer events do not go back. Key, text and wheel events keep the time they are handled. | Behaviour | The page handles input between the frames it draws, and a frame that takes longer than 40 ms (a page that draws on its own thread on a slow phone, a CI runner without a GPU) made the gap between the last move of a finger and its release longer than the 40 ms after which the velocity tracker of the scroll gesture takes the pointer for stopped: a flick did not scroll on. Test: `a_pointer_event_is_stamped_with_the_time_the_page_raised_it`, and the touch flick of `scripts/browser/tests/control_catalog.test.mjs`, whose events carry the times of a touch screen. | 2026-10-17 |

### Native backend (`src/FerroUI.Native/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TopLevelImpl` is its own software render surface (`IFramebufferPlatformSurface`), and its `FramebufferRenderTarget` reads `_savedLogicalSize`, `_savedScaling`, `Native` and `_syncRoot` of the top level. | `TopLevelFramebufferSurface`, an object of its own, is the surface; it shares a state (the lock, an alive flag, the saved size and scaling) with its render targets, and the top level writes the size and the scaling into it when they change and clears the flag when it is disposed. | Representation | A render surface is shared with the thread that renders (`IPlatformRenderSurface: Send + Sync`), and the top level is an object of the UI thread (`Rc`, cells). | Render thread R5.3 |
| `GlPlatformSurface`, `MetalPlatformSurface` and the top level (as software surface) hold the native top level and call it from whichever thread asks; `CreateGlRenderTarget`, `CreateMetalRenderTarget` and `CreateFramebufferRenderTarget` throw `RenderTargetNotReadyException` off the UI thread. | The surfaces hold the native top level in `ThreadBound` (`SurfaceTopLevel`): only the UI thread reaches it, and the top level takes it back when it is disposed. The same check and the same failure ("The render target is not ready.") are kept. | Representation | The reference counts of the native objects are not atomic (`ComObject::AddRef`/`Release` in `native/FerroUI.Native/inc/comimpl.h`), so a reference to the native top level cannot be taken or released by the render thread; upstream's proxies hold one native reference each and the garbage collector shares the proxy. | Render thread R5.3 |
| The surfaces do not override `IPlatformRenderSurface.IsReady`; `ServerCompositionTarget` catches the `RenderTargetNotReadyException` of the creation and waits for a ready render target. | The surfaces (the OpenGL surface too, since 2026-10-15) answer `is_ready() == false` off the UI thread and once the top level is disposed, so the composition target waits without creating. | Behaviour | The port's composition target has no handler for the "not ready" panic; the outcome (the target waits for a ready render target, and the next frame of the UI thread creates it) is upstream's. | Render thread R5.3 |
| `TopLevelImpl.Surfaces` keeps returning the surfaces after `Dispose`. | `surfaces()` and the function of `render_surfaces()` read one shared cell, filled by `init` and emptied by `dispose`. | Behaviour | The surfaces give their native top level back at `dispose`, on the UI thread, so nothing is left to create a render target with; the function is called by the thread that renders and cannot ask the top level. | Render thread R5.3 |
| `DeferredFramebuffer` holds the `IAvnSoftwareRenderTarget` and its lock callback passes the native top level (`Action<Action<IAvnTopLevel>>`). | The framebuffer holds no render target: the callback lends it (`&IFrnSoftwareRenderTarget`) under the lock of the surface. `FramebufferRenderTarget` owns the only reference, in a mutex taken after `sync_root`. | Representation | With reference counts that are not atomic, the native render target may be used by the render thread only if no second reference exists and every use is under one lock; upstream has that discipline through its single proxy and `lock (_parent._syncRoot)`. | Render thread R5.3 |
| `lock (_parent._syncRoot)` can be entered again by the thread that holds it. | `sync_root` is a `std::sync::Mutex`, which cannot. | Representation | Nothing under the lock (`SetFrame`, the release of the render target) calls back into the render target. | Render thread R5.3 |
| `IMetalPlatformSurface` is the surface object itself. | `try_get_surface_kind` hands out a view (`MetalPlatformSurfaceView`) that forwards to the surface. | Representation | The Metal contract hands out `Rc<dyn IMetalPlatformSurface>` and the surface lives in an `Arc`. | Render thread R5.3 |
| The native `PlatformRenderTimer` has no destructor: its display link, whose callback context is the object, keeps running after the last reference is given back. | The destructor stops and releases the display link, as a patch of `PlatformRenderTimer.mm` kept in `native/FerroUI.Native/patches/` (`0002`). | Behaviour | The render timer is released when the thread-local state of the main thread is destroyed at the exit of the process; with OpenGL the exit handlers of the system take long enough for a tick to read the freed object (a crash in `PlatformRenderTimer::OnTick` after a clean run of `examples/platform_window.rs --opengl`, three of three; none with the patch, three of three). | 2026-10-15 (`apple-opengl.md`) |
| `ComObject::AddRef` and `Release` of the native library are plain increments. | Atomic (`std::atomic<unsigned int>`), as a patch of `comimpl.h` kept in `native/FerroUI.Native/patches/`. | Behaviour | A native object is referenced and released by the UI thread and by the render thread. | Render thread R5.4 |
| `MetalExternalObjectsFeature` holds the `IAvnMetalDevice` of its `MetalDevice`, the same wrapper the device disposes. | The feature holds its device weakly and asks it for the native device at each call. | Representation | Every holder of a native object has a reference of its own here, so a feature with its own reference would keep the native device alive and usable after `MetalDevice::dispose`; through the device a disposed device fails the feature, as upstream. | Row 9 of the tracking list |
| `MetalPlatformGraphics` keeps the factory and hands it to every `MetalDevice`, whose `GpuHandleWrapFeature` asks it for a memory management helper (`CreateMemoryManagementHelper`): one helper per device. | `MetalPlatformGraphics::new` asks the factory for the helper once, on the UI thread, and every device and its `GpuHandleWrapFeature` get a reference to it; `GpuHandleWrapFeature::new` takes the helper, not the factory. | Representation | The graphics are shared with the render thread (`Send + Sync`, R5.8), which creates the devices. The factory is an object of the UI thread: the destructor of the native factory releases the application events, the deallocator and the dispatcher, so its last reference must not be given back by another thread. The helper (`memhelp.mm`) has no state and only calls `CFRetain`, `CFRelease`, `retain` and `release`, which any thread may call; the argument is written next to the `unsafe impl` in `metal.rs`. | `GpuHandleWrapFeature.cs` |
| `IGlPlatformSurface` is the surface object itself (`GlPlatformSurface`). | `try_get_surface_kind` hands out a view (`GlPlatformSurfaceView`) that forwards to the surface, as for Metal. | Representation | The OpenGL contract hands out `Rc<dyn IGlPlatformSurface>` and the surface lives in an `Arc`. | 2026-10-15 (`apple-opengl.md`) |
| `GlContext` holds its `GlExternalObjectsFeature`, which holds the context. | The feature holds the context weakly; a feature whose context is gone fails as a lost context. | Representation | Two `Rc` that hold each other are never freed. | 2026-10-15 (`apple-opengl.md`) |
| `GlContext.CreateSharedContext` throws when the native display cannot create the context. | The failure is logged and the member returns `None`, as for the contexts of GLX and EGL. | Behaviour | The contract of the port (`IGlContext::create_shared_context`) returns an option. | 2026-10-15 (`apple-opengl.md`) |
| The constructor of `AvaloniaNativeGlPlatformGraphics` throws (a failed native call, a missing helper), and the platform catches it. | `FerroNativeGlPlatformGraphics::new` returns an `Err(OpenGlException)` and the platform goes on with the next mode. | Representation | An exception the caller catches is a result in Rust. | 2026-10-15 (`apple-opengl.md`) |
| `NSObjectWrapper.Dispose` and `CFObjectWrapper.Dispose` of `GpuHandleWrapFeature.cs` release the object at every call. | `dispose` releases at the first call only. | Behaviour | A second release would free an object the wrapper holds no reference to, and `dispose` is a safe function. Upstream disposes a wrapper once (`CompositionInterop`), as the port does. A wrapper that is never disposed keeps its reference, as upstream. | `GpuHandleWrapFeature.cs` |
| A window that extends into its title bar keeps the close, minimise and zoom buttons where AppKit puts them, at the top of a title bar of the system's height, whatever `ExtendClientAreaTitleBarHeightHint` asks for. | When the hint is higher than the buttons, the container of the title bar takes its height and the buttons are centred in it vertically (`FrnWindow layoutStandardButtons`), again after a resize, a change of the key window or of the title, and on leaving full screen; in full screen AppKit's layout stays. | Behaviour | A header drawn by the client in a taller title bar (the developer tools) had the buttons above its middle. | #293 |

### Platform settings (`src/FerroUI.Base/platform/platform_color_values.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PlatformColorValues` carries the theme variant, the contrast preference and three accent colours (`AccentColor1..3`). | It also carries the six shades of the primary accent as the system computes them, `accent_shades()` (`Option<AccentColorShades>`: `dark1..3`, `light1..3`), set with `with_accent_shades`; `None` by default. The Windows backend fills them; every other backend reports none. The WinUI theme (`ferroui-themes-winui`, `SystemAccentColors`) takes them where they are reported and computes them otherwise; the Fluent theme computes them as upstream. | Addition | WinUI 3 reads `SystemAccentColorDark1..3` and `Light1..3` from the system (`SystemThemingInterop::GetSystemVariantAccentColor`); computed from the accent they differ from what Windows reports (on the default blue of Windows 11 the light accent fill was #00589B against #0067C0, the dark one #61BAFF against #4CC2FF, in the comparison of the WinUI theme gallery). Values compare with their shades, so a change of the shades alone is a change of the colour values. | 2026-10-16 |
| No counterpart: `IPlatformSettings` reports no motion preference. | `IPlatformSettings::animations_enabled` (`i_platform_settings.rs`), a defaulted member (`true`), asked for each animation and not kept: the Windows backend reads `UISettings.AnimationsEnabled` (Windows platform backend, below), the browser `prefers-reduced-motion` (`browser-platform.md` section 14); every other backend lets animations play. | Addition | WinUI's controls leave out their non-essential animations when the user turns animations off (`SharedHelpers::IsAnimationsEnabled`, which reads `UISettings` each time); the ports of WinUI controls (the transition provider of `ItemsRepeater`) ask the platform settings. A further setting the WinUI ports need (the material settings) is a defaulted member of its own beside it, recorded here. | 2026-10-23 |

### Windows platform backend (`src/Windows/FerroUI.Win32/`)

The design is `win32-platform.md`. What a later stage builds is listed there (the file table and section 11) and is not repeated here as rows of the kind Missing, except where the port answers something in the meantime.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Every window registers a class whose procedure is a delegate of the instance; `s_instances` roots the windows. | Every class has one procedure (`window_wnd_proc`), which finds the window of a message in a map of the thread from handle to weak reference; a window that is being created is found through a slot of the thread. The list of windows roots a window until `WM_DESTROY`. | Representation | A function the system calls cannot be a closure over an object; no pointer to a window is stored in a window. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| An exception thrown in a window procedure travels through the frames of the system to the caller. | `wnd_proc_guard`: a panic in a window procedure is caught and kept, the procedure answers with the default processing, later procedures do not run their bodies, and the message loop (or the member that made the call) raises the panic again. | Behaviour | A panic must not unwind through frames of the system. The message that panicked gets the default processing, and the panic surfaces one return later than an exception would. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| `PopupImpl` and `EmbeddedWindowImpl` derive from `WindowImpl` and override `CreateWindowOverride`, `Show`, `WndProc`, `ShouldTakeFocusOnClick`, `MaxAutoSizeHint`. | A window has a kind (`WindowKind`); the members that differ ask it. `PopupImpl::new` and `EmbeddedWindowImpl::new` return a `WindowImpl`, which implements the popup contract too (`as_popup_impl` answers for a popup only). | Representation | Five overrides do not need a class hierarchy; the derived classes upstream are windows as well. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| `Win32Platform.s_instance`, `s_options` and `s_compositor` are statics, the instance created when the type is first used. | Thread-local, created by the first use on the thread that initialises the platform. | Representation | The objects belong to the UI thread (`Rc`). | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| `WindowsKeyboardDevice` derives from `KeyboardDevice`. | The registered keyboard device is the `KeyboardDevice` of the base library; `WindowsKeyboardDevice` holds the instance and has `modifiers()`. | Representation | The input manager and the focus code recover `KeyboardDevice` from the device contract. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| `WindowsMouseDevice` derives from `MouseDevice` and is the primary mouse device (`MouseDevice.GetOrCreatePrimary<WindowsMouseDevice>()`). | `WindowsMouseDevice` holds a `MouseDevice` made with the pointer of the backend; it is the device of the windows of the backend and not what `MouseDevice::primary()` of the base library returns. | Behaviour | The base library creates its primary mouse device itself and has no way to be given one; the contracts are not changed by this stage. Code that asks for the primary device gets a second device, without the platform capture. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| `WindowImplPlatformHandle` reads the handle, the client size and the scaling of its window. | The handle surface holds the handle and the scaling as atomics the window writes; the size is `GetClientRect`. `ITopLevelImpl::handle` hands out a view of it. | Representation | A render surface is shared with the render thread; a window is an object of the UI thread. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| `FramebufferManager.Lock` enters a monitor that the locked framebuffer leaves when it is disposed; the pixels are an unmanaged blob. | The lock is a flag with a condition variable; a framebuffer that is dropped without being disposed releases it without presenting. The pixels are a `Vec<u8>`. | Representation | A mutex guard cannot be kept in an object behind a contract. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| `WM_PAINT` runs under `NonPumpingSyncContext`; the platform registers `NonPumpingWaitHelperImpl`. | Neither. | Missing | Deliberate: they keep a managed lock from running the message loop while it waits, which a Rust lock never does; the base library has no such contract. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| Window class names end in a new GUID. | The process identifier, a counter of the process and the thread identifier. | Representation | Unique in the process, which is what the system requires; no source of GUIDs is needed. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| `WM_CHAR` is delivered as a string of its one UTF-16 code unit; a character outside the basic plane is two text inputs of half a surrogate pair each. | The high surrogate is kept and the pair is delivered as one text input (`text_from_char_message`); an unpaired half is dropped. | Behaviour | A Rust string cannot hold half a surrogate pair. The text that reaches a control is the same; it arrives in one event. | Windows backend, stage 1 |
| A data format is an object of the class `DataFormat` or of `DataFormat<T>`; the Windows backend reads a block of global memory as a string or as bytes by asking which (`format is DataFormat<string>`). | A `DataFormat` of the base library carries the type of its values when it was created as a typed format (`DataFormat::data_type`, an `Option<TypeId>`); the type is not part of the identity of a format. The creators of typed formats ask for `T: 'static`. When a format has no type, the backend writes by the type of the value. | Behaviour | A change of `ferroui-base`, additive: the format of the port was a value of a kind and an identifier, and the system does not say whether a registered format is text. The values of a data transfer are `Rc<dyn Any>`, so their types were `'static` already. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| `DataTransferToOleDataObjectWrapper` is the managed wrapper and, through its callback base class, the COM object; `MicroComRuntime.TryUnwrapManagedObject` recovers it from a pointer. | The wrapper and the COM object are two: the COM object holds the wrapper and tells it when its last reference is released. `new` returns both. The live data objects of the thread are kept by address, which is how one that comes back in a drag is recognised (`try_unwrap`). | Representation | A COM object of the runtime of the port is a value moved behind a vtable; the backend needs a handle to it that is not a COM reference. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| `ClipboardFormatRegistry` is one list for the process behind a lock. | A list of the UI thread. | Representation | A data format is a value of one thread (`Rc<str>`), and OLE calls a data object in the apartment that made it. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| The prefix of the system name of an application format is `avn-app-fmt:`. | `frn-app-fmt:`. | Behaviour | The name of the upstream project does not appear in the port; an application of the port and one of upstream do not see each other's application formats, as on the other platforms. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| `FormatEnumerator.Next` does not write the count of elements when it returns fewer than asked for. | It writes it whenever the caller gives one. | Behaviour | The contract of the interface; a caller that asks for several formats reads the count. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| The GDI conversions of `OleDataObjectHelper` release their memory device contexts with `ReleaseDC`, and read a bitmap handle into a section whose rows run from the bottom through a mirrored `StretchBlt`. | The memory contexts are deleted (`DeleteDC`); the section has its rows from the top and the bitmap is copied with `BitBlt`. A device independent bitmap whose header announces more pixels than the block has is not given to the system. | Behaviour | `ReleaseDC` does not free a memory context; the result is the same picture; the bounds are checked because the block comes from another process. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| A bitmap of a format other than `CF_DIB` whose bytes are not an encoded image is an exception of the decoder that reaches the reader of the clipboard. | A clipboard error for an asynchronous reader; a synchronous reader (a drop target) gets it as the contract of the item defines. | Representation | The item contract of the port returns a result. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| The virtual files of `OleVirtualFileData` may be read and disposed on a worker thread: the stream of a file and the asynchronous capability are marshaled for it. | A file belongs to the UI thread (a storage item is shared with `Rc`); the pointers still travel through the marshaling streams. A file that is dropped without being disposed completes its part of the operation. `OpenReadAsync` returns a reader (`Read`); the length and the position of the stream are members of the concrete stream. | Behaviour | The storage contracts of the port are not `Send`. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| `OleDropTarget` asks the root element for the point of a drag in client coordinates; `_currentDrag is OleDataObjectToDataTransferWrapper` decides what is disposed. | It asks the window (which is what the root element asks); the wrapper that was made for a data object is kept beside the data transfer. | Representation | A data transfer contract cannot be asked for its type. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| An exception in a method OLE calls travels through the frames of the system. | A panic is kept by the guard of the window procedures; the method answers with no effect or `E_UNEXPECTED`, and the panic is raised again by the message loop or after `DoDragDrop`. | Behaviour | A panic must not unwind through frames of the system. | Windows backend, stage 2d (2026-10-10) |
| ANGLE is upstream's own build, shipped as a library beside the application (`av_libGLESv2.dll`, the package `Avalonia.Angle.Windows.Natives` 2.1.27548), and loaded through the `EGL_GetProcAddress` it exports. | ANGLE is the crate `mozangle` 0.7.1 (Mozilla's fork as shipped in Firefox ESR 153, packaged by the Servo project, BSD-3-Clause), compiled from C++ source by its build script and linked statically into the application: the default feature `angle` of `ferroui-win32`. `mozangle::egl::get_proc_address` is the loader. Building with the feature needs libclang (LLVM) beside the MSVC tools; without the feature the rendering mode fails to initialise with a message that names it and the next mode of the options is tried. | Behaviour | Owner's decision (2026-10-10): the port takes crates from crates.io only. It is another ANGLE than upstream's: EGL 1.5, "ANGLE 2.1.19767", without `EGL_ANGLE_flexible_surface_compatibility` (optional upstream too). Verified on both Windows runners of CI and recorded in `win32-platform.md`, section 6.2.1. | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10) |
| `D3D11AngleWin32PlatformGraphics` keeps the EGL interface and the display it probed with, and gives that display to the first context. | The graphics hold the versions of OpenGL ES asked for and the identifier of the adapter the probe chose; the display of the probe is disposed, and a context loads the interface and creates its display (with a device on that adapter) on the thread that asks for it. | Behaviour | The platform graphics are shared with the render thread (`IPlatformGraphics: Send + Sync`), and a display and the EGL interface are objects of one thread. One Direct3D device more is created at start; a context is the same. | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10) |
| `AngleWin32EglDisplay.CreateD3D11Display` asks the adapter selection callback of the platform options on every creation of a display. | The callback is asked once, on the UI thread, when the graphics are probed (`D3D11Adapter::Select`); later displays ask for the adapter with the identifier chosen then (`D3D11Adapter::Chosen`), or the first adapter if it is gone. An index the callback returns outside the list is an error where upstream fails with the exception of the index. | Behaviour | The callback is a value of the UI thread (`Rc`), and the displays after the probe are created on the render thread. | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10) |
| `AngleWin32EglDisplay.GetDisplayOptions` reads `AngleOptions` from the services where a display is created. | The versions are read on the UI thread and passed to the display. | Representation | The services of the render thread are not the ones of the UI thread. | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10) |
| `AngleWin32EglDisplay` and `Win32AngleEglInterface` derive from `EglDisplay` and `EglInterface`. | They hold a display and an interface (`AngleWin32EglDisplay` dereferences to its display). A display handle of zero is the error of the EGL call that returned it, where upstream's base constructor throws for the argument. | Representation | The types of the OpenGL crate are structures, not classes; its display panics for a zero handle, and the callers of upstream catch the exception to go on to the next mode. | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10) |
| `AngleD3DTextureFeature` and `AngleExternalObjectsFeature` take the ANGLE display of a context by casting `context.Display`; they are objects without state of their own context. | A feature is created for its context with the display wrapper of that context and keeps both, the context weakly. | Representation | A context of the port holds the EGL display, which the ANGLE display wraps instead of deriving from; the context holds its features, so a feature must not hold the context. | Windows backend, stage 2c (2026-10-10) |
| The connection of a composition mode binds itself as `IWindowsSurfaceFactory` and as the render loop from its own thread; the surface it creates is cast to `ICompositionEffectsSurface` and to `IDisposable` by the window. | The thread hands the connection to the UI thread, which binds a handle to it as the factory, and the render loop; the factory returns the surface together with its view as a surface with blur effects and with what releases it (`WindowsSurface`). | Representation | The services of the port belong to the thread that binds them; a surface behind the render surface contract cannot be asked for another trait. | Windows backend, stage 2c (2026-10-10) |
| `SyncRoot` of a composition mode is an object whose monitor a transaction enters and leaves on disposal; MicroCom proxies are used from every thread. | `SyncRoot` (`sync_root.rs`) is a monitor written with an owner, a count and a condition variable; the free-threaded COM objects of a composition mode are held in `SharedCom`, whose constructor is `unsafe` with the contract that the object may be called and released from any thread. | Representation | A guard of a Rust mutex cannot live in an object behind a contract, and a Rust mutex is not entered twice by one thread; the COM pointers of the port are of one thread. | Windows backend, stage 2c (2026-10-10) |
| `IDirect3D11TextureRenderTarget.BeginDraw` and `CreateRenderTarget` throw (`RenderTargetCorruptedException` with a `COMException` inside, or the `COMException`); a failure of `Commit` or `EndDraw` throws out of the disposal of a session. | They return the error (`RenderTargetCorruptedException` of the base library with the result code as its cause; the result code). A failure while a frame is begun that is not `BeginDraw` of the surface is also the corrupted render target with its cause. A failing commit or end of a draw at the end of a session is not reported: the next frame meets the device. | Behaviour | Errors are values; a destructor cannot fail. | Windows backend, stage 2c (2026-10-10) |
| An application has an application manifest from its project template (`app.manifest`, with the supported versions of Windows), compiled in by the build. | The example of the backend is linked with a manifest through arguments its build script gives the MSVC linker; the other hosts of the port have none yet. | Missing | Found by the first run of the DirectComposition mode: without the entry for Windows 8 or later a layered child window (the holder of the native control host under a window of a composition mode) is refused on a server system. A way for an application of the port to get its manifest (a documented build script line, or a crate) is still to decide. | Windows backend, stage 2c (2026-10-10) |
| The default order of the composition modes takes Windows.UI.Composition on Windows 10 1803 and later. | It takes DirectComposition: Windows.UI.Composition is passed over like a mode the system does not support, and the DXGI swap chain likewise. | Missing | The rest of stage 2c (`win32-platform.md`, section 6.4): the effects of Windows.UI.Composition need COM objects with several interfaces, which the MicroCom runtime of the port does not make yet. The blur, acrylic blur and mica levels are unsupported until then. | Windows backend, stage 2c (2026-10-10) |
| `Win32GlManager.Initialize` tries the rendering modes in order: ANGLE, WGL, Vulkan, software; with ANGLE it registers the first composition mode that is supported. | All four are built, and the composition modes. Vulkan initialises only in a build with the feature `vulkan` of `ferroui-win32` (next row but one); without it the mode is passed over with a warning that names the feature. The graphics of Direct3D 9, which upstream's manager also does not take, are disposed instead of left to a collector. The manager remembers the kind of graphics it registered, where upstream tests the type of the registered object. | Behaviour | Vulkan: the Skia backend draws on the graphics of Vulkan only with its Vulkan GPU, which needs Skia compiled from source on Windows (`win32-platform.md`, section 6.1); a mode that initialises and that the renderer cannot draw with would leave the window empty. (WGL and the composition modes were missing when this row was written and are built since.) | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10); Vulkan 2026-10-15 |
| The four interface definition files map type names to types of the C# runtime (`@clr-map`, `@clr-namespace`, `@clr-access`). | The interface declarations are unchanged; the head of each file has `@rust-map NAME type` and `@rust-types-from path` lines for the Rust generator. `directx.idl` is compiled; the other three are generated by a test of the generator until the stages that use them. | Representation | The lines are instructions to a generator for another language; the upstream project's name may not appear under `src`. | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10) |
| The MicroCom vtables of the port were `extern "C"`. | `extern "system"` in the runtime crate and the generated code. | Representation | COM is `stdcall` on 32-bit x86 Windows; on every other target the two are the same convention, so the macOS backend is unchanged. No 32-bit target is built; a method that returns a structure is not modelled for it. | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10) |
| The MicroCom runtime keeps a shadow for an object it exposes, with one callable wrapper per interface, made when the interface is first asked for; `QueryInterface` finds the interface type of an identifier in a registry and tests whether the object is of that type. | An object is made with the list of its interfaces (`make_com`, `make_com_with` with `InterfaceEntry::of`; `src/FerroUI.MicroCom/com_object.rs`): one allocation holds the count, the value and the wrappers of all of them, and `QueryInterface` walks the list and answers with the first interface that answers to the identifier. | Representation | Rust has no run-time test of the traits a value implements, so the interfaces are named where the object is made. An interface of the list answers for its bases; the pointer of `IUnknown` is the one of the first interface. | 2026-10-10 |
| `DirectXUnmanagedMethods` declares the functions with `PreserveSig = false`: a failure is an exception of the runtime. | The two functions return the `HRESULT` as an error, which the display turns into the OpenGL failure the callers handle; `CreateDXGIFactory`, declared and not called upstream, is not declared. | Representation | There is no runtime that throws for an `HRESULT`. | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10) |
| The Skia backend on Windows has Ganesh on OpenGL and Vulkan (SkiaSharp carries every back end). | The Skia bindings are built with the feature `gl` on Windows (Ganesh on OpenGL, for ANGLE and WGL); no Graphite there. Ganesh on Vulkan only with the feature `vulkan` of `ferroui-win32`, which compiles Skia from source. | Behaviour | The published Skia binaries carry Ganesh on OpenGL or Graphite on Vulkan, not Ganesh on OpenGL and Vulkan together; the bindings have no Direct3D back end of Graphite (`win32-platform.md`, sections 6.1 and 6.2). | Windows backend, stage 2 (2026-10-10); Vulkan 2026-10-15 |
| `OleContext.Current` and `NativeWinRTMethods.EnsureRoInitialized` ask the runtime for the apartment state of the thread, which the runtime decides when the thread starts (the attribute of the entry point); the runtime initialises COM on every thread. | A thread has the apartment COM gave it (`CoGetApartmentType`). A UI thread on which COM is not initialised is valid for OLE, and initialising OLE makes it single-threaded; a thread in the multithreaded apartment is not valid. The Windows Runtime is initialised single-threaded on a thread that is single-threaded or is the UI thread without an apartment, multithreaded otherwise. | Behaviour | There is no managed runtime that initialises COM on a thread; an application of the port does not mark its entry point. An application that initialises its UI thread as multithreaded before the platform gets no OLE (no drag and drop, no OLE clipboard), as an application of the reference without the attribute. | Windows backend, stage 2a |
| `NativeWinRTMethods` keeps one flag for the process that the Windows Runtime is initialised; `WinRTApiInformation` keeps its statics object in a static; the two answers of `Win32PlatformSettings` about present types are statics. | Each is of the thread that asks. | Representation | A thread of the port is not initialised for COM by a runtime, so every thread that activates initialises itself; an interface pointer (`ComPtr`) belongs to the thread that got it. | Windows backend, stage 2a |
| `WinRTInspectable` is a base class: `GetIids` and `RuntimeClassName` find the interfaces and the name of the deriving class by reflection. | Functions an implementation of the generated `IInspectableImpl` calls with the identifiers of its interfaces and with its class name (`FerroUI.Win32.WinRT.WinRTPropertyValue`). The count `GetIids` writes is 32 bits wide, as the interface of the system declares it (the interface definition file declares 64). | Representation | No reflection; a COM object of the MicroCom runtime exposes one interface chain. | Windows backend, stage 2a |
| A call of the Windows Runtime that fails inside `Win32PlatformSettings` (an activation, a property) is an exception that reaches the caller of the settings. | It is logged as a warning and the settings answer what they answer on a system without the types: the light theme with the default accent colour, the language of the process. | Behaviour | The contract of the settings has no failure; a panic for a theme colour would end the application. | Windows backend, stage 2a |
| `Win32Platform.WndProc` asks the services for the platform settings and tells them about a setting change when they are a `Win32PlatformSettings`. | The platform keeps the settings object it registered and tells that one. | Representation | A service is registered as its contract and cannot be asked for its type. Settings an application registers in their place are not told, as upstream does not tell them. | Windows backend, stage 2a |
| `WindowImpl.SetIcon` casts the icon it is given to `IconImpl`. | The backend keeps the icons it created that are alive, per thread, and finds the one it is given by its address; an icon of another implementation is made into an icon of the backend from what it saves. | Representation | The contract of a window icon cannot be asked for its type and is not changed by this stage. Upstream fails for an icon of another class. | Windows backend, icons of 2e |
| `IconImpl(Stream)` holds one `Win32Icon` as the small and as the big icon. | Two icons made from the same bytes. | Representation | An icon owns its handle; the two are disposed separately. | Windows backend, icons of 2e |
| `Win32Icon` hands `CreateBitmap` rows of whole bytes (`(width * bits + 7) / 8`), which the system reads as rows of whole 16-bit words: for a mask whose row has an odd number of bytes the system reads past the buffer. `LoadIconFromData` copies the image to an aligned buffer only when its offset is not aligned. | The bytes are the same, in a block as long as what the system reads; the image is always copied. | Representation | Memory safety; the content of the bitmaps is the reference's, with its row layout, its mask taken from the first byte of a pixel and its bit order. | Windows backend, icons of 2e |
| `TaskBarList` keeps one interface pointer for the process behind a lock and never releases it; it declares `MarkFullscreenWindow` and `SetOverlayIcon` as returning nothing. | The task bar list belongs to the thread that asked for it and is released when the thread ends; the two methods are declared with the result code the interface has, which is not read. | Representation | An interface pointer is used in the apartment that created it; the windows of the backend are objects of the UI thread. | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |
| `TrayIconImpl.s_trayIcons` is a static dictionary that holds the tray icons; an icon that is not disposed is removed by its finalizer. `SetIcon` casts the icon to `IconImpl`. | The table is of the UI thread and holds the icons weakly; an icon that is dropped without being disposed removes itself from the notification area while the platform lives. An icon of another implementation is made into an icon of the backend from what it saves, as a window does. An icon that cannot be made for the scaling of the task bar is logged and the empty icon is shown. | Representation | No finalizers; the contract of an icon cannot be asked for its type. | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |
| `TrayPopupRoot` hands its positioner helper a delegate of itself; the name of the window starts with the name of the upstream project. | The helper finds the window through a weak reference that is set once the window exists; the name starts with `FerroTrayPopupRoot_`. The handler of the deactivation is removed when the window is closed. | Representation | An object cannot hand out a reference to itself while it is constructed; the name of the upstream project does not appear in the port. | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |
| `NativeMenuBarPresenter.CreateContainerForNativeItem` is internal to the controls library and reached by the Windows backend through a friend declaration. | `ferroui_controls::create_container_for_native_item`, a public function hidden from the documentation. | Representation | A change of `ferroui-controls`, additive: Rust has no friend declarations between crates. | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |
| `Win32StorageProvider` reads the options of a picker and makes the storage items on the thread of the dialog. | The options are read into values on the UI thread before the thread of the dialog starts; the thread reports paths and the index of the file type, the future of the picker is woken on the UI thread through the dispatcher, and the storage items are made there. A panic on the thread of the dialog is an error of the picker. | Representation | The options and the storage items of the port are objects of one thread (`Rc`). | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |
| `FiltersToPointer` leaves the entry of a file type without patterns zeroed in the array and passes a count that is smaller by one, so the array has an entry without strings and loses its last file type; the index of the suggested file type and the file type that was selected count the file types as they were given. | The file types without patterns are left out, and the indices count the file types the dialog shows. | Behaviour | An array with an entry of null strings is refused by the dialog; with the entry left out the index a dialog reports names the file type the user sees. Differs from the reference only for a list that has a file type without patterns. | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |
| A failure of a dialog is a `COMException` with the message of the system for the code. | An error of the picker (`std::io::Error`) that names the call and the code. | Representation | The contract of the pickers returns a result. | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |
| `WindowsMountedVolumeInfoListener` asks `DriveInfo` of the class library of its runtime. | The system: `GetLogicalDrives`, the root directory exists, `GetDiskFreeSpaceEx`, `GetVolumeInformation`. The timer of the listener is stopped when the listener is disposed, as in the reference. | Representation | No class library; these are the calls it makes. | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |
| `Win32NativeControlHost` throws `ArgumentException`, `InvalidOperationException` and `ObjectDisposedException`; a holder window that is not disposed is destroyed by its finalizer; its class name starts with the name of the upstream project and ends in a new GUID. `WindowImpl` creates the storage provider and the native control host in its constructor. | Panics with the messages; a holder is destroyed when it is dropped; the class is `FerroDumbWindow-` with the process identifier and a counter. The two are created when the feature is first asked for. | Representation | No exceptions and no finalizers; the name of the upstream project does not appear in the port; a window cannot hand out a reference to itself while it is constructed. | Windows backend, stage 2e (2026-10-10) |

| `WinUIEffectBase` is an abstract class that derives from `WinRTInspectable`; `BorderEffect`, `BlendEffect`, `CompositeStepEffect`, `OpacityEffect`, `ColorSourceEffect`, `WinUIGaussianBlurEffect` and `SaturationEffect` derive from it and override the identifier and the properties. | An effect is a value that implements `WinUIEffect` (the identifier, the properties as values, the class name), compiled and tested on every host; `WinUIEffectBase` is the COM object over a boxed effect and its sources, made with `make_com_with` for the three interfaces. | `GetPropertyValue(UIA_FrameworkIdPropertyId)` answers the name of the reference framework. | It answers `FerroUI`. | Behaviour | The framework a client is told is the port. | UI Automation (2026-10-11) |

| The provider interfaces are C# declarations for the COM source generator of the runtime (`Interop/I*.cs`). | One interface definition file, `automation/interop/uia.idl`, written from them and compiled by the MicroCom generator. | Design | The port has one COM model. The identifiers and slot orders were compared with the declarations of the `windows` crate (generated from the metadata of the Windows SDK): all 25 are the ones of the system. | UI Automation (2026-10-11) |
| `WglDisplay.InitializeCore` goes from step to step whatever each answered, in any session. | Every step of the bootstrap is asked whether it succeeded (the window, its device context, the pixel format chosen and set, the context created and made current), `wglGetProcAddress` answering 1, 2, 3 or -1 is no entry point, and a process of session 0 does not ask the system for OpenGL: the mode does not initialise there. | Behaviour | In session 0 of the owner's virtual machine the test of the crate that creates the platform graphics against the driver ended its process with an access violation, twice (`win32-platform.md`, section 10.5); which call faults is not known. A service has no desktop of a user to draw on with a display driver; software rendering is what it gets. | WGL (2026-10-11) |
| `UnmanagedMethods.BitmapIntent` declares `LCS_GM_ABS_COLORIMETRIC = 1`, `LCS_GM_BUSINESS = 2`, `LCS_GM_GRAPHICS = 4`, `LCS_GM_IMAGES = 8`, so the version 5 header of a bitmap on the clipboard (`bV5Intent = LCS_GM_ABS_COLORIMETRIC`) carries 1, which the system reads as `LCS_GM_BUSINESS`. | `BitmapIntent` has the values of `wingdi.h` (`LCS_GM_BUSINESS` 1, `LCS_GM_GRAPHICS` 2, `LCS_GM_IMAGES` 4, `LCS_GM_ABS_COLORIMETRIC` 8), and the header carries 8. | Behaviour | The header says the intent the reference names; the value of the reference is a slip of its declaration. | Windows backend, stage 2d (the enumeration declared 2026-10-15) |
| `SwapChainTopLevelImpl`, `SwapChainGlSurface`, the string and activation functions of the Windows Runtime are internal, visible to `Avalonia.WinUI` (`InternalsVisibleTo`). | They are public, documented as being for the WinUI integration. The guard of window procedures is public too, with a hook (`set_pending_hook`) that a message loop the backend does not run sets to end itself when a panic is kept. | Representation | Rust has no visibility to one other crate; the XAML message loop of the integration does not ask for a kept panic after each message. | WinUI integration (2026-10-15) |
| The project is referenced with its default rendering mode, ANGLE, built in. | The workspace dependency on `ferroui-win32` is without its default feature `angle`; `ferroui-desktop`, the desktop catalog and the integration tests ask for it, as do the applications of the WinUI integration. | Representation | `ferroui-winui` compiles without ANGLE (the application chooses it), and is checked for a Windows target on any host that way; with the default feature on the workspace dependency every dependent would build ANGLE's C++. | WinUI integration (2026-10-15) |
| `Win32PlatformSettings.GetColorValues` reads `UIColorType.Accent` (and `Background`) of `UISettings`. | `read_color_values` also reads `AccentDark3..AccentLight3` and reports them as the accent shades of the colour values (in the normal and the high contrast modes); a failure to read them leaves the shades out and keeps the other values. | Addition | The shades of the system for the WinUI theme (Platform settings, above); WinUI reads them the same way. | 2026-10-16 |
| Windows render text the same way whatever the system's settings (the backend reports nothing). | The platform handle of a window reports the text rendering parameters of the system (`win32_text_rendering.rs`: ClearType and its order from `SystemParametersInfoW`; DirectWrite's enhanced contrast and gamma when the user tuned ClearType, else contrast 1.0 and sRGB as Chromium), composited for a window of a composition mode (Windows.UI.Composition, DirectComposition); `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` reads them again. The swap chain top-level of a WinUI host returns a second surface that reports them as composited. | Behaviour | Text of a composited window is greyscale as WinUI 3's; ClearType where Win32 applications use it; DirectWrite's weight (`text-rendering.md`, sections 2.1 and 5.2). | Text rendering (`text-rendering.md`) |
| `winrt.idl` declares the interface `IUISettings3` of `UISettings` (the colours), and `Win32PlatformSettings` activates `UISettings` where it reads them. | `winrt.idl` also declares `IUISettings` (its six members before `AnimationsEnabled` are kept for the layout of the table of the interface; `AnimationsEnabled` is a WinRT boolean, one byte, not a `BOOL`). `Win32PlatformSettings::animations_enabled` reads it for each animation; a failure is logged and animations play. Every setting of `UISettings` is read through one source in `win32_platform_settings.rs` (`ui_settings::<I>()` and `read_ui_setting`, which the colours use too). | Addition | The motion preference (Platform settings, above). One source, so that each further setting of `UISettings` the backend reads (the advanced effects WinUI's material policy reads, `IUISettings4`) is an interface declared once in `winrt.idl` and read the same way. `src/tools/MicroCom.CodeGenerator/tests/win32_idl.rs` checks the generated signature and identifier; `win_rt_tests.rs` reads the setting on Windows. | 2026-10-23 |

### WinUI integration (`src/Windows/FerroUI.WinUI/`)

The design is `winui.md`.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The projections of C#/WinRT: classes, interfaces, delegates, `async`, collections, strings. | The interfaces in `interop/winui.idl` for the MicroCom generator, written from the metadata of the Windows App SDK 1.6 and the Windows SDK by `scripts/winui/winui-idl.py`; delegates, the await of an operation, collections and boxes written in `interop/`. | Design | The port has one COM model on Windows (`win32-platform.md`, section 2.2); the `windows` crate has no WinUI. | 2026-10-15 |
| `AvaloniaSwapChainPanel : SwapChainPanel`, the automation peer `: FrameworkElementAutomationPeer`, the proxy `: AutomationPeer`, the overlay `: Grid`: classes derived in C#. | Composition of the Windows Runtime: an outer object of the MicroCom runtime with the overridable interfaces it overrides, the inner object created by the composable factory, every other interface asked of it (`ferroui_microcom::set_inner`, added for this). | Representation | What a derived class of the Windows Runtime is; the runtime of the reference does it underneath. | 2026-10-15 |
| The panel is the element: the XAML tree keeps it alive. | The application owns `Rc<FerroSwapChainPanel>`; the element holds it weakly, as do the handlers; dropping the panel does what `Unloaded` does, and its element is then a plain swap chain panel. | Behaviour | Counted references from the element to the panel and back would never be freed; the garbage collector of the reference breaks that cycle. | 2026-10-15 |
| The automation peer of the panel holds the panel; a proxy holds the peer of the panel. | Both weakly. | Representation | As above: the element holds its peer. | 2026-10-15 |
| `ConditionalWeakTable<AutomationPeer, AvaloniaToXamlPeerProxy>`: a proxy lives while its peer does. | A table of the UI thread from peer to proxy that does not count the proxy; a proxy leaves it when it is destroyed. The same peer gets the same proxy while XAML holds that proxy. | Behaviour | A counted proxy holds its peer; a table that held the proxy would keep every peer alive. | 2026-10-15 |
| `CharacterReceived` sends each UTF-16 unit as a string, a lone surrogate among them. | The first half of a surrogate pair is kept and sent with the second as one text. | Behaviour | A Rust string holds no lone surrogate. | 2026-10-15 |
| One static list for the intermediate points of every move event. | Each event has its own list. | Representation | The lazy points of the base library own their list. | 2026-10-15 |
| The swap chain is handed from the render thread to the UI thread uncounted. | Counted while it is on its way. | Representation | A pointer that crosses threads keeps its object. | 2026-10-15 |
| The pattern methods let an `ElementNotEnabledException` of a provider reach XAML. | The method answers `UIA_E_ELEMENTNOTENABLED`. | Representation | A failure of a COM method is its result code. | 2026-10-15 |
| An exception in a handler travels through the frames of the system. | A handler runs under the guard of the Windows backend: a panic is kept, the XAML loop is ended, and `XamlApplication::start` raises the panic once `Application.Start` returned. | Behaviour | A panic must not cross the frames of the system. | 2026-10-15 |
| The samples merge `XamlControlsResources` into `Application.Resources` (`App.xaml`); the XAML compiler gives `App` the type information of the markup (`IXamlMetadataProvider`), which hands the types of the controls library to `XamlControlsXamlMetaDataProvider`. | `XamlApplication::start` does both for every application: the composed application answers `IXamlMetadataProvider` through `XamlControlsXamlMetaDataProvider`, and `XamlControlsResources` is appended to the merged dictionaries of its resources at the start of `OnLaunched` (the controls library initialised first), before the windows are created. | Representation | A Rust program has no XAML compiler; what it generates for the `App.xaml` of both samples is the same, so the integration does it once. The creation inside the callback of `Application.Start`, where the generated constructor loads the markup, failed with `E_UNEXPECTED` (`winui.md`, section 3.4). An application cannot give the resources of an `App.xaml` of its own. | 2026-10-15 |
