| `WinUIEmbedSample`: `MainWindow.xaml`, compiled by the XAML compiler, with the handlers named in the markup and the panel as an element of it. | The same markup loaded at run time (`XamlReader.Load`): the grid only (a window cannot be loaded; the title is set in code), the handlers added in code to the elements of their names, the panel added to the second column in code. | Representation | No XAML compiler for Rust; a loaded document cannot name the methods of a class, and the panel is not a type XAML knows. | 2026-10-15 |
| `WinUIEmbedSample`: `peer.GetType().Name` and `Environment.CurrentManagedThreadId` in the lines of the diagnostics. | The last part of the runtime class name of the peer (`FerroSwapChainPanelAutomationPeer` for the composed peer) and the thread identifier of the system. | Representation | A COM object has no type of the runtime of the reference; Rust has no managed thread identifiers. | 2026-10-15 |
| The samples keep the main view in the static lifetime until the process ends. | They let go of the main view (and of the window) in the handler of `Closed`, while the threads of the framework run. | Behaviour | On Windows the destructors of the thread-local values of the main thread run after the system has ended the other threads; a destructor of the tree of controls that takes a lock an ended thread held never returns (the process of ControlGallery.WinUI stayed after its window had closed on the CI runner; `winui.md`, section 7.3). The runtime of the reference finalizes nothing at the end. | 2026-10-15 |
| The generated `Main` returns from `Application.Start`; the .NET runtime ends the process without finalizing anything (statics and thread statics are never destroyed, the wrappers of the objects of the Windows Runtime never released), and `exit` of the C runtime calls `ExitProcess` on the main thread. | The samples end with `ferroui_winui::end_process(code)` after `MddBootstrapShutdown`: the output is flushed, a thread of its own calls `exit` of the C runtime (the `atexit` handlers, the static destructors of the C++ libraries, `ExitProcess` with the detach callbacks of the libraries on that thread), and the UI thread waits until the system ends it, so that its thread-local values are never destroyed. With `FERROUI_WINUI_TRACE` the end is traced (`[exit]` lines: the threads with where each started, the `atexit` handler, a line from inside `ExitProcess`, a watchdog). | Behaviour | When `main` returns, `ExitProcess` ends the other threads wherever they are and then destroys the thread-local values of the main thread on it; the UI thread's values are the framework, objects of the Windows App SDK and ANGLE's state of the thread, and a destructor that takes a lock an ended thread held never returns (ControlGallery.WinUI stayed with one thread in a lock wait after `main returns`, run 38832399344; `winui.md`, section 7.7). Not destroying those values is what the reference's runtime does. | 2026-10-16 |
| The sample builds against the Windows App SDK 1.8 and gets its bootstrap from the SDK's build tasks. | The bindings are written from 1.6 and work with any runtime from 1.6 on; the program calls the bootstrap (`WindowsAppSdk::initialize`), release 1.6 by default, without the dialog that offers the runtime. | Design | A Rust program has no build tasks of the SDK; 1.6 is the release whose metadata is at hand, and the interfaces of the Windows Runtime do not change between releases. | 2026-10-15 |

### X11 platform (`src/FerroUI.X11/`)

The design, the binding choice and what each stage builds are in `x11-platform.md`. The systematic difference of this backend is a representation: the structures and functions upstream declares for platform invokes (`XLib.cs`, `X11Structs.cs`, `XIStructs.cs`) are those of the `x11-dl` bindings, and every call into the libraries is a safe function of `xlib.rs` that copies its results out of C memory.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Environment variables named after the project: `AVALONIA_IM_MODULE`, `AVALONIA_X11_USE_SESSION_MANAGEMENT`, `AVALONIA_DEBUG_FORCE_X11_ACTIVATION_TRACKING_MODE`, `AVALONIA_GLOBAL_SCALE_FACTOR`, `AVALONIA_SCREEN_SCALE_FACTORS`, `AVALONIA_USE_PHYSICAL_DPI`, `AVALONIA_SCREEN_SCALE_IGNORE_QT`. | The same names with the prefix `FERROUI_`. | Behaviour | The upstream name does not appear in the port. An application ported from upstream that sets these variables has to rename them. | x11-platform |
| The atom `AVALONIA_SAVE_TARGETS_PROPERTY_ATOM` (the property the clipboard manager answers `SAVE_TARGETS` on) and the prefix `application/avn-fmt.` of application clipboard formats. | `FERRO_SAVE_TARGETS_PROPERTY_ATOM`; `application/frn-fmt.` (the prefix of the browser backend). | Behaviour | As above. The names are visible to other clients of the server; an application format copied by an upstream application is not recognised by a FerroUI application and the other way round. | x11-platform |
| `X11PlatformOptions.WmClass` defaults to the name of the entry assembly. | The file stem of the executable. | Behaviour | The counterpart of the assembly name for a native program; usually the same text. | x11-platform |
| `X11PlatformThreading` waits with `epoll` (the backend runs on Linux only). | `epoll` on Linux; on other Unix systems the same two descriptors are waited on with `poll`, and the pipe is made non-blocking with `fcntl`. | Addition | The crate compiles and its tests run on the development machine (macOS). On Linux nothing differs. | x11-platform |
| `X11FramebufferSurface` keeps the retained framebuffer (`_fb`) and its `FuncFramebufferRenderTarget` locks through the surface. | The surface holds the connection, the window and the depth; the framebuffer belongs to each render target it creates. | Representation | A surface is shared with the thread that renders (`Send + Sync`); the framebuffer is an object of one thread. One target per window exists at a time, so the frames are the same. | x11-platform |
| The window is read by its surfaces (`SurfacePlatformHandle` reads `_owner.ClientSize`, `RenderScaling` with interlocked operations). | The scaling, the size and the render window identifier are atomics shared by the window and its surfaces (`WindowShared`). | Representation | The window is an object of the UI thread. | x11-platform |
| Several parties subscribe to `X11Screens.Changed` (`+=`): the render timer, every window, the controls layer. | The contract has one `changed` slot, taken by the controls layer; `X11Screens::changed_event` is raised beside it, by a job posted after the one of the base class at the same priority. | Behaviour | The contract of the port stores one delegate. The event is raised for every change (the slot only when it has a subscriber or a screen was read), and a burst of changes raises it once per change where the base class coalesces. | x11-platform |
| `X11Window.SetIcon` casts the icon to `X11IconData`. | The icons the loader made are remembered and found again by identity (`X11IconData::from_icon_impl`); an icon of another origin is converted through its encoded form, where upstream fails the cast. | Representation | `IWindowIconImpl` has no way to ask for the concrete type, and the contracts are not changed for a backend. | x11-platform |
| `CursorImpl` and its subclass `XImageCursor`. | One type, `CursorImpl`, that knows the connection to free an image cursor on. | Representation | No inheritance; `set_cursor` recognises both by one downcast. | x11-platform |
| `XResources` notifies removed and changed resources by enumerating two hash sets. | Sorted by name within each group. | Behaviour | The order of a hash set is not defined upstream. | x11-platform |
| `ChangeWMAtoms` rewrites `_NET_WM_STATE` of an unmapped window from a hash set. | The atoms that stay keep their order and new ones follow. | Behaviour | As above; window managers read the property as a set. | x11-platform |
| The scroll valuators are indexed by the number of the scroller (`Valuators[scroller.Number]`), which throws when a device reports a number beyond its valuators. | A number beyond the valuators is passed over (leave) or counts as a value that was reset (motion). | Behaviour | A malformed device description does not end the application. | x11-platform |
| A missing extension library shows as a `DllNotFoundException`, caught per extension in `X11Info`; `libXcursor` is not guarded. | Every extension library is optional (`xlib::Libraries`); without `libXcursor`, theme and image cursors fall back to font cursors. | Behaviour | One rule for all libraries. | x11-platform |
| Failures while answering a selection request escape the event handler. | Logged, and the request is refused. | Behaviour | A panic in the event loop would end the application for a clipboard read by another client. | x11-platform |
| Clipboard formats carry their value type (`DataFormat<T>`). | When serving, the stored value decides the bytes; when reading, a format that is not a universal one returns bytes (`Rc<[u8]>`). | Behaviour | `DataFormat` of the port has no value type; asking a custom format for a string gives nothing. | x11-platform |
| Awaited waits of the selection code start when first awaited; continuations run inside the event handler that completed them. | A wait registers its listener when it is created; a completion wakes a job of the dispatcher. | Behaviour | How futures on the dispatcher of the port work; the order of protocol steps is the same. | x11-platform |
| `SelectionReadSession.SendDataRequest` returns nothing for an answer with zero items before it looks at its type. | The type is looked at first: a property of the type `INCR` starts an incremental read whatever its length. | Behaviour | The convention asks for a lower bound of the size in the announcement, and xclip (0.13) announces with an empty property: upstream reads nothing from it when the text is larger than its part size. Found by the smoke run against xclip. | x11-platform stage 2 |
| The environment variable `AVALONIA_GLX_IGNORE_RENDERER_BLACKLIST`. | `FERROUI_GLX_IGNORE_RENDERER_BLACKLIST`. | Behaviour | As the other renamed variables. | x11-platform stage 2a |
| `X11Window` casts the registered `IPlatformGraphics` to `GlxPlatformGraphics` or `EglPlatformGraphics`. | The platform remembers which of its own graphics it registered (`FerroX11Platform::glx_graphics`, `egl_graphics`), and the window asks it; a render window is still used for any registered graphics. | Representation | `IPlatformGraphics` of the port has no way to ask for the concrete type, and the contracts are not changed for a backend. | x11-platform stage 2a |
| `GlxDisplay` and its `DeferredContext` are shared by the UI thread and the render thread; a `GlxContext` is guarded by a monitor; every context has a `GlxInterface` object of its own. | `GlxDisplay` is `Send + Sync` and holds values (the connection, the configuration, the visual, the extensions, the version behind a lock); the probe context is bound to the thread that created the display; a context is an object of its thread without a lock; the interface is a handle to one table of entry points. | Representation | How the port divides what two threads share from what one owns (`render-thread.md`). The calls made and their order are upstream's. | x11-platform stage 2a |
| `EglPlatformGraphics` of the OpenGL project with one `EglDisplay`, registered by `InitializeGraphics`; the default constructor of `EglInterface` loads `libEGL.so.1`. | `X11EglPlatformGraphics` (`x11_egl_helper.rs`): the display of the probe stays with the UI thread, and another thread gets a display object of its own over the same connection (the same display of the EGL library, the same configuration); the visual of the configuration is read once. The library is loaded as upstream does, with `EglInterface::new_default` (`EglInterface()`: `libEGL.so.1`, the entry points through `eglGetProcAddress`). | Representation | The EGL display of the port is an object of one thread and its platform graphics panic on another (`EglPlatformGraphics`). | x11-platform stage 2a |
| A context whose `GlInterface` cannot be built (a required entry point is missing) is passed over by the `catch` of `GlxDisplay.CreateContext`, and the next profile is tried. | A context that cannot be made current is passed over; a missing required entry point panics in `GlInterface::new`. | Behaviour | The interface of the OpenGL crate panics for a missing required entry point, on every platform. No driver is known to create a context of a version without its entry points. | x11-platform stage 2a |
| `GlxContext.Dispose` destroys the context whenever it is called; the session of a GLX frame swaps whenever it is disposed. | Each does its work once. | Behaviour | A second call would destroy a destroyed context, or present twice. | x11-platform stage 2a |
| `AvaloniaX11Platform.DeferredDisplayDispatcher`: one dispatcher of the events of the render connection, created on the platform and handed to every shared memory surface. | `X11DeferredDisplayDispatcher::for_current_thread`: one per thread and connection, taken by the render target when it is created. | Representation | The dispatcher is only used by the thread that renders, and its callbacks hold render targets, which are objects of that thread; a surface is shared between the threads and holds values only. With one thread that renders there is one dispatcher, as upstream. | x11-platform stage 2b |
| An `X11ShmImage` that nobody disposes stays with the server and the system; the locked framebuffer submits the image whenever it is disposed. | The image is released when its last owner lets go of it; a locked framebuffer submits once. A failed `XShmCreateImage` fails with a message where upstream dereferences null. | Behaviour | Leaks and a double submit that upstream's callers do not cause. | x11-platform stage 2b |
| `WorkArea` reads four items of `_NET_WORKAREA` unchecked; refresh rates are read per output; a failed geometry read is not handled. | A property with fewer than four items gives the bounds; the modes are read once per update, and without screen resources the default rate is used; a failed `XGetGeometry` keeps the previous geometry. | Behaviour | Reads past the end of a property and null dereferences upstream. | x11-platform |

Input methods (stage 2c; the crate `ferroui-freedesktop`, `src/FerroUI.FreeDesktop/`, and the X11 crate):

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Tmds.DBus.Protocol`, with proxies generated from `DBusXml/`. | `zbus` 5.19.0, with proxy traits written from the same descriptions (`dbus_ime/ibus/dbus.rs`, `dbus_ime/fcitx/dbus.rs`), holding the members that are called and the signals that are listened to. | Representation | `x11-platform.md`, section 3. | x11-platform stage 2c |
| `DBusTextInputMethodBase` is an abstract class; `IBusX11TextInputMethod` and `FcitxX11TextInputMethod` derive from it. `DBusInputMethodFactory<T>` is generic over the class. | The base is the object (it implements `IX11InputMethodControl` and `ITextInputMethodImpl`) and holds the part that differs as a `DBusTextInputMethodCore`, which reaches the protected members of the base through a weak reference. `IBusX11TextInputMethod::new` and `FcitxX11TextInputMethod::new` return the base. The factory holds a function. | Representation | No inheritance; the contracts of the port are implemented once. | x11-platform stage 2c |
| `async void` methods (`DBusCallQueue.Process`, `OnOwnerChanged`, `Dispose`, `X11Window.ProcessNextImeEvent`) run up to their first incomplete await inside the call. | Each is a task of the UI dispatcher (`invoke_async_task_local`), which is first polled by the next job of the dispatcher. The flags that guard them (`_processing`, `_connecting`, `_processingIme`) are set inside the call, as upstream. | Behaviour | The dispatcher of the port starts a task from a job. A queued call is made one dispatcher job later; the order of calls and of key events is the same. | x11-platform stage 2c |
| A task whose `TaskCompletionSource` is never completed never completes. | The future of a queued call whose queue item was dropped resolves as canceled. | Behaviour | Nothing waits for ever on a call that was dropped. Upstream's callers do not drop items. | x11-platform stage 2c |
| `IX11InputMethodControl.HandleEventAsync(RawKeyEventArgs, ...)`; `Commit` and `ForwardKey` are events; the interface is `IDisposable`. | `handle_event_async` takes the raw input as `Rc<dyn IRawInputEventArgs>` (the form the window has it in; the input methods read the key event out of it); `commit()` and `forward_key()` give the events (`Event`); `dispose` is a member. | Representation | | x11-platform stage 2c |
| `GetAppName` falls back to the name of the entry assembly and then to the name of the upstream project. | The name of the application object, else the file stem of the executable, else `FerroUI`. | Behaviour | The counterpart for a native program; the upstream name does not appear in the port. | x11-platform stage 2c |
| `IBusX11TextInputMethod.OnUpdatePreedit` uses the internal `Utf16Utils.CharacterOffsetToStringOffset`. | The same computation in the file (`character_offset_to_string_offset`). | Representation | The helper of the base library is not public, and the contracts of other crates are not changed for a backend. | x11-platform stage 2c |
| `XimInputMethod.SetCursorRect` queues a job that takes the queued rectangle and then places the spot for the rectangle of the call that queued the job (`rect`, not `rc`): of a burst of calls, the first. | The spot is placed for the rectangle that was set last. | Behaviour | A defect of upstream: the variable it takes out of the queue is not the one it uses. | x11-platform stage 2c |
| The queue of key events of a window keeps the X event with each (`xev`). | Not kept. | Representation | It is never read. | x11-platform stage 2c |
| `AVALONIA_IM_MODULE`. | `FERROUI_IM_MODULE` (`IM_MODULE_VARIABLE` of the FreeDesktop crate, re-exported by the X11 crate). | Behaviour | As the other renamed variables. | x11-platform stage 2c |

Drag and drop (stage 2d; `src/FerroUI.X11/selections/drag_drop/`):

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `X11DragSource.Handler` calls Xlib, the platform, the data provider, the cursor factory and the timeout manager directly. | The handler has the protocol and calls a host (`IDragSourceHost`); `XlibDragSourceHost` is the host on the connection of the platform. A target of the application is a flag of the target (`in_process`) and its window is found again by its identifier. | Representation | The protocol runs in the tests against a host that is not a server. | x11-platform stage 2d |
| `X11DropTarget` calls Xlib directly. | Through `IXdndTargetConnection` (`XlibXdndTargetConnection` on the platform); the reader of the data of a drag is what the connection makes (`IDragDropItemsSource`). | Representation | As above. | x11-platform stage 2d |
| `DragDropDataTransfer` derives from `PlatformDataTransfer`. | It holds one (`data_transfer()`), which is what the drag events carry. | Representation | No inheritance. | x11-platform stage 2d |
| `DragDropTimeoutManager` uses a timer of the thread pool; its callback completes the drag from that thread. | A timer of the UI dispatcher: the timeout runs on the UI thread. | Behaviour | Everything else of a drag runs on the UI thread, and its state is not shared between threads here. A UI thread that is blocked for the whole timeout sees it late, where upstream completes the task while the thread is blocked. | x11-platform stage 2d |
| `SynchronousXEventWaiter` puts the events it took and did not want back with `XPutBackEvent` first to last, which leaves them on the queue in reverse order. | Last to first: the queue has them in the order they arrived in. | Behaviour | A defect of upstream: `XPutBackEvent` pushes onto the head of the queue. A press and its release, or two configure events, taken during a read would be handled in the wrong order. | x11-platform stage 2d |
| `X11DragSource` casts the registered `ICursorFactory` to `X11CursorFactory`. | The platform keeps its cursor factory as its own type (`FerroX11Platform::cursor_factory`). | Representation | The contract has no way to ask for the concrete type. | x11-platform stage 2d |
| An invalid drag source throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. | A panic with the message. | Representation | As the other drag sources of the port. | x11-platform stage 2d |
| The formats of `XdndEnter` are collected in a `HashSet`. | In a list, each once, in the order the source gave them. | Behaviour | A defined order of the formats of a data transfer. | x11-platform stage 2d |

FreeDesktop services (stage 2e; `src/FerroUI.FreeDesktop/`):

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DBusPlatformSettings` derives from `DefaultPlatformSettings`. | It holds the default settings and answers the other members of the contract with them. | Representation | No inheritance. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `DBusPlatformSettings`: a value of `color-scheme` that is not a number, or of `accent-color` that is not three numbers, fails in the signal handler or in the task of the first read; the accent colour of a portal before version 2 (the deprecated `Read`, which wraps the value in a second variant) is not unwrapped, and so never read. | A colour scheme that is not a number is passed over; an accent colour that is not three numbers is no accent colour; the nested variant is unwrapped for both settings. | Behaviour | Failures in handlers nobody observes; the missing unwrapping is a defect of upstream. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `LinuxMountedVolumeInfoListener` parses with regular expressions and LINQ, and fails in its timer when `/proc/partitions` or `/proc/mounts` cannot be read or a line has too few fields. | The same parsing as functions over text, without a regular expression library; a file that cannot be read is an empty one, a line with too few fields is passed over. An escape that is not a valid octal number of three digits is left as it is, where upstream fails. | Behaviour | The crate also compiles and tests on systems without these files; no new dependency for three patterns. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `NativeMethods.ReadLink` calls `readlink` with a buffer of 4097 bytes. | `std::fs::read_link`. | Representation | The same system call. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `DBusSystemDialog` reads `Results["uris"]` of a response in the handler of the signal, whatever the response code: a dialog that was canceled (response 1, no results) fails there. | A response without `uris` is a dialog without a choice: no files, no folder. | Behaviour | A canceled dialog is not an error for the callers of a storage provider. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| The token of a portal request is the name of the upstream project and a timestamp. | `FerroUI_`, the process identifier and a counter. | Behaviour | The upstream name does not appear in the port; the token only has to be unique for the connection. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| The path of the request object has the unique name of the connection as an element. | The same; a connection without a bus (the tests) has no name, and its request path has no such element. | Addition | An empty element is not an object path. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| A picker task fails with the exception of the D-Bus library or `InvalidOperationException` (the unexpected request path). | The picker futures of the storage provider base resolve to `std::io::Result`; a D-Bus failure is an error with its message. | Representation | The contract of the base library. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `IPortalParentLease : IAsyncDisposable`. | `dispose_async` is a member that returns a future. | Representation | | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `DBusMenuExporterImpl` and `StatusNotifierItemDbusObj` are method handlers (`DBusHandler`) the D-Bus library calls on the synchronization context of the UI thread. | The object a connection exports (`DBusMenuObject`, `StatusNotifierItemObject`) is called on the thread of the connection and holds a handle (`UiThreadHandle`): a call is a job of the UI dispatcher at input priority, and the method awaits its result. The properties of the tray item are read from a value behind a lock. After the object of the UI thread is disposed a call answers `UnknownObject`. | Representation | `zbus` calls exported objects on its own thread and asks them to be `Send + Sync`; the menu and the tray icon are objects of the UI thread. The answers are computed on the UI thread, as upstream. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| A connection has no object until a handler is added. | Every connection of the crate (`DBusHelper::try_create_new_connection`) is built with the standard object manager (`org.freedesktop.DBus.ObjectManager`) at `/`. | Addition | The object server of `zbus` starts with the first object; given to the builder it listens before the first message is read, started later it was measured to miss calls (`x11-platform.md`, section 13). Visible to other clients: the root object has that interface, and adding or removing an object emits its `InterfacesAdded` and `InterfacesRemoved`. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `AddMethodHandler` and `RemoveMethodHandler` are synchronous (the constructor of the menu exporter, `CreateTrayIcon`, `DestroyTrayIcon`); `Emit...` sends a signal inside the call. | Each is a task of the UI dispatcher: a menu is exported one dispatcher job after its exporter was made (an exporter that was disposed before that is never exported), and signals are sent by tasks in the order they were emitted. `_itemExported` is set before the export is awaited and reset when it fails. | Behaviour | Adding an object and sending a message are asynchronous in `zbus`, and the UI thread does not block on them. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| The object path of a menu is `/net/avaloniaui/dbusmenu/` and a new GUID. | `/org/ferroui/dbusmenu/` and 32 hexadecimal digits of two hashes with keys the standard library draws at random. | Behaviour | The upstream name does not appear in the port; no dependency for a GUID. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| The generated handler of `com.canonical.dbusmenu` describes every member of the interface. | The exported interface has the methods, the four properties and the signal `LayoutUpdated`; `ItemsPropertiesUpdated` and `ItemActivationRequested`, which upstream never emits, are not declared. | Behaviour | Only the introspection data differs. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `icon-data` of a menu item: a failure of `IWindowIconImpl.Save` escapes the handler. | An icon that cannot be written is no icon. | Behaviour | A failure in a handler nobody observes. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `DBusTrayIconImpl` keeps the proxy of the watcher (`_statusNotifierWatcher`) and its name request and release as `Task` fields that several callers await and compare by reference. | A flag for the proxy, which is made where it is called; the request and the release are shared futures with a number each, compared by number. A release is also a task of the dispatcher, where upstream's starts inside the call. | Representation | A future runs when it is awaited; a task cannot be compared by reference. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `DBusTrayIconImpl.SetIcon` indexes the icon data by the size it states (`x11iconData[i + 2]`), and fails for data shorter than that. | Data without a size is no icon; pixels the data does not have are transparent. | Behaviour | Reads past the end upstream. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `X11Window` keeps its menu exporter as `ITopLevelNativeMenuExporter` and disposes it through `IDisposable`. | It keeps the concrete exporter (`DBusMenuExporterImpl`). | Representation | The contract has no way to ask for the other interface. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| GLib, GTK, `libSM` and `libICE` are platform invokes: a library that is missing is an exception at the first call, wherever that is (the constructor of `X11PlatformLifetimeEvents` at platform start, for one). | The libraries are opened with `dlopen` and their functions resolved into tables (the loader of the base crate, `platform/native_library.rs`). A missing library is an error value: the GTK dialogs answer "none", session management is logged as unavailable and the platform starts without it, `GtkInteropHelper::run_on_glib_thread` fails with upstream's message; only `use_g_lib_main_loop` panics, with the name of the library. | Behaviour | No link-time dependency, and a platform that starts on a system without the session management library. | x11-platform stage 2e, 2f |
| `Glib.ConnectSignal` connects with `g_signal_connect_object` and no object, passing a function its runtime generates for a delegate, and frees the delegate's handle when the connection is disposed (also from inside the handler). | `g_signal_connect_data` with the handler as the data of the connection and a destroy notification: the handler is freed when GLib finalizes the closure, after its last call returned. Two typed entry points (`connect_signal_generic`, `connect_signal_dialog_response`) for the two delegate types. `g_main_loop_run`, `g_main_loop_quit` and `g_main_loop_unref` are methods and the destructor of a `GMainLoop` value, and `g_main_context_find_source_by_id` is inside the two functions that use its result (`g_source_get_can_recurse`, `g_source_set_can_recurse` take the identifier of the source). | Representation | A closure reaches its state through the data of the connection; freeing it while it runs would be a use after free. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `g_timeout_add_once` uses `g_timeout_add` and never frees the handle of a timeout that is removed before it fires (every replaced dispatcher timer leaks one); `GlibDispatcherImplBase` keeps the tag of a timer that fired and removes it again at the next update, which GLib reports as a critical warning. | `g_timeout_add_full` with a destroy notification that frees the state; the tag is cleared when the timer fires. | Behaviour | A leak per timer update and a warning per fired timer upstream. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| A callback of a GLib source is a delegate; an exception that escapes one is the caller's problem (the dispatcher catches in its own callbacks, `RequestBackgroundProcessing` does not). `RunLoop` rethrows one captured exception, or an `AggregateException` of several. | A callback is a `Send` closure; a panic is caught at the boundary and logged (it cannot unwind through C). The dispatcher catches in all its callbacks, the one of background processing included, and `run_loop` resumes the first panic of a frame and logs the others. | Behaviour | Unwinding into C aborts the process; a panic payload cannot be aggregated. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `GlibDispatcherImpl`: the events of the connection are handed out only by the descriptor source, which fires when the socket can be read; `RequestBackgroundProcessing` does not flush the connection afterwards. | After jobs of the framework ran (a signal, the timer, background processing) the connection is flushed and, when Xlib has events queued, an idle source of the priority of the connection hands them out (`dispatch_queued_events_later`). | Behaviour | Found on a server: a job that makes a round trip (`XSync`, a property read) lets Xlib read the socket and queue the events before the answer; nothing then wakes the source, and the events (a button press, `WM_DELETE_WINDOW`) waited for the next unrelated event. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `Gtk.InitializeGtk` sets `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` to a path that cannot be opened, through the runtime's environment, and names the application `avalonia.app.a<guid>`. | The variable is not set: the runtime's environment is a copy the C library does not read (recalled, not measured), so GTK never saw the value, and what keeps GDK on X11 is `gdk_set_allowed_backends("x11")`, which is called. The application identifier is `ferroui.app.a` and 32 hexadecimal digits from the process identifier, a counter and the clock. | Naming | Setting the variable for real would change what child processes and other libraries of the application see. The name of the upstream project does not appear in the port. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `GtkSystemDialog` keeps the window and makes the dialog, the storage items and the extension of a saved name on the thread of GTK; the filters are a dictionary from the filter of GTK to the file type. | It keeps the identifier of the window. The options cross as values (`GtkDialogRequest`), the answer as paths and the index of the chosen filter (`GtkDialogResult`); the storage items and the extension are made on the UI thread. `gtk_file_chooser_dialog_new`, a function with a variable argument list, is declared as one. | Representation | File types and storage items are objects of the UI thread. Calling a variadic function through a fixed declaration is not portable. | x11-platform stage 2e |
| `X11PlatformLifetimeEvents` is one object used from the thread that reads the session manager and from the UI thread; its pump is a task of the thread pool. | The shared part (`Shared`: the connection, the phase and the cancellation as atomics, the calls behind a trait) is what the pump thread and the handlers hold; the event and its handlers stay with the object of the UI thread, which the job of the interaction reaches. The pump is a named thread. The option `enable_session_management` is read once, when the platform starts. | Representation | The handlers of the event are objects of the UI thread. | x11-platform stage 2f |
| `X11NativeControlHost`, `Attachment`: exceptions (`ArgumentException`, `InvalidOperationException`, `ObjectDisposedException`); `XEmbedPlug.Create(IntPtr.Zero)` throws. | Panics with the messages. | Representation | No exceptions. | x11-platform stage 2f |
| `XEmbedClientWindowMode` finds the mode of the top-level the focus moved into by casting the platform implementation of the root and its mode; its members read the window it was initialized with. | A registry of the windows of this mode by their identifier (per thread), and `X11WindowMode::x_embed` in place of the cast; every member takes the window, as the other modes. `ProcessInteractiveResize` sets the size through `X11Window::interactive_resize`. | Representation | A mode does not keep its window in the port (section 7 of the design). | x11-platform stage 2f |
| The catalog's `EmbedSampleGtk` starts `mplayer` (and fails without it) with a video its build copies beside the executable; `GtkHelper.CreateGtkFileChooser` fails when GTK cannot be initialized. | Without `mplayer` the first control stays the empty default control and a line is printed; the video is looked for beside the executable and then in the source directory of the sample; without GTK the second control is the first. | Behaviour | A cargo build copies nothing; a sample page should open on a system without the two. | x11-platform stage 2f |

Accessibility over AT-SPI (stage 3; `src/FerroUI.FreeDesktop/at_spi/` and `src/FerroUI.X11/x11_at_spi_accessibility.rs`; the design is `atspi.md`):

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A project of its own (`Avalonia.FreeDesktop.AtSpi`) that compiles the sources of the D-Bus library `external/Avalonia.DBus` into itself and generates proxies and handlers from `DBusXml/`. | A module tree of `ferroui-freedesktop` over `zbus`. Of that library only the object table of a connection, the dispatch of a method call, the built-in properties and introspection handlers and the shape of a generated handler are ported (`at_spi/dbus/`); the handler code the generator writes is written by hand from the same descriptions. | Representation | One D-Bus library in the port (`atspi.md`, sections 1 and 5). | atspi |
| The handlers are called by the D-Bus library on the synchronization context of the UI thread. | The accessibility connection has no object server; one task of the UI dispatcher reads its message stream and dispatches method calls by path and interface. Replies and signals are sent by one task, in the order they were made. | Representation | The object server of `zbus` calls exported objects on its own thread and has no handler for a subtree of paths (`atspi.md`, section 4). | atspi |
| The node paths are `/net/avaloniaui/a11y/<n>`; the toolkit name of `org.a11y.atspi.Application` and the attribute `toolkit` are the name of the upstream project; the toolkit version is the version of the assembly. | `/org/ferroui/a11y/<n>`; `FerroUI`; the version of the crate. | Behaviour | The upstream name does not appear in the port. Visible to clients: a script that looks for the upstream toolkit name does not find a FerroUI application. Clients take paths from references. | atspi |
| `RegisterObjects` is asynchronous: a node keeps the task of its registration (`PathRegistrationTask`), replaces it by chaining, and disposes it when it completes; `AtSpiServer.StartAsync` and `DisposeAsync` await registrations and the connection. | Registration is synchronous: a node keeps the registration itself, `start_on_connection` and `dispose` do not wait. A node answers from the moment it is attached, where upstream's answers `UnknownObject` until its registration completed. | Behaviour | Registering is an insertion into a table of the UI thread. | atspi |
| `DBusConnection.ConnectAsync` to the accessibility bus. | A blocking connect, as the other connections of the crate (`DBusHelper`). | Behaviour | `x11-platform.md`, section 3. | atspi |
| `RootAtSpiNode` subscribes to `IWindowBaseImpl.Activated` and `Deactivated` with `+=` and unsubscribes with `-=`. | The callbacks of a window are one slot each: the node replaces the callback by one that calls the previous one and then the node, and after `detach` the node's part does nothing (the wrapper stays in the slot). | Representation | The contract of the port stores one delegate, and the contracts are not changed for a backend. A party that sets the slot after the node was created removes the node's part; the framework sets its callbacks when the window is created, before it is shown. | atspi |
| `AtSpiRegistryEventTracker` subscribes to the signals of the registry with the unique name of its owner as the sender and watches `NameOwnerChanged` to subscribe again. | The proxy of `zbus` for the well-known name does both. | Representation | The library tracks the owner of a proxy's destination for its signal streams. | atspi |
| `AtSpiAccessibilityWatcher` uses a generated proxy of `org.a11y.Status`. | The properties proxy of `zbus` (`GetAll`, `PropertiesChanged` for that interface); a property a change does not carry counts as false, as upstream's generated structure has it. | Representation | | atspi |
| `RootAtSpiNode` derives from `AtSpiNode`. | A part of the node (`AtSpiNode::as_root`). | Representation | No inheritance. | atspi |
| `GetAccessibleName`, the state mapping and the action list read the peer inside the member. | The same reads, gathered into a value (`StateFacts`, `ActionFacts`) that a function maps. | Representation | The mappings are tested without controls. | atspi |
| A provider that throws (`ElementNotEnabledException`) fails the call with `org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.Failed` through the catch of the library; `AtSpiValueHandler.CurrentValue` set on such a provider fails the `Set`. | The same error for actions, selection and editable text; a refused value is left as it is and `Set` succeeds. | Behaviour | The property setter of a handler has no error to return in the port. | atspi |
| `AtSpiTextHandler.GetTextAfterOffset` indexes the string with a negative offset plus one and fails for offsets below -1. | Such an offset starts at the text. | Behaviour | An exception in a handler upstream (answered as `Failed`). | atspi |
| `X11AtSpiAccessibility` holds the windows (`List<X11Window>`); `X11Window.Cleanup` reads the internal `Control.GetAutomationPeer`. | Weak references to the windows; `ControlAutomationPeer::from_element`, the public member with the same answer. | Representation | A tracked window that is dropped is not kept alive; the internal member is not visible to the X11 crate. | atspi |
| `AtSpiServer : IAsyncDisposable`, `AtSpiAccessibilityWatcher : IAsyncDisposable`. | `dispose` is a member of each. | Representation | | atspi |

Not built yet (each fails, logs or answers "not available" as stated, and is tracked as missing in `tracking/Avalonia.X11.md`):

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Stage |
|---|---|---|---|
| X11 windows render text the same way whatever the desktop's settings. | The surface handle of a window reports the subpixel order of `Xft.rgba` from the resource database (`x_resources.rs`), nothing when it is not set. | Behaviour | Subpixel text where the desktop asks for it, greyscale otherwise (`text-rendering.md`, section 5.2). | Text rendering (`text-rendering.md`) |

### Wayland platform (`src/FerroUI.Wayland/`)

The design, the binding choice and what each stage builds are in `wayland-platform.md`. Two differences are systematic. The protocol bindings are those of the `wayland-client` family where upstream has NWayland: an object has user data and the worker's state implements `Dispatch` for it, where upstream gives it a listener object. And upstream's rule "no cross-thread variable access, messaging only" is in the types: what both threads hold is `Send + Sync` (`WaylandWorker`, the proxies of sinks), what the worker alone has is a value of its thread (`WaylandWorkerThread`), and an object of one side is named on the other by a number.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NWayland, generated from the protocol descriptions; `libwayland-client`, `libwayland-cursor`, `libwayland-egl`, `libxkbcommon` as platform invokes (`WaylandEglNativeMethods.cs`, `XkbCommonNativeMethods.cs`, the cursor declarations of `UnsafeNativeMethods.cs`). | `wayland-client`, `wayland-protocols`, `wayland-egl` over `libwayland-client` and `libwayland-egl` opened with `dlopen`; `xkbcommon-dl`; the cursor themes read by `wayland-cursor`. The three declaration files are not ported. | Representation | The declarations exist as maintained crates; nothing is linked. | wayland-platform stage 1 |
| The cursor theme is loaded by `wl_cursor_theme_load(null, 24, shm)`, which gives built-in cursors when the theme is not found. | `CursorTheme::load_from_name("default", 24)`: a system without the theme files has no themed cursors, and the pointer is hidden over a window. | Behaviour | The crate has no built-in images. | stage 1 |
| A missing `libxkbcommon` fails the first keyboard (the runtime cannot load the library). | No keymap: keys are translated from their evdev codes, as upstream does for a keymap that does not compile. | Behaviour | The library is opened at run time, and a missing one is a value. | stage 1 |
| Proxies generated by `[GenerateCrossThreadProxy]` (`WSurfaceProxy`, `WXdgShellSurfaceProxy`, `WXdgTopLevelProxy`, `WaylandCursorProxy`, the sink proxies), which hold their target object. | Written by hand. A proxy of the UI thread holds the number of the worker's object and posts closures that look it up; a sink proxy of the worker holds the sink behind `UiThreadRef` (reached and dropped on the UI thread only). The interfaces are implemented by the proxies; the worker's objects have the same members with the globals as an argument. | Representation | No source generator; an object of one thread cannot be held by the other. | stage 1 |
| `WSurface`, `WXdgShellSurface`, `WXdgTopLevel` derive from one another and override virtual members; listener classes hold their surface; frame events are closures run at `frame`. | A top-level holds its shell surface, which holds its surface; an event handler applies what the base returned (`SurfaceChange`); the `Dispatch` implementations are the listeners; the events of a pointer or touch frame are values (`PointerAction`, `TouchAction`) applied at `frame`. | Representation | No inheritance; a closure cannot capture a handler while the state is borrowed for the dispatch. | stage 1 |
| `WaylandWorker` is one class used from both threads by convention; its constructor creates the compositor of the framework. | `WaylandWorker` (shared) and `WaylandWorkerThread` (a value of the worker thread, reached through `with_worker_thread`); the compositor is created by `WaylandWorkerClient` on the UI thread, after the worker bound its globals, and the worker gets the handle of its server side. | Representation, order | The compositor of the port is an object of the UI thread. Creating it after the probe leaves the dispatcher and the media context untouched for a fallback backend. | stage 1 |
| `WaylandGlobals` is constructed while its registry listener writes to it. | The announcements of the first round trip are recorded (`RegistryBootstrap`), then the globals are made and outputs and seats are bound in the order they were announced. | Order | A value cannot be written to before it exists. The requests reach the compositor in another order than upstream's (required globals before outputs and seats). | stage 1 |
| `IWaylandGraphics.CreateRenderSurface(WSurface)`; `WSurface.RenderSurfaces` evaluated on the render thread; `WaylandSurfaceCreateResult.GetRenderSurfaces` a callback. | A window always lists the EGL surface and the framebuffer surface, as handles that name the worker's surface; the renderer takes the first it can use (the EGL one only when the worker has a GPU backend). | Representation | `IPlatformRenderSurface` of the port is `Send + Sync` and created on the UI thread. | stage 1 |
| `WaylandFramebuffer` throws `RenderTargetNotReadyException` from `Lock`. | A frame that is locked while the surface is not ready is drawn and not presented. | Behaviour | The contract of the port has no error there; its renderer asks the state of the target first. | stage 1 |
| `WaylandBitmapCursor` draws through the framebuffer render target of its surface, which makes a round trip after the commit. | The cursor attaches its frame with the function the render target uses; the worker makes the round trip after it connected the cursor. | Representation | A round trip needs the connection and the whole state, which the cursor does not have while it is being connected. | stage 1 |
| An exception that escapes the worker thread (a protocol error, a lost connection with reconnects off) ends the process as an unhandled exception. | Logged at the fatal level, printed to the error stream, and the process is aborted. | Representation | A panic of a thread other than the main one does not end a Rust process, and the UI thread would wait for a worker that is gone. | stage 1 |
| `xdg_wm_base` is required at version 3 (for `xdg_popup.reposition`). | Version 2 is taken. From version 3 a popup is repositioned with the request, as upstream; before it the popup is created again: `xdg_popup` and `xdg_surface` destroyed, a null buffer committed, both created again on the same `wl_surface` from the new positioner. Equal parameters do nothing on that path; a popup with an open child popup is not moved (a warning). | Behaviour | sway 1.9, the compositor of the tests and of Ubuntu 24.04, has version 2 (measured by the first run of the CI job, which failed for want of the global). Creating the popup again is what toolkits did before the request existed; requiring version 3 refuses that compositor, ignoring new parameters leaves popups at stale places. One frame without the popup is the cost. `wayland-platform.md`, section 7.1. | stage 1; the popups in stage 2, part 1 |
| `XdgPopupConfigureBatch` has the position, the size and the serial. | Also `recreated`: the first batch of a popup that was created again; the sink then asks the framework for a frame (`paint`). | Addition | The surface of such a popup has no buffer, and nothing else draws a popup whose content did not change. | stage 2, part 1 |
| `SetPendingAckSerial` stores the serial the UI thread answers with. | A shell surface keeps the serials of the configure events of its current `xdg_surface` and drops an answer that names none of them. | Behaviour | A popup that is created again has another `xdg_surface`; the answer to a configure of the first one, which is under way from the UI thread, would be acknowledged on the second: a protocol error ("wrong configure serial", measured with the catalog at scale 1.5). Upstream cannot meet it: it requires the version of the shell that repositions. | stage 2, part 1 |
| Nothing records where a popup was asked to be. | `WXdgPopup::placement`: the anchor rectangle as the framework gave it, the numbers of the positioner, the window geometry of the parent, the last configure. | Addition | The tests compare the place the compositor chose with the control the popup belongs to. | stage 2, part 1 |
| A frame callback clears the pending callback of its surface. | Only when it is the pending one. | Behaviour | A popup that is created again drops the callback of its last frame, which the compositor may still answer. | stage 2, part 1 |
| `WXdgPopup` holds its parent object and calls it (`RegisterPendingChildPopup`, `IsMapped`, `XdgSurface`, `LastWindowGeometry`); the parent attaches its pending popups inside `OnBeforeNewBufferAttached`. `PopupImpl` holds its parent as `WindowBaseImpl`. | The worker's popup names its parent by number; what it reads of the parent is handed in as a value (`PopupParent`), and the state of the worker registers and attaches (`try_attach_popup_to_parent`, `on_before_new_buffer_attached`, which the render surfaces call in place of the surface's own method). The popup of the UI thread holds its parent as the trait `IPopupParent`, which the window and the popup implement. | Representation | Parent and popup are entries of the same maps of the worker; no inheritance. The order of the requests is upstream's (the popups are attached before the buffer of the parent). | stage 2, part 1 |
| `CreatePopupHandle` completes `BasicInitCompleted` at once with an empty batch. | `WaylandSurfaceCreateResult::basic_init_completed` is `None` for a popup. | Representation | Nothing waits for it. | stage 2, part 1 |
| `Math.Round` (halves to even) for the anchor rectangle, the offset and the logical size of a surface. | `round_ties_even`; the logical size of stage 1 rounded halves away from zero and was changed. | None since part 1 | Exactness. | stage 2, part 1 |
| `WaylandPlatformOptions.AppId` defaults to the name of the entry assembly (upstream 4b32663482, #22209). | `WaylandPlatformOptions::app_id` defaults to the file name of the executable; `WaylandProperties::app_id_property` and `IWaylandOptionsToplevelImplFeature` as upstream. | Naming | A native executable has no entry assembly; its file name is what a desktop entry of the application names. (The port had the option before upstream, as an addition; it now follows upstream's semantics: `None` sends no identifier.) | stage 1, upstream sync 66b7273 |
| `WaylandPlatform` binds no object of the backend under its own type. | The worker client and the screens are also registered under their types. | Addition | The example asks the backend about itself. | stage 1 |
| `WaylandGlibDispatcher` is an empty subclass of `GlibDispatcherImplBase`. | A function that makes the base of the X11 crate; it is asked for before the worker is started. | Representation, order | Nothing to override; a missing GLib then leaves nothing running. | stage 1 |
| `UseWaylandWithFallback` logs why Wayland could not be initialized (a warning) and calls the fallback. | The same; when the fallback fails too, the reason Wayland was refused is also written to the error stream before the failure of the fallback goes on. | Addition | Measured in the virtual machine: under a compositor the backend refused, the only thing the catalog said was that the X11 backend found no display. | stage 1b |
| `ControlCatalog.Desktop` calls `UseWaylandWithFallback()` always. | The same since stage 2 was completed (2026-10-11; before that only with `FERROUI_CATALOG_WAYLAND=1`). The variable remains as the port's own: `0` (or `x11`) leaves the backend of the platform detection alone, `software` renders through shared memory buffers alone. | Addition | The runs that need one backend: the catalog through XWayland on a Wayland session, and software rendering in the job. | stage 1b, closed with stage 2 |
| A pipe of a data transfer is read and written with the asynchronous streams of the runtime on pool threads (`Pipe2Stream`), awaited or blocked on. | `Pipe2Stream` is a file over the descriptor. An asynchronous read is a read on a thread of its own whose result is awaited on the UI thread (`CrossThreadResult`, which is also the task of `InvokeOobAsync`); a synchronous read (the item of a drop) reads on the calling thread; a write is a thread of its own. | Representation | No asynchronous runtime; the threads that must not block (the UI thread while this process is the owner, the worker always) do not. | stage 2, part 4 |
| `SerializeForMimeAsync` writes into the stream of the pipe as it goes, and encodes a bitmap from a pool thread into the pipe. | The bytes for a MIME type are made on the UI thread (a bitmap is encoded there, into memory) and written by a thread of its own. | Representation, memory | A bitmap of the port belongs to the UI thread. A large bitmap is held once in memory as its PNG while it is written. | stage 2, part 4 |
| `WaylandOfferCookie` holds the data device and the offer wrapper; `WaylandInputEventCookie` holds the globals it came from. | The offer cookie holds the protocol object of the offer and the registry name of the seat, and finds the device on the worker; the input cookie holds the number of its connection and the worker. | Representation | An object of the worker cannot be held by the UI thread. | stage 2, part 4 |
| A data source is kept alive by its listener; the callbacks of a source are closures over the device and the globals. | The sources of a connection are in `WaylandGlobals::data_sources` by the identity of the protocol object; a callback receives the state of the worker. They are destroyed with the connection. | Representation | Events are dispatched to the state, not to a listener object. | stage 2, part 4 |
| The drags of this process (`s_inProcessDrags`) are a table "of the Wayland thread", read by the sink when a drag enters. | A table of the UI thread, where the transfers live and where the sink runs: an entry is made when the drag is asked for and removed by a posted call when the source ends or the drag could not start. | Representation | A data transfer is an object of the UI thread. | stage 2, part 4 |
| When the compositor was replaced between the press and `SetAsDrag`, the callback of the input cookie is not run and the task of the drag never completes. | The drag resolves to no effect. | Behaviour | A future that never resolves would keep the caller of `do_drag_drop_async` waiting for ever. | stage 2, part 4 |
| The MIME prefixes `application/x-avalonia-` (application formats) and `application/x-avalonia-inproc-dnd-` (the sentinel of a drag of the process). | `application/x-ferroui-` and `application/x-ferroui-inproc-dnd-`. | Naming | The upstream name does not appear in the code. An application format copied by an application of the port is not the same clipboard format as upstream's. | stage 2, part 4 |
| `WaylandDataTransferItem.ConvertData` lets the exception of the bitmap constructor go on for data that is no image. | No value. | Behaviour | The synchronous item contract has no error. | stage 2, part 4 |
| `WaylandMimeMapper.FromMimeType` is declared to return null "if not mappable". | Declared optional as well; every MIME type maps (an unknown one is a platform format of bytes), as upstream's body does. | None | Noted because the declaration suggests otherwise. | stage 2, part 4 |
| The seats of the input dispatcher are a dictionary in the order they were announced; "the first data device" is the first of that order. | The first by registry name. | Order | A hash map has no order; with one seat there is no difference. | stage 2, part 4 |
| An offer that was announced and never taken (a second `data_offer` before `selection` or `enter`) is left to the garbage collector. | Destroyed when the next one is announced. | Behaviour | Nothing collects a protocol object. | stage 2, part 4 |
| `PopupImpl.Sink.OnPopupConfigure` sets the serial to acknowledge "on next commit" and does nothing else. | The same, and a frame of the popup is asked for (`paint`). (Since part 1 only for a popup that was created again; since part 5 after every configure.) | Behaviour | The serial is acknowledged with the next buffer, and a compositor moves a repositioned popup when its configure is acknowledged and committed: measured under sway 1.10.1 (CI run 38105648789), a popup that was only moved stayed at its old place until its content next changed. Upstream has the same order at the tracked commit. | stage 2, parts 1 and 5 |
| `WaylandTextInputV3` holds the generated protocol objects and is their listener; it keys its state by the `WSurface` object and reads the shadow extents and the session token of that object. | The facade is written against three contracts (`ITextInputV3`: the requests of the protocol object; `ITextInputManagerV3`; `ITextInputSurfaces`: the shadow extents and the session token of a surface by its number), which the bindings and the surfaces of the worker implement on Linux; the listener is the `on_*` methods, called by the `Dispatch` implementation. | Representation | The whole state machine (enable and disable, the done and serial protocol, the conversions) runs in tests on every system, without a compositor. | stage 2, part 5 |
| The text input members of `WSurface` (`RegisterTextInputSink`, `SetTextInputActive`, ...). | Methods of the state of the worker (`WaylandWorkerState::set_text_input_active`, ...); the surface keeps the flag, the sink and the token. | Representation | Each needs the surface and the facade of the connection at once, and the surfaces are entries of the state. | stage 2, part 5 |
| `SetTextInputOptions(TextInputOptions)` crosses to the worker, which converts the options whenever it sends the content type. | The proxy converts on the UI thread, and the facade keeps the content hint and purpose. | Representation | The options hold a reference counted list (the locale hints), which cannot cross threads. The conversion is a pure function, so the result is the same. | stage 2, part 5 |
| `TextInputOptionsConverter.Convert` returns the enumerations of the generated bindings. | `ContentHint` and `ContentPurpose` of the crate, with the values of the protocol, turned into the types of the bindings where the request is sent. | Representation | The bindings exist on Linux only; the conversion and its tests (upstream's six) run everywhere. | stage 2, part 5 |
| `WaylandTextUtils` works on UTF-16 strings; an index between the halves of a surrogate pair is a position of the string. | The text is UTF-8 and is walked as UTF-16 code units; half a pair counts as the replacement character (three bytes), as the runtime of upstream counts it. `utf16_length` and `utf16_substring` are additions (what `string.Length` and `Substring` are there). | Representation | The indices of the framework are UTF-16 code units; a `str` cannot hold half a pair. Upstream's tests pass unchanged. | stage 2, part 5 |
| The seats of the text input facade are a dictionary in the order they were added. | A map ordered by registry name. | Order | As for the data devices; with one seat there is no difference. | stage 2, part 5 |
| `ConvertDeleteToChars` casts the byte counts of `delete_surrounding_text` to `int`, which wraps for a count beyond its range. | Such a count is clamped to the range. | Behaviour | A count that large is clamped to the text by the conversion either way; a wrapped one would delete in the wrong direction. | stage 2, part 5 |
| `WaylandTextInputV3.SetActive` sends `enable` and a `commit` at once (`PushStateToEnteredSeats`), before the UI thread has sent the surrounding text, the cursor rectangle and the content type of the new client, each of which then goes in a commit of its own. | `set_active` marks the surface; `flush`, which the worker calls after it ran the commands of the UI thread (after the queue and the tick of every iteration), sends `enable` with all the state that arrived, in one commit. A kind of state that first arrives after the enabling commit (a cursor rectangle a frame later) is sent by enabling again at the flush (`disable`, `commit`, `enable`, the state, `commit`: the sequence of a reset). The serials differ accordingly (fewer commits). | Behaviour | Measured (CI run 38101694610, sway 1.9): the protocol says the state of the enabling commit is what the client supports for the session, and wlroots holds to it (`active_features`): with upstream's order the input method was told no surrounding text and no content type, and its popup got no cursor rectangle and was not placed at the cursor. Compositors that do not gate (Mutter) see one commit where upstream sends four. | stage 2, part 5 |
| The framework sets the options of the focused element before the client (`InputMethodManager`: `SetOptions`, then `SetClient`); `SetTextInputActive` aborts the composition, which forgets the options of the surface; nothing sends them again. | The text input method of a window keeps the options that were set last and sends them again after the client, and after a new surface was made. | Behaviour | Measured in the same run: the worker had no content type for the surface of a focused text box. With upstream's order a password box is not announced as one until its options change. | stage 2, part 5 |
| `WaylandTextInputMethod` remembers the surface proxy it registered its sink against and compares references. | It remembers the number of the surface. | Representation | A proxy of the port is a value; a new surface has a new number. | stage 2, part 5 |
| `IWaylandXdgTopLevelExport.HandleTask` is a task that may be awaited any number of times; `ExportToplevel` returns the worker's object wrapped for the UI thread. | `handle_task` gives the result to its one reader. What crosses from the worker is the number of the export and the result of its handle; the handle of the UI thread (`UiThreadHandle`) is made on the UI thread and names the export by the number of its top-level and its own. | Representation | A result of another thread is read once; an object of the worker cannot be held by the UI thread. | stage 2, part 5 |
| The protocol tracer (`WaylandWorker.Tracer`, installed on the display when the log area `WaylandProtocol` is enabled at the verbose level: one log line per request and event). | None. A probe that finds the log area enabled logs once that `WAYLAND_DEBUG=1` makes `libwayland-client` print the same messages to the error stream. | Missing by construction | The bindings have no hook between a proxy and the wire (`wayland-client` and `wayland-backend` at the pinned versions), and the port does not generate its own bindings. The class is private to the worker and is not counted by the tracking. | stage 2, part 5 |

Stage 3 (2026-10-15): the dmabuf swapchain and a display another library opened. Nothing of the project is unbuilt since then.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DrmGbmUnsafeNativeMethods` declares platform invokes of `libgbm.so.1` and `libdrm.so.2`, each resolved when first called. | Both libraries are opened, and all 16 functions resolved, the first time the swapchain asks for them, through the loader of the base crate (`ferroui_base::platform::native_library`: `NativeLibrary`, `native_functions!`; the X11 crate's until 2026-10-15). A library or a function that is missing (for example `gbm_bo_create_with_modifiers2` of a Mesa older than 21.3) makes the swapchain fail to start, with the error logged, and the backend renders in software. | Behaviour | Nothing is linked, as for every library of the backends; upstream would fail later, in the first frame that calls the missing function. | stage 3 |
| `WaylandEglDisplay` derives from `EglDisplay`; a render target casts the display of its context to it. | It holds the display of the OpenGL crate (`Rc<EglDisplay>`) and adds its members; a render target finds it through the platform graphics of the worker and checks that its context belongs to that display. | Representation | The display of the OpenGL crate is a value the contexts hold, not a class to derive from. | stage 3 |
| `WaylandEglDmaBufSurface` creates the `zwp_linux_buffer_params_v1` on a temporary queue, takes the `wl_buffer` of `created` with a listener after a round trip of that queue, and moves the buffer to the main queue (`SetQueue`). | The parameters object has object data of its own (`DmaBufCreateParamsListener`), which the backend calls when it reads `created`: it records the buffer and gives it the object data of the worker's queue with the release listener of the slot. The round trip is one of the connection without a queue (`Connection::roundtrip`). The parameters object is destroyed after the round trip. | Representation | `wayland-client` cannot move an object to another queue; the object data of a child is chosen when the event that creates it is read. | stage 3 |
| The feedback object of `get_default_feedback` is not destroyed (left to the collector). | Destroyed once it was read. | Behaviour | Nothing collects a protocol object; the compositor would keep sending it the changes of the feedback. | stage 3 |
| The dispose callback of the display destroys the GBM device and closes the node each time the display is disposed. | Once. | Behaviour | A second dispose would close a descriptor number that may belong to someone else by then. | stage 3 |
| `NegotiateFormats` collects the pairs of the feedback and the formats in hash sets. | In vectors without duplicates, in the order they first appear. | Order | The order of the modifiers given to `gbm_bo_create_with_modifiers2` is then the compositor's, not that of a hash. | stage 3 |
| A swapchain whose slot fails half way keeps an array with empty entries, which the next frame of the same size reads (a null reference). | The slots that were made. | Behaviour | Exactness would be a crash. | stage 3 |
| The finish of a frame attaches the buffer to `WlSurface!` (the surface is assumed). | Without a `wl_surface` (the surface is gone) nothing is attached or committed. | Behaviour | As the other render surfaces of the port do. | stage 3 |
| `use_dmabuf_swapchain` with a compositor without `zwp_linux_dmabuf_v1` takes the `wl_egl_window` path without a word. | The same, with a warning of the area `OpenGL` that says so. | Addition | The CI jobs and the virtual machine measure which path was taken (the runners' compositor has no render node). | stage 3 |
| `RenderSurfaces` of a surface: the surface of the bound backend and the framebuffer surface. | Both surfaces of EGL are listed (the one of the window system integration and the dmabuf one) with the framebuffer surface; each answers as an OpenGL surface only while its backend is the bound one. | Representation | The render surfaces of a window are handles made on the UI thread (the row on `CreateRenderSurface` above). | stage 3 |
| The render target of an `EGLImage` throws `RenderTargetNotReadyException` from `BeginDrawCore`. | `EglPlatformImageSurfaceRenderTarget::try_begin_draw_core` of the OpenGL crate (a seam added beside the one of the window render targets) returns the error. | Representation | `begin_draw` of the contract returns a session and cannot throw. | stage 3 |
| `Start(options, WlDisplay foreignDisplay, outputsSink)` and `WaylandConnection(WlDisplay)` take the managed display of the bindings. | `start_with_foreign_display` and `WaylandConnection::from_foreign_display` take a `wayland_client::Connection`; `from_foreign_display_ptr` (an addition) makes one of a `wl_display*` that is never closed (`Backend::from_foreign_display`). | Representation, addition | The connection is what the port calls the display everywhere; the pointer is what a library of C hands over. | stage 3 |

### Linux framebuffer platform (`src/Linux/FerroUI.LinuxFramebuffer/`)

The design, the bindings and what each stage builds are in `linux-framebuffer-platform.md`. The systematic difference of this backend is where input is turned into raw events: on the UI thread, from values the input thread sends, because a raw event holds objects of that thread.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `LinuxFramebufferPlatform.Initialize` calls `UseSkia()`, and the project references the Skia project. | The crate does not depend on a renderer; the caller chooses one on the builder before a start function (`use_skia()`, or another renderer behind the same contracts). | Behaviour | The crate and its tests build, and check for a Linux target from another system, without the native libraries of Skia; a second renderer exists in the port. An application that does not choose a renderer fails at start as on any other platform. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| Environment variables named after the project: `AVALONIA_USE_EVDEV`, `AVALONIA_EVDEV_DEVICE_{name}`. | `FERROUI_USE_EVDEV`, `FERROUI_EVDEV_DEVICE_{name}`. | Behaviour | The upstream name does not appear in the port. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| `EvDevDevice` reads the name, the event types, the two absolute axes and the events of a device through `libevdev.so.2`. | The same facts are read with the device's own `ioctl`s (`EVIOCGNAME`, `EVIOCGBIT`, `EVIOCGABS`) and `read`. After `SYN_DROPPED` the events up to the next `SYN_REPORT` are passed over and the handler ends what was in progress (contacts end, held buttons are released), where the library would re-synchronise by asking the device for its state. | Behaviour | Five calls that are system calls on a file do not justify a run-time library a board image may lack. A dropped report is rare (the reader would have to fall 64 reports behind), and ending the gesture is the safe reading of it. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| The input threads create `RawInputEventArgs` and call `onInput` on their own thread; `FramebufferToplevelImpl` posts each event to the UI thread with the priority `Send`. `IScreenInfoProvider.ScaledSize` is read on the input thread. | The input thread posts what it read (the `input_event` records of one read) to the UI thread with the priority `Send`; the device handlers run there, create the raw events and call `on_input`, which hands them to the raw event grouper directly. The scaled size is read on the UI thread. | Representation | A raw event holds the input root and the input device, objects of the UI thread (`Rc`). The order of events and the priority are unchanged. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| `EvDevDeviceDescription` is an abstract class with one subclass; `EvDevDeviceHandler` raises `RawInputEventArgs`. | The descriptions are the cases of an enumeration; a handler is a state machine that reports values (`EvDevRawEvent`), which the backend turns into raw events. | Representation | The kinds are closed; values let the state machines be tested against recorded event streams without an application. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| The evdev backend knows one kind of device, a touch screen with one contact. Neither backend reads a key or a relative pointer. | Three more kinds, selected by `type=keyboard`, `type=mouse` and `type=multitouch` in the description: keys (the kernel's key codes as physical keys, the keys and symbols of a US layout, text input for printable symbols without a command modifier), a relative or absolute pointer with three buttons and the wheel, and several contacts with the multi-touch protocol B. | Addition | A UI without a window system has no other source of keys; upstream's default backend covers pointers and multi-touch through `libinput`. No keyboard layout is read from the system. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| `FbdevOutput` calls `open`, `ioctl` and `mmap` itself and keeps its back buffer (`_backBuffer`). | The output talks to its device through `IFbDevice` (`LinuxFbDevice` makes the same calls; `FileFbDevice` is a plain file); `FbdevOutput::with_device` takes one. The back buffer belongs to each render target the output creates; the mapped memory is released when the output is disposed and the frame that uses it has ended. | Addition, Representation | A file stands in for the device in tests and on machines without one. A surface is shared with the thread that renders (`Send + Sync`) and the back buffer is an object of one thread; one target exists at a time, so the frames are the same. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| `FbDevBackBuffer.Lock` holds a monitor until the locked framebuffer is disposed; the blit thread has the highest priority. | The locked framebuffer takes the pixels out of the back buffer and puts them back when it is disposed; a frame that is dropped without being disposed gives them back and is not shown. The blit thread has the default priority. | Representation, Behaviour | A guard of a mutex cannot be stored in an object behind a contract; the standard library has no thread priorities. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| The constructors of `FbdevOutput` and the start functions throw `Exception` with the error number. | They return `io::Result`, with upstream's message texts and the system's description of the error. | Representation | Errors of the environment (a missing device, a refused mode) are results in the port; panics are for errors of the program. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| `LinuxFramebufferPlatform.Initialize` uses `DefaultRenderTimer` unless rendering on the UI thread. | `SleepLoopRenderTimer`, as the X11 platform. | Behaviour | Upstream's default timer ticks on threads of a pool. The compositor of the port is confined to the one thread that first renders it, and the port's `DefaultRenderTimer` starts a new thread each time its last subscriber left and one comes back: a compositor that stopped and restarted its loop was then rendered by a second thread and panicked ("a compositor that is confined to its render thread was rendered by another thread"; seen once in the virtual machine, in a run whose view needed no second frame). | linuxfb stage 2a |
| `EpollDispatcherImpl` arms the timer descriptor with zero seconds and zero nanoseconds when the next timer is due exactly now, which disarms it. | One nanosecond in that case. | Behaviour | The loop would sleep until something else woke it. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| `LibInputNativeUnsafeMethods` declares platform invokes of `libinput.so.10`; the input thread maps events to raw events with the screen size it reads there. | The library is opened with `dlopen` and its 23 functions are resolved when the backend starts (a missing library or function is a panic that names it). The thread sends each event as a value (`LibInputEvent`) with positions as fractions of a fixed range (`libinput_event_*_get_*_transformed` with 2^20), and the UI thread scales them to the whole width and height the reference passes. | Representation | No link-time dependency; raw events and the screen are objects of the UI thread. The transformation of the library is linear, so the positions differ by rounding only. | linuxfb stage 2a |
| `LibInputBackend.HandlePointer` maps the right button to "up" for state 1 and to "down" for state 2, and the middle button to "up" for state 2. | The three buttons go down for state 1 and up for state 0. | Behaviour | The library has two button states, 0 and 1: upstream's right button never goes down and its middle button never comes up. | linuxfb stage 2a |
| – | `use_linux_framebuffer(output, input)` on the builder: upstream's internal `LinuxFramebufferPlatform.Initialize`, public. | Addition | For a host that sets the application up and runs the loop itself. | linuxfb stage 1 |
| `DrmOutput.Init` creates the EGL display on the GBM device, the window surface and the one context, clears and swaps, sets the CRTC with the first buffer, creates the objects of the rotation and swaps twice, all in the constructor on the UI thread; `SharedContextGraphics` is constructed with that context. | The constructor does the part that is handles (the CRTC, the GBM device and surface; it opens `libdrm`, `libgbm` and `libEGL`). The rest of `Init`, in upstream's order, runs on the thread that first calls `get_shared_context` of the output's platform graphics (the render thread), which creates the context then and keeps it bound to that thread. `uses_shared_context` stays true. | Representation, Behaviour | An EGL display and context are objects of one thread in the port (`Rc`), and the compositor renders on its render thread; a context created on the UI thread could not be used there, and a second context would not share the objects of the rotation. The mode is set with the first frame instead of in the constructor, and a failure of EGL or of `drmModeSetCrtc` is a panic on the render thread with upstream's message instead of an exception of the constructor. | linuxfb stage 2b |
| `RenderSession.Dispose` calls `drmModePageFlip` (declared without a result) and waits for its event with `poll` and `drmHandleEvent`. | The result is read: when the flip is refused the error is printed, the new buffer goes back to the surface and the old one stays on the screen. | Behaviour | Upstream waits for an event that never comes when the flip fails, and the frame loop hangs. | linuxfb stage 2b |
| `GetFbIdForBo` stores the framebuffer number as the user data of a buffer object, and the destroy callback is a delegate over the output that reads `_card.Fd`. | The user data is a boxed record of the framebuffer number and a weak reference to the output; the callback (a C function) frees it and removes the framebuffer with the card's descriptor when the output is alive. The page flip handler finds its flag through the user data of the flip, not through a closure. | Representation | A C callback has no closure; the record carries what the delegate captured. | linuxfb stage 2b |
| `DrmModeConnection`, `DrmModeSubPixel`, `DrmModeType`, `DrmModePageFlip`, `GbmBoFlags`, `DrmConnectorType` are enumerations; the functions of `libdrm` and `libgbm` are platform invokes declared with the reference's signatures. | Numbers (`#[repr(transparent)]`) with the known values as constants; the libraries are opened at run time into two tables (`output/drm.rs`, with the macro of `native_library.rs` that the `libinput` bindings use as well), with the C signatures (`drmModePageFlip`, `drmHandleEvent` and `drmModeRmFB` return `int`, `gbm_bo_set_user_data` and the second page flip handler return nothing). A null result of `drmModeGetCrtc`, `drmModeGetEncoder` or `drmModeGetConnector` is an error, where upstream dereferences it. | Representation | A field of a C structure may hold a value the declaration does not name, which a Rust enumeration may not; no link-time dependency on the libraries. | linuxfb stage 2b |
| – | `DrmOutput::card`, `DrmOutput::crtc_id` and `output/drm_scanout.rs` (`read_scanout`: the framebuffer a CRTC scans out, read through `DRM_IOCTL_MODE_GETFB` and `DRM_IOCTL_MODE_MAP_DUMB`; `driver_name`: the driver of a card through `DRM_IOCTL_VERSION`); `DrmResources::dump` is public. `--drm` of the smoke example with `--expect-driver`; `FERROUI_LINUXFB_EXPECT_DRIVER` and the dumps of the catalog's `--drm`. | Addition | The checks compare and save the frame the card shows, and refuse a card of another driver before setting a mode on it. | linuxfb stage 2b |

### Vulkan (`src/FerroUI.Vulkan/`, `src/Skia/FerroUI.Skia/gpu/vulkan/`)
