Design and what was verified where: [`vulkan.md`](vulkan.md).

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Reason | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VulkanStructs.cs` and `VulkanEnums.cs` declare the structures, handles and enumerations the project uses; `VulkanGlobalApi`, `VulkanInstanceApi` and `VulkanDeviceApi` are generated tables of function pointers. | The types are those of the `ash` bindings (0.38.0), each under its upstream name as an alias (`unmanaged_interop/vulkan_enums.rs`, `vulkan_structs.rs`); the three tables are the tables of the bindings, filled through the same lookups, with upstream's members as safe functions over typed handles. | Representation | The headers are not written a second time; the values and fields are not counted as members of the port (`path-overrides.toml`). | vulkan |
| A missing entry point is a null delegate, which fails when called. | A missing entry point is a function that stops the program when called. | Behaviour | The form of the tables of the bindings. The optional entry points are not called without their extension, as upstream. | vulkan |
| `VulkanInstance.Create` names the application (unless the options do) and the engine after the framework. | Both names are `FerroUI`. | Behaviour | The name of the port. | vulkan |
| With `UseDebug`, `CreateDebugUtilsMessengerEXT` is called whether or not `VK_EXT_debug_utils` was enabled; without the extension the delegate is null. | The messenger is created only when the extension was enabled. | Behaviour | The call without the extension would stop the program. | vulkan |
| An enumeration (`vkEnumerate...`, `vkGet...` with a count) makes two calls. | The same two calls, repeated when the second answers `VK_INCOMPLETE` (the list grew in between). `vkEnumerateInstanceExtensionProperties` failing at its first call is a failure (upstream reads the result of the second only). | Behaviour | The loop the specification asks for. | vulkan |
| Failures are exceptions (`VulkanException`). | `Result<_, VulkanException>` inside the crate, with upstream's messages; a panic with the same message where a contract cannot report (creating a context, beginning and ending a frame, importing an object). `VulkanPlatformGraphics::try_create` logs and returns `None`, as upstream catches. | Representation | Porting guide: exceptions. | vulkan |
| `IVulkanInstance`, `IVulkanDevice`, the delegates of the options and the platform features (`Dictionary<Type, object>`) are plain objects. | `Send + Sync` and held in `Arc`: the platform graphics, which two threads share, hold them. The platform features map a `TypeId` to an `Arc` of the feature. | Representation | `IPlatformGraphics` is `Send + Sync` (`render-thread.md`). | vulkan |
| The device lock is a monitor with a count and an owner thread. | A mutex and a condition variable with the same count and owner. | Representation | No reentrant lock in the standard library. | vulkan |
| The Skia backend tests `is IVulkanPlatformGraphicsContext`; a surface is tested with `is IVulkanKhrSurfacePlatformSurface`. | The context announces itself through its optional features; a surface through `try_get_surface_kind`. | Representation | Porting guide: `is` on an interface. | vulkan |
| `VulkanContext.Dispose` does nothing. | Disposes the command pool of its external objects feature and lets go of the feature. | Behaviour | The feature holds its context and the context its feature; without a collector the pair would never be freed. | vulkan |
| `ImportSemaphore` leaves the semaphore it created when the import fails. | Destroys it before failing. | Behaviour | | vulkan |
| `VulkanPlatformGraphics.GetSharedContext` stores the context it creates in `_currentSharedContext`, which nothing reads. | The field is not kept. | Representation | | vulkan |
| `Utf8BufferArray` is disposable and has a finalizer. | Owned vectors, freed when dropped. | Representation | | vulkan |
| `System.Version` for the API version of the options. | `ferroui_vulkan::Version` (major, minor, build). | Representation | | vulkan |
| `VulkanGlobalApi` fails to fill its table when the lookup lacks one of its three entry points. | `VulkanGlobalApi::try_new` names the entry point that is missing; `VulkanInstance::create` fails with it. | Representation | The table of the bindings cannot be left unfilled. | vulkan |
| `DeviceCheckSurfaceFactory` returns the surface and throws when it cannot create one. | Returns `Result<u64, VulkanException>`. | Representation | The failure is the failure of the device creation, as upstream. | vulkan |
| The X11 platform declares `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` of `libvulkan.so.1` as a platform invoke: the library is found when the creation of the instance first calls it, and a missing library is the exception `TryCreate` logs. | `VulkanSupport::try_initialize` opens the library first; without it the same message is logged and the mode fails to initialize before the window for the device check is created. | Behaviour | A lookup that cannot find the loader has no entry point to call. | vulkan |
| `X11VulkanInterface` and `VkXlibSurfaceCreateInfoKHR` are declared by the X11 project. | The table and the structure of the bindings (`ash::khr::xlib_surface`). | Representation | | vulkan |
| `AndroidVulkanInterface`, `VkAndroidSurfaceCreateInfoKHR` (Android) and `Win32VulkanInterface`, `VkWin32SurfaceCreateInfoKHR` (Windows) are declared by the platform projects. | The tables and the structures of the bindings (`ash::khr::android_surface`, `ash::khr::win32_surface`). | Representation | As on X11. | vulkan (Android and Windows, 2026-10-15) |
| The Android and Windows platforms declare `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` of `libvulkan.so` and `vulkan-1.dll` as platform invokes, found when the creation of the instance first calls them. | `VulkanSupport::try_initialize` opens the library first; without it the message of the reference is logged and the mode fails to initialise. | Behaviour | As on X11. | vulkan (Android and Windows, 2026-10-15) |
| The surface factories of Android and Windows take a surface that is an `INativePlatformHandleSurface` (Windows: whose descriptor is `HWND`). | They take the handle surface of the platform by its type (`SurfaceViewHandle`, `WindowImplPlatformHandle`; on Windows with the descriptor `HWND` as well). | Representation | A render surface behind the contract cannot be asked for another trait; the handle surfaces of the platforms are the only ones among their surfaces. As on X11. | vulkan (Android and Windows, 2026-10-15) |
| `AndroidVulkanSurface.CreateSurface` passes the address of the native window the surface view holds. | The surface of Vulkan is created while a counted reference to the native window is held (`SurfaceViewHandle::native_window`). | Behaviour | The UI thread may release the window of a destroyed surface while the render thread creates the Vulkan surface; the framebuffer and the EGL surface hold a reference likewise (Android platform, below). | vulkan (Android, 2026-10-15) |
| The Skia backend has the Vulkan GPU on every platform (SkiaSharp carries every back end), so the Vulkan mode of every platform draws. | On Linux always; elsewhere behind the feature `vulkan` of `ferroui-skia`, off by default. The features `vulkan` of `ferroui-android` (the published binary with OpenGL and Vulkan) and of `ferroui-win32` (Skia compiled from source) turn it on, and only with them does the Vulkan mode of those platforms initialise; without them a list that names the mode passes it over with a warning that names the feature. | Behaviour | The published Skia binaries (`vulkan.md`, section 2): none with Ganesh on OpenGL and Vulkan for Windows, so the default build of Windows cannot have it without a source build of Skia; on Android the default build keeps the binary it was verified with. A mode that initialises and that the renderer cannot draw with would leave the view empty. | vulkan (Android and Windows, 2026-10-15) |
| `VulkanDisplay.StartPresentation` recreates the swap chain when `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` answers `VK_SUBOPTIMAL_KHR` and acquires again; `EndPresentation` throws for any result of `vkQueuePresentKHR` but `VK_SUCCESS`, `VK_SUBOPTIMAL_KHR` and `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DATE_KHR` among them. | `VK_SUBOPTIMAL_KHR` is a success at both (the acquired image is used, the frame was presented); `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DATE_KHR` at present drops the frame without a failure. Either marks the swap chain (`SwapchainState`); before the next frame an out-of-date swap chain is recreated, and a suboptimal one only when the capabilities of the surface now give other parameters than it was created with. Out of date at acquire and surface lost are handled as upstream. | Behaviour | An upstream defect, fixed under the owner's policy of 2026-10-15. The specification makes `VK_SUBOPTIMAL_KHR` a success code (the image was acquired or presented). Upstream keeps the identity transform for a surface rotated by a quarter turn (the compositor rotates), and Android then answers every present with `VK_SUBOPTIMAL_KHR` while the screen is rotated: each frame in landscape was a failure of the render session, which the compositor caught and logged (21 in two seconds on the emulator, `android-platform.md` 10.5). Recreating on every suboptimal result would recreate the swap chain for each of those frames, because the new one keeps the identity transform too; hence the comparison of the parameters. Acquiring again after a suboptimal acquire also reused a semaphore with a signal pending. Taking the current transform as the pre-transform instead was not done: the frame is copied into the swap chain image by `vkCmdBlitImage`, which cannot turn it by a quarter, so the renderer would have to render rotated (`vulkan.md`, section 6). | vulkan (2026-10-15) |
| `VulkanSkiaGpu` gives Skia no API version (`GRVkBackendContext.MaxAPIVersion` stays 0). | Gives Skia the version 1.1. | Behaviour | The Skia of the bindings takes the version of the loader when it is given none and then asks for the entry points of that version; Mesa hands out the entry points of the version the instance was created for only, so the context was refused on lavapipe (CI, 2026-10-11). 1.1 is the default of `VulkanInstanceCreationOptions` and the least this Skia accepts; an instance created for a later version is used as 1.1. | vulkan |
| `VulkanSkiaGpu` passes the formats, layouts and tilings of an image to Skia as numbers. | The numbers are mapped to the enumerations of the Skia bindings for the values the framework produces (two formats, the layouts of Vulkan 1.0 and of a presented image, two tilings); any other value is a panic that names it. | Behaviour | The enumerations of the bindings are Rust enumerations, which cannot hold a value they do not declare. | vulkan |

### iOS platform (`src/iOS/FerroUI.iOS/`)

The design, the binding choice and what each stage builds are in `ios-platform.md`. The systematic difference of this backend is how its classes are declared: upstream's are managed classes exported to the Objective-C runtime by attributes, with their state in fields; the port declares each class with `define_class!` of the `objc2` bindings and keeps its state in the instance variables of the object, and where a class has logic that can be tested without UIKit the class forwards to a plain Rust type that holds it (`ViewControllerState`, `AppDelegateEvents`, the functions of `input_handler.rs` and `ios_screens.rs`).

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AvaloniaAppDelegate<TApp>` is a generic class an application derives from, overriding `CreateAppBuilder` and `CustomizeAppBuilder`; the sample's `Main` calls `UIApplication.Main` with its type. | One class of the runtime, `FerroAppDelegate`, and the trait `FerroApplicationDelegate` with the two members; `run_application::<App>()` or `run_application_with(delegate)` is the `main` of an application and calls `UIApplicationMain`. | Representation | A class of the Objective-C runtime can be neither generic nor derived from in Rust, and UIKit creates the delegate object from the name of its class. | ios-platform |
| Branches for systems before iOS 13 and 13.4: a window created by the application delegate where there are no scenes; no modifier keys and no button mask on touch events. | Not ported: the later branch everywhere. The platform needs iOS 13.4. | Scope | The published Skia binaries and the Rust simulator target need a later system than either. | ios-platform |
| tvOS and Mac Catalyst are targets of the project (`#if TVOS`, `#if MACCATALYST`, the swipe gestures of a remote). | Not targets: those branches are left out. | Scope | Neither is a target of the port yet. | ios-platform |
| `DisplayLinkTimer` creates the display link on the thread that registers the platform and adds it to the run loop of the timer thread; the link is paused while the render loop is idle or the application is in the background, by setting `CADisplayLink.Paused` under the timer's lock from whichever thread changed the state (upstream ab7126382c, #22395). | The link is created on the timer thread and stays an object of it. The state (the tick, the background, whether paused) is changed under the timer's lock by any thread as upstream; the link itself is paused and resumed on the timer thread, by a block given to the run loop of that thread (`CFRunLoopPerformBlock`, `CFRunLoopWakeUp`). Each tick runs in an autorelease pool. | Thread-safety | The port does not share an object of Core Animation between threads on the strength of upstream's statement alone; a `CFRunLoopRef` is documented as thread-safe, so the change reaches the link through it. The link pauses a moment later than upstream's, and a tick that arrives meanwhile is dropped as upstream drops one already scheduled. | ios-platform, upstream sync 66b7273 |
| `MetalRenderTarget.PendingLayout` is a property the view sets from the UI thread (`SetRenderTarget`, `LayoutSubviews`) on an object the render thread uses. | The size and the scaling of the last layout are in a state the view shares with its surface, under a lock; the render target reads it. Before the first layout it is one pixel at scaling 1 (upstream: what the view had, an empty size). | Thread-safety | The render target is an object of the thread that renders. | ios-platform |
| `MetalDevice.TryGetFeature` returns nothing. | The device announces itself as `IMetalDevice`. | Representation | How the render backends of the port find the Metal contract of a graphics context (as the macOS backend). | ios-platform |
| `TopLevelImpl.Handle` is created with the top-level. | Created when asked for. | Ownership | A handle retains its view, and the view owns its top-level: kept, the two would never be released. | ios-platform |
| No counterpart. | `FerroView::frames_presented`, `frames_begun`, `pending_layout`, `capture_next_frame` (the pixels of a frame, copied from the drawable's texture before it is presented) and `DisplayLinkTimer::ticks`, `has_tick`. | Addition | What the smoke mode of the example checks itself with; an application cannot observe the screen otherwise. | ios-platform |
| Branches for systems before iOS 14: the preferred tint colour is read only where `OperatingSystem.IsIOSVersionAtLeast(14)`. | The tint colour is read on every system; the platform needs iOS 14.0. | Scope | The constant of UIKit is linked by name, and a binary that names it does not load on an earlier system; 14.0 is also the minimum of the published Skia binary for the simulator. Supersedes the 13.4 of the row above. | ios-platform-2 |
| `AvaloniaView.PressesChanged` passes a press that was not handled to `base.PressesBegan`. | To the `pressesChanged:withEvent:` of the superclass. | Behaviour | A changed press would reach the responders behind the view as a second beginning of the same press. | ios-platform-2 |
| The view finds the settings of the platform in the locator and casts them to `PlatformSettings` (`traitCollectionDidChange`, `tintColorDidChange`). | `Platform::settings()` keeps the object the platform binds, and the view asks it. | Representation | `IPlatformSettings` of the port has no cast to the implementing type. | ios-platform-2 |
| `StartInertiaScrolling` overwrites `_momentumDisplayLink` with a new link. | A link that is still in the field is invalidated first. | Ownership | A display link retains its target, here the view: a link that was overwritten while running would tick on and keep the view. | ios-platform-2 |
| The delegate of the display link of inertia scrolling and the action of the scroll gesture are methods of the input handler. | Two methods of the class of the view (`ferroUpdateInertiaScrolling:`, `ferroHandleScrollWheel:`) that forward to the input handler. | Representation | A target of a display link or of a gesture recognizer is an object of the Objective-C runtime; the input handler is not one. | ios-platform-2 |
| The timestamp of a wheel event is `Environment.TickCount64`. | The uptime of the system in milliseconds (`NSProcessInfo.systemUptime`). | Representation | The same clock as the timestamps of the events of UIKit, which the other events of the handler carry. | ios-platform-2 |
| `IOSPlatformFeedback` plays the sound with a `SystemSound` object of the AudioToolbox bindings. | `AudioServicesPlaySystemSound`, declared in the file. | Representation | The function the binding calls; no crate for one function. | ios-platform-2 |
| Tasks completed by the completion handlers of UIKit (`OpenUrlAsync`, the pickers). | `completion.rs`: a value set once on the main thread and the future that waits for it. | Representation | The contracts of the port return futures that are polled on the dispatcher thread. | ios-platform-2 |
| `AvaloniaView` implements `ITextInputMethodImpl` itself, and its top-level returns the view for that feature. | An object of the top-level (`ViewTextInputMethod`) implements the contract and forwards to the view, which keeps the client, the cursor rectangle and the options. | Representation | A feature of the port is an `Rc` of a trait object; a view is an object of the Objective-C runtime. | ios-platform-2 |
| The classes of positions and ranges are nested in `TextInputResponder`, which is nested in the view: `AvaloniaTextPosition`, `AvaloniaEmptyTextPosition`, `AvaloniaTextRange`. | Classes of `text_input_responder.rs`: `FerroTextPosition`, `FerroEmptyTextPosition`, `FerroTextRange`, and the responder as the class `FerroTextInputResponder`. | Representation | Names of the Objective-C runtime are process-wide and none has the upstream name. | ios-platform-2 |
| `TextInRange` with marked text slices the surrounding text with the selection of the client as it is (`AsSpan(0, Start)`, `AsSpan(Start, End - Start)`), which throws for a selection outside of the text. | The selection is brought into the text first. | Behaviour | A failure cannot leave a method UIKit called: it would end the process. | ios-platform-2 |
| `WeakInputDelegate` is a property that holds the delegate UIKit sets. | Held weakly. | Ownership | `inputDelegate` is declared weak by `UITextInput`; the delegate is an object of UIKit that outlives nothing of the port. | ios-platform-2 |
| The responder notifies its delegate with `objc_msgSend` and selector handles. | The methods of the `UITextInputDelegate` binding. | Representation | The same four messages, type checked. | ios-platform-2 |
| `TextInputContextIdentifier` is a new GUID. | The string of a new `NSUUID`. | Representation | The same kind of value from the system. | ios-platform-2 |
| `CombinedSpan3<T>` is a `ref struct` over three spans. | A struct over three slices with the same `copy_to`. | Representation | Slices are the spans of Rust. | ios-platform-2 |
| No counterpart. | `FerroView::is_driving_text`, `text_input_responder`, `marked_text`. | Addition | What the smoke mode of the example talks to the text input responder through; the first responder of an application is not public in UIKit. | ios-platform-2 |
| The application prefix of the clipboard formats of an application is `net.avaloniaui.app.uti.`. | `net.ferroui.app.uti.`, as on macOS. | Naming | The upstream name does not appear in the port; an application of the port and one of upstream do not exchange application formats. | ios-platform-2 |
| `ClipboardImpl` and the item wrapper choose between a string and bytes by the value type of the format (`DataFormat<string>`, `DataFormat<byte[]>`). | Writing: the value the item holds decides (a `String` or bytes). Reading: the type of the pasteboard decides (text by its uniform type, as when the format was made of it). | Representation | A format of the port does not carry the type of its values (the same as the macOS and X11 backends). | ios-platform-2 |
| The uniform types come from the `UniformTypeIdentifiers` binding (`UTType.CreateFromExtension`, `UTTypes.Folder`, `ConformsTo`), with the legacy `MobileCoreServices` types before iOS 14. | The class `UTType` is found by name and sent the same messages, and the constants are declared in `ios_storage_provider.rs`; no legacy branch. | Representation | One class and six constants; the bindings crate of the framework would be a new package. The port needs iOS 14. | ios-platform-2 |
| `IOSStorageItem` is an abstract class with the subclasses `IOSStorageFile` and `IOSStorageFolder`. | `IosStorageItem` holds what both have; `IosStorageFile` and `IosStorageFolder` each hold one and implement the contracts. | Representation | No inheritance; the members of the contracts forward. | ios-platform-2 |
| `GetItemsAsync` completes a task from the accessor of the coordinated read and awaits it. | The accessor fills a cell that is read when the coordinated read returns. | Representation | The accessor is called before `coordinateReadingItemAtURL` returns. | ios-platform-2 |
| `TryGetWellKnownFolderAsync`, `OpenFileBookmarkAsync` and `OpenFolderBookmarkAsync` throw the error of Foundation (`NSErrorException`); `MoveAsync` throws for a destination of another provider. | The first three log the error and answer with no item; `move_async` answers with an error. | Behaviour | The contracts of the port return an option, or a result, and a failure cannot be raised through a future otherwise. | ios-platform-2 |
| The delegates of a picker are objects the picker's `Delegate` property keeps alive. | They live with the future that waits for the choice (`ShownPicker`). | Ownership | UIKit refers to its delegates weakly, and nothing of the port holds a managed wrapper. | ios-platform-2 |
| `UIPresentationControllerDelegate.Dispose` calls the dismiss handler. | Not ported. | Missing | The class of the port has no disposal; a dismissal is reported by `presentationControllerDidDismiss:`, and the result of a picker is set once. | ios-platform-2 |
| The scene delegate casts the delegate of the application to `IAvaloniaAppInternalDelegate`. | It looks for the class `FerroAppDelegate` and calls the two members (`IFerroAppInternalDelegate`), whose logic is in `AppDelegateEvents` (`open_url`, `continue_user_activity`). | Representation | An interface of the port is not visible through an object of the Objective-C runtime; the logic is plain so that the host tests reach it. | ios-platform-2 |
| `NativeControlAttachment` wraps the handle of a control that is not a `UIViewControlHandle` in a `ViewHolder`, a managed `UIView` over the native pointer. | The pointer of a handle whose descriptor is `UIView` is retained as a `UIView`; a handle with another descriptor is refused with a panic. | Representation | There are no managed wrappers; the descriptor is what says that the pointer is a view, and a pointer of anything else must not be used as one. | ios-platform-2 |
| `UIViewControlHandle.Destroy` and the disposal of an attachment dispose the managed wrapper of the view. | `destroy` removes the view from its superview; the reference to the view is released with the handle or the attachment. | Ownership | Reference counting in place of a wrapper with a disposal. | ios-platform-2 |
| `CreateNewAttachment(Func)` disposes the attachment when attaching it throws. | No such path. | Behaviour | Adding a subview does not fail with an error. | ios-platform-2 |
| `EmbedSampleIOS` (the catalog): a `UIButton` with a managed handler as its target; a `WKWebView` of the WebKit bindings. | A subclass of `UIButton` that is its own target and keeps the count (`FerroEmbedSampleButton`); the web view is created through the runtime by the name of its class, and the default control stands in if the class is not there. | Representation | A target is an object of the Objective-C runtime; WebKit has no bindings among the dependencies of the workspace. | ios-platform-2 |
| `AutomationPeerWrapper` is a `UIAccessibilityElement` with its state in fields, and sets the properties of the element (`AccessibilityLabel`, `AccessibilityFrame`, ...) on itself. | `AutomationPeerWrapper` is a plain type that keeps the values; the class `FerroAutomationPeerElement` holds its wrapper weakly and overrides the getters of the accessibility protocols to answer from it. The object of UIKit is made when UIKit first asks for the element. | Representation | The logic runs in the host tests without UIKit; a wrapper owns its object, never the other way, so the two are released. What assistive technology reads is the same. | mobile-accessibility |
| `GetAccessibilityElementAt` indexes the list and fails for an index outside of it; `AccessibilityActivate`, `AccessibilityIncrement` and `AccessibilityDecrement` let the exception of a provider whose element is not enabled leave the method; `AccessibilityScroll` catches the invalid operation only. | No element for an index outside of the list; a provider that refuses makes the activation answer false, an increment or decrement do nothing and a scroll answer false. | Behaviour | A failure cannot leave a method UIKit called: it would end the process. | mobile-accessibility |
| No counterpart. | The module `automation_peer_wrapper` is public with the three value types of the accessibility protocols (`AccessibilityTraits`, `AccessibilityContainerType`, `AccessibilityScrollDirection`) and `NOT_FOUND`; the wrapper itself is internal, as upstream. | Addition | What the smoke mode of the example compares the answers of UIKit with. | mobile-accessibility |
| `EaglPlatformGraphics` creates its `GlContext` in the constructor, on the main thread, and the compositor uses it on the thread of the display link. | The constructor creates a context and releases it (so `try_create` fails where upstream fails); the context of the graphics is created at the first `get_shared_context`, which the context manager of the compositor makes on the thread that renders, and is bound to that thread (`ThreadBound`). | Representation | The context is an `Rc` with an interface of OpenGL ES that is one too; created on one thread and used on another it would need an argument about every handle to it. Nothing of the EAGL context reaches the main thread. | 2026-10-15 (stage 4) |
| `EaglLayerSurface` reads the graphics and the timer from the static `Platform` on the thread that renders. | The view hands the surface the graphics, the timer and the state it shares with the view. | Representation | The state of the platform of the port belongs to the main thread. | 2026-10-15 (stage 4) |
| `AvaloniaView.SetRenderTarget` hands the Metal render target the view's pending layout. | No counterpart (waived): the view writes its layout into the state it shares with its surface in `layoutSubviews`, which every render target reads. | Representation | The render target is created on the render thread, where the view cannot be reached. | 2026-10-15 |
| No counterpart. | `Platform::rendering_mode()`; the frame count and the capture of a frame (`glReadPixels` before the renderbuffer is presented) in the OpenGL ES mode as in the Metal mode; `FERROUI_IOS_RENDERING_MODE` in the example and the catalog host; `FromStr` for `IosRenderingMode`. | Addition | What the smoke runs check the OpenGL ES mode with; upstream's sample has no switch for the mode. | 2026-10-15 (stage 4) |
| SkiaSharp draws both modes, Metal and OpenGL ES, in one build. | The OpenGL ES mode draws only with the Skia backend built with `--cfg ferro_skia_apple_ganesh` (Ganesh on OpenGL ES and on Metal); the default build is Graphite on Metal and refuses the context of EAGL at the first frame. | Behaviour | No published Skia binary has Ganesh on OpenGL next to Graphite (`apple-opengl.md`, section 2). | 2026-10-15 (stage 4) |

### Android platform (`src/Android/FerroUI.Android/`)

The design, the choice of bindings and what each stage builds are in `android-platform.md`. The systematic difference of this backend is where the classes live: upstream derives from classes of the Android framework in managed code; here each such class is a small Java class of the crate (`java/org/ferroui/android`) that forwards to a native method, and a Rust type that holds the logic of the upstream class.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The classes that derive from the framework: `AvaloniaAndroidApplication<TApp>`, `AvaloniaActivity`, `AvaloniaMainActivity`, `AvaloniaView`, `InvalidationAwareSurfaceView` with `TopLevelImpl.SurfaceViewImpl`, and the handler, runnables and idle handler of `AndroidDispatcherImpl`. | A Java class each (`FerroApplication`, `FerroActivity`, `FerroMainActivity`, `FerroView`, `FerroSurfaceView`, `MainLooperBridge`) that holds the number of its Rust object and calls a registered native method for every override; `PlatformHelper` for calls into the framework that return several values or differ between versions of the system. | Representation | A Rust library cannot derive from a Java class. No address of a Rust object is given to Java: a native method finds its object by the number, on the UI thread. | android-platform |
| Names of the project: the namespace `Avalonia.Android`, the class names with the prefix `Avalonia`. | The package `org.ferroui.android`, the prefix `Ferro`; the meta-data key `org.ferroui.android.library` names the native library; the log tag is `ferroui`. | Behaviour | The upstream name does not appear in the port. | android-platform |
| An application derives its application class from `AvaloniaAndroidApplication<TApp>` and overrides `CreateAppBuilder` and `CustomizeAppBuilder`; its main activity derives from `AvaloniaMainActivity`. | `ferroui_android::android_application!(build)` with a function that returns the builder; the manifest names `FerroApplication` and `FerroMainActivity` (or Java classes of the application derived from them). `FerroActivity::current_main_activity`, `finish`, `files_dir` and `intent_string_extra` give an application written in Rust what it has upstream by being the activity class. | Addition | The application is a shared library with one exported symbol (`JNI_OnLoad`), which registers the native methods. | android-platform |
| AndroidX: `AppCompatActivity`, `ViewCompat`, `WindowCompat`, `WindowInsetsCompat`, `WindowInsetsControllerCompat`, `WindowMetricsCalculator`. | The classes of the platform: `android.app.Activity`, `WindowInsets`, `WindowInsetsController`, `WindowManager.getMaximumWindowMetrics`; below API 30 the deprecated calls the compatibility classes make themselves. | Behaviour | No Gradle and no Maven artifacts: the Java layer is compiled against `android.jar` alone. Consequences: `SetFrameThemeVariant` sets the system bar theme and no local night mode (an activity of the platform has none); below API 30 the visibility of the input method is estimated from the system window insets. | android-platform |
| The managed runtime has thread-static fields without a limit. | On Android the thread-local variables of the framework are slots of one table per thread behind one key of the C library (`ferro_thread_local!`), because the standard library distributed for the Android targets keeps each `thread_local!` under a key of its own and bionic has 128 for a process; on every other target the macro is `thread_local!`. Values are created at their first use on a thread and destroyed in the reverse order of their creation when it ends, as with `thread_local!`. | Representation | Nothing observable differs. The library is built with stable Rust; before 2026-10-15 it was built with a nightly toolchain and a rebuilt standard library. `android-platform.md`, section 5.1. | android-platform; 2026-10-15 |
| `minSdkVersion` 21. | 26. | Behaviour | The published Skia binaries for Android are built for API 26. | android-platform |
| `AndroidMotionEventsHelper` reads the `MotionEvent`, and the history of a move lazily. | The Java view copies what the helper reads into primitive arguments and two arrays, the history included, and the helper translates the copy (`MotionEventData`). | Representation | A motion event is a Java object that is recycled after the dispatch; one native call per event. The history is copied only for a move. | android-platform |
| `InvalidationAwareSurfaceView` keeps the handle of the native window in a field and releases the window in `SurfaceDestroyed`; the framebuffer and the EGL surface read the handle. | The native window is a counted reference under a lock (`SurfaceShared`); the framebuffer of a frame and the surface information of EGL hold a reference of their own while they use the window, and the EGL information remembers the last window, size and scaling. | Behaviour | The surface is destroyed on the UI thread while the render thread may be in a frame; upstream's frame would then use a released window. | android-platform |
| `FramebufferManager.Lock` throws when the surface has no native window. | The frame is drawn into a framebuffer of one pixel and dropped, with a warning; the three surfaces of a top-level report that they are not ready while there is no window. | Behaviour | The exception would be raised on the render thread, for a surface that was destroyed between scheduling and drawing a frame. | android-platform |
| `EglPlatformGraphics.TryCreate()` of the OpenGL project: one display, loaded from `libEGL.so` by name, whose contexts are created on any thread. | `android_egl.rs`: the loader of the EGL library of the system, and platform graphics that hold nothing of a thread: a context creates the display object it belongs to on the thread that asks for it. | Representation | The EGL objects of the port belong to one thread and its platform graphics are bound to the thread that made them; the compositor renders on the render thread. The display of the system is one per process and counted. | android-platform |
| `ChoreographerTimer` posts with `AChoreographer_postFrameCallback64` on API 29 and with the 32-bit-typed `AChoreographer_postFrameCallback` below. | The 64-bit function is looked up at run time (`dlsym`); the older function is declared with `long`, which is what its C declaration has. | Behaviour | The library is linked against API 26, which does not have the newer function. | android-platform |
| `AndroidScreens` reads the display objects; the working area is the bounds less the system bars of an empty `WindowInsetsCompat`. | `PlatformHelper.getDisplays` describes every display in one call; the working area is the bounds (the insets upstream subtracts are always zero). | Representation | One call instead of a dozen per display. | android-platform |
| `AndroidActivatableLifetime.CurrentIntendActivity` keeps the last activity that was created. | It is cleared when that activity is destroyed. | Behaviour | The lifetime would keep a destroyed activity and its views alive. | android-platform |
| `AvaloniaView.Content` and `IAvaloniaActivity.Content` are compared by reference. | Two contents are the same when they hold the same control, or are the same boxed value. | Representation | A control is boxed anew each time it is passed as an untyped value. | android-platform |
| `AvaloniaMainActivity` derives from `AvaloniaActivity` and overrides three members. | The activity has a kind; `FerroMainActivity` (Rust) has the three overrides as functions the activity calls. | Representation | No inheritance, as `PopupImpl` of the Windows backend. | android-platform |
| A managed exception in a callback of the framework crosses into Java and ends the application. | A panic in a native method is caught, written to the log of the system under the tag `ferroui`, and thrown as `java.lang.RuntimeException`. | Representation | A panic must not unwind into Java frames. | android-platform |
| `ClipDataItemToDataTransferItemWrapper.TryGetRawCore` and `ClipboardImpl.TryCreateDataItemAsync` ask a format whether it is `DataFormat<string>` or `DataFormat<byte[]>`. | Reading: a format whose MIME type is `text/*` has text; any other has the bytes of the file the item names and, when it names none, its text. Writing: a value that is a string is text. | Representation | A format does not carry the type of its values at run time in the port. | android-platform 2d |
| The application prefix of clipboard formats is `application/avn-fmt.`. | `application/frn-fmt.`, as in the other backends of the port. | Naming | | android-platform 2d |
| `AndroidNativeControlAttachment` takes the view of an `AndroidViewControlHandle`, or the object behind the raw handle of any other handle. | The handle must be an `AndroidViewControlHandle`; another one is a panic that says so. | Behaviour | A number cannot be turned into a reference to a Java object safely. | android-platform 2d |
| `button.Click += ...` of the managed binding (the embed sample). | `interop::listeners::set_on_click_listener` with the Java class `NativeClickListener`; a handler lives as long as the process. | Representation | A listener of the system is a Java object; a closure needs a class that calls back. | android-platform 2d |
| `AndroidLauncher.LaunchUriAsync` catches `ActivityNotFoundException` and `FileUriExposedException` around `StartActivity`. | `PlatformHelper.tryStartActivity` catches them in Java. | Representation | An exception of a Java call is a panic in `interop/java.rs`. | android-platform 2d |
| `AndroidStorageItem` and `AndroidStorageProvider` use `DocumentFile` (AndroidX) and catch exceptions of the content resolver. | `StorageHelper` (Java): `DocumentsContract` and queries of the content resolver, each call catching its exception and keeping the text; with an error channel (create, delete, list, open a stream) the text comes back as an `io::Error`, without one (`GetFolderAsync`, `GetFileAsync`, the properties of a file, the bookmarks) it is logged and the member answers nothing, where upstream lets the exception reach the caller. | Behaviour | No AndroidX; no exception across JNI. | android-platform 2d |
| The streams of a storage file are Java streams of the content resolver. | A `std::fs::File` over the detached file descriptor of `openAssetFileDescriptor` (which is what `openInputStream` and `openOutputStream` use), limited to the offset and length of the descriptor; virtual files through `openTypedAssetFileDescriptor`. | Representation | Reading and writing without a JNI call per buffer. | android-platform 2d |
| `IStorageFolder.GetItemsAsync` enumerates lazily. | A list. | Representation | The children are one query. | android-platform 2d |
| `PlatformSupport` awaits a `TaskCompletionSource` and adds and removes handlers of the result delegates of the activity. | `Completion<T>` and a chain of handlers on the one handler slot of `IActivityResultHandler`; a handler that is removed while a later one is installed stays as a pass-through. | Representation | The contract has a slot, not a multicast delegate. | android-platform 2d |
| `AndroidKeyboardEventsHelper` reads the `KeyEvent`. | The Java view copies what the helper reads (`KeyEventData`), as for motion events. | Representation | The event is an object of the virtual machine; the translation is a function of values that the host tests run. | android-platform 2a |
| `AndroidKeyboardDevice` derives from the keyboard device of the framework and adds the key table. | `AndroidKeyboardDevice::new` gives the keyboard device of the framework; the table is an associated function. | Representation | The class adds no state. | android-platform 2a |
| `AndroidPlatformSettings` reads the context inline; its receiver is a nested class registered through `ContextCompat`. | The context is behind `ISettingsContext`, answered by `PlatformHelper` in two calls; the receiver is the Java class `ConfigurationChangedReceiver`, registered with the flag "not exported" from API 33 and without a flag below. | Representation | Values that tests can give; no AndroidX. | android-platform 2a |
| `AvaloniaInputConnection` implements every member of `IInputConnection` in managed code. | `FerroInputConnection.java` forwards the 18 members with behaviour to the Rust object and answers the seven that are `false` for every argument, and the null handler, itself. | Representation | A native method per constant would be code without content. | android-platform 2b |
| `AvaloniaInputConnection` guards its batch level and its queue for other threads (`Interlocked`, `ConcurrentQueue`). | Cells of the UI thread. | Representation | The connection has no handler of its own, so the system calls it on the main thread. | android-platform 2b |
| `DeleteRegionInCodePointsCommand` indexes the text one unit past the selection without looking at its length, and fails with an exception when that is past the end. | A position outside the text is no surrogate pair. | Behaviour | An exception in the input connection ends the application; the case is an ordinary one (deleting forward one unit before the end). | android-platform 2b |
| `AvaloniaInputConnection.GetTextBeforeCursorFormatted` subtracts the count from the selection start without looking for an overflow. | A saturating subtraction. | Behaviour | An input method may ask for "everything". | android-platform 2b |
| `TextEditBuffer`, `EditCommand` and the connection hold the input method and the top-level. | They hold them weakly, behind `IAndroidInputMethod` and `IInputConnectionTopLevel`, with the calls upstream makes on the view and the input method manager as members of the trait. | Representation | No reference cycle; tests without a view. | android-platform 2b |
| `AvaloniaView` keeps the function that initialises the editor info (`IInitEditorInfo`). | The host object of the input method keeps it, owned by the top-level. | Representation | The top-level and its input method exist before the object of the view does. | android-platform 2b |
| `BackPressedCallback` (AndroidX) disables itself and asks the dispatcher again when nobody handled the back request. | The back callback of the window of the platform; the answer of the Rust object tells the Java activity to call `super.onBackPressed()`. | Behaviour | No AndroidX; the default action of the activity is what the dispatcher falls back to. | android-platform 2c |
| `IActivityResultHandler.ActivityResult` carries a `Result` and an `Intent`, `RequestPermissionsResult` an array of `Permission`. | The result code and the grant results as numbers (with the constants of the system), the intent as a Java object. | Representation | | android-platform 2c |
| `TopLevelImpl.SetFrameThemeVariant` sets the local night mode of the AppCompat activity. | The system bar theme only. | Missing | An activity of the platform has no night mode of its own (`android-platform.md`, 3.3). | open |
| `AvaloniaAccessHelper` derives from `ExploreByTouchHelper` of AndroidX and is set with `ViewCompat.SetAccessibilityDelegate`. | The Java class `FerroAccessHelper`, a `View.AccessibilityDelegate` with an `AccessibilityNodeProvider` of the platform, and `explore_by_touch_helper.rs` with the state and rules of the library class (the focus of virtual views, hover, the node defaults and checks, the events); `android-platform.md`, section 7.3. | Behaviour | No AndroidX. What differs is listed in the next row. | mobile-accessibility |
| `ExploreByTouchHelper` moves the keyboard focus between virtual views with the direction keys and the tab key, and into them when the host view gains the focus from a direction (`FocusStrategy`). | Not built: the helper clicks the keyboard-focused virtual view on enter and the centre of the direction pad, clears the keyboard focus of a virtual view when the focus of the host changes, and takes no other key. "No modifiers" of a key is "neither control nor shift", the two modifiers a key event is copied with. | Missing | Deliberately left out with the library; the keyboard focus of the framework moves between its own elements. | mobile-accessibility |
| The node info providers fill an `AccessibilityNodeInfoCompat`, and `PerformNodeAction` takes the `Bundle` of the system. | They fill `NodeInfo`, plain data, which `write_node_info` writes to the node of the system with the members of the platform (the unique id from API 33, "screen reader focusable" from API 28, the three-state checked from API 36 and the boolean one below; "labelled by" is `setLabeledBy`); the arguments of an action are `NodeActionArguments` with the text of the set text action, read from the bundle in Java. | Representation | The providers and the helper run in the host tests; one call into Java per value in one place. | mobile-accessibility |
| `NodeInfoProvider<T>` is an abstract class with the virtual `PeerPropertyChanged`; a provider throws `InvalidOperationException` when its peer has not the contract; `TryPerformNodeAction` catches three exception types; `OnPopulateNodeForVirtualView` lets a provider's exception leave. | A provider holds a `NodeInfoProvider<T>` and implements `INodeInfoProvider`; the override is a function given to the constructor; the members return `Result` with `NodeActionError`, which an action answers with "not performed" and a node passes over. The handler on the peer is removed with the provider (upstream never removes it). | Representation | No inheritance; an exception cannot leave a method the system called. | mobile-accessibility |
| `GetVirtualViewAt` indexes `_peerIds` with the focused peer and fails when the peer has no virtual view. | No virtual view. | Behaviour | As above. | mobile-accessibility |
| The helper's maps are keyed by peer; the three handlers of a registration are removed by the handler of the detachment. | Keyed by the address of the peer, which the map of virtual views keeps alive; a registration holds its handlers and disposes them when it is removed. | Representation | | mobile-accessibility |
| No counterpart. | `FerroView::accessibility_virtual_view_id(control)`. | Addition | What the smoke application asks the node provider of its view with: the number of a virtual view is not public in the platform. | mobile-accessibility |
| `AndroidRenderingMode.Vulkan` (`VulkanSupport`). | Built; initialises only with the feature `vulkan` of `ferroui-android` (section "Vulkan"). | Behaviour | | vulkan (2026-10-15) |

### Diagnostics (`src/FerroUI.Base/diagnostics/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Diagnostic` (`Diagnostic.cs`, `Diagnostic.Activities.cs`, `Diagnostic.Metrics.cs`) is the instrumentation of the framework: activities and histograms around measuring and arranging a layoutable, the layout passes, raising a routed event, finding a resource, attaching and evaluating a style, hit testing, and the compositor's update and render passes, switched on by an `AppContext` switch. Layout, routed events, styling, the renderer and the compositor call it. | Nothing: the calls are left out at every one of those sites. The three counters the facade reads (`Interactive::total_handlers_count`, `Visual::rooted_visual_children_count`, `DispatcherTimer::active_timers_count`) are ported, and the meter and tag names are in `diagnostics/diagnostic_consts.rs`. | Missing | The facade is built on `System.Diagnostics.ActivitySource` and `System.Diagnostics.Metrics`, which have no counterpart the port depends on, and its call sites are the hot paths of layout, styling and rendering. Porting it means choosing an instrumentation interface for the port (and showing that it costs nothing when switched off); that is a decision of its own, not a gap of the core port. The tracking data lists the three files as not applicable for that reason. | #66 |
| No counterpart: upstream measures with the profilers and event counters of its runtime. | `diagnostics::perf_counters` (`perf_counters.rs`) and the feature `perf-counters` of `ferroui-base` (forwarded by `ferroui-controls`, `ferroui-markup-xaml` and `control-catalog`): per-thread counters of property changes raised and listened to, virtual calls by member, inheritance ancestor changes, styles and control themes evaluated and matched, resource lookups, bindings and binding expressions created, values published, text layouts created, text run cache hits and misses, containers recycled. The counting sites are the `perf_count!` and `perf_count_virtual!` invocations in the ported functions (the table of `docs/porting/performance/designs/09-measurement.md` lists them); `ferro_class!` counts the virtual calls. Each site is one added statement and changes no logic. Off by default: without the feature the macros expand to no code, the snapshot type has no size (constant assertions check both) and no type has a field for the counters in either configuration. | Addition | The performance designs start by counting the work per recycled row, and the port has no managed profiler to ask (`docs/porting/performance/designs/09-measurement.md`, item 2). | Performance design 09 |

### Markup metadata and markup events (`src/FerroUI.Base/markup_types/`, `src/FerroUI.Controls/markup_types/`, `src/Markup/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `XamlIlRuntimeHelpers.DeferredTransformationFactoryV3` captures the resource nodes above the declaration, the root object and the name scope, and the deferred content holds all three as references. | The deferred content holds them weakly (`WeakValue` for the nodes and the root, `Weak<dyn INameScope>` for the scope; `DeferredContent`, `xaml_il/runtime/xaml_il_runtime_helpers.rs`). An instantiation is given the ones that are alive: the nodes that are gone are left out of the parent stack, a root that is gone is no root, a scope that is gone gives the instantiation a scope without a parent. Instantiations that overlap share one stack of the alive nodes. | Representation | The content belongs to a template or a deferred resource declared under those objects, which own it; held strongly the content and they keep each other alive, which the collector resolves upstream and nothing resolves here: an element with a template declared under it was never freed (catalog gap C317: the home page of the catalog, every page with a data template). Differs from upstream only for a template that outlives what it was declared under: its static resources and the names of its document are then looked up without the objects that are gone. | 2026-10-09 |
| `DynamicResourceExpression` holds its resource host (`_host`) and its anchor (the element or host above the place the resource is used) as references. | The host and an element or host anchor are held weakly (`WeakResourceHost`, `WeakRef<StyledElement>`; `data/dynamic_resource_expression.rs`); a resource provider anchor stays held. | Representation | An element owns the expressions of its values, and the host of a dynamic resource is most often that element: held strongly the two keep each other alive, which the collector resolves upstream and nothing resolves here. Every control whose theme sets a property from a dynamic resource stayed alive after it left the tree (catalog gap C314: popped pages and removed tabs). | 2026-10-09 |
| `DynamicResourceExtension` holds its anchor (`_anchor`, the first element, resource provider or host above the place it is declared) as a reference. | The extension keeps the anchor as its expressions keep it (`HeldAnchor`): an element or a host weakly, a resource provider as before (`markup_extensions/dynamic_resource_extension.rs`). | Representation | The extension that is the value of a setter is anchored to the element the style is declared under, which owns the style, the setter and the extension: held strongly the element stayed alive (catalog gap C325: the text box of the `CalendarDatePicker` of the Fluent theme, which has a style under it). An extension whose anchor is gone creates expressions without one. | 2026-10-09 |
| `ReflectionBindingExtension.ProvideValue` sets `TypeResolver = serviceProvider.ResolveType`: a delegate over the service provider, which is the context of the build. | The resolver holds the `IXamlTypeResolver` service of the provider, asked for when the binding is provided (`markup_extensions/reflection_binding_extension.rs`). `ResolveType` reads that one service, so a name resolves to the same type. | Representation | The context of a build has the root object, the parents and the name scope it built with, and the instance of a multi binding of an element holds its bindings: through the resolver the tree a compiled template built kept itself alive (catalog gap C323: the text box of the Fluent theme, whose template has a multi binding of reflection bindings). The resolver of the document holds no object of the build. | 2026-10-09 |
| `CustomPopupPlacementCallback` is a delegate over the record `CustomPopupPlacement`, a reference type the method changes; markup creates the delegate of the named method (`XamlLoadMethodDelegateNode`). | The callback is `Rc<dyn Fn(&mut CustomPopupPlacement)>` over a value. For markup the delegate type is declared with an `Invoke` over the shared form `Rc<RefCell<CustomPopupPlacement>>`, and the delegate of the named method is cast to the callback, which copies the parameters into a shared object, calls the method and copies them back (`markup_types/plain.rs`). | Representation | A method called through metadata receives values, not a mutable borrow. The method named in markup declares the shared form as its parameter. | pull request 96 |
| `XamlTransformHelpers.TryConvertValue` finds the method a delegate is created from by the exact parameter types of `Invoke` (`FindMethod(name, returnType, allowDowncast: false, parameters)`). A handler `void H(object, EventArgs e)` of an `EventHandler` event receives whatever object the event raises (`PopupFlyoutBase.Opening` raises `CancelEventArgs`). | When no method matches exactly, `FerroXamlIlLanguage::try_convert_method_delegate` accepts the method of that name whose parameters are assignable from the parameters of `Invoke`; the handler of such an event declares the arguments untyped (`BoxedValue`) and downcasts them. | Behaviour | The arguments of events are values, not objects of one class hierarchy: a parameter declared `EventArgs` cannot hold `CancelEventArgs`. A document that names a method with wider parameters, which upstream rejects, loads. | pull request 96 |
| A method name assigned to an event or to a property of a delegate type that no method of the root object answers to is reported by the compiler as any assignment no setter takes (`Unable to find suitable setter or adder for property Click of type .. for argument System.String, available setter parameter lists are: ..`), in the build as at run time. | The run-time loader gives that text unchanged. A compile (`rust_emitter::transform`, `method_not_found`) puts a description before it: the method, the member it is named for, the delegate type, and whether the document has a class (``No method `OnGo` for `Click` (..): the document has no class ..``). The code (`FRN3000`), the document and the place are the compiler's. | Diagnostic text | The name of the method is a text to the compiler; a build should say which method is missing and where a method a document can name is declared (`methods:` of the markup metadata of the class). | branch `xaml-emit-event-handlers` |
| A delegate created from a method named in markup (`new TDelegate(root, &Method)`) keeps its target alive, and a handler that throws propagates to whoever raised the event. | Both back ends hold the root object weakly (`load_method_delegate` of the interpreter, `rt::method_delegate` of generated code): a handler whose root object is gone does nothing, as does one that is called with arguments the method does not take or whose fallible method fails. | Behaviour | The root object owns the element the handler is attached to; a strong reference would close a cycle that reference counting never frees. A handler called through metadata has nowhere to report a failure to. | the interpreter: stage 2 (open issue O1 of `xaml.md`); generated code: branch `xaml-emit-event-handlers` |
| The trampolines of a method used as a command (`XamlIlTrampolineBuilder`) cast the command parameter to the parameter type (`castclass`, `unbox.any`) and call the method. | The interpreter invokes the method through its metadata, which adapts an argument that is a type value, an array or a list of the run-time loader to the Rust type the member declares; generated code reads the parameter with the reader of a handler's arguments (`rt::DelegateArguments`), which converts an object passed to an untyped parameter and nothing else (`xaml.md` 9.4.10). | Behaviour | A command parameter is what an element holds as its `CommandParameter`; a process without the run-time loader has none of those three values. The panics of a parameter that does not convert are the same in both back ends. | branch `xaml-catalog-last-refusals` (no pull request yet) |
| The setter chosen at run time for a value in property element syntax is one method (`XamlPropertyAssignmentNode` emits the type checks and one call); for the adder of a collection the call converts the value and then reads the collection. | Generated code writes the branch of an adder as one statement that reads the collection first and converts the value in the argument list (`Emitter::adder_statement`; `xaml.md` 9.4.11); the interpreter keeps upstream's order. | Behaviour | The two orders differ only when both steps fail, and a value that passed the type check of its branch converts. | branch `xaml-catalog-last-refusals` (no pull request yet) |
| The handler of `AvaloniaObject.PropertyChanged` attached from markup receives the `AvaloniaPropertyChangedEventArgs` the object raises. | `FerroPropertyChangedEventArgs<'_>` borrows the values of the change, so the event is declared for markup with `OwnedFerroPropertyChangedEventArgs` (`FerroPropertyChangedEventArgs::to_owned_args`): the sender, the property, the priority and copies of the old and new value in untyped form. Markup knows it as `FerroPropertyChangedEventArgs`. | Representation | A handler called through metadata receives values it can keep. The copies are made only for handlers attached through metadata. | pull request 96 |
| A property a compiled binding streams (`Property^`) is declared `IObservable<T>` or `Task<T>`, and the path element after the stream has the type `T`. | An observable or a task held as a value is `ObservableValue` or `TaskValue`, which hold their values untyped. Where no metadata declares the instantiation for the handle (`generic: "IObservable`1" [T]`), the run-time type system resolves it to `IObservable<object>` or `Task<object>` (`RuntimeTypeSystem::resolve_untyped_stream`), so the stream has the type `object`. | Representation | One Rust type holds every observable; a crate can declare one instantiation for it. A path that continues after the stream (`Property^.Member`) needs that declaration to resolve the member. | pull request 96 |
| A member that markup calls through metadata (the deferred construction of a resource, a markup member invoker) throws its `XamlLoadException`, and the exception reaches the caller of the load with its type and inner exception. | Such a member has no error channel, so the runtime's throw (`throw` of `ferroui-markup-xaml`) panics with the message of the exception. Inside a scope of `xaml_error_scope::with_xaml_errors` (hidden from the documentation) the throw records the exception and unwinds with a private token instead, and the scope returns `Err(exception)`, typed and with its inner exception; other panics leave the scope unchanged, a scope is not a transaction, and recovering the error needs a build that unwinds. Without a scope nothing changes. | Behaviour | A loader that reports errors rather than panicking (the WinUI importer of `ferroui-markup-xaml-winui`, `winui-controls.md`) needs the exception of a deferred resource as a value; Rust has no exceptions. Tests: `xaml_error_scope::tests` (`ferroui-markup-xaml`). | W2 (xaml_error_scope) |

### Parsers of markup (`src/Markup/FerroUI.Markup/markup/parsers/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SelectorParser` refuses the nesting selector `^` (`NotSupportedException`, "Unsupported selector grammar"): only the selector transformer of the markup compiler builds a nesting selector. | `SelectorParser` builds `Selectors::nesting` for `^`, so the selector of a nested style written in code parses (`^.selected`, `^:pointerover > Button`). Every other syntax is parsed as upstream does. | Addition | The declarative API writes nested styles in code (`style("^:pointerover")` of `ferroui-view`, `docs/declarative-ui.md`, section 10.2); markup is not affected, its selectors go through the compiler. | #237 |

### Build integration of markup (`src/FerroUI.Build.Tasks/`, `tests/XamlIncludeFixture/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CompileAvaloniaXamlTask` runs after the C# compiler: `XamlCompilerTaskExecutor` reads the compiled assembly with Cecil, so it sees every type of the assembly being built, and writes the compiled markup into it. | The compiler runs in the build script of the crate (`ferroui_build::Build::run`), before rustc, and writes Rust source to `OUT_DIR`. It reads types from the registries of its own process: the build script links the crates whose types the documents name and registers them. It cannot link the crate it builds, so a document that names a type of that crate (every `x:Class` document) is generated by a test of the crate and checked in instead. | Missing | A Rust crate cannot be inspected after it is compiled, and the build-time type system that reads the sources (xaml.md 9.5) is not written. The seam closes with it. | `xaml-e5-build-integration` (no pull request yet) |
| A referenced assembly is found through the references of the project; its compiled documents are read from its metadata (`CompiledAvaloniaXaml.!AvaloniaResources`, the `x:Class` types). | A crate with compiled markup has a `links` key and its build script prints `cargo::metadata=xamlmeta=<path>`; the build script of a direct dependent reads the `.xamlmeta` from `DEP_<CRATE>_XAML_XAMLMETA`, and each file names the files of its own dependencies. | Representation | Cargo hands data from one build script to another only through `links` metadata, and only to direct dependents. | `xaml-e5-build-integration` |
| `GenerateAvaloniaResourcesTask` packs the resource items of the project (`AvaloniaResource` items of the project file) into the `!AvaloniaResources` stream; the compiler removes each compiled document from it. | `Build::embed_assets(directories)` lists every file below the directories as `(rooted path, include_bytes!)` in `compiled_markup::ASSETS`, without the documents the build compiled. The directories are stated in the build script, not in the manifest. | Representation | There is no project file with item groups; `include_bytes!` is how a crate embeds a file. | `xaml-e5-build-integration` |
| The task reports diagnostics with codes and severities through MSBuild, filtered by `XamlCompilerDiagnosticsFilter` (`TreatWarningsAsErrors`, `NoWarn`). | A document the emitter cannot compile is a `cargo::error=<document>: <reason>` line and the build fails after every group was compiled. No codes, no warnings, no filter. | Missing | The emitter reports a reason as text; the codes and the filter come with the diagnostics of xaml.md 9.6.5. | `xaml-e5-build-integration` |
| A member a collection inherits from `AvaloniaList<T>` (`Capacity`, `Add`) is called on the collection itself: `Points` *is* an `AvaloniaList<Point>`, and the compiler emits the call on the instance. | The named collections are Rust types of their own. Generated code passes `&collection` to the typed function of the member of the list only for a pair a crate registers as dereferencing (`ValueTypes::register_deref::<RowDefinitions, FerroList<Ref<RowDefinition>>>(\|c\| c)`: `KeyFrames`, `Transitions`, `Controls`, `InlineCollection`, `DataTemplates`, `RowDefinitions`, `ColumnDefinitions`); for every other collection (`Points`, `TickList`, ..) it converts the instance with the cast the crate registers, which yields the same list, as the run-time loader does. | Representation | Rust has no inheritance between the collection types; whether one dereferences to the list is a fact of an `impl Deref`, which neither type system of the compiler has, so it is a registration both read (xaml.md 9.5.18). | branch `xaml-catalog-rustc` |
| The compiled markup of an assembly names the types of the assembly like any other type. | The compiled markup of a class names the types of its crate through `crate`; the compiled markup of a document without a class names them by the name of the crate, so a crate with such a document that names its own types declares `extern crate self as <crate>;`. | Representation | A crate cannot name itself by its name without that declaration; the file of a group has the form the compiled markup of another crate would have (xaml.md 9.5.19). | branch `xaml-catalog-rustc` |
| `XamlCompilerTaskExecutor` writes one `!XamlLoader.TryLoad` for the assembly. | A crate with documents on both paths has one table per generated file; `compiled_markup::try_load` asks each in turn, the checked-in ones first (`Build::loader`). | Representation | The checked-in file of a class and the files of the build script are generated by different hosts. One table when the scanner removes the checked-in path. | `xaml-e5-build-integration` |
| `LocaleCollection` of `ResourceIncludeTests` is a type of the test assembly, which also holds the document that uses it. | The type is in the fixture library (`xaml-include-fixture-theme`, the assembly `Tests`); the document that uses it is in the fixture application and names it there. | Test | The build script of the application compiles the document and cannot link a type of the application. | `xaml-e5-build-integration` |
| `XamlCompilerDiagnosticsFilter` takes the analyzer config files of the build as `ITaskItem[]` and finds the entries `avalonia_xaml_diagnostic.<code>.severity = <severity>` with a regular expression; `Handle` is internal to the build task. | `XamlCompilerDiagnosticsFilter::new` takes the paths of the files; the key is `ferro_xaml_diagnostic.<code>.severity`; the pattern is matched by hand (`parse_severities`), with a letter, a digit or an underscore as a word character; `handle` and `handle_code` are public. | Representation | The build script has no task items, the key carries the name of the framework (rule 2 of the porting guide), and the crate has no regular expressions. The class is not called yet: `compile_xaml()` reports no diagnostics with codes (xaml.md 9.6.8), and the keys `warnings-as-errors` and `no-warn` of 9.6.1, which upstream leaves to MSBuild, are not part of it. | Row 4 of the tracking list |

### Selection and pages (`src/FerroUI.Controls/primitives/`, `src/FerroUI.Controls/page/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TabbedPage` learns of a moved selection from `TabControl.SelectionChanged` only. When the selected page is removed and the page after it takes its index, the tab control raises that event while the container of the removed page is still the one at the index (the selection model handles the change of the collection before the panel does), so `ResolvePageAtIndex` returns the removed page again: `SelectedPage` and `CurrentPage` stay the page that left, and no `SelectionChanged`, `NavigatedFrom` or `NavigatedTo` is raised. | `TabbedPage` also handles `ContainerIndexChanged` of its tab control (`on_container_index_changed`, `page/tabbed_page.rs`): when a container arrives at the selected index and the selected page no longer has a container, the page of that container is committed as the selection with `NavigationType::Remove`. | Behaviour | The stale selection keeps the removed page alive for as long as the tabbed page lives (catalog gap C316), and shows a tabbed page whose current page is not one of its pages. After the removal `SelectedPage` and `CurrentPage` are the page of the selected tab, `SelectionChanged` is raised once, the removed page receives `NavigatedFrom` and the new one `NavigatedTo`. | 2026-10-09 |
| `SelectingItemsControl.OnSelectionModelLostSelection` reports the items selected before a reset (`_selectedItemsBeforeReset`) as removed and leaves `_selectedItemsSnapshot` and `SelectedValue` as they were: the selection model raises no `SelectionChanged` for a selection lost to a reset, so both keep the removed items until the selection changes again. | When it reports them, the port empties the snapshot and sets `SelectedValue` from the selected item, which is none (`on_selection_model_lost_selection`, `primitives/selecting_items_control.rs`). | Behaviour | Both hold the removed item, which nothing collects here: the selected tab of a tabbed page whose pages were cleared stayed alive (catalog gap C316). `SelectedValue` is null after the items are cleared, where upstream still returns the removed item; a second reset without a selection in between does not report the same items as removed again. | 2026-10-09 |

### Native control host (`src/FerroUI.Controls/native_control_host.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NativeControlHost.PropertyChangedHandler` moves the native control (a job at the `AfterRender` priority that reads `TransformToVisual` to the root) only when `Bounds` or `IsVisible` of the host or of a visual ancestor changes. `TransformToVisual` includes the render transforms of the chain, so a host whose first arrange falls into a frame in which an ancestor has a render transform is placed with that transform and is not placed again when the transform changes or is removed. A page pushed on a `NavigationPage` with its `PageSlide` is that case in every navigation: `UpdateActivePage` sets the content of the presenter and starts the transition in the same call; the next `MediaContext.RenderCore` pulses the clock first (the first key frame: a translation by the width of the parent), runs the layout pass second (the host gets its bounds and queues the job), and the job runs after the render with the translation in place. Nothing changes `Bounds` afterwards (`PageSlide.Start` ends with `to.RenderTransform = null`), so the native control stays one page width off screen until the next change of layout. | The host also moves its control (the same job) when `RenderTransform` or `RenderTransformOrigin` of itself or of a visual ancestor changes, and when a mutable transform of that chain changes in place (`subscribe_render_transforms`: one subscription per transform of the chain, made at the attachment and again when a `RenderTransform` of the chain is replaced). A host under no transform that changes does no more than upstream; under an animated one it follows the animation, one job a frame. | Behaviour | An upstream defect, fixed. Seen in the port's catalog on three platforms, the native controls of the Native Embed page one page width to the right: iOS (x = 422 on a screen of 402 points, 20 after another layout), Android (1132 on 1080 px, 52 after another layout), X11 (the holder windows at +1120 in a window 1100 wide). Reproduced without a device by `native_control_of_a_page_that_slid_in_is_at_the_position_of_its_host` (`native_control_host_tests.rs`), whose frames are render passes of the media context: before the change the control was shown once, at x = 420 in a window 400 wide for a host at x = 20, in the first frame, and never again. Upstream's behaviour was established by reading, not by a run: `NativeControlHost.cs`, `Page/NavigationPage.cs` (`UpdateActivePage`, `RunPageTransitionAsync`), `Animation/PageSlide.cs`, `Animation/Animation.cs` (`RunAsync` subscribes before it returns), `Animation/AnimationInstance`, `Media/MediaContext.cs` (`RenderCore`), `VisualExtensions.cs` (`GetOffsetFrom`) and `Threading/DispatcherPriority.cs` at the tracked commit are, step for step, what the port has, and the first three are unchanged at upstream's head of 2026-10-10. | `native-control-host-transforms` (2026-10-10) |

### Popup (`src/FerroUI.Controls/primitives/popup.rs`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| An open popup is positioned again (`UpdateHostPosition`) when its window moves (`PositionChanged`), when a parent popup root moves, and when the bounds of its placement target change in a layout pass (`PlacementTargetLayoutUpdated`). The anchor rectangle is the place of the target in the root at the time of the request, render transforms included, so a popup opened while an animated render transform moves its target (a page of a navigation page that slides in) stays where the target was when it opened. | The same three triggers, and while the popup is open (placement other than `Pointer`) a subscription to the render transforms of the placement target and of its visual ancestors: when one is set, replaced or removed, its origin changes, or a mutable transform changes in place, one job per frame at the `AfterRender` priority positions the popup again if the rectangle of the target in the root differs from the last one (`subscribe_placement_target_transforms`, `placement_target_moved`). | Behaviour | A popup that stays beside the place its target had during a transition is a fault of upstream (the decision document `decisions/popup-opened-while-its-target-moves.md`; the owner decided on 2026-10-15 to record it as a deviation and fix it after the first preview). The native control host has the same correction. On a Wayland shell older than version 3 of `xdg_wm_base` each new place creates the popup again, so it flickers while its target moves. Tests: `popup_moving_target_tests.rs`. | 0.1.0-preview.2 |

### Remote protocol (`src/FerroUI.Remote.Protocol/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The BSON serializer (`MetsysBson.cs`) finds the properties of a class by reflection (`TypeHelper`, `MagicProperty` with compiled getter and setter delegates) and the resolver finds the message classes by reflection over the exported types of an assembly and their `AvaloniaRemoteMessageGuidAttribute`. | A class states its properties with `bson_class!` (the names on the wire, in the order reflection returns them: the declared ones, then the ones of the base classes) and an enumeration with `bson_enum!`; `ferro_remote_message_guid!` puts the identifier on a class (`FerroRemoteMessageGuidAttribute`, the constant `GUID`); an assembly is the table of its exported types (`Assembly`, `ExportedType`, `ASSEMBLY`). `object` is `Value` (owned, what the deserializer produces) and `ValueRef` (borrowed, what the serializer is given); `System.Type` is `Type`/`ClassType`. The types of the serializer are public, where upstream's are internal. | Representation | Rust has no reflection. The declarations of a message class of another crate name the types of the serializer. | 2026-10-09 |
| `Type.GetProperties` returns the properties of a derived class before those of its base classes (the order is not specified, it is what the runtimes do), and the serializer writes them in that order. | The order is written into the declarations (`input_messages.rs`: `Button, X, Y, Modifiers`). | Representation | The reader finds properties by name, so the order only decides the bytes; the declarations fix what upstream leaves to the runtime. | 2026-10-09 |
| `Enum.Parse` of the deserializer accepts a number that no member of the enumeration has (`ReadEnum`). | The number is read, and assigning it to the property is `Error::Argument` (`bson_enum!`). | Behaviour | A Rust enumeration cannot hold a value that is not a member. A message with such a number ends the reading of a stream connection with the exception event, as any other error of the deserializer does. | 2026-10-09 |
| `InputModifiers` of the protocol is marked `[Flags]`, with the members 0 to 6. | A plain enumeration (`input_messages.rs`), not `bitflags!`. | Representation | The members are not bits and a message carries an array of members. | 2026-10-09 |
| `Serializer.Serialize` lets a `Regex`, an `ObjectId` or a `ScopedCode` pass as the root and writes its properties by reflection; `ReadObject` creates a default value of a type that is not a class (a `Guid` property whose element is not binary, an item of a `byte[]` written as an array) and reads the rest of the enclosing document as members it does not have. `ScopedCode<T>` is a second class with a typed scope. | The first is "Root type must be an object", as for the other values that are not objects; the second is `Error::InvalidCast`; `ScopedCode` is one struct whose scope is a `Value` (`metsys_bson.rs`). | Behaviour | The three classes are not declared as classes with properties, and a value of a simple type has no instance to fill. None of it is reached by the messages of the protocol. | 2026-10-09 |
| `ObjectIdGenerator` hashes `Dns.GetHostName()` with MD5 for the three machine bytes of an identifier. | The name is read from the environment (`HOSTNAME`, `COMPUTERNAME`) and hashed with the hasher of the standard library (`metsys_bson.rs`). | Behaviour | The standard library has neither a host name nor MD5, and the crate has no dependency. The bytes only tell machines apart; the protocol does not use object identifiers. | 2026-10-09 |
| The configuration of the serializer names a property with an expression tree (`x => x.Name`, `ExpressionHelper`, `TypeHelper.FindProperty(LambdaExpression)`), and its instance is "not thread safe". | A property is named by its declared name (`ITypeConfiguration::use_alias("Name", ..)`); `ExpressionHelper` and the lookup by expression have no counterpart; the tables of `BsonConfiguration` are behind locks. | Representation | No expression trees; a static of Rust has to be `Sync`. | 2026-10-09 |
| The transports are asynchronous: `Send` returns a `Task`, streams are read and written with `async` calls, events are raised on the thread pool, `TcpTransportBase.Connect` returns a task and `Listen` accepts with `AcceptTcpClientAsync`. | Threads and blocking calls (`i_transport.rs` states the contract): a reader thread per connection on which the message event and the exception of a failed read are raised, one at a time; a sender thread per `TransportConnectionWrapper`, started by its first send; a listener thread per `listen` and a short thread per accepted client for its callback; `connect` blocks. `send` returns a `Task` of the port (`task.rs`: a result another thread may still be producing, `wait` blocks); a stream connection writes on the calling thread. Handlers are `Send + Sync` and receive the connection by reference; adding one returns a `HandlerToken` that `remove_on_message`/`remove_on_exception` take. | Behaviour | The library is a leaf without an asynchronous runtime, and the dispatcher of the framework is not available to it.
