Order and exactly-once delivery are as upstream; the thread a handler runs on is a thread of the connection instead of a pool thread. | 2026-10-09 |
| `BsonStreamTransportConnection.Dispose` cancels the pending reads and writes with a cancellation token and ignores the `OperationCanceledException` of that token; the dispose callback of a connection accepted by `Listen` only completes a task and leaves the socket open. | `dispose` sets a flag and calls the dispose callback, which closes the socket (also for an accepted connection); the reader thread then reads the end of the stream, and nothing is raised by a disposed connection: every error after `dispose` is taken for the cancellation (`bson_stream_transport.rs`, `tcp_transport_base.rs`). | Behaviour | A blocking read of the standard library cannot be cancelled; closing the socket is what ends it. A connection that is dropped without `dispose` keeps its reader thread and its socket until the other end closes. | 2026-10-09 |
| `TcpTransportBase.Listen` returns an `IDisposable` whose `Dispose` stops the `TcpListener`; `Listen` and `Connect` take an `int` port. | `DisposableServer::dispose` sets a flag and wakes the listener thread with a connection to its own address, after which the thread closes the listening socket; `DisposableServer::local_addr` is an addition, so that a caller can listen on port 0; the port is a `u16`. `TcpTransportBase` is a trait (`resolver`, `create_transport` with the two directions of the socket as two streams). | Representation | A blocking `accept` cannot be stopped from another thread. The port is closed shortly after `dispose` returns, not when it returns. | 2026-10-09 |
| An exception thrown by a handler of `OnMessage` is caught by the reader loop and raised as `OnException`, which ends the loop; `EventStash` catches the exceptions of handlers while it replays. | A panic of a handler is caught in the same two places and raised as `Error::Handler` with the message of the panic (`error.rs`: `catch_handler`). | Representation | Handlers of Rust do not throw; a panic is what would otherwise kill the reader thread without a word. | 2026-10-09 |
| `ExceptionDetails(Exception e)` reads the class name of the exception by reflection, unwraps a `TargetInvocationException` and takes line and position from an `XmlException`. | `ExceptionDetails::from_exception(exception_type, error, line_info)`: the caller states the name of the error type and the line information (`design_messages.rs`). | Representation | An error of Rust has no class name at run time, and the library does not know the error types of the markup crates. | 2026-10-09 |
| `Key.cs` and `PhysicalKey.cs` of the base library are compiled into the protocol assembly (`<Compile Include>`), in the input namespace of the protocol. | `lib.rs` compiles `key.rs` and `physical_key.rs` of `src/FerroUI.Base/input/` into the crate with `#[path]` and exports them from `input`; `input_messages.rs` states that they are written as numbers. | Representation | The same arrangement: the crate stays a leaf and has the same numbers as the base crate. The types are distinct from the ones of the base crate, as upstream's are; a caller converts by the number. | 2026-10-09 |

### Remote rendering (`src/FerroUI.Controls/remote/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RemoteServerTopLevelImpl` is one object: the offscreen top-level implementation, the framebuffer surface (`Surfaces => [this]`) and the handler of the connection, with `_lock` between the threads. | The top-level implementation (`RemoteServerTopLevelImpl`) is an object of the UI thread over `OffscreenTopLevelImplBase`; what the reader thread of the connection and the thread that renders use (the handling of the messages, the frame counters, the framebuffer, the surface) is a shared part behind an `Arc` (`RemoteServerSurface`, `server/remote_server_top_level_impl.rs`), as the surface of a headless window is. The client size, the scaling and the disposed flag the render path reads are mirrored into it by `set_client_size`, `set_render_scaling`, `render_and_send_frame_if_needed` and `dispose`. | Representation | A surface is `Send + Sync`; a top-level implementation is `Rc`-based. A size set directly on the offscreen base (not through the setters of this class) reaches the render path at the next `render_and_send_frame_if_needed`. | 2026-10-09 |
| The handlers of `OnMessage` capture `this` in the delegate they post to `Dispatcher.UIThread` (`RemoteServerTopLevelImpl.OnMessage`, the lambda of `RemoteWidget`). | A handler runs on the reader thread of the connection and holds no object of the UI thread: the object is registered on its thread under a number (`UiThreadHandle`, `remote/ui_thread_handle.rs`), the handler posts an action with the data of the message and the number, and the action looks the object up on the UI thread. The dispatcher is the one of the thread that registered, read at registration. An action whose object is gone is not run. | Representation | `Ref<T>` and `Rc` do not cross threads. `Dispatcher::ui_thread()` on the reader thread would name another dispatcher in a unit test, which is why the dispatcher is captured. Upstream's delegate keeps the object alive until it has run. | 2026-10-09 |
| `OnMessage` and `Measure` are protected virtual members; `PreviewerWindowImpl` overrides `OnMessage`, `DesktopScaling` and `TryGetFeature`. | `RemoteServerTopLevelImplOverrides` (a trait of the UI thread: `measure`, `desktop_scaling`, `try_get_feature`) and `OnMessageOverride` (a `Send + Sync` function that runs on the reader thread before the handling of the class and says whether it handled the message), both given to `RemoteServerTopLevelImpl::with_overrides`. `EmbeddableRemoteServerTopLevelImpl` of `RemoteServer`, which adds nothing, is not a type. | Representation | The override of `OnMessage` runs on another thread than the other overrides, so it cannot be a method of the same object. | 2026-10-09 |
| `Framebuffer.Lock` pins `_data` and holds the monitor `_dataLock` until the locked framebuffer is disposed. | The locked framebuffer takes the pixels out of the framebuffer and puts them back when it is disposed; the monitor is a flag with a condition, on which `lock`, `get_status` and `to_message` wait while a frame is rendered (`server/remote_server_top_level_impl_framebuffer.rs`). It is not reentrant. | Representation | A guard of a mutex cannot be stored in the object that outlives the call; no `unsafe`. | 2026-10-09 |
| `Framebuffer.Empty` is one static framebuffer for every top-level, so its status and its monitor are shared; a locked framebuffer whose backend fails before it draws (Skia cannot create a surface of no pixels) is never disposed, and the monitor stays held by the thread that failed. | Each top-level has an empty framebuffer of its own (`Framebuffer::empty`), and a locked framebuffer that is dropped without having been disposed gives its pixels back and leaves the monitor, without a frame and without the unlocked callback (`impl Drop for LockedFramebuffer`). | Behaviour | With the monitor of the port (not reentrant, and waited on by the UI thread in `get_status`) the original arrangement hangs: a frame rendered before the client has stated its pixel formats locks the empty framebuffer, the backend panics on the surface of no pixels, and every top-level of the process then waits forever for the shared framebuffer. Found by the Skia tests of the designer support crate, which hung on a condition variable. | 2026-10-09 |
| `CreateFramebufferRenderTarget` returns a `FuncFramebufferRenderTarget`, whose state is always ready. `RemoteServer` starts the rendering of its top-level when it is constructed, so the compositor renders it at the first tick (`_redrawRequested` of a new target is set, whatever its size), before the client has stated its pixel formats and allocated a viewport: `Lock` hands out a framebuffer without pixels (`Framebuffer.Empty`, or one of the stated format with a client size of zero), `FramebufferRenderTarget.CreateSurface` of Skia throws ("Unable to create a surface with size 0x0"), the exception leaves `ServerCompositor.Render` (the filter of its `catch` is always false) and is caught and logged by `RenderLoop.TimerTick`, at every tick until the client has done both. | The render target of the surface (`RemoteServerFramebufferRenderTarget`, `server/remote_server_top_level_impl.rs`) is not ready (`PlatformRenderTargetState::NOT_READY_TRY_LATER`) while the framebuffer it would lock has no pixels (`stride() > 0`, the test `RenderAndSendFrameIfNeeded` paints by), so the compositor does not ask for a drawing context and tries again at the next tick; `lock` is unchanged. The check of Skia is unchanged. A size that loses its pixels between the question and the lock on a render thread still fails as upstream. | Behaviour | The panic of Skia unwinds through whatever ticks the render loop: the render loop of an application catches it as upstream does, the manual render loop of the tests does not, and the Skia tests of the designer support crate failed whenever a frame was rendered before both messages of the client had been handled on the UI thread (74 of 300 runs; the messages arrive on the reader thread of the connection and are posted, so it is a matter of timing). No frame is lost: upstream sends nothing for a framebuffer without pixels either, with its one backend. Forced by `nothing_is_rendered_while_the_framebuffer_of_the_server_has_no_pixels` (`src/FerroUI.DesignerSupport/remote/remote_rendering_tests.rs`, Skia) and `the_render_target_is_not_ready_while_the_framebuffer_has_no_pixels` (`remote_tests.rs`). The mock backend of `remote_tests.rs` does not ask for the state and still renders into a framebuffer without pixels. | 2026-10-09 |
| `RenderAndSendFrameIfNeeded` returns without doing anything while the last frame sent is not acknowledged (`_lastReceivedFrame != _lastSentFrame`), and `FrameReceivedMessage` posts `SendLastFrameIfNeeded` only, which sends a frame only if the compositor has rendered one meanwhile. A render asked for while a frame is on its way (by `ClientSupportedPixelFormatsMessage`, `ClientRenderInfoMessage`, `ClientViewportAllocatedMessage` or the previewer) is therefore lost unless something invalidates the top-level later. | The refused request is remembered (`State::render_requested`, set in `render_and_send_frame_if_needed` when a format is known and a frame is unacknowledged, cleared when a render is done), and the action posted for `FrameReceivedMessage` calls `render_and_send_frame_if_needed` instead of `send_last_frame_if_needed` when it is set. The protocol is unchanged: still one frame on its way at a time, numbered and acknowledged as before. | Behaviour | The first frame with pixels was lost about one run in three of a test (`the_server_sends_no_pixels_before_the_client_states_its_pixel_formats`): the compositor rendered its second frame of the allocation into the empty framebuffer and the frame was sent (no pixels); the pixel formats arrived while it was unacknowledged, so the request to render was refused; the acknowledgement found the empty framebuffer already copied to a message and nothing invalidated the top-level again, so the client never got pixels. The original has the same window; its previewer states the formats before anything is rendered and its scenes keep being invalidated, so it does not show. The interleaving is forced by `a_render_asked_for_while_a_frame_is_unacknowledged_is_done_when_the_frame_is_acknowledged` (`remote_tests.rs`), over a connection the test delivers the messages of. | 2026-10-09 |
| `Framebuffer.ToMessage` returns a message that shares `_dataCopy` ("must NOT be kept around"). | The message owns a copy of `_dataCopy`: one more copy of the frame per message. | Performance | `FrameMessage::data` is an owned `Vec<u8>` of the protocol crate, and a message is handed to a connection that may queue it. | 2026-10-09 |
| `(Key)key.Key` and `(PhysicalKey)key.PhysicalKey` cast a number that may be no member; `Marshal.Copy` in `RemoteWidget.Render` throws for a frame without data or with less data than its size says. | A number that is no member cannot arrive (the protocol crate rejects it when it reads the message); the cast falls back to `None`. A frame without data or shorter than it says panics in `render`. | Behaviour | The enumerations of Rust; the exception of the original. | 2026-10-09 |
| `RemoteServer` and `RemoteWidget` are internal, visible to the designer support assembly. | Public (`ferroui_controls::remote`), as is `RemoteServerTopLevelImpl` (`remote::server`). `RemoteWidget` is not in the table of known types (`register_types.rs`): markup cannot name an internal class. | Representation | No friend assemblies. | 2026-10-09 |

### Designer support (`src/FerroUI.DesignerSupport/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RemoteDesignerEntryPoint.Main(string[])` is the entry point of a host process (`Avalonia.Designer.HostApp`) that loads the assembly of the application from the path in its arguments (`Assembly.LoadFrom`), finds its entry point and obtains the application builder by reflection (`AppBuilder.Configure(entryPoint.DeclaringType)`). | `RemoteDesignerEntryPoint::main(cmdline, build_app)`: the binary of the application is the host of its own previewer and calls the function with its arguments and the function that builds its application builder (`xaml.md`: "the previewer host is the user's own binary"). The positional argument (the path of the application) is still required and parsed, and is only handed on to the file watcher transport. The host registers the run-time XAML loader before the call, as the host application of the original binds its loader. `IAppInitializer`, which exists upstream to keep the reflection out of the trimmed path, is the function `configure_app`. | Architecture | A Rust application is linked statically: there is no assembly to load and no reflection. An IDE integration has to start the binary of the application, not a separate host. | 2026-10-09 |
| The method `avalonia-remote` (`Methods.AvaloniaRemote`), the value of `--method` and its default. | `ferroui-remote` (`methods::FERRO_REMOTE`). | Compatibility | The upstream name does not appear in the sources. An IDE integration written for the original passes the upstream value, which the port does not know: the application is then set up without a windowing subsystem, as for any unknown method. Decided by the owner on 2026-10-09: the rename stays; the upstream value is not accepted as an alias. | 2026-10-09 |
| The `win32` method initializes the Win32 platform it finds by reflection, on any system. | On Windows the method initializes the Win32 platform of the port (`ferroui_win32::Win32Platform`, with the options of the service locator, as the parameterless `Win32Platform.Initialize()` the original calls); elsewhere the platform is not linked and the method ends the previewer with a message. | Behaviour | The Win32 platform is built for Windows only; where the original would load it, it cannot start either. | branch `tests-gaps` (no pull request yet) |
| `HtmlWebSocketTransport` runs `AcceptWorker` and `SocketWorker` as tasks of the thread pool and `SocketReceiveWorker` as an `async void` method per socket; `SimpleWebSocketHttpServer`, `SimpleWebSocketHttpRequest` and `SimpleWebSocket` are asynchronous (`AcceptAsync`, `RespondAsync`, `SendMessage` and `ReceiveMessage` return tasks; an `AsyncLock` queues the senders and the receivers of a socket; `Dispose` of a stream ends its pending reads). | Threads and blocking calls, under the threading contract of the protocol library (`remote/html_transport/`): a thread that accepts the requests, a thread that sends the frames, a reader thread per web socket; the members of the server and of the socket block and return `Result` with the errors of the protocol library where the original throws; the two locks of a socket are mutexes (`AsyncLock` has no counterpart). A message of the page is raised on the reader thread of its socket, a message of the signal transport on the reader thread of that connection, and the pixel formats of `Start` on the calling thread, one event at a time over all of them (a mutex): a handler must not call `start` of the connection. A socket is made over the two directions of a stream and a callback that closes it (`SimpleWebSocket::new`), as a stream connection of the protocol library is; `dispose` of the server wakes a waiting `accept` with a connection to its own address, and the port is closed when that thread has let go of the listener. `SimpleWebSocketHttpServer::local_addr` and `HtmlWebSocketTransport::local_addr` are additions, for a port the system chooses. The workers hold the connection: it lives until it is disposed. | Behaviour | No asynchronous runtime (the row on the transports of "Remote protocol"); a blocking `accept` or read cannot be cancelled. Order and exactly-once delivery are as upstream; the thread a handler runs on is a thread of the connection instead of a pool thread. | 2026-10-09 |
| `AcceptWorker` is an `async void` method without a `catch`: an exception in it is unhandled and ends the process. That is the listener stopped by `Dispose`, a client that went away before its response was written, and `throw new Exception("Origin doesn't match Url")` for a web socket request whose origin is not the URI the transport listens on (any page a browser of the machine has open can make that request). | The worker ends when the server no longer listens; the failure of one request is printed to the standard error stream, the connection of the request is closed, and the next request is served (`HtmlWebSocketTransport::accept_worker`). | Behaviour | A library thread does not end the process of the application, and a request from another origin is refused rather than fatal. Forced by `a_web_socket_is_accepted_by_its_origin_and_kept_by_its_cookie`. | 2026-10-09 |
| `SocketWorker` catches the exception of a failed send, prints it and returns: after the first frame that cannot be sent (the page was closed or reloaded while a frame was on its way, or stated a wrong cookie and had its socket disposed) no page is sent a frame again. | The error is printed, the socket is disposed and forgotten, and the loop goes on: the page that connects next is sent the last frame (`HtmlWebSocketTransport::socket_worker`). | Behaviour | Reloading the page is the ordinary use of the transport. Forced by `a_page_that_connects_takes_the_place_of_the_page_before_it`. | 2026-10-09 |
| The web application under `Remote/HtmlTransport/webapp` is built with bun and esbuild when the project is built (`BunInstall`, `BunRunBuild`; the HTML plugin gives the bundle a hashed name and the compress plugin writes every file as gzip, with source maps), and its output is embedded as gzip resources that the constructor of the transport decompresses. | The sources are ported under `remote/html_transport/webapp/src`; `scripts/build-designer-webapp.sh` checks their types, runs their tests and bundles them with the esbuild the script module of the browser platform pins; the output (`webapp/build`: `index.html`, `index.js`, and the digest of the sources they were built from) is checked in and embedded as it is (`include_bytes!`). The bundle is `index.js`, loaded as a module, and there is no source map. A test of the crate (`the_built_page_is_built_from_its_sources`) fails when the sources changed without a build; `scripts/build-designer-webapp.sh --check` compares the bytes of the bundle and needs the bundler. | Representation | Building the crate needs neither Node nor a decompressor: the workspace has no gzip crate of its own, and the browser backend builds its script module in its build script only because a site is assembled there anyway. The page a browser gets differs in the name of the script and in nothing it does. | 2026-10-09 |
| The page is a React application (`FramePresenter.tsx`, `index.tsx`: `React.Component`, `ReactDOM.render`, synthetic events; `mobx` is a dependency nothing uses), and its input messages take `React.MouseEvent`. `window["avaloniaPreviewerSecurityCookie"]` holds the cookie. | The presenter is a class over the document (`FramePresenter.ts`: the canvas is created in the constructor, `render` hands it out, `componentDidMount` and `componentDidUpdate` are called by `index.ts` and by the constructor as React calls them); the listeners are added to the canvas, the one of the wheel not passive; the input messages take the `MouseEvent` and `WheelEvent` of the document. The cookie is in `window["ferroPreviewerSecurityCookie"]`. The tests of the input messages (`InputEventTests.ts`) run on the test runner and the assertions of Node, with plain objects as events in place of the mocks of `moq.ts`. | Behaviour | No package is downloaded for a canvas with four listeners: the page has no dependency, and its bundle is 5 kB instead of React. React 17 registers its wheel listener as passive, so `preventDefault` of `handleWheel` does nothing upstream and the page scrolls under the pointer; here it keeps the page still, which is what the handler asks for. The upstream name is not in the sources. | 2026-10-09 |
| The handshake of a web socket uses `SHA1.Create()` and `Convert.ToBase64String`; the status line of a response carries the name of the `HttpStatusCode` member (`404 NotFound`). | `sha1` and `to_base64_string` are written out in `simple_web_socket_http_server.rs` (RFC 3174, RFC 4648), tested against the vectors of the RFCs and the example handshake of RFC 6455; the names of the status codes are a table (`http_status_code`). | Representation | The workspace has no crate with SHA-1 or Base64, and the handshake is their only use: about fifty lines against two dependencies. | 2026-10-09 |

### Automation peers (`src/FerroUI.Controls/automation/peers/`)

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ControlAutomationPeer.GetVisualParent` is `Owner.GetVisualParent()`: the peer holds its control, so the control is there for every peer a platform still has. | The peer holds its control weakly (the control owns its peer), and `get_visual_parent` of a peer whose control is gone is `None` (`try_owner`), so `GetParent` of such a peer has no parent instead of panicking. | Representation | The Windows run of the automation tests (2026-10-16) panicked in the sample: the native control host of the embedding page changed its handle while the page was being replaced, its peer raised a change of its children, UI Automation asked for the fragment root of the node, and the walk to the root reached the peer of the page that was already gone ("The control of the automation peer no longer exists"). Test: `control_automation_peer_tests::parent::a_peer_whose_control_is_gone_has_no_parent`. | integration-tests-fixes (2026-10-16) |
| A peer holds its control (`ControlAutomationPeer.Owner`), so its element exists for as long as a platform holds the peer, and every member of the peer reaches the control. | The peer holds its control weakly, and a platform can still hold the peer of a control that is gone (the children of the peer of a page that was replaced). `AutomationPeer::is_element_available` (the virtual `is_element_available_core`; `ControlAutomationPeer`: `try_owner().is_some()`) tells it, and every public member of a peer whose element is gone answers an empty value instead of calling its `*_core` member (no name, class name or automation ID, no children, parent or roots, no provider, not enabled, not focusable, off screen, live setting off, control type `None`). The platform bridges report such an element as gone: the nodes of UI Automation (Windows) answer `UIA_E_ELEMENTNOTAVAILABLE` (`AutomationNode::peer_of`), the handlers of AT-SPI (Linux) answer an unknown object (`handlers::node_of`); the AT-SPI attributes and states and the native peer of macOS (`get_templated_parent`) read the control through `try_owner`. | Behaviour | A Windows run (2026-10-23) caught 19 panics "The control of the automation peer no longer exists." during page navigation: UI Automation asked a node for its fragment root (`AutomationNode::get_fragment_root`), and the walk to the root reached `owner()` of a `NoneAutomationPeer` whose control was gone. Upstream cannot get there; WinUI answers `UIA_E_ELEMENTNOTAVAILABLE` for an element that is gone. Tests: `control_automation_peer_tests::a_control_that_is_gone` (Controls) and `the_node_of_a_peer_whose_control_is_gone_answers_that_its_element_is_not_available` (Win32, run on every host). | 2026-10-24 |

| WinAppDriver finds `Reset` on the embedding page (`EmbeddingTests`). | When one search of UI Automation (`FindAll` of the subtree) finds nothing, the harness walks the control view. | Test | The search of UI Automation loops through the hosted native window: after the `EDIT` below the host it goes on from the parent that window has among the windows of the system (the holder window, which the tree also shows as a child of the main window), so it visits the window 41 times (2717 elements for a tree of 66) and never the button after the host; the walk goes from the host to its next sibling. The providers answer as upstream's (`InteropAutomationNode`, `Win32NativeControlHost` with its holder window, identical); whether WinAppDriver walks or upstream's tree differs could not be checked without upstream's application in the virtual machine. | integration-tests-fixes (2026-10-16) |

XCTest's wait for an idle application has no counterpart in the accessibility API; 250 ms was too short for the action of a native menu item (`MacOS_View_Menu_Select_Button_Tab`). | integration-tests-fixes (2026-10-16) |

### TreeDataGrid (`src/TreeDataGrid/`, `samples/TreeDataGridDemo/`, `tests/FerroUI.TreeDataGrid.Benchmarks/`)

The port of TreeDataGrid (<https://github.com/wieslawsoltes/TreeDataGrid>); the plan and its decisions are in `treedatagrid.md`. The reference is that project, not upstream.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Two data layers: the core's sources, rows and selection (`TreeDataGridCore`) and a classic copy in the framework's namespace, with interfaces that convert `GridLength` and `IndexPath` between them. | One data layer: the core's types, with the members the classic copy adds (`FlatTreeDataGridSource::filter`, `refresh_filter`, the selection interaction) on them. Both entry points of the control stay (`Source` and `Model`). | Representation | Two copies of the same types would need conversions in both directions with no observable gain. | #240 |
| Columns take `Expression<Func<TModel, TValue>>` (getter, synthesized setter, member name, observed path); the cells bind through a vendored typed-binding engine (`Experimental/`). | Columns take an `Accessor<M, V>` (getter, optional setter, member name, the links of a nested path); a cell reads and writes through it and follows `PropertyChanged` of the members it reads. | Representation | Rust has no expression trees; the accessor carries what the reference extracts from the expression. | #240 |
| The control talks to a typed presentation created by a visitor (`TreeDataGridPresentation<TModel>`). | The presentation is untyped (models are boxed values); typed sources register their model type when they are handed to the control. | Representation | A source arriving through a binding is an untyped value and Rust has no generic dispatch over a trait object. | #240 |
| The reference's pages of the demo are tabs of its main window, with their handlers in `MainWindow.axaml.cs`; the core demo is an application of its own. | Every page is a view of its own (`Views/`), with its handlers; the tabs are the view `MainView`, which the window and the ControlCatalog page `TreeDataGrid` show; the core demo is the tab "Core". | Behaviour | The ControlCatalog shows the pages of the demo, so they are views both can host. | #240 |
| The core demo window sets its view model as its data context. | `MainView` sets the view model of the core page as the page's local data context, from code. | Behaviour | As in the reference: a binding to the view model would lose it while the page is out of the tabs, and its grids would see their models change before the presentation options their columns need. | #240 |
| The Wikipedia page loads with the runtime's `HttpClient`. | The demo has a default feature `online` that loads with `ureq`; without it (the ControlCatalog, the browser) the request fails and the page shows the reference's error text. The user agent names the port. | Behaviour | The workspace has no HTTP client; the catalog makes no network requests. | #240 |
| The names of the drag and drop items come from the Bogus library (seeded). | A generator of names from fixed lists, seeded with the reference's seed. | Behaviour | Bogus has no port; the names are stable but are not the reference's. | #240 |
| The Files page follows a directory with `FileSystemWatcher`. | An expanded directory is scanned again every second (a dispatcher timer) and the differences are applied as the watcher's events would be; renames are found by inode on Unix. | Behaviour | The framework has no file system watcher. Changes appear within a second instead of at once. | #240 |
| `TreeDataGridElementFactory.GetDataRecycleKey` and `GetElementRecycleKey` return a `string`; the keys of the factory's own elements are the full names of their types. | They return a `RecycleKey` (`Cow<'static, str>`); the keys of the factory's own elements are the full names of their types, computed once per type and kept (`type_recycle_key`). | Representation | The reference's type names are interned strings; building the name of a type on every lookup made reusing an element several times slower than the reference. A factory may still return any text. | #240 |
| The benchmarks run with BenchmarkDotNet; the revision comparison loads other builds of the library into one process. | The benchmarks run on the harness of `tests/FerroUI.Benchmarks`; the revision runner measures the current build, and revisions are compared by running it in each build. | Test | A Rust process cannot load other builds of itself; the harness is the workspace's. | #240 |
| `ITextCellOptions` has `StringFormat`, which a text cell reads at every realization; the typed binding expression of a cell pushes its values to its observers (`IObserver<BindingValue<T>>.OnNext`), which keep the reference. | `ITextCellOptions` adds `has_default_string_format` (with a default implementation from `string_format`), with which a text cell tests for the default format `{0}` without copying the format; an observer of `ICellBinding` takes the value by value: every observer but the last receives a copy and the last the value itself. | Representation | The same text and the same values. A string of the reference is a reference that is not copied; in the port it is copied for every reader, and these copies were the most common allocation of scrolling the grid. | #244 |
| `TreeDataGridPresenterBase.RealizeElements` takes the items it realizes by the count read when the measure began. | The items are counted again before each element is realized; an index past their end is not realized. | Fix | Realizing an element can change the items: the column headers of a grid whose page is taken down while a layout pass measures it lost columns mid-loop, and the item past the end panicked (seen in the catalog tour, every renderer). The change of the items measures the presenter again. | #266 |

### Reactive (`src/FerroUI.Reactive/`, `src/FerroUI.Reactive.Testing/`, `tests/FerroUI.Reactive.Tests/`)

The port of Rx.NET's System.Reactive and Microsoft.Reactive.Testing (`docs/porting/reactive.md`). The reference is that project, not upstream.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Observables, observers, schedulers and subjects may be used from any thread; operators guard their state with locks and interlocked operations. | Everything is single-threaded and reference counted (`Rc`, `RefCell`); the schedulers run their work on the thread that scheduled it. `Synchronize` of a subject returns the subject; `synchronize` of an observer gives an observer that ignores what follows a terminal notification, and `synchronize_prevent_reentrancy(true)` or `synchronize_with_lock` one that queues re-entrant calls through an `AsyncLock` (`AsyncLockObserver`). Where upstream's locks also guard against re-entrancy (a notification that reaches back into the operator), the port keeps upstream's order with the trampoline of the current-thread scheduler, `AsyncLock` and the queues of the scheduled observers. | Representation | The object model of FerroUI is single-threaded; a sequence and its subscriptions live on the thread that created them (plan, section 5.1). | #248 |
| Absolute times are `DateTimeOffset`, relative times `TimeSpan`, which may be negative and are normalized to zero by the schedulers. | Absolute times are `SystemTime`, relative times `Duration`, which cannot be negative; a due time in the past keeps its place before the work due now (`VirtualTimeClock::subtract`). The historical scheduler starts at the epoch of the system time instead of `DateTimeOffset.MinValue`; the test scheduler's time 0 is that epoch. | Representation | The time types of the standard library; `SystemTime` has no minimum. | #248 |
| An exception thrown by user code (a selector, a predicate, an observer) is caught by the operator and delivered as `OnError`, or rethrown to the caller. | Selectors and predicates that can fail are the `try_` forms of the operators (`try_select`, `try_where`), whose `Err` ends the sequence with the error; the plain forms cannot fail. A panic in an observer disposes the subscription and unwinds, as the rethrown exception does upstream. An observer without a function for errors panics on an error. | Representation | Rust has no exceptions; a failure that is part of the sequence is a value. | #248 |
| `BehaviorSubject.Value` and `TryGetValue` throw the error of a subject that ended with one; `AsyncSubject.GetResult` blocks until the subject ends and throws `InvalidOperationException` when it ended without a value. | `value` and `try_get_value` return the error as `Err`; `get_result` returns `Ok(None)` for a subject that ended without a value and panics for one that has not ended (waiting would never end on the only thread). Using a disposed subject panics where upstream throws `ObjectDisposedException`. | Behaviour | A result is the form of a failure the caller handles; a wait on the one thread cannot end. | #248 |
| `Notification<T>.Value` throws for an error and for the end. | `value` returns `None` for them. | Representation | | #248 |
| `System.Reactive.Unit` is a struct. | `Unit` is Rust's unit type, `()`. | Representation | | #248 |
| `ScheduledItem<TAbsolute>` is compared by due time and can be subclassed; `VirtualTimeScheduler` takes a comparer of its absolute times. | `ScheduledItem` is a concrete type without ordering; the virtual-time scheduler orders by the `Ord` of its absolute time (`VirtualTimeClock::Absolute`). | Missing | Not needed by the port; recorded with the tests that use them. | #248 |
| Several operators look at the type of their source to fuse with it: `Skip`/`Take` of a `Skip`/`Take` (count and time), `SkipUntil`/`TakeUntil` of a time, `AsObservable` and `IgnoreElements` of themselves, `Dematerialize` of a `Materialize`, a chain of `Append`/`Prepend` on one scheduler, nested `Concat`s (`IConcatenatable`), the sources of `Catch`/`OnErrorResumeNext`/`Concat` that are themselves of the operator (`TailRecursiveSink.Extract`). | No fusion: each call wraps the previous one; a handle `Rc<dyn IObservable<T>>` does not reveal the type of its sequence. | Representation | The values, the errors, the times and the subscriptions to the sources are the same (the ported tests check them); a deep chain of one operator uses more stack. | #249 |
| Long-running schedulers (`ISchedulerLongRunning`) run the loops of `Range`, `Repeat`, `Generate`, `ToObservable`, `TakeLast`, `Delay`, `ObserveOn` and `AppendPrepend` on a dedicated thread. | Not ported: the loops always use recursive scheduling, upstream's path for every other scheduler. | Missing | No scheduler of the port is long-running (single-threaded). | #249 |
| `Cast<TResult>` and `OfType<TResult>` take any `IObservable<object>`. | They are methods of a sequence of `Rc<dyn Any>` (`AnyObject`) and give `Rc<U>`; `Cast` ends with `InvalidCastException` for a value that is not a `U`. | Representation | Rust has no object type and no casts between unrelated types. | #249 |
| Counts and indexes are `int`. | They are `usize` (`Take(0)` is an empty sequence); `Count` gives `usize`, `LongCount` `u64`, an overflow of either ends with `SequenceError::Overflow`. | Representation | | #249 |
| `ToObservable(IEnumerable<T>)` enumerates the enumerable on each subscription; `GetEnumerator` and `MoveNext` may throw. | The iterable is `Clone` and each subscription iterates a clone; the `try_` forms take items that are `Result`s for the failures. The enumerable forms of `Catch`, `OnErrorResumeNext`, `Concat`, `Merge` and `Amb` are the same. | Representation | A Rust iterator cannot throw. | #249 |
| `Distinct`, `DistinctUntilChanged` and `GroupBy` with an `IEqualityComparer<TKey>` hash the keys with the comparer. | A comparer is an equality closure without a hash: the keys seen are kept in a list and compared one by one (the default forms use `Eq` + `Hash`). `ToDictionary` and `ToLookup` take `Eq` + `Hash` keys only; their comparer overloads are not ported. | Representation | A closure has no hash code to go with it. | #249 |
| `RepeatWhen` signals each completion to the handler with `null`. | It signals `Unit` (`()`). | Representation | | #249 |
| `TakeUntil(CancellationToken)` and `Subscribe(.., CancellationToken)` act when the token is cancelled from any thread, on that thread. | They act on the thread that subscribed: at once for a cancellation on that thread, and for one from another thread through the poster of the thread, or when it runs `run_pending_wakes` (`CancellationToken::register_local`, docs/porting/reactive.md, section 5.8). | Behaviour | The sinks are not `Send`. | #249 |
| `Generate` passes the state to its functions by value (a struct is copied). | The step function takes the state by value, the condition and the result selector by reference; the state is `Clone`. | Representation | | #249 |
| `Timeout` with a timeout selector and `TakeUntil` with a predicate call the selector after the value is forwarded. | They call it before forwarding (the value is moved into the observer) and act on its result after, as upstream. | Representation | The order of notifications is the same. | #249 |
| `Synchronize(IObservable)` and `Synchronize(IObservable, object gate)` serialize the notifications with a lock. | `synchronize()` returns the sequence itself; the form with a gate is not ported. | Missing | One thread serializes the notifications already. | #249 |
| `Min(IComparer<T>)` and `Max(IComparer<T>)` of a nullable or reference type ignore nulls and give null for an empty sequence. | `min_with`/`max_with` pass every value to the comparer, `None` included, and an empty sequence ends with `SequenceError::NoElements` (the expectation of upstream's own `NO_PERF` branch). The forms without a comparer (`min`, `max` on `Option` values) ignore `None` as upstream. The `decimal` overloads are not ported: there is no decimal type in the crate. | Behaviour | A comparer closure of the port has no notion of null. | #249 |
| `Buffer` gives `IList<T>`, `ToArray` an array. | `Vec<T>`, handed over by move. | Representation | | #249 |
| `Observer.Synchronize(IObserver<T>, object gate)` locks the gate. | `synchronize_with_gate(Rc<dyn Any>)` keeps the gate and never locks it. | Representation | Rust has no object type, and a re-entrant monitor does nothing on one thread. | #249 |
| `Scheduler.DisableOptimizations(IScheduler, params Type[])` hides the services named by their types; the re-wrapped schedulers handed to actions are cached in a `ConditionalWeakTable`. | `disable_optimizations_of(&[SchedulerOptimization])` names the services by an enum (`StopwatchProvider`, `Periodic`); the cache holds the re-wrapped schedulers weakly, and one is made again after it has been dropped. | Representation | No lookup of a service by type; `ISchedulerLongRunning` is not ported; a strong cache would be an `Rc` cycle. | #249 |
| `ObserverBase<T>` and `ObservableBase<T>` are abstract classes. | They are structs that wrap a core (`ObserverCore<T>`, `ObservableCore<T>`) or closures; `subscribe_core` returns a `Result`, whose error goes to the observer, or panics when the observer has stopped. | Representation | Rust has no inheritance, and a blanket implementation of the foreign `IObservable` is not allowed. | #249 |
| `IObserver<TValue, TResult>` | `IObserverWithResult<T, R>`, accepted by `Notification::accept_observer_with_result`. | Representation | The name `IObserver` is taken by the trait of one type parameter. | #249 |
| `ObservableEx.Create` of an iterator takes an `IEnumerable<IObservable<object>>`. | `Observable::create_iterator` and `create_iterator_void` take an iterable of sequences of one type parameter; the `try_` forms take items that are `Result`s for an iterator that throws. | Representation | Rust has no object type. | #249 |
| The asynchronous forms take functions that return a `Task<T>`; a task is the awaiter of `RunAsync`, `GetAwaiter` and `ToTask`. | The functions return a future of `Result<T, ObservableError>`, run as a task of the thread (`ferroui_system::threading::tasks`, docs/porting/reactive.md, section 5.8); `run_async` and `get_awaiter` give the `Task<T>` of their `AsyncSubject` (`AsyncSubject::get_awaiter`), whose continuations run where the subject ends, or, when a `SynchronizationContext` was current when the awaiter was made, when the context runs the posted end of the subject (upstream posts only the continuation and captures the context in `OnCompleted`). The forms of a `Task` without a result are those of `T = ()`. | Representation | A Rust future is polled by one owner; the shared outcome of upstream's task is the port's `Task`. | Phase 4 |
| `TaskObservationOptions` has a scheduler and `IgnoreExceptionsAfterUnsubscribe`; a task that faults after the subscription is disposed reports an unobserved exception. | The options reduce to the scheduler (`_on` forms); there are no unobserved task exceptions. | Missing | The tasks of the port have no task scheduler and no finalizer that reports unobserved exceptions. | Phase 4 |
| A canceled `RunAsync`, `ToTask`, `Sleep` or `Yield` throws `OperationCanceledException` (`TaskCanceledException` for a task). | They end with a `TaskCanceledError`. | Representation | One error type for cancellations, which a task recognises. | Phase 4 |
| `Scheduler.Yield` and `Sleep` return a `SchedulerOperation`, which schedules the continuation when it is awaited; `ConfigureAwait(true)` posts it to the captured `SynchronizationContext`. | They return a `SchedulerOperation` that implements `IntoFuture`, with the awaiter's `is_completed`, `get_result` and `on_completed`; `configure_await(true)` posts the continuation with `post_local` to the context current when it is awaited. | Representation | A Rust future is awaited through `IntoFuture`; the continuation is not `Send`. | Phase 4 |
| `Scheduler.ScheduleAsync` has 12 overloads, with the scheduler's cancellation token found through `GetCancellationToken` (a type test of `CancelableScheduler`). | Six methods take a closure `(Rc<dyn IScheduler>, CancellationToken) -> Future`, whose output is `()` or a disposable (`ScheduleAsyncResult`); the token is `IScheduler::cancellation_token`. | Representation | | Phase 4 |
| `ToAsync` has 34 overloads, arities 0 to 16 of `Func` and `Action`, with and without a scheduler. | Four generic methods (`to_async`, `to_async_on` and their `try_` forms) take a closure of any of those arities, whose argument types are written out. | Representation | The arity comes from the closure (traits `ToAsyncFunction`, `TryToAsyncFunction`). | Phase 4 |
| `SynchronizationContext` (ferroui-system) is a class whose base posts to the thread pool and sends inline; `Post`/`Send` take a `SendOrPostCallback` and a state. | A `Send + Sync` trait with no base implementation: `post`/`send` take `Send` closures, `post_local`/`send_local` closures of the context's thread; `current_synchronization_context`/`set_synchronization_context` replace `Current`/`SetSynchronizationContext`, and setting a context makes it the thread poster of the tasks (section 5.8). | Representation | Closures carry their state; work that is not `Send` stays on its thread. | Phase 4 |
| `SynchronizationContextScheduler`, `ObserveOn`/`SubscribeOn(SynchronizationContext)`, `ContextDisposable` and `NotifyOn(SynchronizationContext)` post to the context from any thread. | They post with `SynchronizationContext::post_local`, from the thread of the context; the delayed work of the scheduler waits on the default scheduler of the thread, then is posted. | Behaviour | Observables, observers, disposables and scheduled work are not `Send` (reactive.md, section 5.1). | Phase 4 |
| `IProgress<T>` and `Progress<T>` are in the base class library. | `IProgress<T>` is in `ferroui-system`; `Progress<T>`, which posts to the synchronization context, is not ported. | Missing | | Phase 4 |
| `Merge(IObservable<Task<T>>)` has a sink of its own (`Merge<TSource>.Tasks`). | A task is an `ObservableSource` (`linq/merge.rs`): `merge_inner`, `concat_inner` and `switch` take a sequence of tasks, merged as the sequences of their outcomes. | Representation | The notifications are the same; upstream's `Concat` and `Switch` of tasks are `Select(ToObservable)`. | Phase 4 |
| The task forms of `SelectMany`, `Create`, `Defer` and `Using` work on the task the function returns. | They run the future the function returns as a task, and use a `Task` returned as it is (`future_as_task`); `Create` with a `Task<Action>` runs a task that wraps the future. | Representation | | Phase 4 |
| `ForEachAsync` catches a `Dispose` that throws. | Disposables do not fail; an action that fails is a `try_` form. | Representation | | Phase 4 |
| `FromEvent` and `FromEventPattern` add and remove a delegate, and remove it by its identity; the sender of an event pattern is an `object`. | The add function takes the handler (`Rc<dyn Fn(T)>`, or `Rc<dyn Fn(S, A)>` for a pattern) and returns a token, which the remove function takes, as `ferroui_system::events::Event` does; the sender is a type parameter. The forms that build the delegate type through reflection, and those that find the event by name, are not ported. | Representation | A closure has no identity to remove it by, and Rust has no reflection. | #249 |
| A handler of `ToEvent` or `ToEventPattern` that receives the error of the sequence rethrows it. | It panics with the error. | Behaviour | An event handler has no way to return an error. | #249 |
| `ToListObservable` is an `IObservable<object>` that gives `null`; its `Value`, indexer and list members wait for the source to end and throw its error. | `ListObservable` is a sequence of `()`; its members return the error of the source as `Err`, and panic when the source has not ended. | Representation | Waiting would deadlock the only thread. | #249 |
| The join patterns (`And`, `Then`, `When`) take sequences of any type. | Their values are `Clone`, since a plan may name the same sequence twice; a sequence is recognised by the address of its `Rc`. | Representation | | #249 |
| `Collect`, `Chunkify` and `MostRecent` give an `IEnumerable<T>` whose `MoveNext` throws an error of the source or of a collector, again at each later call. | They give a `PullEnumerable<T>`: `get_enumerator` subscribes (an error of the first collector is its `Err`), and the enumerator is an iterator of `Result<T, ObservableError>` that ends after its first `Err`; dropping it disposes it. | Representation | A Rust iterator reports a failure as an item, and a fused one is safe in a `for` loop. | #249 |
| Recorded notifications of a test compare with predicates through subclasses of `Notification<T>` (`ReactiveTest.OnNext(long, Func<T, bool>)`). | Expected notifications are `Expected<T>`: a notification, or a predicate on a value or an error; `assert_messages!` takes either. | Test | `Notification<T>` is an enum of the public API. | #248 |

### Splat (`src/FerroUI.Splat/`, `tests/FerroUI.Splat.Tests/`)

The port of Splat (`docs/porting/reactive.md`, phase 6). The reference is that project.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The locator (`AppLocator`, `InternalLocator`), the containers of `GlobalGenericFirstDependencyResolver` (`Container<T>`), the mode detector and the state of the builder (`HasBeenBuilt`, `UsingBuilder`) are static state of the process; the override of `WithResolver` flows with the asynchronous context (`AsyncLocal`). | They are state of the thread (`ferro_thread_local!`); the override is that of the thread. | Representation | The resolvers hand out `Rc` services, which belong to their thread (reactive.md, section 5.1). | Phase 6 |
| Services are registered and resolved by `Type` (`GetService(Type)`, `Register(Func<object>, Type)`, `RegisterConstant(object, Type)`, `NullServiceType`) or by generic methods; `FuncDependencyResolver` is built on the `Type` forms. | Only the generic forms (`get_service::<S>`, `register::<S>`, ...), keyed by the `TypeId` of the service type, which may be a trait object; the `Type` forms, `NullServiceType` and `FuncDependencyResolver` are not ported, nor `Register<TService, TImplementation>()`. A registration counts once, where upstream mirrors a generic one in its `Type` registry and counts it twice. | Missing | Without reflection a `Type` names no constructor; a generic `Rc<I>` does not coerce to `Rc<dyn S>` in a generic method, so the factory forms take the coerced service. | Phase 6 |
| A constant that is `IDisposable` is disposed with the resolver. | `register_constant_disposable` and `register_lazy_singleton_disposable` are the forms whose values are disposed. | Representation | There is no run-time test for a trait. | Phase 6 |
| The typed overloads of the loggers (`Debug<TArgument>(..)` and the rest, about 5,000 generated lines) format their arguments when the level is enabled. | Generic methods that take a closure or `format_args!`, called only when the level is enabled. | Representation | Rust formats lazily with closures. | Phase 6 |
| An exception of a logged action, of a feature usage tracking action or of a releasing function is caught or propagated; `MemoizingMRUCache.InvalidateAll` throws an `AggregateException`. | A panic, caught with `catch_unwind` where upstream catches; `invalidate_all_with(true)` returns an `AggregateReleaseError`. | Representation | | Phase 6 |
| `ModeDetector` also scans the loaded assemblies for test frameworks, and reads the `AppContext` switch and the main module of a `dotnet` host. | Those checks are left out; the environment variables are read. | Missing | No reflection over assemblies; the others are .NET's. | Phase 6 |
| `Guid` of .NET. | A version 4 identifier of the crate, made from the random keys of the standard library's hasher. | Representation | The standard library has no `Guid`. | Phase 6 |
| `MemoizingMRUCache` takes a lock. | Single-threaded, with lookups linear in the size of the cache. | Representation | | Phase 6 |

### DynamicData (`src/FerroUI.DynamicData/`, `tests/FerroUI.DynamicData.Tests/`)

The port of DynamicData (`docs/porting/reactive.md`, section 5.4). The reference is that project, not upstream.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Caches, lists, their operators and the binding adaptors may be used from any thread. Edits run under a lock, and `DeliveryQueue`, `SharedDeliveryQueue` and `SynchronizeSafe` deliver the notifications outside it, one at a time. | Everything is single-threaded (`Rc`, `RefCell`). `DeliveryQueue` and `SharedDeliveryQueue` keep upstream's algorithm without the locks: the lock is a scope (`DeliverySubQueue::acquire`), the scope that found the queue idle drains it, a source that re-enters it delivers inline, and a notification raised by an observer during a delivery is delivered after the current one. The version guards of `Connect` and `Watch` are kept. `SynchronizeSafe` with a lock that several operators share gives each subscription a queue of its own, and the internal `Synchronize` returns its source. | Representation | The object model of FerroUI is single-threaded (`reactive.md`, sections 5.1 and 5.4); on one thread a lock serializes nothing that is not serialized already, and the queue keeps the order of notifications that the tests check. | #253 |
| `Page`, `Virtualise`, `Sort`, `SortAndPage`, `SortAndVirtualize`, `BatchIf` and `QueryWhenChanged` serialize their sources (the data, the requests, the comparers, the pause selector, the timers, the triggers) through one `SharedDeliveryQueue`. The list `TransformAsync` serializes its change sets with a `SemaphoreSlim` as well as with `Concat`. | The sources are merged as they come. `batch_if` queues only its deliveries, so that a change set caused by an observer is delivered after the current one. The list `transform_async` relies on `Concat` alone to handle one change set at a time. A `SortException` of `sort`, `sort_and_page` or `sort_and_virtualize` ends the sequence with that error, as upstream's exception reaches `OnError`. | Representation | On one thread the notifications of the sources cannot interleave. | #253 |
| The static properties of `DynamicDataOptions` (`Binding`, `SortAndBind`) are global. | They are per thread (`ferro_thread_local!`). | Behaviour | The observables that read them are per thread (`reactive.md`, section 5.1). A default set on one thread is not seen by another. | #253 |
| `Change<TObject, TKey>` writes `-1` for a `CurrentIndex` or `PreviousIndex` that is not known. | The indexes are `Option<usize>`. | Representation | The plan's representation of a change (`reactive.md`, section 5.4). | #253 |
| Counts are `int`; `PageRequest` (`Page`, `Size`) and `VirtualRequest` (`StartIndex`, `Size`) hold `int`s: a negative page or size throws `ArgumentException`, and the operators ignore a negative virtual request. | The counts of `count_cache`, `count_list` and `count_aggregate` are `usize`, and the parts of the requests are `usize`, so a negative request cannot be made. Two requests are equal when their parts are (`PageRequest.DefaultComparer`, `VirtualRequest.StartIndexSizeComparer`). | Representation | The type of counts and indexes in Rust. `ThrowsForNegativePage` and `ThrowsForNegativeSizeParameters` have no counterpart. | #253 |
| `UpdateIndex` takes and gives `ISortedChangeSet`. `Bind` has an overload for `IObservable<ISortedChangeSet<TObject, TKey>>`, chosen at compile time for the sorted, paged and virtual change sets, which returns the sorted change sets. | `update_index` gives change sets of the type of its source (sorted, paged or virtual). `bind` tells a sorted source by its type when it runs and binds it with the sorted adaptor; its result is a sequence of plain change sets in both cases. | Representation | The operators are provided methods of one trait over every kind of change set (`reactive.md`, section 5.4). | #253 |
| `And`, `Cast`, `OfType`, `Switch`, `Count`, `IsEmpty` and `Sum` of caches, lists and aggregate change sets; `Top(int size)` of a sorted stream. | An operator whose name is that of an operator of `ferroui_reactive::ObservableExt`, which applies to the same sequences, takes the suffix of its kind: `and_cache`, `cast_cache`, `of_type_cache`, `switch_cache`, `count_cache`, `is_empty_cache`, `sum_cache`; `and_list`, `cast_list`, `switch_list`, `count_list`, `is_empty_list`, `sum_list`; `count_aggregate`, `sum_aggregate`. `And` has the suffix on the collections of sources too. `Avg`, `Maximum`, `Minimum`, `StdDev` and `IsNotEmpty` keep their names. The `Top` of a sorted stream is `top_sorted`. | Representation | Both traits can be in scope at once; `and` would hide the join pattern `ObservableExt::and`, and `top` is the operator of every cache, which a sorted stream also has. | #253 |
| `ObservableCacheAlias` and `ObservableListAlias` give the operators LINQ names (`Select`, `Where`, `SelectMany`). | Not ported. | Missing | The names are those of operators of `ObservableExt`, which apply to the same sequences. | #253 |
| Overloads that differ by their arguments: the factory of `TransformAsync` (the item, its key, the replaced item, a `CancellationToken`), an observable of predicates, `TransformAsyncOptions`, the `transformOnRefresh` flag of the list `TransformAsync`, an `IComparer` or an `IEqualityComparer`, and default arguments (`emptyValue` of the aggregations). | Methods of their own names: `_with_key`, `_with_previous`, `_with_token`, `_forced` and `_with_options` (`_and_options` after another suffix) for `transform_async`; `transform_async_with`, `transform_async_with_previous_and_index` and `transform_async_with_token` take the flag of the list `TransformAsync`, and its other forms use `false`; `_ordered_by`, `_with_comparer`, `_with_source_comparer` and `_with` elsewhere. `BatchIfFixture.SupportedCallShapesRemainUnambiguous` checks that each method of `batch_if` is available. | Representation | Rust has no overloading (`reactive.md`, section 5.3). | #253 |
| The static overloads of `And`, `Or`, `Xor`, `Except` and `MergeChangeSets` over a collection of sources (`ICollection<IObservable<IChangeSet>>`, an enumerable, an `IObservableList` of sequences, of caches or of source caches or lists, a sequence of sequences), and the overloads of `Switch` on a sequence of containers or of sequences of change sets. | Methods of extension traits on those collections: a sequence with others (provided methods of `ObservableCacheEx` and `ObservableListEx`), a slice or a `Vec` (`CacheSourcesCombineEx`, `ListSourcesCombineEx`, `CacheChangeSetSourcesEx`, `ListChangeSetSourcesEx`), an observable list (`CacheSourceListCombineEx`, `ListSourceListCombineEx`), a sequence of sequences (`ObservableCacheChangeSetsEx`, `ObservableListChangeSetsEx`). A source is any `CacheChangeSetSource` or `ListChangeSetSource`, a sequence of change sets or a container whose `Connect()` is its sequence, so `switch_cache` and `switch_list` are one method each (`SwitchCacheEx`, `SwitchListEx`). The combiners of lists count the items in a dictionary, so the items are `Eq + Hash`. | Representation | An overload on the type of a collection is a trait implemented for that collection. | #253 |
| `MergeManyChangeSets` is overloaded by the kind of change sets the selector returns; `TransformMany` and `TransformManyAsync` by the kind of children (an enumerable, an `ObservableCollection<T>`, a `ReadOnlyObservableCollection<T>`, an observable cache); `AsAggregator` by the type of the change set, groups included. | The name says it: `merge_many_change_sets*` and `merge_many_list_change_sets*` on caches, `merge_many_change_sets*` and `merge_many_cache_change_sets*` on lists; `transform_many_observable_collection`, generic over the collection (`ObservableCollectionEx`); `transform_many_async*` for a `Vec`, `transform_many_async_observable_collection*` and `transform_many_async_observable_cache*`; `as_group_aggregator` of `GroupChangeSetAggregatorEx`. | Representation | Rust does not choose a method by what a closure returns, and `as_aggregator` of `ObservableCacheEx` applies to sequences of groups too. | #253 |
| `ChangeSet<TObject, TKey, TContext>` and the two classes `ObservableCollectionAdaptor` share a name with types of fewer type parameters. | `ChangeSetWithContext`, which dereferences to its `ChangeSet` as upstream's derives from it; `ListObservableCollectionAdaptor` for lists. | Representation | Rust has no overloading of a type by the number of its parameters. | #253 |
| The change sets of groups hold the interfaces `IGroup` and `IGrouping`; the classes behind them are internal. | Sorted caches hold `KeyValuePair<TKey, TObject>`s; `KeyComparer.IndexOf` and `SortExtensions.GetCurrentPosition` find a pair or an item with the default equality (that of `KeyValuePair` compares the key and the item); `SortedKeyValueApplicator` takes a `SortAndBindOptions`. | A pair is the tuple `(key, item)` (`.0` is `Key`, `.1` is `Value`), and a `KeyValueCollection` shares its pairs, so cloning it is cheap. A pair is found by its key, as a sorted cache holds one pair per key; `get_current_position` takes the equality as a parameter. The applicator takes the two parts of the options it reads (`ApplicatorOptions`). | Representation | The items need no `PartialEq`. | #253 |
| The list `Sort` finds an item already in its place by reference (`ReferenceEquals`). | It finds it by equality. | Behaviour | A generic item has no identity. Two equal items in each other's place are not moved. | #253 |
| The keys of a sorted cache that the comparer finds equal are ordered by `IComparable<TKey>` when the key has it, otherwise by their hash codes; strings compare by the current culture. | The standard types that have an order (the strings, the integers, `char`, `bool`) are found at run time and compared by it, the strings by their code points (ordinal). | `PageContext<TObject>.Empty` and `VirtualContext<TObject>.Empty` hold `Comparer<TObject>.Default`. | The comparer of `PageContext::empty` and `VirtualContext::empty` finds every pair equal. | Behaviour | There is no default comparer for every item. | #253 |
| A `Node` of `TransformToTree` references its parent. | A node holds its parent weakly; the parent is the node of the tree, which the tree keeps. | Representation | A parent and its children would keep each other alive. | #253 |
| `DisposeMany` (of caches and lists) and `AsyncDisposeMany` test the type of an item at run time (`IDisposable`, `IAsyncDisposable`, `IDisposable` preferred when both). `IAsyncDisposable` is a type of the base library, and `DisposeAsync` gives a `ValueTask`. | The type of the items says how they are disposed: the traits `AsDisposable` and `AsAsyncDisposable` are implemented for every `Rc` of a disposable (`Rc<dyn IDisposable>`, `Rc<X>` for `X: IDisposable`, an `Rc` of an `IAsyncDisposable`); another type implements them, or `dispose_many_with` and `async_dispose_many_with` take the function that disposes an item. `IAsyncDisposable` lives in this crate until `ferroui-system` has it, and its `dispose_async` gives a `Task<()>`. | Representation | Rust has no run-time test of a generic type. | #253 |
| `AsyncDisposeMany` catches an exception of `Dispose` and fails the disposals with it; `KeyedDisposable` throws an `AggregateException` of the exceptions of its items; `GroupOn` subscribes the disposal of its groups without an error handler, which rethrows an error. | `IDisposable::dispose` cannot fail, so only the tasks of asynchronous disposals fail the disposals; a panic of a disposal is not caught. `KeyedDisposable` panics with a message that counts the failures. | `InnerJoin` reads the prior key of a refreshed right item with the indexer, which throws for a key never added. `InnerJoin`, `LeftJoin` and `RightJoin` do not dispose the grouped cache or the cache of the foreign keys, which stays subscribed to the shared right side. | Such a refresh (of an invalid change set) is forwarded as a refresh. | `IgnoreSameReferenceUpdate`, the combiners of caches and the update of `TransformMany` compare items with `ReferenceEquals`. `MergeChangeSets` and `MergeManyChangeSets` (with and without a comparer of the sources) apply `IgnoreSameReferenceUpdate` to their sources. | The reference of an item is the object its pointer refers to (`T: Deref`, an `Rc<..>` for upstream's classes): two items are the same reference when they point to the same object. `ignore_same_reference_update` and the combiners of caches require `T: Deref`. | `GroupOnDynamic` takes a new group selector when it differs from the last (delegates compare by method and target). | A selector is the same when it is the same `Rc`. | Behaviour | A closure has no equality; two closures over the same function and target are different selectors. | #253 |
| The forms of `Or`, `And`, `Xor` and `Except` on an `IObservableList` of caches, source caches, lists or source lists first make an observable list of their `Connect()` sequences. | `connect` is applied as the containers are made. | Representation | It subscribes the same sequences in the same order. | #253 |
| `LimitSizeTo` orders the items by `DateTime.Now` at their addition or update, and leaves the items stamped within the same tick in the order of its dictionary. | The items are ordered by the order in which they were added or updated. | Behaviour | Items changed within the same tick of the clock keep the order of their changes. | #253 |
| `StdDev` of integral values computes its products in `decimal`. | In `i128`, with the quotient of the sum of the squared deviations by `count * (count - 1)` as its integral part plus its fraction in `f64`. | Representation | The result is exact under the same condition as upstream's: the accumulators have not overflowed. | #253 |
| The aggregations (`Sum`, `Avg`, `StdDev`, the numbers of `Accumulate`) have overloads for `decimal`. | Not ported, with their tests (`ForDecimal`, `ForNullableDecimal`, the `Decimal` rows). | Missing | No decimal type in the crates the port depends on yet (phase 1b). | #253 |
| The asynchronous factories and selectors of `TransformAsync`, `TransformSafeAsync`, `TransformManyAsync` and `TransformManySafeAsync`, of caches and lists, return a `Task<..>`; an exception of the task is the error. | They return a future of `Result<.., ObservableError>`, which runs as a task of the thread (`Observable::from_async_with_token`; for `transform_many_async`, when the children are subscribed); its `Err` is upstream's exception. | A selector, factory or predicate that throws reaches `OnError`. `Optional<T>.ValueOrThrow` throws the exception. | The selectors that can fail are the `try_` forms of the operators (`try_transform`, `try_transform_with_key`, `try_transform_with_previous` and its forced forms, `try_transform_immutable`, `try_transform_with_inline_update`, `try_filter_immutable`, `try_group_on_observable`, `ObservableChangeSet::try_create_cache` and `try_create_list`, `try_transform_with_previous_and_index` of lists), whose `Err` ends the sequence; the plain forms cannot fail. `Optional<T>` is `Option<T>`, and `value_or_throw` returns the exception as the `Err` of the result. | Representation | Rust has no exceptions; a failure that is part of the sequence is a value (`reactive.md`, section 5.2). | #253 |
| The time selectors of `ExpireAfter`, the key and expiration selectors of `ToObservableChangeSet`, the getters of `WhenPropertyChanged` and the reevaluator of `AutoRefreshOnObservable` may throw. | They have no `try_` form and cannot fail. | An exception that an observer throws is caught by the combiners (and by the creation of a combiner) and reported as an error; `ValidateSynchronization` of the tests delivers it as an error too; `WhenPropertyChanged` detaches its handler when the initial observer throws; an error the subscriber does not handle is rethrown to the caller. | A panic of an observer is not caught and propagates through the subscription. `OnItemRemovedFixture` checks that the panic reaches the caller with the message of the error rather than the identity of the error. `*_InitialObserverThrows_Detaches*` are not ported. | Behaviour | A panic is Rust's form of an unhandled exception (`reactive.md`, sections 5.2 and 5.4). | #253 |
| `UnspecifiedIndexException`, `KeySelectorException`, `MissingKeyException` and the messages of `SortException` and of the failures of `Transform` and `TransformWithInlineUpdate` name the item or the key. | The exception types are not types of the port: it panics, or ends the sequence with an `ObservableError` carrying their message. The messages of `transform`, `transform_with_inline_update`, `sort_and_bind` and the sorted caches name the type of the item, or nothing, rather than the item or the key. `EnumerableIListFixture.ExceptionTests` is not ported. | Behaviour | Naming the item or the key would need `T: Debug` or `K: Debug`. | #253 |
| The list `RemoveIndex` gives a refresh without an index, for which `Change` throws; the list `TransformMany` fails for a refresh of an item that was not transformed. | `remove_index` keeps the index of a refresh, which the change of a refresh needs. A refresh without a previous item changes no item in `transform_many`. | Behaviour | A refresh is passed on instead of failing. | #253 |
| `ObservablePropertyFactory` changes the value when the name of a notification is that of the property. | A notification of every property (an empty name, `null` upstream) changes the value too, as the steps of a `PropertyPath` see it. | Behaviour | A notification of every property changes the property. | #253 |
| `AbstractNotifyPropertyChanged` is an abstract class; `SetAndRaise` takes the property name from `[CallerMemberName]`. | It is a part that a type embeds: the type implements `INotifyPropertyChanged` by returning `property_changed`, and its setters call `set_and_raise` with the name, the PascalCase name of the getter that `prop!` derives. | Representation | Rust has no inheritance and no caller information. | #253 |
| The reference checkout has no `AbstractNotifyPropertyChanged.SuspendNotifications`; an earlier API marked it obsolete ("This never worked properly in the first place"). | `suspend_notifications` is ported from that API; the suspension is one flag, so the first disposal of nested suspensions ends it. | Addition | Kept from the earlier API. | #253 |
| `ObservableCollectionExtended<T>` is a subclass of `ObservableCollection<T>` that intercepts the notifications of every change. `ReadOnlyObservableCollection<T>` is a type of the base library that raises the notifications of the collection it wraps with itself as the sender. | `ObservableCollectionExtended` is a handle over an `ObservableCollection`: the changes made through `collection()` directly are not suspended. `ReadOnlyObservableCollection` lives in this crate until `ferroui-system` has it, and shares the notifications of the collection it wraps. | Behaviour | Rust has no subclasses, and the wrapper raises nothing of its own. | #253 |
| `IObservableCollection<T>` and `IIndexAware` are implemented by objects (references); `IIndexAware.Index` is an `int` property.
