| `SortExpressionComparer<T>` holds getters `Func<T, IComparable>` and puts `null` first. | It holds each expression as the comparison of the values it reads, which need `PartialOrd`; `Option` puts `None` first, and two values without an order (a `NaN`) compare as equal. | Representation | Rust has no common type of the values of different getters. | #253 |
| The tests that check thread safety with real threads (`MultiThreadedStressTest`, `*_IsThreadSafe`, the concurrent tests of `DeliveryQueue`, `SharedDeliveryQueue` and `CacheParentSubscription`, the race-condition tests). | Not ported. | Missing | The port is single-threaded (`reactive.md`, section 5.1). | #253 |
| The tests that check the `ArgumentNullException` or `ArgumentException` of a null or invalid argument (`*IsNull_ThrowsException`, `NullChecks`). | Not ported. | Missing | Rust's types make a null argument impossible (`reactive.md`, section 5.7). | #253 |
| `ObservablePropertyFactoryCache` caches the factories of the property expressions that reflection parses. | Not ported, with `EquivalentConversionPaths_DifferentParameterNames_ReuseFactory`: a `PropertyChain` is built once, at compile time, by `prop!`. | Missing | Rust has no reflection (`reactive.md`, section 5.5). | #253 |
| `ParallelEx` and the overloads of `Filter` and `Transform` with `ParallelisationOptions` run with PLINQ. | Not ported. `FilterParallelFixture` runs against the sequential `filter`, which gives the same results for `ParallelType.Ordered`; the tests of `TransformFixtureParallel` are not ported. | Missing | The port is single-threaded (`reactive.md`, section 5.4). | #253 |
| `Bind` and `SortAndBind` have forms for `BindingList<T>` (Windows Forms). | Not ported, with `SortAndBindToBindingList`. | Missing | A platform other than FerroUI (`reactive.md`, section 5.4). | #253 |
| The integration tests run their stress on the task pool (`TaskPoolScheduler.Default`, `Environment.ProcessorCount` streams in parallel) and subscribe and unsubscribe between `Task.Yield()`s. | `MergeManyChangeSets` of list change sets on caches and of cache change sets on lists run on one thread, on a `TestScheduler`: the clock advances by a millisecond before each subscription and each unsubscription, the streams are interleaved by their random due times, and their number is fixed. | Test | A seed gives the same run on every machine. | #253 |
| The clocks of the tests start at `DateTimeOffset` year 1, advance to the year 9999, and accept a negative expiration (`TimeSpan`); the recorders default to `GlobalConfig.DefaultScheduler` (the task pool); the work of `FakeScheduler` may be invoked more than once. | `FakeScheduler` starts at the epoch of `SystemTime`; the largest time of `ExpireAfterFixture` is the largest one the clock of `TestScheduler` converts to a `SystemTime` (292 years after the epoch); an expiration already passed is a lifetime of zero, which expires the item at the next management as the negative one does; the recorders default to the default scheduler of the thread, which only stamps the notifications; the work of `FakeScheduler` runs once, and a second invocation returns an empty disposable. | Test | The time types of the standard library (`reactive.md`, section 5.6) and a single-threaded scheduler. | #253 |
| The domain of the tests: Bogus draws the data; `Guid.NewGuid()` is random; `MarketPrice` and the fakers use `decimal`, and the price is stamped with `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow`; `Pet` has settable properties; `PersonWithFriends` compares the enumerable of friends by reference; `PersonWithEmployment` is made from a group. | Bogus is not ported: the fakers use a deterministic generator (SplitMix64) and lists of names and words of their own, so a seed gives the same data on every run but not the data Bogus gives. A `Guid` is a number from a counter of the process, formatted as a GUID. `decimal` is `f64`, and the stamp of a price is a clock of the process that advances with every price set. `Pet` is a value with public fields. Every assignment of friends raises (a `Vec` has no identity), and `AutoRefreshOnOtherProperty` and `DirectRefresh` of `TransformManyRefreshFixture` are not ported, as a person holds its own list. `PersonWithEmployment::new` takes the key and the cache of the group. `TestSpy` is not ported. | Test | The tests check properties of their results rather than particular values (`reactive.md`, section 5.7); the domain does not depend on the types of the port's groups. | #253 |
| Names that sort first by the current culture: `_Aaron` (`SortAndBindFixture`) and `__A` (the list `SortFixture`); the default comparer of `Person` compares names by the culture. | `!Aaron` and `!A`; names compare by their characters (ordinal). | Test | Strings of the port compare by their code points. | #253 |

### ReactiveUI (`src/FerroUI.ReactiveUI/`, `src/FerroUI.ReactiveUI.Testing/`, `tests/FerroUI.ReactiveUI.Tests/`, `tests/FerroUI.ReactiveUI.Testing.Tests/`)

The port of ReactiveUI in its System.Reactive flavour, of the run time of ReactiveUI.Binding, of the System.Reactive extras of ReactiveUI.Primitives and of ReactiveUI.Testing (`docs/porting/reactive.md`, sections 3.1 and 3.2). The reference is those projects, not upstream.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The default packages of ReactiveUI run on its own Rx engine, ReactiveUI.Primitives (`RxVoid`, `ISequencer`, `TaskPoolSequencer`, `Signal<T>`, `MultipleDisposable`). | Not ported. The port is the `Reactive` flavour (`REACTIVE_SHIM`): `Unit`, `IScheduler`, `TaskPoolScheduler` and `CompositeDisposable` of `ferroui-reactive`. | Missing | The owner chose the System.Reactive flavour (`reactive.md`, section 3.1). | #255 |
| The System.Reactive extras of ReactiveUI.Primitives that the flavour calls (`ReturnSignal<T>`, `ImmutableReturnRxVoidSignal`, `FromAsyncSignal<T>`, `EmptyDisposable`, `ActionDisposable`, `OnceDisposable`, `SwapDisposable`, `MutableDisposable`, `MultipleDisposable`, the `IsDisposed` interface); `Broadcaster<T>` removes an observer by reference (`Remove(IObserver<T>)`). | Each maps to the type or operator of `ferroui-reactive` with the same behaviour (`Observable::return_`, `Observable::from_async`, `Disposable::empty`, `Disposable::create`, `SingleAssignmentDisposable`, `SerialDisposable`, `CompositeDisposable`); `IsDisposed` is `ICancelable` (`IReactiveProperty`). Only `Broadcaster`, `StartSignal`, `IThreadAffineSequencer` and `DelayableNotificationSignal` are ported as types of their own (`primitives/`, `reactive_object/`). `Broadcaster::add` returns an id and `remove` takes it, so disposing the same subscription twice removes it once. | Representation | One implementation of each operator; an observer trait object is removed by its id. | #255 |
| A C# source generator reads each lambda at build time and writes direct reads and subscriptions in place of the stubs of `WhenAnyValue`, `WhenChanged`, `Bind`, `OneWayBind`, `BindTo`, `BindCommand`, `BindInteraction`, `ToProperty` and of the view dispatch; ReactiveUI.SourceGenerators writes the properties of `[Reactive]` and `[ObservableAsProperty]`. | `macro_rules!` macros expand at compile time: `prop!` gives a `PropertyPath` (the getter and the property name of each step), `settable_prop!` a `SettableProperty`, `reactive_properties!` the fields marked `#[reactive]` with their getters and setters, `reactive_object!` the traits of a reactive object, `view_for!` the traits of a view. The bindings and observers are methods of the run time that take the paths as values (`PropertyBindingMixins`, `SourceBindingMixins`); a view model stores the helper that `to_property` returns and reads it in its getter. The tests of the generators are replaced by tests of the macros. | Representation | Rust has no source generators; a macro expands a path into a getter and a name, which is the same idea (`reactive.md`, sections 3.2 and 5.5). | #255 |
| The generated code ignores every registered converter of affinity 2 or less (the built-in ones), because it writes the same conversions inline (`ToString`, `int.TryParse`, `ToString` with a format hint for a string target). | The built-in converters convert between two different types; between two values of one type only a converter of a higher affinity is used. With no converter (and no `converter_override`), a value passes only when it already is of the target's type, and is dropped otherwise (`binding_conversion`). | Behaviour | No generator writes the conversions inline. | #255 |
| The `ToProperty` scenarios of the generated-code tests differ by how the generated code raises the owner's notifications (`PartialEvent`, `NameOf`, `InitialValueDeferScheduler`, `FactoryOutResult`, `PartialProtectedBase`, `PublicRaiseMethod`, `ReactiveObject`); `SummaryViewModel` is a plain `INotifyPropertyChanged`. | `to_property` raises through `IReactiveObject`, so every view model of these tests holds a `ReactiveObject`; the strategies collapse into that one, and the scenarios keep their options (the name, the initial value or its factory, the deferred subscription, the scheduler). | Test | There is one way to raise the notifications of an owner. | #255 |
| The generated `WhenChanged` of a deep chain drops the repeat of a leaf when an intermediate object is replaced by one with an equal leaf. | The port observes as upstream's run-time observation (`ExpressionChainSink`) does: the re-attached step reports the leaf of the new object even when it is equal, so the value comes again once (`when_changed_tests_deep_chain`). | Behaviour | The port has one engine of observation, that of the run time. | #255 |
| Bindings and observers take an `Expression<Func<..>>`; `ObservableForProperty<TValue>(string, ..)` reads the property by reflection; `IObservedChange.Expression` is the member expression. | They take a `PropertyPath`; `observable_for_property_named` takes the getter of the property; `IObservedChange::expression` is the text of the path (`Address.Street`). The `ToProperty` forms without an initial value start from `T::default()`, as upstream starts from `default(T)`. | Representation | No reflection or expression trees (`reactive.md`, section 5.5). | #255 |
| `WhenAnyValue`, `WhenAny`, `WhenChanged`, `WhenChanging` and `WhenAnyObservable` have overloads of 1 to 16 expressions (2 to 16 with a selector), with values of `PropertyValues<T1, ..., Tn>`; `WithInstance` has overloads for actions of 1 to 16 arguments. | One argument: a path or a tuple of 2 to 16 paths (`WhenAnyPaths`, `WhenAnyObservablePaths`, `WhenAnyObservableCombinePaths`), whose values are the tuple `(V1, ..., Vn)`; the action of `with_instance` takes the services as one argument, a tuple for more than one, whose type says what to resolve (`ResolveServices`). | Representation | Rust has no overloading; the tuples are implemented by macros. | #255 |
| A binding takes one expression for the property it writes, which the generator turns into an assignment. | The written property is a `SettableProperty`: a property path with a setter. `settable_prop!` writes it with the setter `set_<last getter>` of the owner of the last property; `SettableProperty::new` takes any setter. | Representation | A getter path cannot be assigned to. | #255 |
| An observed chain produces nothing while an intermediate value is `null`. | A step whose object may be absent is a step of an `Option` (`PropertyPath::optional_property`) and produces `None`. | Behaviour | A path of the port says by its type where a value may be missing. | #255 |
| The property observers (`ICreatesObservableForProperty.GetAffinityForObject`) and the activation fetchers (`IActivationForViewFetcher.GetAffinityForView`) compute their affinity on a `Type`, with the member `Expression`; `ResolveNotifyFactory` and `ResolveActivationFetcher` memoize the choice per type (and property) in a `MemoizingMRUCache`, which `ResetActivationFetcherCacheForTesting` clears. | The affinity is computed on the object: a `PropertyOwner` (the type-erased owner of the property, with its `PropertyChanged` when it has one) for the observers, the view for the fetchers; a step is named by its property name. It is computed each time a step is observed or a view sets up its activation, so there is no cache, and `RxAppBuilder::reset_for_testing` has none to clear. `GetAffinityForView_WithICanActivate_Returns10` and `GetAffinityForView_WithNonICanActivate_Returns0` are not ported. | Representation | Without reflection a `Type` cannot be asked what it implements. | #255 |
| While no `ICreatesObservableForProperty` is registered, observing a property fails; `DefaultViewLocator`'s `this.Log()` throws without a log manager. | `observers_for_property` uses the default observers of `ReactiveUIBindingModule` (`INPCObservableForProperty`, `POCOObservableForProperty`) while none is registered; the view locator logs only when the service locator has a log manager. | Behaviour | A view model and a view locator work before the application is built, as in a unit test. | #255 |
| The observations hold their source strongly and the garbage collector collects the cycle of an object and the observation of its properties; values compare with `EqualityComparer<T>.Default`. | `ExpressionChainParameters`, `ExpressionChainSink`, `ObservableForPropertySink`, `NotifyPropertyChangedObservable`, `PluginPropertyObservable`, `PropertyChangingObservable`, `PropertyObservable` and the change observables of `IReactiveObject` (`ChangeArgsObservable`) hold their source weakly: once it is dropped they produce nothing more (the getter of `PluginPropertyObservable` returns `None`), and a change raised while the object is dropped is not delivered. `ExpressionChainSink` holds the intermediate objects weakly too, and reads them again from the root when a step notifies. A view model holds its screen by a `Weak`, and `host_screen` is an `Option` (`None` once the screen is gone; the tests that return `null!` return `None`). | The tests that check thread safety with real threads (`*_ConcurrentFirstRead_*`, `SendMessage_ConcurrentCalls_ThreadSafe`, `ConcurrentReads_DuringRegistration_ShouldBeThreadSafe`, `AutoPersist_StartedAndDisposedFromManyThreads_NeverSavesAfterDisposal`). | Not ported. | Missing | The port is single-threaded (`reactive.md`, section 5.1). | #255 |
| The tests that check the `ArgumentNullException` or `ArgumentException` of a null or invalid argument (`*_Null*_Throws*`, `WithInstance_*_handles_null_action`, `WithInstance_*_throws_when_instance_null`), and the null checks of the arguments in `ReactiveCommand`, the builders, `CommandInvoker`, `ViewLocatorMixins`, `InvokeCommand` and `SwitchSubscribe`. | Not ported; the null checks have no counterpart. | Missing | Rust's types make a null argument impossible (`reactive.md`, section 5.7). | #255 |
| The built-in converters of `DateTime`, `DateTimeOffset`, `DateOnly`, `TimeOnly`, `Uri` and `Decimal` (`RegisterDefaults`). | Not ported, with their tests. | Missing | Those types wait for `ferroui-system` (phase 1b). | #255 |
| The platforms other than FerroUI: `WithDrawing` (ReactiveUI.Drawing), `WithBlazor` and `WithBlazorWasm` (ReactiveUI.Blazor), `ItemsControlTemplateBinding` (the `ItemsControl` of WPF and WinUI). | Not ported, with their tests. | Missing | A platform other than FerroUI (`reactive.md`, section 5.7). | #255 |
| The serialization of reactive objects, records and routers (`ReactiveObjectShouldntSerializeAnythingExtra`, `ReactiveRecordShouldSerializeCorrectly`, `ReactiveRecordShouldNotSerializeInternalReactiveProperties`; `[DataMember]` and `[JsonRequired]` on the test fixtures). | Not ported. | Missing | No reflection-based serializer in the port. | #255 |
| Formatting with the current culture (`TestCulture_WithoutOverride_UsesEnglishCulture`, `TryConvert_FrenchCulture_UsesCurrentCulture`, `TryConvert_CommaDecimalCulture_UsesCurrentCulture`). | Not ported. | Missing | The port has no current culture; it formats with the invariant culture. | #255 |

### ReactiveUI controls (`src/FerroUI.ReactiveUI.Controls/`)

The reference is the reference integration, the integration of ReactiveUI with the reference UI framework (the ReactiveUI repository for it), together with the parts of ReactiveUI.Binding for that framework and the scheduler of ReactiveUI.Primitives for it.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ReactiveUserControl<TViewModel>` and `ReactiveWindow<TViewModel>` are generic classes, which markup creates and names; each overrides `IsValidViewModelValue` to take only a `TViewModel` from its `DataContext`. | The classes are non-generic, `ReactiveUserControlBase` and `ReactiveWindowBase`, which markup creates and names (`register_types`). `ReactiveUserControl<VM>` and `ReactiveWindow<VM>` are aliases of a typed handle over an instance, `ReactiveView`: it dereferences to the control, gives and takes the view model as `Rc<VM>`, and sets a filter on the instance so that it takes only view models of type `VM` from its data context; shared as an `Rc`, it is the control's `IViewFor<TViewModel>` (`into_view_for_of`). The code behind a document types the instance with `from_control`. | Representation | A class of the object model is not generic: `ferro_class!` gives each class one type descriptor. | #256 |
| A view is a control, and the integration reaches the control behind a view with `view as Control`. | A control is an object of the class model (`Ref<T>`) and a view of ReactiveUI is a shared trait object (`Rc<dyn IViewFor>`), so a control is seen as a view through an adapter: `ControlViewFor` for a control class that states its view model (`IControlViewFor`), `ReactiveViewFor` and `ReactiveView` for the reactive views. Each adapter type registers itself on the thread (`register_view_control`), so that untyped code finds the control behind any view (`view_control`). | Representation | A trait object cannot be cast to a class of the object model. | #256 |
| The `DataContext` of a view holds the view model object itself. | A value of a property is a `BoxedValue` (`Rc<dyn AnyValue>`), which needs `PartialEq`. The types of view models are registered per thread (`register_view_model`; the typed views and `ControlViewFor` register theirs); a view model of a type that was never registered is boxed in an `UntypedViewModel`, which compares by identity and is unwrapped again on the way back. The bindings of markup see the view model itself only for registered types. | Behaviour | An `Rc<dyn Any>` cannot be turned into a `BoxedValue` without its type. | #256 |
| An untyped object is tested with `is FerroObject`. | `FerroObjectHandles` recognises the handles of the classes through the type model of the framework (`object_of`, `handle_of`); a view of another type registers its type (`register`, `register_class`). | Representation | Rust cannot ask a `dyn Any` whether it is a class of the object model. | #256 |
| `FerroObjectObservableForProperty` computes its affinity on a `Type` with the member expression, and its changes carry the value; `FerroPropertySubscription` is a record of the sender and the handler, disposed by a `Scope`. | The affinity is computed on the object, as every observer of the port does, and the registered property is found by the class of the object. The changes carry no value, as those of every observer of the port: the reader reads the value from the object. `FerroPropertySubscription` is itself the disposable, and the handler is its token; only the first disposal removes it. | Representation | No reflection or expression trees (the ReactiveUI section). | #256 |
| An expression such as `x => x.NameBox.Text` reaches the property of a control, which the observer of the integration observes through the property system. | Additions for the integration. In `ferroui-system`: `PropertyPath::owned_property`, a path of one property whose owner and observation the caller gives; `PropertyPath::object_property`, a path of one property of an `Rc` that does not raise `PropertyChanged` itself, whose step gives the object as its owner for an observer chosen at run time; and the arm `prop!(object Type => getter)` of the macro, which writes it. In the crate: `ferro_property`, the macro `ferro_prop!` and `ferro_property_owner`, the steps of the properties of FerroUI objects, joined to the path of a view with `PropertyPath::then`. | Addition | A FerroUI object is a handle (`Ref<T>`), not an `Rc` of a type that raises `PropertyChanged`, so its steps are written by the caller. | #256 |
| `FerroScheduler` may be scheduled from any thread; its shared instance and the schedulers of other dispatchers are static. | The work is not `Send`: the scheduler posts its drains with `Dispatcher::post_local` and is used on the thread of its dispatcher. The shared instance (`instance`) and the schedulers of other dispatchers (`for_dispatcher`) are kept per thread. | Behaviour | The observables of the port are single-threaded (`reactive.md`, section 5.1). | #256 |
| The constructor of `FerroScheduler` that takes the posting and the timers of a test (internal). | Not ported. | Missing | The tests drive the scheduler through the dispatcher of the unit test application. | #256 |
| The dispatcher of the reference framework installs its synchronization context on the user-interface thread itself. | `FerroScheduler::install_on_ui_thread` installs the dispatcher's `FerroSynchronizationContext` as the context of `ferroui-system`, which also makes it the thread poster of the thread; the tasks and the reactive libraries read that context. | Representation | The base crate of FerroUI keeps its own current context, apart from that of `ferroui-system`. | #256 |
| `FerroViewThreadInvoker` derives from `SequencerViewThreadInvoker<FerroObject, FerroScheduler>`, which claims the targets of the type `FerroObject`. | The invoker implements `IViewThreadInvoker` itself, with the same members: a target is any untyped form of a FerroUI object (`FerroObjectHandles::object_of`), and its scheduler is `FerroScheduler::for_dispatcher` of its dispatcher. The shared instance is per thread, as the objects are. | Representation | A FerroUI object comes in several untyped forms, which a generic base over one type cannot claim. | #256 |
| `FerroCreatesCommandBinding` sets the `ReactiveCommand` itself as the `Command` of a control, with the parameter as the same `object` on both sides. | The command is set through a `ReactiveCommandAdapter`, which bridges the `ICommand` of ReactiveUI and that of the controls. The parameter is converted by `boxed_parameter`: a `BoxedValue` or a handle of a FerroUI object as it is, a string, a number, a character or a boolean boxed as itself so that it compares as the value, anything else in a `ReactiveCommandParameter`. | Representation | The port has two `ICommand` traits, and its `Rc<dyn Any>` does not compare. | #256 |
| `FerroCreatesCommandBinding` asks whether a type is an `ICommandSource`, and binds to an event whose arguments are of the type it is given (`TEventArgs`), throwing for an event of other arguments. | A type is a command source when its class registers the `Command` property of buttons; an instance, when it is an `ICommandSource` with that property. The event is found by its name alone, so an event of other arguments is bound; `BindCommandToObject_ToAnEventWithOtherArguments_Throws` is not ported. | Behaviour | `is ICommandSource` cannot be asked of a type, and the trait of the port has no `TEventArgs` (the ReactiveUI section). | #256 |
| `FerroActivationForViewFetcher` computes its affinity on the type of the view; the activation follows `Loaded` and `Unloaded` or the attachment to the visual tree. | The affinity is computed on the view, as every fetcher of the port does. The activation of a `Window` also follows its visibility: hiding the window deactivates it, and showing it again activates it. | Behaviour | The views of a hidden window are not shown. | #256 |
| The extension members of C# (`AppBuilderExtensions`, the `WithFerro` of the ReactiveUI builder and of the binding builder), `UsingSplatModule(new FerroBindingModule())`, and the cast of an `IAppBuilder` to `IReactiveUIBindingBuilder`. | Traits: `AppBuilderExtensions` on the application builder of `ferroui-controls`, `ReactiveUIBuilderFerroExtensions` on ReactiveUI's builder, and the extensions of the binding builder. The body of `FerroBindingModule.Configure` is also a function (`register_binding_integration`), which `with_ferro` registers. The binding builder is found by its address among the builders of the thread (`ReactiveUIBindingBuilder::find`) and returned as its `Rc`. | Representation | Rust has no extension members and cannot cast a trait object to another trait. | #256 |
| `WithFerro` registers the services of FerroUI, and the core services of ReactiveUI are registered when the application is built, after them, unless they were registered before. | `with_ferro` registers the core services first, so that the services of FerroUI come after them in the resolver and win. | Behaviour | The builder of the port registers its core services before the inner builder applies the registrations (the ReactiveUI section). | #256 |
| `WithFerro` sets the task pool scheduler of the thread pool. | The task pool scheduler is the default scheduler of `ferroui-reactive` (`SchedulerDefaults::default_scheduler`). | Behaviour | There is no thread pool for the single-threaded observables (`reactive.md`, sections 5.1 and 5.8). | #256 |
| `AutoSuspendHelper` is a class that the application keeps and disposes. | The helper is shared (`Rc`) and released with `dispose`. | Representation | The helper keeps a weak reference to itself (`this`) for the subscriptions it makes. | #256 |
| `AutoSuspendHelper` subscribes to `AppDomain.UnhandledException` to invalidate the persisted state. | The application calls `on_unhandled_exception` where it handles a failure (a panic hook, the handler of a dispatcher). | Behaviour | Rust has no event of the process for an unhandled error. | #256 |
| On the exit of a controlled lifetime, `AutoSuspendHelper` blocks until the observers dispose the token they are handed (`ManualResetEvent.WaitOne`). | Nothing blocks: an observer persists the state before it returns and disposes the token then, and the helper logs whether that happened. | Behaviour | The threading model of the port has no blocking waits. | #256 |
| `SubscriptionErrors.Throw` rethrows the error of a subscription (`ExceptionDispatchInfo`); `FerroPropertySignal.OnError` throws its error. | Both raise the error again as a panic with its message. | Representation | A panic is Rust's form of an unhandled exception (the ReactiveUI section). | #256 |
| `GetSubject` and `GetBindingSubject` have overloads on a property and on its typed form, with and without a priority. | The overloads are named by their difference (`_untyped`, `_with_priority`); the signal is the `ISignal` of the `Primitives` flavour, `FerroPropertySignal`. | Representation | Rust has no overloading. | #256 |
| `RoutedViewHost` and `ViewModelViewHost` log with `this.Log()`, which throws without a log manager. | The hosts log only when the service locator has a log manager, as the port's `DefaultViewLocator` does. | Behaviour | The hosts work before the application is built, as in a unit test. | #256 |
| `NavigateToViewModel` of `RoutedViewHost` and `ViewModelViewHost` is internal. | `navigate_to_view_model` is public. | Representation | The tests, in another module, reach it. | #256 |
| `RegisterReactiveUIViews` scans assemblies by reflection for the types that implement `IViewFor<TViewModel>`, reads their `ViewContract` attribute and creates the views with `Activator`. | An assembly is a `ViewAssembly`, the list of the views an application declares (`ViewAssembly::new("App").view::<LoginViewModel, LoginView>(..)`), with each view's view model type, contract and constructor; `ViewRegistrar` registers them. The registration, its contract, the preference of the service locator over the constructor and the skipping of duplicate assemblies are as upstream. | Representation | Rust has no reflection over assemblies or types. | #256 |
| Reflection: `RegisterReactiveUIViewsFromAssemblyOf<TMarker>` and the parameterless `RegisterReactiveUIViewsFromEntryAssembly`, which find assemblies; the tests that inspect types (`AppBuilderExtensions_IsStaticClass`, `FerroObjectReactiveExtensions_IsStaticClass`, `AutoDataTemplateBindingHook_TypeExists`, `AutoSuspendHelper_TypeExists`, `AutoDataTemplateBindingHook_HasExpectedMethods`, `AutoSuspendHelper_HasExpectedProperties`, `ReactiveUIFerro_HasPublicExtensionMethods`, `PublicTypes_HaveParameterlessConstructors`, `ReactiveUserControl_IsGeneric`, `ReactiveWindow_IsGeneric`, `ReactiveWindow_Resolves_Single_Typed_ViewModel_Property`) and the tests of the scanning (`RegisterReactiveUIViewsFromAssemblyOf_Registers_Types`, `RegisterReactiveUIViewsFromAssemblyOf_Returns_Same_Builder`, `RegisterReactiveUIViewsFromEntryAssembly_Returns_Same_Builder`, `RegisterReactiveUIViewsFromEntryAssembly_Does_Not_Throw`, `CreateView_WhenActivatorReturnsNull_ThrowsInvalidOperationException`). | Not ported. The views are declared as `ViewAssembly` lists; the overload of `RegisterReactiveUIViewsFromEntryAssembly` that takes the entry assembly is ported. | Missing | Rust has no reflection over assemblies or types. | #256 |
| `WithFerro_RegistersFerroServices` checks the type of each registered service (`IsTypeOf<..>`). | The services are recognised by what they do: the fetcher gives a control view the affinity of visuals, the hook gives an items control its template, and the view thread invoker is the shared instance. | Test | The traits of the services have no `Any`. | #256 |
| The dependency injection adapters of the integration (Autofac, DryIoc, Ninject, Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection) and their tests (`MixinsAutofacTests`, `MixinsNinjectTests`, `DependencyInjectionTests`, `UseReactiveUIWithDIContainerTests`, `AutofacIsolatedTestExecutor`, `ReactiveDependencyInjectionMixinsCoverageTests`, `MixinsDryIocTests`, `MixinsDryIocMoreTests`, `MixinsMicrosoftTests`, `MixinsMicrosoftMoreTests`). | Not ported. | Missing | The owner tracks dependency injection in issue 251. | #256 |
| The tests that use real threads (`CheckAccess_OnAnotherThread_IsFalse`, `Post_FromAnotherThread_RunsTheCallbackOnTheOwningThread`). | Not ported. | Missing | An object of FerroUI cannot be handed to another thread. | #256 |
| The null checks of the arguments (the builder extensions, `FerroBindingModule.Configure`, `FerroScheduler`, `FerroViewThreadInvoker.Post`, the command binder, `AutoDataTemplateBindingHook`) and their tests (`UseReactiveUI_ThrowsOnNullBuilder`, `UseReactiveUI_WithBuilderOverload_ThrowsOnNulls`, `WithFerro_ThrowsOnNullBuilder`, `WithFerro_NullBuilder_ThrowsArgumentNullException`, `RegisterReactiveUIViews_Throws_On_Null_Builder`, `RegisterReactiveUIViews_ThrowsOnNullBuilder`, `Configure_NullResolver_ThrowsArgumentNullException`, `FerroScheduler_ScheduleWorkItem_ThrowsOnNull`, `FerroScheduler_ScheduleWorkItemWithTimestamp_ThrowsOnNull`, `Post_WithANullCallback_Throws`, `BindCommandToObject_WithHandlers_RejectsNullDelegates`, `ExecuteHook_NullGetCurrentViewProperties_Throws`). | Not ported; the null checks have no counterpart. | Missing | Rust's types make a null argument impossible (`reactive.md`, section 5.7). | #256 |
| The infrastructure of the test projects (`AssemblyHooks`, `TestExecutor`, `TestSession`, `ControlledLifetimeFixture`, `Mocks/`); the files of the `.Reactive.Tests` project that compile the tests of the main project in the System.Reactive flavour (`AutoSuspendHelperShutdownTests`, `ReactiveUserControlActivationTests`). | The unit test application of the framework (`view_support`) replaces the infrastructure, with `ControlledLifetime` for the fixture; `Mocks/` is not ported, as no ported test uses it. The shared files are ported once. | Test | The System.Reactive flavour is the only flavour of the port. | #256 |
| `ViewHostNavigation.ResolveView` asks the view locator's `ResolveView`, which finds the views of the generated dispatch and the mappings; the source generator writes the dispatch of every view of the application. | The hosts resolve views with `resolve_view_unsafe`, which asks the generated and mapped views first and then the service locator, where `ViewRegistrar` registers the views of an application. | Behaviour | Without a source generator, the views that `ViewRegistrar` registers are only in the service locator. | #256 |
| A view in markup is the control itself: it implements `IViewFor<T>` and raises `PropertyChanged` for its `ViewModel`, and the bindings name its elements through generated fields. | A view binds through a `BindableView` of its control, an `Rc` that implements `IViewFor`, `IViewForOf<VM>` and `INotifyPropertyChanged` and follows the control's `ViewModel` property; `element_property`, `owned_element_property` and `element_control` give the paths from the view to the named elements of its document. | Representation | A control of FerroUI is a handle of the object model, while the bindings of `ferroui-reactiveui` take a view shared as an `Rc`. | #256 |

### Samples (`samples/ReactiveDemo/`, `samples/ReactiveUIExample/`)

The references are `ReactiveDemo`, the getting-started sample of ReactiveUI (ReactiveUI.Samples, `wpf/getting-started/ReactiveDemo`, commit `8e7ca27`), a WPF application that uses NuGet.Protocol, and the example application of the reference integration (`src/examples` of the ReactiveUI repository for it). The sources mark each entry with `Deviation (DEVIATIONS.md, samples)`.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The root of a view document is the generic `ReactiveWindow<TViewModel>` or `ReactiveUserControl<TViewModel>` (`x:TypeArguments`): `MainWindow.xaml` and `NugetDetailsView.xaml` of ReactiveDemo, the main window of the example. | The root is the non-generic class markup creates, `ReactiveWindowBase` or `ReactiveUserControlBase`, and the code behind types the instance with its view model (`ReactiveWindow::<AppViewModel>::from_control`, `ReactiveUserControl::<CrateDetailsViewModel>::from_control`, `typed` of the example's `MainWindow`). | Representation | A class of the object model is not generic (the ReactiveUI controls section). | #257 |
| ReactiveDemo's views are the controls themselves: they implement `IViewFor<TViewModel>`, raise `PropertyChanged` for `ViewModel`, and the bindings name their elements through generated fields. | A `CodeBehindView` (`code_behind_view.rs`) is the view of one class: it reads and writes the class's `ViewModel` property, raises `PropertyChanged` when it changes, gives the class to the activation of the integration (`IViewControl`) and holds the class weakly, while the class holds its view. The named elements are reached through getters of the view, which the property paths of the bindings start with (`prop!(MainWindowView => search_text_box)`). | Representation | A class of FerroUI is a handle (`Ref<T>`), while the bindings of `ferroui-reactiveui` take a view shared as an `Rc`. | #257 |
| `App.xaml` of ReactiveDemo names its main window with `StartupUri="MainWindow.xaml"`. | The class of the document (`app.rs`) sets the main window of the desktop lifetime. | Representation | FerroUI has no `StartupUri`; an application creates its main window in its lifetime. | #257 |
| ReactiveDemo's application has no styles of its own, and the example's takes the Fluent theme. | Both samples take the WinUI 3 theme (`ferroui-themes-winui`). | Behaviour | WPF draws its controls itself; the samples show the WinUI 3 theme of FerroUI. | #257 |
| ReactiveDemo finds the classes that implement `IViewFor<TViewModel>` by reflection (`RegisterViewsForViewModels`). | The sample lists its one view, as `ViewRegistrar` of the integration takes it. `MainWindow` is not located, as in the reference, where it is the startup window. | Representation | Rust has no reflection over assemblies or types. | #257 |
| The example's hosts resolve its views through the dispatch that upstream's source generator writes for the view locator. | The views are registered in the service locator and also as the view dispatch of the locator (`ViewRegistrations`), whose mappings the hosts of the port read (`IViewLocator::resolve_view`). | Representation | The port has no source generator. | #257 |
| ReactiveDemo binds the visibility of the list through WPF's global boolean-to-`Visibility` converter, and its search results to `ItemsSource`, which takes any `IEnumerable`. | The boolean is bound to `IsVisible`, which needs no converter; the results are converted to a list of boxed items, the `ItemsSource` of a FerroUI list. | Representation | A control of FerroUI is shown by a boolean, and its items are boxed values. | #257 |
| The text block of `NugetDetailsView.xaml` ends with an inline `Hyperlink`. | The link is a `HyperlinkButton` below the text (`CrateDetailsView.xaml`), a command source as the `Hyperlink` is. | Behaviour | FerroUI has no inline hyperlink. | #257 |
| ReactiveDemo searches the packages of NuGet (NuGet.Protocol's `PackageSearchResource`) and shows the icon, title and description of each package (`NugetDetailsViewModel`, `NugetDetailsView`); `OpenPage` opens the project of the package (`ProjectUrl`), and fails for a package that declares none. | The sample searches the crates of crates.io (`GET https://crates.io/api/v1/crates?q=<term>&per_page=10`, the term percent-encoded, with the descriptive `User-Agent` crates.io requires) and shows the name, version, description and downloads of each crate (`CrateDetailsViewModel`, `CrateDetailsView`); `OpenPage` opens the page of the crate on crates.io (`https://crates.io/crates/<name>`), which every crate has. Without the feature `online`, and in the tests, the in-memory catalogue holds about thirty well-known crates and the crates of FerroUI. | Behaviour | FerroUI is a Rust framework: its libraries are crates, published on crates.io. | #257 |
| The icon of a package is the `Source` of an `Image`, a `BitmapImage` that downloads itself from `IconUrl`, with a default icon for a package that declares none. | A badge drawn in markup, a rounded square with the first letter of the name of the crate (`CrateDetailsViewModel::initial`, bound to the text of the badge); nothing is downloaded. | Behaviour | crates.io has no icons for crates. | #257 |
| `OpenPage` opens the page with `Process.Start(new ProcessStartInfo { UseShellExecute = true, .. })`. | The page is opened with the launcher of the platform (`TopLevel::launcher`); the view model is given a way to reach it (`LauncherSource`): the main window gives its own, a test gives one that records. | Representation | The launcher of FerroUI belongs to a window. | #257 |
| `AppViewModel` searches with NuGet.Protocol in the view model itself; the search method has no parameter. | The search is a service behind a trait (`ICrateSearchService`, `crate_search.rs`), as the reference suggests, given to the view model with the launcher, so that the tests give their own. The view models of the crates are made on the thread of the view model when the search ends. | Representation | The tests run on an in-memory catalogue, and the view models are not `Send`. | #257 |
| The cancellation token of the search is handed to the HTTP client of NuGet.Protocol. | The token stops a search that has not started; a request that has started runs to its end, and the reactive pipeline drops its result when the subscription is gone. | Behaviour | The HTTP client of the sample is blocking and takes no token. | #257 |
| The `Throttle` of the search waits on the default scheduler of the thread pool. | The throttle waits on the main thread scheduler (the timers of the dispatcher), and the search itself runs on a worker thread. | Behaviour | The observables of the port are single-threaded, and the default scheduler of a thread waits by blocking it, which would block the user-interface thread (`reactive.md`, section 5.1). | #257 |
| `ExampleAppBuilderExtensions` is a C# extension block (`extension(AppBuilder builder)`). | The extension is the trait `ExampleAppBuilderExt`; the static class keeps its name. | Representation | Rust has no extension members. | #257 |
| The example resolves its services through the Microsoft dependency injection container (`UseReactiveUIWithMicrosoftDependencyResolver`). | The services are registered in Splat's resolver, which ReactiveUI's builder of FerroUI configures with the core services (`WithCoreServices`). | Representation | The dependency injection adapters are not ported (the ReactiveUI controls section). | #257 |
| The overview names the products of the reference. | The names are those of the port (`ReactiveUI FerroUI`). | Behaviour | The sample shows the port's products. | #257 |
| `ViewModelBase` and `PageViewModel` are base classes; a derived class overrides `Dispose(bool disposing)`. | Each base class is a part the view model holds; `reactive_object!` and `page_view_model!` write the traits a derived class inherits (`ReactiveObject`, `IActivatableViewModel`, `IRoutableViewModel`), and the view model implements `IDisposable` by forwarding to the part. A derived class's `dispose` disposes what it owns, then the part. A page holds its screen weakly. | Representation | Rust has no inheritance; the screen owns the router, whose stack owns the page. | #257 |
| The example's views bind their commands in markup directly to the `ReactiveCommand`s. | The bindings of markup read an adapter of each command (`ReactiveCommandAdapter`), created once by the view model. | Representation | The commands of ReactiveUI and of the controls are two traits in the port (the ReactiveUI controls section). | #257 |
| The asynchronous work of the command lab waits with `Task.Delay`. | The delay is a timer of the dispatcher (`mini_mvvm::delay`). | Behaviour | The tasks of the port run on the thread that starts them. | #257 |
| The metrics come from `System.Diagnostics.Process`; the managed heap is that of the garbage collector; the browser service counts the collections of generation 0 (`GC.CollectionCount(0)`) and reads the uptime from `Environment.TickCount64`. | The measurements come from the operating system (`process_metrics.rs`); the managed heap is the heap of the process as the allocator of the example counts it (`heap_usage.rs`, the global allocator), and the card of the managed heap is named for it. In the browser, the allocations of that allocator stand for the collections, and the uptime is that of the service. | Behaviour | Rust has no managed heap or garbage collector, and a browser has no counterpart of `Environment.TickCount64`. | #257 |
| The interval of the metrics services ticks on the thread pool. | It ticks on the scheduler of the user-interface thread (`RxSchedulers::main_thread_scheduler`), or on the scheduler the service is given. | Behaviour | The port has no thread pool for observables (`reactive.md`, section 5.1). | #257 |
| `MachineSnapshot` is a record, copied with `with { Status = .. }`; a measurement of `ILocalMachineMetricsService` that fails throws, and a service is told by `service.GetType()`. | The snapshot is a value (`Clone` and `PartialEq`, as a record compares by value) with `with_status`. A measurement that fails is an `Err`, and the trait derives from `Any`, so that a service is told by its type. | Representation | Rust has no records or exceptions, and no `GetType()`. | #257 |
| The input of `ShowcaseState` is a `string`, which the serializer reads as `null` for a document without it. | The input is an `Option`. | Representation | Rust has no null strings. | #257 |
| `ShowcaseSession` is a class that the application keeps and disposes; a storage failure is an `IOException` or an `UnauthorizedAccessException`. | The session is shared (`Rc`), as its subscriptions call it back, and released with `dispose`; a storage failure is any error of the file system. | Representation | The subscriptions hold the session; Rust's file errors are one type. | #257 |
| The example has no DynamicData page. | The DynamicData page (`ViewModels/dynamic_data_view_model.rs`, `ViewModels/feature_item_view_model.rs`, `Models/showcase_feature.rs`, `Views/DynamicDataView.xaml`) with its tests: a `SourceCache` of the features, `auto_refresh`, `filter_dynamic` on a throttled search text, `transform` and `sort_and_bind`. | Addition | No ReactiveUI sample uses DynamicData (`reactive.md`, section 7). | #257 |
| The tests of the example run their asynchronous assertions on a test executor, which `ExampleTestExecutorTests` checks; `CreateMetricsService_Matches_Platform(bool, Type)` is a test with arguments. | The tests have no executor: a test awaits on its own thread with `await_task`, which `example_test_executor_tests.rs` checks the same way. The test with arguments is one test per argument set. | Test | Rust's test harness has no executors and no parameterized tests. | #257 |
| A headless test compares the command of the fail button with `FailWork` by reference. | The command of the button is an adapter of `FailWork` (`ReactiveCommandAdapter`): the test checks that the button has a command that can execute and that clicking it runs `FailWork`. | Test | The button holds an adapter, not the `ReactiveCommand` itself. | #257 |
| A headless test publishes a measurement from a worker thread (`Task.Run(service.Publish)`) and checks that the change of `SampleCount` arrives on the UI thread. | The test publishes on its own thread and checks that the change arrives through the scheduler of the user-interface thread (`ObserveOn(MainThreadScheduler)`), in a job of the dispatcher, on the UI thread. | Test | The observables of the port belong to the thread that creates them (`reactive.md`, section 5.1). | #257 |

### WinUI composition (`src/FerroUI.Base/rendering/composition/`, `src/FerroUI.Controls/animation/`, `src/FerroUI.Controls/hosting/`)

The port of the WinUI 3 composition API (`winui-composition.md`) changes upstream behaviour where WinUI's differs. The differences from WinUI are in `winui-composition.md` section 7.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A `CompositionPropertySet` has no server counterpart; an expression that refers to one reads a copy of its values taken when the animation starts, and its entries cannot be animated. | A property set made with `CompositionPropertySet::new` is live: it has a server counterpart (`ServerCompositionPropertySet`) that receives its entries with each batch, its entries are animatable (`start_animation`), and a reference to it is resolved by id, so expressions read its current values. Its entries are dynamic composition properties, interned by name (`CompositionProperty::dynamic`). The parameters of an animation remain a set without a server counterpart. | Behaviour | WinUI's property sets are live; the property sets of `ElementCompositionPreview`, the effect property set of a connected animation and the interaction tracker depend on it. | #304 |
| `ConnectedAnimation` flies a control (a border with the background, corner radius and border of the source, or an image of it) over the overlay layer with UI-thread animations: a gravity dip of up to 50 px, a box shadow, a cross-fade of backgrounds, coordinated elements faded in over the last 40 %. | In a window rendered by a compositor, the engine is that of WinUI (`connected_animation_flight.rs`, a port of `CConnectedAnimation`): composition sprites in a layer above the content, animated by the compositor, the source hidden from `prepare_to_animate` on, the destination shown live through a visual surface, WinUI's configurations and coordinated entrance; a prepared animation times out after 2 s. The upstream flight remains for a window without a compositor, unchanged. The public members are kept; `cancel`, `is_scale_animation_enabled`, `set_animation_component` and `ConnectedAnimationService::get_for_current_view` are added. | Behaviour | The port of the WinUI composition API (`winui-composition.md`). | #304 |
| An animation is first evaluated when something it reads invalidates it or its clock ticks, so an animation that reads nothing (a constant expression such as `"0.25"`) never writes its property. | An animation started on an active object, and the animations of an object that becomes active, are evaluated with the next frame (`ServerObjectAnimations::schedule_evaluation`). | Behaviour | WinUI applies such an animation; the implicit show and hide animations of `ElementCompositionPreview` are often constant expressions. | #304 |
| The operators of expressions take the same variant on both sides: a `Vector3` with a `Vector3D` (or a `Vector2` with a `Vector`) is invalid. | The single- and double-precision forms of a vector combine in `+`, `-`, `*`, `/` and `==`, in the double-precision form (`unify` in `expression_variant.rs`). | Behaviour | The properties of visuals are double-precision and the entries of WinUI property sets single-precision; an expression written for WinUI combines them (`visual.Offset + props.Offset`). | #304 |
| The expression functions `Matrix3x2.CreateTranslation` and `Matrix4x4.CreateTranslation` build a scale matrix; the 6- and 16-argument `Matrix3x2` and `Matrix4x4` constructors repeat the fifth argument in every later component; function names are matched with case. | Translation matrices, every argument in its component, and function names matched ignoring case, as WinUI. | Behaviour | Defects of upstream, and WinUI compatibility; expressions written for WinUI work unchanged. | #304 |

### Developer tools (`src/FerroUI.Diagnostics/`)

The port of upstream's developer tools at their last open-source version (`src/Avalonia.Diagnostics` at the parent of `71ce62ae32`, where upstream removed them). The sources mark each entry with `Deviation (DEVIATIONS.md, Developer tools)`.

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The property grid of the control details is a `DataGrid` (compiled into the project from the DataGrid submodule) over a `DataGridCollectionView` with a group description, a sort description and a filter. | The grid is the port's `TreeDataGrid` over `PropertiesView`: the groups are root rows of a hierarchical source, with the same groups, order and filter; the columns are made in code; sorting by a column header sorts within each group; collapsed groups stay collapsed across refreshes. `RowBackground` has no counterpart. | Behaviour | The owner's decision: the workspace has a TreeDataGrid and no DataGrid. | #288 |
| `MainView.xaml` shows a banner (`HyperlinkButton`, row 1) that links to upstream's commercial developer tools. | The row is empty. | Missing | The port has no such product; the link would name another project's offer. | #288 |
| The tabs are the four pages of the tools. | `MainViewModel::register_page` adds pages of other crates after them (`DevToolsPage`, `FIRST_EXTENSION_TAB`); disposing the handle it returns removes the page. | Addition | The components page of the declarative API (`ferroui-view`) needs a place in the tools. | #288 |
| The filters use the regular expressions of .NET. | The filters use the `regex` crate: the shared syntax behaves the same; lookarounds, backreferences and other .NET-only constructs do not parse and show as an error of the filter. | Behaviour | The workspace has no engine of .NET's regular expressions. | #288 |
| The clipboard format of a copied selector is `Avalonia_DevTools_Selector`. | `Ferro_DevTools_Selector`. | Behaviour | The name of the format names the framework (`PORTING-GUIDE.md`, naming); an IDE that recognizes upstream's name does not recognize this one. | #288 |
| The selector of a visual names the assembly of its type by its full name (version, culture, public key token). | The name of the markup assembly that declares the type, or the crate of the type when no assembly is registered. | Behaviour | The port has no assembly identity. | #288 |
| The element under the pointer is `TopLevel.PointerOverElementProperty`, which notifies its changes. | `pointer_over_element` of the input root, read when the tools open and after every raw pointer event of the root has been processed. | Representation | The port's top level has no such property; the pipeline updates the input root without a notification. The status bar shows the same element. | #288 |
| The highlight of a control uses `CompositionVisual.TryGetServerGlobalTransform()`. | The transform of the visual to the root of its visual tree (`transform_to_visual`), from the layout. | Representation | The port's composition visual has no server transform to read; both give the same transform once the frame is rendered. | #288 |
| The view locator tests `data is ViewModelBase`. | The markup type of the value and its base types are compared with the list of the view model classes that derive from `ViewModelBase` upstream. | Representation | The view models embed `ViewModelBase` as a field, and markup metadata does not state it as their base type. | #288 |
| The value frames convert a resource key with the markup library's internal conversion, and the brush editor uses the colour picker's internal `ColorHelper.GetRelativeLuminance`. | The crate repeats both (`resource_key_of`, `get_relative_luminance`) with the same cases and formula. | Representation | Both are private to their crates. | #288 |
| The brush editor names the type of a brush by its run-time type. | A brush that is neither an object nor a type with markup metadata shows the name of its contract. | Behaviour | Rust has no run-time type names. | #288 |
| The static `DefaultEvents` of the events page is initialized before the constructor runs, which registers its events. | The constructor makes the set before it reads the registered events. | Representation | Routed events register on first access in the port; without this the defaults would be missing from the tree the first time the page is made. | #288 |
| The window includes `SimpleTheme`, the `Simple` styles of the colour picker and of its DataGrid, and ships the Source Sans Pro font (`Assets/Fonts`). The documents use the resources of the Simple theme (`ThemeControlMidBrush`, `ThemeBorderHighBrush`, `HighlightBrush`, `ThemeAccentBrush`, `ThemeAccentColor`, `ThemeAccentColor4`, `ThemeAccentBrush4`, `ThemeControlHighlightMidBrush`, `SimpleTextBoxClearButtonTheme`). | The window includes `WinUITheme` with `DensityStyle="Compact"` and the Fluent styles of the colour picker and of the TreeDataGrid, and uses the default font of the theme; the font is not shipped. The documents use the resources of the WinUI 3 theme (`CardStrokeColorDefaultBrush`, `ControlStrongStrokeColorDefaultBrush`, `AccentFillColorDefaultBrush`, `AccentTextFillColorPrimaryBrush`, `SubtleFillColorSecondaryBrush`, `ControlFillColorDefaultBrush`, `ControlFillColorSecondaryBrush`, `TextFillColorPrimaryBrush`, `TextBoxDeleteButtonStyle`, ...); the alignment buttons of the layout explorer and the dashed underline of the events page follow the light and dark variants (upstream: black on `WhiteSmoke`, a black stroke); the check boxes, toggles, radio buttons and text boxes of the tools have no WinUI 3 minimum size; the rows of the TreeDataGrids are 24 px high. | Behaviour | The owner's decision: the tools match the default theme of the ControlCatalog and take less space. | #290 |
| The logical and visual tree pages show the nodes in a `TreeView` (items `Nodes`, `SelectedItem` two-way with `SelectedNode`, `IsExpanded` of the item containers two-way with the nodes); the events page shows the routed events in a `TreeView` whose item containers are hidden by `IsVisible` of the nodes. The highlight of the control under the pointer finds the `TreeViewItem` under it. | The trees are `TreeDataGrid`s over hierarchical sources the view models hold (`TreePageViewModel::nodes_source`, `EventsPageViewModel::nodes_source`): one expander column without a header, whose cells are data templates (`TreeNodeCell`, `EventTreeNodeCell`), whose children are the children of a node (`TreeNodeCollection` is a model collection; the events tree has the visible children of a node, kept by `sync_visible_nodes`) and whose expansion is `IsExpanded` of a node. The selection of the rows and `SelectedNode` follow each other; selecting a node expands the rows of its ancestors and brings its row into view, also when the row did not exist. The highlight finds the row of the grid under the pointer and its node; leaving the tree forgets the hovered node, so the highlight comes back when the pointer returns to it. Double tapping a node expands or collapses it, as on the header of an item of a tree view. The nodes of template parts are shown in italics, the classes and the name in lighter colours. | Behaviour | The owner's decision, for the performance of large trees: the rows are virtualized and only the children of the nodes that are shown are listed (a tree view lists them the same way, but realizes a container for every item of an expanded node); and a selection now expands and scrolls to rows that were not realized (upstream selects an item only when its container exists). | #290 |
| The views take the sizes, margins and colours of the Simple theme: a property grid of 100 px values whose editors keep their own heights, a "^" button above the properties, splitters of 4 px, headers in bold and italic text, a status bar without padding. | One sheet of styles for the tools (`DevToolsStyles.xaml`): a spacing of 4, 8, 12 and 16, section headers and captions, dividers of one pixel with a grip of five, a status bar with secondary labels; in the property grid, rows of 28 px, a Value column of 200 px, and editors of one compact form (24 px, the text in the middle), with values that cannot be edited shown as text; the button to the parent property is an icon. | Behaviour | The owner asked for a coherent Fluent design of the tools on the WinUI 3 theme. | #293 |
| `BrushEditor.Render` fills `Bounds`, the place of the editor in its parent, with the brush, black for a null brush, and writes the colour or "(null)". | It draws in its own coordinates, with rounded corners and the outline of a control of the theme; a null brush is an empty swatch with "(null)" in the secondary colour, and a brush that is not a solid colour names its type. Its resources are read for its theme variant. | Behaviour | With a margin, `Bounds` moved the swatch out of the editor; a black block for no brush read as a black brush. | #293 |
| The tabs are the logical tree, the visual tree and the events (and the hot keys); the logical tree is the default view (`DevToolsViewKind.LogicalTree`). | A combined tree is the first tab and the default view (`DevToolsViewKind::CombinedTree`): the logical tree, where each element lists first the parts of its template, greyed out and in italics, then its logical children; a part lists the visual children below it that are not in the logical tree (an element is in the logical tree when it and its logical ancestors are among the logical children of their parents). The tabs after it keep their order (`MainViewModel::COMBINED_TREE_TAB` to `HOT_KEYS_TAB`; the extension pages from 5). | Addition | The owner asked for one tree with the logical structure and the templates of each element. | #293 |
| A menu bar (File, Options, Highlight focused element) above a `TabStrip` and a `ContentControl` for the pages; the window keeps the title bar of the system. | A header in the title bar (the window extends into its decorations, with a title bar of 48 px): a button that shows or hides the tree pane (`MainViewModel::IsTreePaneOpen`, `ToggleTreePane`), the name of the tools, and a `CommandBar` with the commands used most (screenshot, freeze popups, margins, the brush of the focus highlight) and the others in its overflow; the pages are the items of a `TabControl`, which the WinUI 3 theme draws as its tab view, extension pages included. | Behaviour | The owner asked for the tab view of WinUI 3, a pane that can be collapsed, a command bar in place of the menus, and a better use of the title bar. | #293 |
| `MainWindow.RawKeyDown` takes the top level under the pointer as the input root itself (`vm.PointerOverRoot is TopLevel root`). | The input root of a top level is its presentation source, whose root element is the host of the window decorations (`TopLevelHost`) with the top level as its child; `top_level_of_root` finds the top level through the host. | Behaviour | Without it the hot keys that need the hovered top level (Ctrl+Shift to inspect the control under the pointer, Ctrl+Alt+F to freeze popups) did nothing: the root element was never a `TopLevel`. | #302 |
| `PropertyValueEditorView` builds a new editor (a check box, a number box, a combo box, a text box, a brush editor) at every change of its data context. | The view keeps the editor of each kind it has built in a panel that is its content, shows the one of the property and binds it to the property; only the colour, image and geometry editors, which few properties use, are built for their property. | Behaviour | The property grid is a TreeDataGrid whose recycled cells keep their editor view and give it the next row: building, attaching and styling an editor for every row made scrolling and selecting slow. Hiding the editors that are not used, as the TreeDataGrid hides its recycled cells, keeps each one built and styled once. A kept editor has its bound value cleared before it is bound to the next property, so it starts as a new one would. The editors look and behave as upstream's. | #302 |
| `ViewLocator` is an `IDataTemplate` that builds a new view for every view model. | It is also an `IRecyclingDataTemplate`: the view it built for the previous content is reused for a view model of the same view, which gets it as its data context. | Behaviour | The details of a control are a new view model at every selection of a node; building their view again (the property grid, the layout, the value frames) took about 0.5 s in a debug build. The selection now takes a sixth of that (median 580 ms to 85 ms, `FERROUI_SMOKE_DEVTOOLS` of the ControlCatalog). The views follow their data context; the property grid of a reused view of the details scrolls back to the top, as a new view starts. | #302 |
| Each setter of the value frames has its own `ContextMenu` (Copy property name, Copy value, Copy resource key). | Three `MenuFlyout` resources of the view, shared by the setters, as their `ContextFlyout`; the flyout takes the setter it opens on as its data context. | Behaviour | The menus and their items were built for every setter of every selected control and almost never opened. | #302 |
| The styles of the tools take the brushes of the Simple theme, and the included themes of the TreeDataGrid and the colour picker take Fluent brushes (`SystemBaseHighColor`, `SystemListLowColor`, `TabItemHeader*`). | `DevToolsStyles.xaml` gives the brushes of the TreeDataGrid (grid lines, header hover and pressed backgrounds and text, selected cell) and of the colour picker's tabs WinUI 3 values, in a light and a dark dictionary; the hard-coded colours of the tools (the white geometry preview, gray icons, the #34c940 handled events, black swatch borders, the black text of the thickness editor, the #333333 size guides, CornflowerBlue) are WinUI 3 brushes of the same roles; the brush editor writes the text of a mostly transparent colour in the text colour of the theme and draws again when the theme variant changes. | Behaviour | The TreeDataGrid theme keys its light brushes "Default", which is the dark dictionary of the WinUI 3 theme: in a light window its header text turned white on hover and its grid lines disappeared. The fixed colours did not follow the light and dark variants of the theme. | #302 |
| The layout explorer draws the margin, border, padding and content boxes in fixed pastel colours with black text and square corners. | The boxes are the tinted backgrounds of the WinUI 3 theme in the same hues (critical, caution, success) and the accent colour for the content, with rounded corners, the theme's text colours, small captions and the accent outline under the pointer. | Behaviour | The owner asked for the WinUI 3 design; the colours follow the light and dark variants. | #302 |
| No counterpart. | The tree pane (the trees, the routed events) has the background of a navigation pane of WinUI 3, a shade apart from the details; a page whose pane is collapsed and that shows nothing says what to do ("Select a control in the tree, or hold Ctrl+Shift ...", "No events recorded yet ..."); a toggle of the command bar of the header (`MainViewModel::IsSidePaneOpen`, `ToggleSidePane`) shows or hides the side pane of the details (the layout, the value frames, the pseudo-classes). | Addition | The owner asked for a tree pane set apart from the details and for a way to hide the side pane; a collapsed tree pane with nothing selected left the page blank. | #302 |

### Dependency injection (`src/FerroUI.DependencyInjection/`, `tests/FerroUI.DependencyInjection.Tests/`)

The port of Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection at dotnet/runtime v10.0.13 and of Splat's adapter to it (issue #251, `dependency-injection.md`).

| Upstream | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Two packages, `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions` and `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection`; providers are thread-safe and the engine compiles call sites (reflection, emitted IL, the switch to compiled resolution, `EventSource` diagnostics). | One crate. Providers are per-thread `Rc` handles, neither `Send` nor `Sync`; resolution interprets the call sites, with no compilation and no event source. | Representation | FerroUI's object model is single-threaded (`Rc`), as the reactive crates are; Rust has no reflection or IL. | #299 |
| A service type is a `Type`; constructors are found by reflection, chosen with `[ActivatorUtilitiesConstructor]`, with `[FromKeyedServices]` and `[ServiceKey]` parameters; `ActivatorUtilities` activates with untyped arguments. | A service type is a `TypeId` (a trait object too). Registrations take typed, fallible factories, and the `service_factory!` macro writes one from a constructor's parameters and records them as dependencies for validation; a plain closure has no dependencies that validation can see, as an upstream delegate has none. | Representation | Rust has no reflection; the macro is the constructor injection. | #299 |
| Open generic registrations (`typeof(IRepository<>)`) are closed at resolution; `IEnumerable<T>` is a service. | Each generic instantiation is registered as a concrete type; enumeration is a `Vec<Rc<S>>` resolution (`get_services`), and an explicit enumerable is `ServiceDescriptor::enumerable_instance`. | Representation | Rust generics are monomorphized; there is no open type to close at run time. | #299 |
| The provider disposes every service it created that implements `IDisposable` or `IAsyncDisposable`. | A factory registration states its disposal (`on_dispose`, `on_dispose_async`); instances registered as instances stay owned by the caller, as upstream. | Behaviour | Rust cannot ask an arbitrary service whether it implements an unrelated trait. | #299 |
| `Dispose` of a provider or scope with a service that has only asynchronous disposal throws `InvalidOperationException`. | `dispose` returns `ServiceError::AsyncDisposalRequired` before it changes anything, so `dispose_async` can still run; the `IDisposable` implementation, which has no result, panics with that error. | Behaviour | Errors are values; the trait method of `IDisposable` cannot return one. | #299 |
| A disposed provider's services are unusable objects; a scope is disposed when its `using` ends. | Handles are cloneable and dropping one disposes nothing: `dispose` or `dispose_async` releases the services and the cycles between them and the provider; `Rc` handles to services stay valid Rust values after disposal. | Representation | Ownership of `Rc` handles; disposal is explicit as upstream's `Dispose` call. | #299 |
| The extension methods of `IServiceCollection` (`AddSingleton`, `TryAdd`, `TryAddEnumerable`, keyed variants); `TryAddEnumerable` compares implementation types found by reflection. | Inherent methods of `ServiceCollection`; `try_add_enumerable` needs the implementation type stated with `with_implementation::<I>()`. | Representation | Rust has no extension members; the implementation type of a factory is not discoverable. | #299 |
| A change of a read-only collection throws `InvalidOperationException`; `TryAddEnumerable` with an implementation type that cannot be told from the service type throws `ArgumentException`. | Both panic (`assert!`). | Behaviour | Programming errors, which panic in Rust; errors of resolution remain `ServiceError` results. | #299 |
| `MicrosoftDependencyResolver.GetService` and `GetServices` throw when the provider cannot resolve; `ServiceRegistrationCallback` throws `NotSupportedException`. | The adapter panics with the error; `service_registration_callback_erased` panics. | Behaviour | `IReadonlyDependencyResolver` returns an `Option`, which cannot carry the error. | #299 |
| `MicrosoftDependencyResolver.UpdateContainer(IServiceProvider)` disposes the current provider, even when it is the provider passed in, and the resolver then holds a disposed provider. | `update_provider` disposes the current provider only when another one replaces it. | Behaviour | Handing the resolver its own provider (to freeze it) leaves it usable. | #307 |
| `RegisterLazySingleton` registers a factory of one shared `Lazy<T>`; the provider that a later registration replaces disposes the value when it is disposable, and the next provider returns the same, disposed value. | The value is shared across rebuilds as upstream, and its disposal runs once, when the resolver is disposed, if the value was created. A constant's disposal callback runs at the resolver's disposal too, resolved or not. | Behaviour | The value stays usable for the life of the resolver. | #299 |
| Contracts are strings, with `null` the default contract. | A contract is a service key; `None` and `Some("")` are different keys. A Splat factory that returns null resolves to `None` and is left out of enumerations. | Representation | Keys are typed values; Splat's null result is an `Option`. | #299 |
| The tests of the call-site compiler, reflection, `EventSource` diagnostics and the CLR's type-assignability and argument-null checks. | Not ported; the generated inventories list the exclusions. Call-site tests assert the metadata and cache identities instead, generic constraints use closed instantiations, and invalid abstract or null constructor arguments are compile tests. | Test | No counterpart in Rust. | #299 |
| `AsyncServiceScope` and the optional capabilities (`IServiceProviderIsService`, `ISupportRequiredService`, `IAsyncDisposable`) are interface casts. | `AsyncServiceScope` wraps a scope; the capabilities are trait methods; `typed_key::<T>()` checks the type of a key at activation. | Representation | Rust cannot cast a trait object to another trait. | #299 |

### Behaviors (`src/Behaviors/`, a port of Xaml.Behaviors)

The port of Xaml.Behaviors (issue #281, plan in `docs/porting/behaviors.md`). The reference is a library on top of upstream, not upstream itself; the rows compare with it. The sources mark each entry with `Deviation (DEVIATIONS.md, Behaviors)`.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Generic bases `Behavior<T>`, `Trigger<T>`, `StyledElementBehavior<T>`, `StyledElementTrigger<T>` throw when attached to an object that is not a `T`. | Non-generic classes (`BehaviorOfT` and the others) store the expected type a deriving class passes to `construct`; attaching to another type panics, and `associated_object_as::<T>()` is the typed accessor. | API | Classes of the class model are not generic. | #298 |
| Members are found by name with reflection (`GetRuntimeProperty`, `GetRuntimeMethods`, `GetRuntimeEvent`). | Members are found in the markup metadata of the types, the registered properties and routed events (`helpers/member_lookup.rs`, `ferroui_base::metadata::members`). | Behaviour | There is no run-time reflection; only types that declare markup metadata are reachable, the rule string-path bindings already follow. | #298 |
| `item is IBehavior` tests an object of a behavior collection for a contract. | A class registers the contracts it implements in its static constructor (`<dyn IBehavior>::register::<MyBehavior>()`). | API | An object of the class model is not a trait object. | #298 |
| Behaviors, behavior collections, event triggers and reversible property changes hold their host, source or target strongly. | They hold them weakly. | Behaviour | The host holds its behaviors through `Interaction.Behaviors`; a strong reference back would keep it alive. | #298 |
| `Interaction` installs static event handlers on the host and replaces them by delegate identity (`-=` then `+=`). | The subscriptions of a host are disposables kept in a private attached property and replaced as a set. | API | Handlers have no identity; subscriptions are disposables. | #298 |
| Behaviors name their logical-tree hooks `OnAttachedToLogicalTree` / `OnDetachedFromLogicalTree`. | `on_attached_to_logical_tree_event` / `on_detached_from_logical_tree_event`. | API | The names would hide the styled element members of the same name. | #298 |
| `ParseHelper` keeps a table of the `Parse` methods of 40 framework types; `TypeConverterHelper` chains `Enum.Parse`, `IConvertible` and `TypeDescriptor`. | The text conversion of the untyped values (every type states its parser in its markup metadata). | Behaviour | One conversion for markup, bindings and behaviors. | #298 |
| `CommandCanExecuteObserver` posts the callback to the UI thread when a command notifies from another thread. | The callback runs on the thread of the command. | Behaviour | Commands and observers are not `Send`. | #298 |
| `ReversiblePropertyChange` and `ReversiblePropertyChange<TTarget, TValue>` share a name. | The untyped one is `UntypedReversiblePropertyChange`. | API | One Rust name cannot be both. | #298 |
| Extension members of `AvaloniaObject` (`extension(AvaloniaObject)`). | The trait `FerroObjectBehaviorsExtensions` implemented for `FerroObject`. | API | Rust has no extension members. | #298 |
| Animations: overloads (`SelectionIndicatorAnimation.TryStart` with four visuals), a constructor with optional named parameters (`CompositionAnimationDefinition`), a property with a private setter (`OrbitAnimation`). | A separately named method; a struct literal with `..Default::default()`; a cell so the methods take `&self`. Argument exceptions are panics with the runtime's message. | API | Rust has no overloads or optional parameters. | #298 |
| Interactions: `Task` and `Task.Delay`. | `TaskHandle<T>` / `ActionTask` (a dispatcher task compared by identity) and a `delay` future; clipboard operations and picker handlers run in dispatcher jobs. | API | No `Task` type; work stays on the UI thread. | #298 |
| Interactions: properties typed `IClipboard`, `IStorageProvider`, `IStorageFolder`, `IAsyncDataTransfer`. | Identity handles (`ClipboardRef`, `StorageProviderRef`, ...); picked items reach commands as handle lists. The picker `Pick` events are Rust handler lists, not declared to markup. | API | The trait objects have no equality. | #298 |
| Interactions: the picker start location blocks with `GetResult()`; `TrackDispatchedPickerOperation` accepts any thread. | Resolved asynchronously (directory creation skipped on wasm); must be called on the UI thread. | Behaviour | No blocking on the UI thread. | #298 |
| Interactions: `ChangePropertyAction` holds the target it changed; `ReversibleActionExecution.Execute` returns the actions. | The target is held weakly; the actions are returned as `Ref<FerroObject>` with a `revert` helper. | Behaviour | An action that changed its host would keep it alive. | #298 |
| Interactions: `ObservableStreamBehavior` error actions receive the exception. | They receive the error message as text; the source is an `Rc<dyn IObservable<..>>`. | API | No exception objects. | #298 |
| Interactions: `GetClipboardDataAction` deserializes `BinaryFormatter` payloads. | Not ported; an invalid format identifier is caught by validation. | Waived | Obsolete and unsafe in .NET; no .NET run time. | #298 |
| Interactions: `FileSystemWatcherTrigger` uses `FileSystemWatcher`; `NetworkInformationTrigger` uses `NetworkChange`; `HttpRequestAction` uses `HttpClient`. | Behind the features `file-system` and `network`: a watcher polling every 500 ms (a rename is a delete and a create, files only); a check every 2 s whether a datagram socket connects to a public address; HTTP/1.1 over a standard socket, `http` only (an `https` URL ends with an error and status 0). | Behaviour | No platform services of these kinds in the framework yet; the browser has neither. | #298 |
| Interactions: the converters' static `Instance` properties; the stream converters return streams. | A markup field `Instance`; the stream converters return a `TaskHandle` around the opened stream, the path converter a `Uri`. | API | Static properties are fields in markup metadata. | #298 |
| Events: the hooks of the event behaviors take `object? sender`; `DoubleTapped`, `RightTapped`, `Tapped` take `RoutedEventArgs`. | The sender is `&Interactive` (the element the handler is attached to); those three hooks take `&dyn IRoutedEventArgs`, so a subclass can downcast to `TappedEventArgs`. | API | The routed handlers of the framework receive the element. | #298 |
| Responsive: `Setters` are `AvaloniaList<AdaptiveClassSetter>` / `AvaloniaList<AspectRatioClassSetter>`; `SourceControl` and `TargetControl` are strong references. | Named lists `AdaptiveClassSetters` / `AspectRatioClassSetters`; the controls are `Option<ElementRef<Control>>` (weak), as `Label.Target`. | API | A generic list of a type of another crate cannot get markup metadata (the orphan rule). | #298 |
| ReactiveUI: `NavigateToAction<TViewModel>`, `NavigateToAndResetAction<TViewModel>` (with the protected virtual `ResolveViewModel`), `InteractionTriggerBehavior<TInput, TOutput>`. | Not generic: the view model type is given at creation (`new::<VM>()`, so markup cannot create those two actions); `resolve_view_model` is a virtual of the class; the interaction is an `Option<AnyInteraction>` (input `Clone + PartialEq`, output `Default`) with typed accessors, and binding one needs a registered cast: the crate registers `Interaction<(), ()>`, and an application registers the casts of its other interaction types in its own crate, with the types stated, so the markup compiler reads them. The actions return a boxed `bool`. | API | Classes of the class model are not generic. | #298 |
| Draggable and DragAndDrop: handlers are removed by delegate identity; `IList` items sources are mutated through `IList`. | Handlers are removed by stored tokens; an items source is mutated only when it is a `FerroList` or an `ObservableCollection` of common item types. | API | Closures have no identity; items sources are read-only. | #298 |
| Draggable: `ItemDragBehavior.MoveDraggedItem` throws when the behavior is attached elsewhere. | Re-attaches only when free or already on that container. | Behaviour | A virtualized container may be reused. | #298 |
