| DragAndDrop: `DropHandlerBase` and `BaseTreeViewDropHandler` are abstract classes with overloads; the context store, the managed drag service and the preview service are static; store keys are GUIDs; the data formats use the reference's namespace. | Traits with blanket impls (`base_*`, `move_item_between`, `swap_item_between`, `validate_tree_view`); per thread; keys from a counter; formats `FerroUI.Xaml.Interactions.DragAndDrop.*`. `PanelDragBehavior.Context`, `TargetControl` and `ItemsControl` are weak `ElementRef`s; dropped files are `FerroList<Rc<dyn IStorageItem>>` (`StorageItemValue` makes them boxable); private `Drop` handlers are `drop_event_handler`. | API | Rust has no class inheritance of this kind or overloads; `drop` names the destructor. | #298 |
| Projects `Interactions.Scripting` (C# run with Roslyn), `Interactions.DragAndDrop.DataGrid` and `SourceGenerators`. | Not ported. | Waived | No C# run time in a Rust application; FerroUI has no `DataGrid`; a procedural macro for typed actions and triggers is a later addition. | #298 |
| Reference defects: `ScrollGestureEndedEventBehavior` declares `ScrollGestureEventArgs`, which the event never carries; `MultiDataTriggerBehavior` subscribes twice to its default conditions; `SaveFilePickerAction.FileTypeChoices` is registered on `OpenFilePickerAction`. | `ScrollGestureEndedEventArgs`; one subscription; registered on its own class. | Correction | Defects of the reference. | #298 |
| Custom: generic bases (`ObservableTriggerBehavior<T>`, `PropertyValidationBehavior<TControl, TValue>`, `IValidationRule<in T>`, the generic validation rules) and nested types (`KeyTrigger.FiredOn`, `HorizontalScrollViewerBehavior.ChangeSize`, `SelectingItemsControlSearchBehavior.SortDirection`). | Non-generic classes built with the associated type (and the value `TypeId`); untyped observables and values; rule bounds compared with `ValueTypes::compare` (an unset or non-comparable bound makes the value invalid); `IValidationRule` is an untyped trait found through a contract table; nested types are module-level (`FiredOn`, `ChangeSize`, `SortDirection`); `FluidMoveScope::Self` is `Self_` in Rust and `Self` in markup. | API | Classes of the class model are not generic; Rust has no nested types. | #298 |
| Custom: `x:TypeArguments` closes the generic classes in markup (`<PropertyValidationBehavior x:TypeArguments="TextBox, sys:String">`, `<RangeValidationRule x:TypeArguments="x:Double">`, `<ObservableTriggerBehavior x:TypeArguments="x:Int32">`). | Markup cannot give a class of the object model type arguments (the run-time and build-time type systems know generic instantiations only of markup types that declare them, `generic:`), so documents name the classes without them: the rules and `ObservableTriggerBehavior` are untyped, and `PropertyValidationBehavior` has a constructor for markup (`new`) whose behavior is attached to any object and validates the property whatever the type of its value, the `PropertyValidationBehavior<AvaloniaObject, object>` of the reference. | API | Classes of the class model are not generic. | 2026-10-24 |
| Custom: `ObservableTriggerBehavior<T>.Observable` is an `IObservable<T>` a binding assigns as it is (`Observable="{Binding Values}"`). | A binding hands the trigger the stream value of the binding system (`ObservableValue`, or its nullable form) for a property of type `IObservable<T>`; the trigger subscribes to its observable of untyped values, as to an `Rc<dyn IObservable<..>>`. | Representation | Observables are held untyped in properties of the port. | 2026-10-24 |
| Custom: element-valued properties a behavior or action does not own (`TargetControl`, `SourceControl`, `TargetObject`, `[ResolveByName]` targets, `TopLevel`, `FocusControl`, `Window`, `ScrollViewer`, `ItemsControl`, ...) and resolved sources are strong references. | Weak `ElementRef`s, in every folder of the crate. | Behaviour | An element holds its behaviors; a strong reference back keeps it alive. | #298 |
| Custom: `ChangeAvaloniaPropertyAction`; collections typed `AvaloniaList<Case>`, `AvaloniaList<SplitViewStateSetter>`, the validation rule list; properties typed `IAnimationBuilder`, `WriteableBitmap`, `Screens`. | `ChangeFerroPropertyAction`; named lists `CaseCollection`, `SplitViewStateSetters`, `ValidationRules` (items that are not rules are skipped); identity handles `AnimationBuilderRef`, `WriteableBitmapRef`, `ScreensRef`. | API | The framework name; a generic list of a type of another crate cannot get markup metadata (the orphan rule); the traits have no equality. | #298 |
| Custom: routed-event triggers and command behaviors receive typed event args and `object? sender`; C# events of behaviors (`ViewportBehavior`, `ContextDialogBehavior`, `ClipboardMonitorBehavior.ContentChanged`) are .NET events; the collection-changed actions receive `NotifyCollectionChangedEventArgs`. | `RoutedEventTriggerBaseOfT` and `RoutedEventTrigger` take an untyped `RoutedEvent` and a `&dyn IRoutedEventArgs` (downcast), the sender is `&Interactive` (`&ItemsControl` for the container events); the events are Rust handler lists (also declared as markup events where triggers find them by name); the actions receive a `CollectionChange`. | API | The routed handlers and events of the framework. | #298 |
| Custom: members found by reflection (`AsyncLoadBehavior`, `CallMethodAsyncAction`, the ViewModel actions and triggers); awaited tasks; `Process.Start`; `Stopwatch`; `Debug.WriteLine`. | Members found in the markup metadata; results not awaited (`AnimationTask::on_completed` with a dispatcher post for animations); the launcher of the sender's top level (no files on wasm); the dispatcher clock; standard error in debug builds. Properties without a getter make Increment/Toggle return false; `ViewModelPropertyChangedTrigger` passes the property name as the parameter. | Behaviour | No reflection, `Task` or process API in the port. | #298 |
| Custom: items sources are mutated through `IList`; `IList`, `IEnumerable` and `INotifyCollectionChanged` are observed separately; out-of-range indexes and items that do not convert throw. | Only a `FerroList` or `ObservableCollection` of common item types is writable; everything is an `ItemsSource` and a list that does not notify is not observed; such operations do nothing and return false. | Behaviour | Items sources are read-only in the framework. | #298 |
| Custom: services the framework does not have: HTTP upload (`UploadFileBehaviorBase`, `UploadFileAction`), debugger break, console beep, screen reader announcements, network availability; `LogAction` formats with composite format strings. | After the reference's checks nothing is uploaded and the command does not run; `BreakAction` returns true; `ConsoleBeepAction` writes the bell character; `ScreenReaderAnnounceAction` returns false; `NetworkStatusTrigger` takes reports through `on_network_availability_changed(bool)`; `LogAction` writes to the framework logger and standard output, ignoring format specifiers. `RegexValidationRule` uses the `regex` crate (no look-around or back-references; an invalid pattern matches nothing). | Behaviour | No such services yet; no .NET regular expressions. | #298 |
| Custom reference defects: `ExecuteCommandBehaviorBase.FocusControl` registered under the name `CommandParameter`; `TextInputTrigger.Text`, `ClearItemsControlAction.ItemsControl` and `HideAttachedFlyoutBehavior.IsFlyoutOpen` registered on another class; `RoutedEventTriggerBehavior` leaves its old handler attached; `ItemsControlContainerEventsBehavior` re-adds a handler on dispose; `CollectionChangedBehavior`/`Trigger` subscribe twice; `ActiveScreenBehavior` and `DragControlBehavior` leak subscriptions; `AutomationNameBehavior` writes `Name`. | Each corrected. | Correction | Defects of the reference. | #298 |
| The tests of the reference run in an application with the Fluent theme. | The ported tests that need control templates start the unit-test application with the Fluent theme and a mock global clock. | Tests | Items controls realize containers only with their templates. | #298 |

### StaticViewLocator (`src/StaticViewLocator/`, `tests/FerroUI.StaticViewLocator.Tests/`, `samples/StaticViewLocatorDemo/`)

The port of StaticViewLocator (<https://github.com/wieslawsoltes/StaticViewLocator>, commit `fc362d57`), a source generator of view locators; the plan, the mapping of every option and member, and the decisions are in `static-view-locator.md`. The reference is that project, not upstream.

| Reference | Port | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| An incremental source generator sees the compilation and adds a `partial class` to the annotated locator; the attribute types are injected source. | The generator runs in the build script of the crate (`ferroui-static-view-locator-build`): it reads the sources with the scanner of the markup compiler and `syn`, and the models of the dependencies, and writes one file per locator below `OUT_DIR`. `#[static_view_locator]` (a proc macro) includes the file, an `impl` block of the struct. | Representation | A proc macro sees one item and cannot find the view models and views of a crate; the build script sees the whole crate, as a generator sees the compilation. | #310 |
| The MSBuild properties `StaticViewLocator*` (`CompilerVisibleProperty`). | The setters of `StaticViewLocatorBuild` in `build.rs`; `property(name, text)` reads the text of a property as the reference does. | Representation | A Rust crate has no MSBuild properties; the build script is where its build is configured. | #310 |
| Namespaces; the default rule `ViewModels=Views`. | Module paths; the default rules `view_models=views` and `_view_model=_view`. | Behaviour | A Rust crate lays out one module per type (`settings_view_model.rs` beside `settings_view.rs`) as often as one module of view models beside one of views. | #310 |
| The hint name of a locator is its name, qualified by its namespace when two locators share a name. | The generated file is named after the struct; two locators of one name in a crate state distinct ids (`id = ".."`, SVL0009). | Behaviour | The attribute includes the file and cannot know the module of the struct. | #310 |
| The generated adapters test the types they cannot enumerate at run time (`data is MappedBase`, `data is IMappedContract`), and infer a non-generic contract for an open generic view model. | The precedence is flattened at build time into the table of the views for every type of the crate; types of other crates resolve exactly. An open generic view model mapped in a generated adapter is reported (SVL0008), unless its mapped traits are ambiguous first (SVL0007). | Behaviour | Rust cannot test a value for a trait at run time, a struct derives from no class, and each instantiation of a generic type has a `TypeId` of its own. | #310 |
| The legacy fallback walks `BaseType`, `GetInterfaces()` and `GetGenericTypeDefinition()` at run time. | The base and interface walks are flattened into tables at build time; the generic definition is the path of the type (`std::any::type_name`) without its generic arguments. | Representation | No run-time reflection over Rust types; the walks give the same view for the types of the crate. | #310 |
| An interface view model is a key of `s_views`. | A trait view model is keyed by `dyn Trait` when the trait is dyn-compatible, else it has no entry of its own; its mapping is flattened onto its implementors in both cases. | Representation | `TypeId::of::<dyn Trait>` does not compile for a trait that is not dyn-compatible. | #310 |
| The default hooks are `protected virtual` (`private` on a sealed locator); an overload with other parameter kinds does not suppress a generated member. | The hooks are private methods; a method of the locator with the name of a hook replaces it, and one that cannot stand for it (another receiver or number of parameters) is reported (SVL0002), as is an inherent `build` or `match_` beside the generated data template. | Behaviour | A Rust struct has no subclasses and a type has no overloads. | #310 |
| `StaticViewLocatorIncludeInternalViewModels` takes internal view models of an assembly that gives `InternalsVisibleTo`. | Accepted and without effect: a view model of another crate is taken only when it is public. Referenced view models are the types of the `.xamlmeta` models of the dependencies. | Missing | Rust has no `InternalsVisibleTo`, and a crate's model holds the types its declaration macros state. | #310 |
| The generated `IViewLocator` assigns `IViewFor.ViewModel` of any view that implements `IViewFor`; it discovers views by `ReactiveUI.IViewFor<TViewModel>`. | A view of ReactiveUI is a control class that implements `IControlViewFor`, shown through a `ControlViewFor`; it is discovered by that `impl`. The generated locator implements FerroUI's `IViewLocator` (`resolve_view_typed`, `resolve_view`, `resolve_view_unsafe`) and the inherent `resolve_view_for::<VM>` and `resolve_view_default::<VM>`; the view locator needs the feature `reactiveui` (SVL0001 without it). | Representation | ReactiveUI's views are shared trait objects in FerroUI; the trait of the locator is the port's. | #310 |
| The tests compile each source text into an assembly of its own; a test of the generator also compiles its output. | The tests of the generator check the text of the output; the runtime tests are modules of one crate whose build script generates their locators, so every locator sees the view models of every module. Tests of C# features Rust lacks (`ref` overloads, `dynamic`, sealed classes, run-time type tests, base-class contracts) check the Rust counterpart under the same names. | Test | A Rust test cannot compile a crate; the generated source of each shape is compiled and run by `tests/FerroUI.StaticViewLocator.Tests`. | #310 |
| The demos write their views in markup (AXAML) with compiled bindings, and `MainWindow` is a window class. | The sample writes its views with the builders of `ferroui-view` in `UserControl` classes, from the view model of their data context; both demos are tabs of one window. | Behaviour | The sample shows the locators, which are independent of how a view is written. | #310 |

### WinUI additions to upstream controls (`src/FerroUI.Controls/`)

WinUI members added to upstream-ported controls (`winui-controls.md`, section 6). Each is opt-in: its default reproduces upstream exactly, and a test checks that default. The WinUI controls that FerroUI ports as separate types (`winui-controls.md`, section 3) are new controls, not deviations; their differences from WinUI are in `winui-controls.md`. Columns: the WinUI source member, the member added and the default that keeps upstream, the kind (**Behaviour (opt-in)**), why, and the pull request.

| WinUI source | Addition and default | Kind | Why | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## Corrected divergences

Places where the port did work that upstream does not do, or did it differently, and a later change made it match upstream again. They are kept here so a regression can be recognised. Extra allocations and copies on hot paths belong here too: `PORTING-GUIDE.md` rules out allocations on hot paths that upstream avoids.

| Upstream | What the port did | Corrected by |
|---|---|---|
| Reflection reports the namespace a type is declared in, at run time and in the compiler alike. | Run-time type system of markup (`runtime_type_system.rs`): projected a static type that links its metadata through `type_info` with the namespace registered for its Rust module, while the build-time scanner (`src/FerroUI.Build.Scan/scanner/mod.rs`) gives it the namespace its metadata declares (`namespace:`), so a type such as a static helper of a feature module declared in a sub-namespace had two different names; a lookup by the module namespace found it as well. Now both use the declared namespace; `a_static_type_has_the_namespace_its_metadata_declares` and `every_static_type_with_a_runtime_type_has_its_declared_full_name` (`runtime/type_system/tests.rs`) cover it. Found by #269. | 2026-10-23 |
| `Compositor.TryGetRenderInterfaceFeature`, `TryGetCompositionGpuInterop` and `IRenderer.TryGetRenderInterfaceFeature` return a `ValueTask`: with a background loop and no cache they await `InvokeServerJobAsync(Server.RT_GetRenderInterfaceFeatures)`, so the first request is answered. `OpenGlCompositionInterop.TryCreateCompatibleGlContextAsync`, `OpenGlControlBase.InitializeAsync` and `Initialize` of the OpenGL interop page of the catalog await them. | They were synchronous. A compositor confined to its render thread without a cache answered `None` and posted the job, so that a later call had the answer: the first request for the GPU interop of a window that had not rendered yet came back empty (the GpuInterop sample asked again after the next job of the render thread), and the OpenGL control and page gave up. | framework-fixes-gpuinterop (no pull request yet) |
| `ContentPresenter.CreateChild` presents a content that is a control as the control (`content as Control`), whatever the static type of the property it came from; `ILogical` content is a logical child. | `Control::from_boxed` and `logical_from_boxed` recognised a control only when the value was boxed as a `Ref<Control>`. A binding passes on the value with the type of its source property, so a control bound to `Content` from a property of the type of a subclass (`Ref<Border>`) or of an interface was shown as its text (the GpuInterop sample typed `GpuDemo.Demo` as `Option<Ref<Control>>` instead of the interface). They now see the object behind a handle of any class, and behind a registered interface handle (`ValueTypes::register_interface_object`); so do the checks of `RelativePanel`, `ItemsControl` and `SelectingItemsControl` that asked for a `Ref<Control>` or `Ref<Visual>`. | framework-fixes-gpuinterop (no pull request yet) |
| `RenderDataStream` records opcodes and payloads into a byte stream. | Recorded a `Vec<RenderDataOp>` of an enum. | #26 |
| `VisualAncestorElementNode` subscribes to `VisualLocator.Track`. | Followed the attachment events of the element itself, and panicked with "Cannot find a Visual to get a visual ancestor." where upstream throws "Cannot find an ILogical to get a visual ancestor.". | #27 |
| `GetLayoutRoot` and `GetLayoutManager` are extension methods of `Visual`. | Declared them on `Layoutable` only. | #27 |
| `ServerCompositionVisual.ServerTreeWalker.Walk` reads `Children.List.Count` and allocates nothing. | Allocated an empty `Rc<Vec>` for every visual without a children collection, every frame (`walker.rs`). | #29 |
| `CompositionTarget.HitTestChildren` and `HitTestFirstCore` index the children from `Children.Count - 1` down. | Copied the children of every visual entered into a new `Vec` (`composition_target.rs`). | #29 |
| `CompositionContainerVisual.OnRootChangedCore` enumerates `Children` in place. | Copied the children of every visual of an attached or detached subtree (`visual.rs`). | #29 |
| `ValueStore` (`OnInheritanceAncestorChanged`, `OnInheritedEffectiveValueChanged`, `OnAncestorInheritedValueChanged` and the other inherited-value loops) indexes `GetInheritanceChildren()` in place. | Copied the inheritance children of every object a change passed through (`value_store.rs`). | #29 |
| `ValueStore.ReevaluateEffectiveValue` and `ReevaluateEffectiveValues` index `_frames` from the last frame. | Copied the frame list for every reevaluation (`value_store.rs`). | #29 |
| `IPseudoClasses.Remove` compares the name in place. | `Classes::remove_pseudo` allocated a `String` of the name for every call (`classes.rs`). | #29 |
| `AvaloniaProperty.GetMetadata` looks the metadata of a type up in a dictionary (`_metadataCache`). | `MetadataTable::get` scanned a vector of every type it had been asked for, which grows with every type a common property (`DataContext`, `Background`, `Foreground`) is read on (`ferro_property_metadata.rs`). Now an `FxHashMap` by the address of the type. | #305 |
| `CompositingRenderer` keeps its dirty visuals and the visuals whose children are recalculated in `HashSet<Visual>`s; a removal is constant time. | `VisualSet::remove` scanned the list for every visual removed (`compositing_renderer.rs`). A removal now drops the key and leaves a stale entry, compacted when the stale entries are half of the list or the set is read; a removed visual is released at the next compaction rather than at once. | #305 |
| `TypeNameAndClassSelector.Evaluate` compares `control.Name` with the name of the selector in place. | Copied the name of the element into a new `String` for every element a name selector was evaluated on (`StyledElement::has_name`, `type_name_and_class_selector.rs`). | #305 |
| `ControlDetailsViewModel` sorts the properties with a comparer of the group and the name (developer tools). | Compared with `property_comparer`, which read the group and copied the name of both properties for every comparison; now `sort_by_cached_key` computes the key once per property (`control_details_view_model.rs`). | #305 |
| `ValueStore.ReevaluateEffectiveValues` indexes `_effectiveValues` to call `BeginReevaluation` of every value. | Copied the list of effective values, with a handle of every value, for that visit, in every reevaluation (the end of every styling pass is one) (`value_store.rs`). | Performance design 04 |
| `ValueStore.SetInheritanceParent` takes the dictionary of old and new values from `AvaloniaPropertyDictionaryPool` (four at most) and releases it. | Made and grew a new list for every change of the inheritance ancestor (`value_store.rs`). | Performance design 03 |
| `IStyleHost.StylingParent` of `StyledElement` is a cast of `InheritanceParent`. | Made a second handle of the inheritance parent and dropped the first, at every step of the walks up the tree (`styled_element.rs`). | Performance design 04 |
| `LogicalAncestorElementNode` subscribes to `ControlLocator.Track`, which finds the ancestor when it is subscribed whether or not the element is attached. | Followed the attachment events of the element itself and reported no ancestor while the element was not attached to a logical tree. | #36 |
| `Classes.AddRange` adds every name of the argument that the collection does not hold yet, duplicates within the argument included. | `Classes::add_range` also dropped a name that appeared earlier in the same argument (`classes.rs`). | #38 |
| `XamlCompilerTaskExecutor` removes every compiled document from the assembly (`res.Remove()`) and answers a load by URI of a public one through the generated `!XamlLoader.TryLoad` (browser-platform.md, section 14, formerly the row "`ControlCatalog.Browser` host: the markup documents of the Fluent and Simple themes"). | The theme crates embedded every compiled document as an asset, and the catalog's browser host selected the feature `remove-compiled-documents` to leave them out, with a hand-written loader that answered only the document of the theme class. | #43 |
| `XamlTypeSystem.FindAssembly(name)` (`SreTypeSystem` of the run-time loader, `CecilTypeSystem` of the build) finds the assembly whose name equals `name` (without regard to case in the SRE type system). | `RuntimeTypeSystem::find_assembly` returned the first assembly whose name contained `name`, so the include transformer looked for the compiled documents of `ferres://Tests/..` in any assembly whose name contains `tests` (`runtime_type_system.rs`). | #53 |
| The `TestRoot` of `Avalonia.UnitTests` is a focus scope (`IFocusScope`). | The `TestRoot` of `ferroui-controls` was not one, so the focus of its content was not remembered for the root (`test_support.rs`). | #51 |
| `TypeUtilities.CanCast<T>` is `value is T`: a value of the type a nullable `T` holds (`int` for `int?`) and an object of class `T` behind a handle of a base class match. | `FuncDataTemplate::for_type` matched a value only when its box held exactly `T` (`func_data_template.rs`). | #51 |
| `LayoutManager.InvalidateMeasure`, `InvalidateArrange`, `ExecuteLayoutPass` and `EnqueueBringIntoView` call `Dispatcher.UIThread.VerifyAccess()` (formerly the Layout row "`LayoutManager` calls `Dispatcher.UIThread.VerifyAccess()`"). | Did not call it, so a call from a thread other than the UI thread was not caught (`layout_manager.rs`). | #51 |
| `UnitTestApplication.Start` disposes the `FontManager` of the scope when the application ends. | Left it undisposed, so a font backend that owns its system fonts (which hold the backend) was never released: about 180 MB per Skia unit test. | #52 |
| `TwoLevelCache` takes an `int` secondary size and throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` for a negative one. | Took a `usize`. | #52 |
| `DataContextNode` and `ParentDataContextNode` pass the data context, an object reference, to `SetValue`. | Put the data context in a new box to normalise it, for every read (`expression_nodes/data_context_node.rs`). | Performance design 05 |
| `UntypedBindingExpressionBase.Convert` and `ConvertBack` call `ShouldLogError` in the handler of the exception of the converter. | Evaluated `should_log_error` before the converter ran, on every conversion (`untyped_binding_expression_base.rs` and its callers). | Performance design 05 |
| `TextBoxTextInputMethodClient.TextViewVisual` is null while the text box has no presenter. | Reported the last presenter the client had (the contract of the base crate had no "none" value), and panicked when it never had one. | `textbox-contract-and-loader` (2026-10-15) |
| `WindowImplBase.FrameSize` (Avalonia.Native) starts the result of `GetFrameSize` at (-1, -1); the native side leaves it so while the window is not shown, and the property is then null, so `Window.SetWindowStartupLocation` centres the client size. | `frame_size` called the generated `get_frame_size`, which starts the result at zero: a window not shown had a frame of no size, and a centred window was placed half its size away from the centre (`WindowTests.StartupLocation` on macOS: 450 for 225, 900 for 700). Now `IFrnWindowBase::get_frame_size_from` with (-1, -1); checked by `tests/accessibility_tree.rs`. | integration-tests-fixes (2026-10-16) |
| `ScrollGestureRecognizer.PointerReleased` starts a `Stopwatch` when the inertia starts, and each frame of the inertia (`OnAnimationRequested`) scrolls by the speed times the time since the previous frame, the first frame by the time since the release. | Timed the inertia with the frame times of the media context and started its clock with the first frame, which therefore scrolled by nothing. A scroll content presenter that chains scrolling (`IsScrollChainingEnabled`, true by default) leaves a scroll event that changes no offset unhandled, and an unhandled inertia event ends the gesture: a touch flick never coasted, on every touch platform, and on a phone in the browser a swipe moved the content only as far as the finger. Not recorded as a deviation. Tests: `the_first_frame_of_scroll_inertia_scrolls` (`input_tests.rs`), `touch_flick_scrolls_on_with_inertia` (`scroll_content_presenter_tests.rs`) and the touch swipe of `scripts/browser/tests/control_catalog.test.mjs`. | `browser-touch-scroll` (2026-10-17) |
